Compared with d06k and v3a on: the f0-invariance probe (now also reported for f0 ≤ 250 Hz); closed-phase F1 on ARCTIC (by voice and vowel group) and PTDB-TUG (by sex, speaker bootstrap; bias, median |error|, the low / mid / high F1 ranges); Hillenbrand by group (paired, informative); σ coverage against the closed phase; guard-rails: PTDB-TUG f0 RPA, the suite, swapped synthetic validation on train_v2's and train_v3b's clips.

**Decision rule.** Adopt train_v3b's distributions for teacher v3 if the closed-phase F1 bias (PTDB-TUG, both sexes) is smaller than d06k's beyond the speaker-bootstrap interval, the low-F1 compression (closed-phase F1 < 450 Hz) shrinks, the probe's |slope| at f0 ≤ 250 Hz is not worse than d06k's by more than a third, and the guard-rails do not regress beyond noise.

**Result (2026-10-10): the rule fails; train_v3b's distributions are not adopted.** `teacher_v3b_d06k` against d06k (and v3a):
- *Closed phase, PTDB-TUG* (`cp_v3b.py`; speaker bootstrap): women's F1 bias +26.3 against +14.6 (v3b − d06k +10.9 [+7.8, +15.5]; v3a +22.6), median |error| +5.4 [+3.3, +8.5], low-F1 bias +14.2 [+11.5, +18.1]; men unchanged (bias −0.4 [−1.6, +0.7]). ARCTIC: bdl +7 (d06k +4), jmk +36 (+26), slt +6 (−7) Hz; equal or higher in every vowel group.
- *Probe*: f0 ≤ 250 Hz |slope| 7.6 (d06k 8.2, v3a 7.8); over the whole sweep 20.8 (13.7, 18.7).
- *Hillenbrand* (`reports/analysis/paired/m2_paired_v3b.txt`): F1 MAE worse in every group (all +2.5 [+1.6, +3.4] Hz); F2–F4 within noise.
- *Guard-rails*: PTDB-TUG f0 RPA worse (all −0.7 [−1.2, −0.3], modal −1.0 [−1.3, −0.7]); swapped validation (`reports/analysis/cross_val/cross_val_v3b.txt`) worse on train_v2's clips (F1 +2.5, F3 +10 Hz MAE), better on its own.

**What this refutes.** D-020's explanation, that removing the coupling raised the F1 prior's centre and the teacher was pulled towards it, predicted that lowering the centre would lower the bias. Across d06k → v3a → v3b the synthetic F1 centre stayed high and then fell to realistic values, while women's closed-phase bias rose +15 → +23 → +26 Hz. Pull towards the prior's centre is therefore not the main cause of the upward F1 bias on real voices; the D-020 "why" paragraph is withdrawn as an explanation (its measurements stand). *Correction (2026-10-10, D-024):* the seed replicate alone moves women's bias +15 → +22 Hz, so the d06k → v3a → v3b trend is not resolved by single runs and does not refute this either way; v3b's Hillenbrand F1 and women's |error| regressions are the parts that stand.

**What it leaves open.**
1. *Run-to-run variance is unmeasured.* Every comparison since D-018 is between single training runs; the bootstrap intervals cover speakers and clips, not initialisation or data order. Differences of 5–10 Hz in F1 bias or 0.7 points of f0 RPA may be within seed-to-seed variation. A replicate of d06k's recipe with another seed sets that floor.
2. *A real-voice property the teacher reads as higher F1*, which Praat largely does not (+2 to +5 Hz on the same frames): open-phase damping (wider F1 bandwidths), source spectrum (H1–H2, tilt), subglottal coupling, or how harmonics sample a real resonance. A sensitivity probe (fixed vowels, one source or physics factor varied at a time, teacher F1 shift measured) can locate it without training.

## D-023 (2026-10-10): glottal coupling within the cycle: what the teacher misreads (planned ablation)

**Terms.** Following Birkholz et al. (2019) and Titze et al. (2015): *resonances* fR1–fR3 are the poles of the closed-glottis tract (what the synthetic labels and closed-phase LPC measure); *formants* F1–F3 are spectral peaks of the actual signal, which also depend on the source. The teacher's target stays fR: it is a property of the tract alone, so before/after comparisons are not moved by changes in the voice source. Formants are what listeners hear, and an effective-formant output may be added later for perceptual questions.

**Sensitivity probe** (`runs/sensitivity*.txt`; 8 Peterson & Barney vowels per group, men f0 120 Hz / women 210 Hz, LF pulse train through per-sample resonators, one factor changed during the open phase only, label fR):
- *The teacher reads open-phase shifts of every formant,* not only F1: about the full open-phase shift for F1 (+5 % → +22 / +25 Hz), 30–50 % of it for F2 and F3 (+5 % → +28 / +34 and +31 / +52 Hz). Wide open-phase F3 bandwidths at women's f0 cause F3 → F4 assignment errors (B3 ×4: F3 +500, F2 +132 Hz).
- *Praat Burg follows open-phase F1 shifts as much as the teacher* (men +28 / +45 Hz for +5 / +10 %). On real voices, whole-cycle LPC agrees with the closed phase to within ±7 Hz (D-019), so open-phase F1 shifts are small in these voices and cannot be what separates the teacher (+15 to +26 Hz above the closed phase for women) from LPC. The earlier claim that Praat ignores them because closure dominates its fit is withdrawn.
- *The teacher reads damping as higher F1, LPC much less:* open-phase B1 ×3 moves the teacher's F1 by +12 (men) / +20 (women) Hz and Praat's by +9 / +7; static B1 ×3 by +8 / +15 against +3 / +8. The teacher−Praat gap is several times larger for women, as on real voices. Leading candidate: **the teacher has learnt "broad F1 peak = higher F1"**, because training never shows wide bandwidths arising from glottal losses on a fixed resonance.

**Physics** (`open_glottis.py`, `reports/analysis/sensitivity/open_glottis.txt`): the lossy tube with the glottis open (Norton form: the glottal slit's kinetic and viscous resistance and inertance, Flanagan 1972, at 800 Pa; in series with a subglottal impedance whose maxima are Ishizaka, Matsudaira & Kaneko's 1976 640 / 1400 / 2100 Hz, scaled by tract length), poles continued from the closed-glottis ones, quasi-static at a fixed open area.
- *Check:* uniform 2.89 cm² duct with a static 0.51 cm² opening, no flow: F1 +12 % with the subglottal load (Barney, De Stefano & Henrich 2007 measured +13 %); +73 % without it, so the subglottal side cannot be left out.
- At open areas 0.05–0.1 cm²: B1 rises 2–4× (to 90–230 Hz), consistent with the +77 to +120 Hz average B1 increase quoted for glottal openings (Hanson's chink analyses). fR1 rises +3 to +12 % for open and mid vowels and +7 to +35 % for close vowels (/i u o/), matching Birkholz et al.'s finding that vowels with fR1 < 350 Hz are much more affected (Uezu & Kaburagi 2017: /i/ up to +20 %, /o/ +10 %). F2 and F3 move +1 to +20 %, but their size and even sign depend on where they sit relative to the subglottal resonances; Birkholz et al. found no systematic F2 or F3 change in LPC estimates. Near the first subglottal resonance F1's continuation can switch branch (negative entries for /æ ʌ ɔ/), so those cells are unreliable.
- Quasi-static values hold the opening fixed; in speech the area rises and falls within the open phase, so the time-averaged effect is smaller. Measured peak glottal areas still need a literature source before any of these are used in training.

**Plan (not started; v3c ablation).**
1. *Synthesis*: a time-domain voiced path (pulse train through `filters.filter_tv`, already used for noise) for some or all voiced clips, with every formant's frequency and bandwidth moving within each cycle from its closed-glottis value towards the open-glottis value at the instantaneous glottal area. Per clip: peak glottal area, glottal length and depth, and subglottal resonances (scaled by length, jittered) are sampled. The tube path computes open-glottis poles per target; the cascade path uses a fit to the tube table. Area goes with the source shape (a longer open phase, a larger opening), a real source → signal link that leaves the labels (fR) untouched, consistent with D-020. Magnitudes come from published glottal-area measurements; ARCTIC and PTDB-TUG are not used to tune them (evaluation sets never fit synthesis priors) and are used only to check afterwards.
2. *Before training*: the renders must reproduce the literature's direction (Praat F1 rises with peak area, more for close vowels; F2 and F3 not systematically moved), and the time-domain path's cost must be measured against the harmonic path.
3. *Heads*: a per-frame source head (Rd or open quotient; labels from the synthesis), evaluated against the EGG contact quotient on ARCTIC and PTDB-TUG for trends only (contact and flow quotients differ).
4. *Decision rule, set now*: adopt if the closed-phase F1 bias (PTDB-TUG, both sexes) falls beyond the run-to-run noise floor (seed replicate `teacher_v2s1`, D-022) and the per-frame correlation of F1 error with the EGG contact quotient shrinks; guard-rails as D-022. Hillenbrand F1 is informative only: its references are LPC formants, which include some coupling, so a better fR tracker may read slightly lower there.

Sources: Birkholz, Gabriel, Kürbis & Echternach (2019), JASA 146(1), 223–232 (`data/downloads/literature`); Barney, De Stefano & Henrich (2007), Acta Acust. 93(6); Uezu & Kaburagi (2017), and Badin & Fant (1984) as cited there; Ishizaka, Matsudaira & Kaneko (1976), JASA 60(1), 190–197, values as quoted by Hanna, Smith & Wolfe and by Wakita & Fant (1983; the latter gives 2150 / 2850 Hz for the upper pair); Flanagan (1972), *Speech Analysis, Synthesis and Perception*; Titze et al. (2015), JASA 137(5).

**Progress (2026-10-10, while `teacher_v2s1` trains).** Implemented, off by default (earlier sets reproduce): `synth/coupling.py` (open-glottis poles; Barney check in the tests), a time-domain voiced path in `voice.synthesize(coupling=...)` that reproduces the harmonic path exactly when nothing moves, and `SynthConfig.p_coupling` for tube clips (glottis drawn per clip, shifts per target, `coupling_scale` per clip). Cost: about 0.9 s per second of audio against 0.25 s without (CPU shared with training), mostly the open-glottis pole search. *Update (2026-10-11), quiet machine:* 0.86 against 0.23 measured; after vectorising the tube chain matrix over sections and computing `open_phase` without the LF root solves, 0.24 coupled and 0.17 uncoupled (`scripts/analysis/coupling_timing.py`). Existing sets regenerate bit-identically. The 40-step continuation jumps to a neighbouring root for about 1 pole in 2000, against 160 steps.

Render check (`coupling_check.py`, `reports/analysis/sensitivity/coupling_check.txt`; Story's vowels, Rd 1.0, peak areas 0–0.2 cm²): Praat's F1 rises with the opening for some close vowels but by less than the quasi-static shifts suggest, and is mixed for open ones; Praat's F3 rises systematically (+50 to +200 Hz), which Birkholz et al. did not find (their simulated F3 trend is positive but not significant). Adding a tracheal impedance floor (ρc / 2.5 cm²) to the subglottal model barely changes the shifts, so the F3 movement comes from the glottal inertance. Early estimates (Flanagan 1965; Badin & Fant 1984) put open-glottis shifts at a few percent, which at F3 is 100+ Hz, so the model is not clearly wrong; the size of F2–F3 shifts is left uncertain, and `coupling_scale` (uniform 0–1 per clip) covers the range from none to the model's. d06k on these renders: F1 +10 to +25 Hz and F2–F3 +10 to +35 Hz at 0.1 cm², and for women's /ɑ/ and /ʌ/ F2 or F3 jumps by 1.1–1.6 kHz (an assignment failure) at any opening.

Still open: glottal areas from published data (Holmberg, Hillman & Perkell 1988 flows, converted with the transglottal pressure; Patel et al. 2026, JSLHR 69(5), open quotient women 0.93, men 0.71, glottal gap index 0.07 / 0.01 of the maximum area), and whether to link the opening to Rd.

**Glottal sizes set (2026-10-10).** From Holmberg, Hillman & Perkell (1988, JASA 84, 511–529, Tables AI–AV, with the erratum's Table AVI, JASA 85, 1787; `data/downloads/literature`), peak flows through Bernoulli at each group's own pressure: peak glottal area men 0.12 cm² (range 0.06–0.24), women 0.07 (0.04–0.12), about the same in soft, normal and loud voice. Sampled log-uniform 0.05–0.22 cm² at 17.5 cm, scaled by length². What changes with loudness is the residual opening in the 'closed' phase (minimum / peak flow 0.22–0.52), which rises about 1.4× as fast as the open quotient within each sex. It is therefore linked to Rd through the rendered open quotient (the LF te), per sample, at no extra rendering cost (`coupling.gap_from_open_quotient`), with a per-clip offset spanning video-measured gaps (Patel et al. 2026) to flow-measured ones. Peak area is not linked to Rd. Transglottal pressure 3.6–12 cm H₂O.

**External check: Birkholz et al.'s (2019) recordings** (`birkholz_eval.py`, `reports/analysis/external/birkholz_eval_d06k.txt`; their supplementary audio, evaluation only: 3D-printed vowel resonators excited by silicone vocal folds, and their simulations, each at five lung pressures, peak glottal area 11–60 mm²; the simulations also include closed-glottis LF renders).
- *Coupling is read by both trackers, the teacher no more than Praat.* Simulations, F1 against each resonator's own closed-glottis render (median): at 11 mm² (about men's normal peak area) d06k +26, Praat +37 Hz; at 60 mm² +101 and +198 Hz. Rise from 11 to 60 mm², physical models: close vowels d06k +59, Praat +84; open vowels +14 and +31 Hz. On real coupling the teacher does not over-read it relative to LPC, so this data does not support the bandwidth explanation from the sensitivity probe; it does show that coupling at speech-like areas raises both trackers' F1 by 25–40 Hz.
- *A coverage hole, unrelated to coupling:* back rounded vowels with F2 near 600 Hz. On the closed-glottis renders of /u/ (F1 212, F2 632 Hz by Praat) and /o/ (290, 612) the teacher misses F1 and reports F2 as F1 and F3 as F2 (606 / 2531, 573 / 2830), with F1 σ of only 180 cents for /o/; the physical models show the same (/u/ 590 against Praat's 340 Hz). Training rarely has F2 that low: the tube cannot round the lips enough (PB-fitted /u/ reaches F2 949 Hz at 17.46 cm, D-022), and the cascade's coverage floor for F2 is 600 Hz × size with F2 ≥ F1 + 200. German, Korean and many other languages' /u o/ sit there.

## D-024 (2026-10-10): run-to-run variance; v1 at d06k's schedule; a faster loop

**Seed replicate** (`teacher_v2s1_d06k`: train_v2, d06k's recipe, training seed 1; `reports/analysis/paired/m2_paired_v2s1.txt`, `invariance/invariance_v2s1.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_v2s1.txt`). Against d06k, with identical data:
- Hillenbrand F1–F4: all within noise (F1 MAE all −0.4, F3 +2.0, F4 −12.7 Hz).
- PTDB-TUG f0 RPA −1.1 [−1.4, −0.9] (all frames), −0.8 (modal), −1.3 (modal < 85 Hz): flagged "worse" by the speaker bootstrap.
- Invariance probe: median |slope| 11.3 against 13.7 Hz/octave; at f0 ≤ 250 Hz 3.3 against 8.2; single vowel-and-length cells move by up to 30 Hz/octave.
- Closed-phase F1, PTDB-TUG: women's bias +7.2 [+5.7, +10.1] Hz (high-F1 range +21), median |error| +0.6; men's bias +0.6, |error| −0.4. ARCTIC: bias +2 / +8 / +6.5 Hz (bdl / jmk / slt).

The bootstrap intervals cover speakers and clips, not training runs, so they understate the uncertainty of any comparison between two runs. One replicate is one sample of the run-to-run spread, so it can rule out small effects but not set a threshold. Re-reading D-020 and D-022 against it:
- *Not attributable to the data change* (within one seed's movement): every f0 RPA difference (v3a +0.2, v3b −0.7 / −1.0); the invariance slopes; v3a's women's bias (+8.0); the high-F1 bias shifts.
- *Stand*: v3b's Hillenbrand F1 regression (all +2.5 [+1.6, +3.4] Hz against −0.4, in every group) and its women's closed-phase |error| (+5.4 against +0.6).
- *Probable*: v3a's men's bias +4.2 and |error| +2.9, against +0.6 and −0.4.
- d06k may be the lucky run: on women's closed-phase bias the replicate sits with v3a, not with d06k (+21.9 against +14.6 and +22.6).

**Two candidate mechanisms for v3b's regression, both ruled out with probes** (no training):
- *Bandwidths* (`sensitivity/sensitivity_bw_cmp.txt`). v3b fixed the cascade's Hawks & Miller f0 term at 132 Hz. train_v2's f0-scaled bandwidths were only ×1.22 wider at 210 Hz and ×1.48 at 300 Hz, on cascade clips only (40 %); the "up to 3.5×" in D-022 applies only at much higher f0. With B1 widened, v3b's F1 moves no more than d06k's (women at 210 Hz: +10 against +15 to +20 Hz; the two v2 seeds +2 to +9).
- *`p_f1_band`* (`sensitivity/f1_ratio_sweep.txt`). F1 300–1000 Hz under f0 120, 210 and 260 Hz, read by F1/f0: no split between the v2 and v3 models near the 2nd and 3rd harmonics; the two v2 seeds differ as much as v2 and v3 do.

**v1 at d06k's schedule** (`teacher_v1_d06k`: v1's cosine run branched at step 6000, where its rate was 0.84e-3, then decayed 1 − √ to zero at 10k like d06k; decay branches now accept cosine parents, main `4e942ab`). Against both v2 seeds (`paired/m2_paired_v1d06k*.txt`, `invariance/invariance_v1d06k.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_v1d06k.txt`):
- *train_v2 is better on F2, F3 and invariance*: Hillenbrand F2 MAE −8.7 [−14.3, −2.8] and −10.8; F3 −35.7 [−56.8, −18.2] and −33.7 Hz. Median |slope| 13.7 and 11.3 against the v1 branch's 49.7 (v1 at 20k: 51.1), so v1's coupling comes from its data, not its schedule.
- *train_v1 is better on closed-phase F1*: PTDB-TUG women's |error| −7.0 [−8.5, −2.9] and −6.1, men's bias −5.0 and −5.5, men's |error| −2.8 and −2.8 Hz, all beyond the seed's movement; ARCTIC lower for bdl and jmk in every vowel group.
- *Within noise*: Hillenbrand F1, f0 RPA (the v1 branch falls between the two v2 seeds). The original comparison's women −2.6 / men +1.9 Hz were partly v1's longer annealed schedule.

Closed-phase F1 bias follows how strongly the training data tie the tract to f0. Women: v1 (fully tied) +11, v2 (half) +15 / +22, v3a (none) +23, v3b (none) +26 Hz. Men: +9, +15 / +17, +20, and v3b +14, where its population tract lengths restore density at men's lengths. This is the trade-off D-020 predicted and accepted. A coupled prior scores better on typical voices but reads the tract from the pitch, as v1's invariance shows. The mechanism is still open. It is not simple pull towards a population centre, which would lower women's F1, not raise it. v1 → v2 also changed other things (v1 trained without codecs, for example), so the tie is the leading candidate, not established.

**Training throughput** (`reports/analysis/bench_small.txt`). Full-size training was GPU-bound at about 60 s of audio per second. The data loader, at about 70 s/s with codecs on, was barely ahead: two ffmpeg processes per coded clip (about 330 ms) serialised across workers on Windows. Opus, AAC and MP3 now run in-process (PyAV, main `77f8289`); the loader gives 134 s/s at 12 workers and 155 at 16, limited by AMR, which PyAV's wheels can't encode. An antivirus exclusion for ffmpeg made no difference. Smaller models on the GPU: S1 (3.2M parameters) 79 s/s, S2 (1.75M) 114 s/s.

**A faster loop: S2 as a proxy (pre-registered).** The planned full-size 3.5k / 5k study is replaced by S2 (channels 8 / 16 / 32, f0 channels 8, embed 192, GRU 96, f0 GRU 64; `va train teacher --model-set`, main `4b6916a`). Recipe: 2000 constant + 1500 decay steps, warmup 1000, comb-truth taper over 1400 steps, 16 loader workers.
- Arms: train_v2, train_v3b and train_v1, seeds 0–2 each (`runs/small/`).
- Measures: Hillenbrand in full, every 3rd PTDB-TUG file per speaker (1579 of 4718, all 20 speakers), and every 3rd closed-phase utterance per speaker. The subsets reproduce the full results (v3b − d06k women's bias +11.7 against +10.9; seed +6.6 against +7.2 Hz). Probe as before.
- *Adopt S2 as the proxy* if it reproduces, beyond its own three-seed spread, the directions known at full size: v1 → v2 F2 and F3 better, invariance much better, closed-phase F1 worse; and v3b's Hillenbrand F1 and women's |error| regressions. Otherwise, fall back to full size at a short budget.

**Weights EMA (pre-registered; main `21558f6`).** Training now keeps a moving average of the weights (decay 0.998, about the last 500 steps), saved as `ema` in each checkpoint and loadable with `<ckpt>#ema`; training itself is unchanged. Seven of the nine S2 runs carry it (all but v2 seeds 0 and 1, which started before it), so each run gives a paired raw-against-averaged comparison with no extra training (`ema_*` reports). *Adopt* if the averaged weights are not worse than the raw ones beyond the paired intervals on any main measure, and narrow the three-seed spread of the v3b and v1 arms on f0 RPA or closed-phase F1 bias. Other optimiser and schedule changes are deferred (`docs/roadmap.md`).

**Result: S2 fails as a proxy (2026-10-11).** Nine S2 runs (`paired/small_*.txt`, all against v2_s0; `invariance/small.txt`; `closed_phase/cp_small.txt`). Arm means across the three seeds, with the range in brackets:

| Measure | v2 | v3b | v1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hillenbrand F1 MAE, all | 36.5 (34.2–37.9) | 37.5 (36.1–39.7) | 34.1 (31.1–36.3) |
| F1 MAE, women | 38.9 (35.7–41.3) | 41.8 (39.8–44.4) | 38.9 (34.6–42.2) |
| F1 MAE, children | 43.3 (41.3–44.7) | 46.6 (43.3–50.2) | 40.4 (37.8–42.4) |
| F2 MAE | 56.4 (52.8–62.7) | 53.8 (53.0–55.0) | 54.4 (49.1–58.3) |
| F3 MAE | 96.8 (87.2–108.4) | 94.9 (82.7–104.1) | 102.4 (88.3–120.7) |
| PTDB-TUG f0 RPA, all frames | 87.0 (86.6–87.3) | 86.6 (85.9–87.0) | 86.3 (85.3–87.8) |
| Invariance median \|slope\| | 22.9 (20.7–24.6) | 21.2 (16.4–25.1) | 56.6 (52.0–63.1) |
| Closed-phase F1 bias, women | +30.9 (+21.4 to +39.3) | +36.6 (+29.5 to +40.5) | +27.4 (+21.5 to +32.0) |
| Closed-phase \|error\|, women | 45.1 (40.7–49.7) | 49.0 (44.8–52.6) | 41.8 (37.7–44.6) |
| Closed-phase F1 bias, men | +28.6 (+16.9 to +39.0) | +28.7 (+26.6 to +29.8) | +25.4 (+18.6 to +31.4) |

Against the pre-registered directions:
- *v1 → v2 invariance much better*: reproduced, far beyond the spread (no overlap; the same size as at full scale).
- *v1 → v2 F2 and F3 better*: not reproduced. F3 is 5.6 Hz better on the means, inside a seed range of 21–32 Hz; F2 is 2 Hz worse.
- *v1 → v2 closed-phase F1 worse*: right direction on every measure (women's bias +3.5, |error| +3.3, men's bias +3.2 Hz on the means), but inside the spread.
- *v3b's Hillenbrand F1 and women's closed-phase |error| regressions*: right direction (women +2.9, children +3.3, |error| +3.9 Hz on the means), but inside the spread; only v3b_s0 shows them clearly.

At this size and budget, seed-to-seed movement is about as large as the effects to be detected. The F3 spread (21 Hz) is two-thirds of the full-size v1 → v2 effect; the women's closed-phase bias spread (18 Hz) is larger than any data effect seen so far. Detecting 3–5 Hz effects would need on the order of 10 seeds per arm, which costs more than full-size runs. Only invariance-sized effects (tens of Hz per octave) can be read from three S2 seeds. So the Conformer goes in as a bench run (one S2 seed: throughput and synthetic validation), not a three-seed comparison. A real-voice architecture comparison falls back to full size, as pre-registered.

Fix found on the way: the EMA chain failed at its first comparison because the evaluation's checkpoint hash did not accept the `#ema` suffix (`teacher_eval._ckpt_id`). The averaged weights now get their own cache id (`<sha>-ema`); the EMA comparisons were restarted at 01:18. EMA verdict: pending.

**Result: EMA rejected (2026-10-11).** Seven paired raw-against-averaged comparisons (`paired/ema_*.txt`, `invariance/small_ema.txt`). The averaged weights are worse beyond the paired intervals on at least one main measure in five of the seven runs: women's F1 MAE +0.6 to +2.2 Hz (four runs), F4 +4 to +12 Hz (two), v3b_s0 F3 +4.7 Hz. f0 RPA is a little better in six of seven (+0.1 to +0.5), and invariance |slope| is lower in all seven (by 1–10 Hz/octave). The three-seed f0 RPA spread does not narrow (v3b 1.1 → 1.0, v1 2.5 → 2.5 points), so both conditions fail. As expected after a decay to zero, which already averages late weights. EMA stays in the checkpoints (it costs nothing) but is not used for evaluation.

**Conformer bench (2026-10-11; `runs/small/conf_v2_s0`).** One seed, on S2 v2's recipe, against the three GRU v2 seeds. Synthetic validation at 3.5k steps (where the three GRU seeds agree to within about 1 Hz on F1 and 3 Hz on F3, so this measure is far less noisy than the real-voice ones):

| Measure | GRU v2 (3 seeds) | Conformer |
|---|---|---|
| F1 MAE | 40.3–41.2 | 35.8 |
| F2 MAE | 71.6–73.6 | 71.6 |
| F3 MAE | 95.4–98.4 | 91.8 |
| F4 MAE | 149.5–154.7 | 141.5 |
| f0 RPA | 98.8–98.9 | 98.7 |
| Throughput (mean of logged s/s) | 94–107 | 92 |

F1 4.5 Hz and F4 8 Hz better than every GRU seed; F3 better; f0 a hair worse. Throughput slightly lower, not 13 % higher as the short GPU check suggested. Wall time 47 min against about 37 min, though some of that was the EMA closed-phase step's CPU load at the start. Synthetic gains have not always carried to real voices (D-020), and real-voice comparisons at S2 size are too noisy (above). The next step is a full-size Conformer run against the full-size v2 seeds.

**Conformer, real voices (2026-10-11; `paired/small_conf_v2_s0.txt`, `invariance/small_conformer.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_small_conformer.txt`).** Against v2_s0, the same seed's GRU:
- *Hillenbrand much worse*, far outside any seed spread: F1 MAE +26.7 [+23.7, +30.0] Hz (women +37, children +26, men +17), F3 +48, F4 +94 Hz. PTDB-TUG f0 RPA −2.3 points. Invariance |slope| 28.8 against 21–25 (at f0 ≤ 250 Hz: 24.3 against 10–12).
- *Closed-phase F1 as good as or better than the best GRU seed* (PTDB-TUG women's bias +23.9, men's +16.7, against v2_s0's +21.4 / +16.9; ARCTIC bdl and jmk |error| the lowest of the four).

The split follows clip length. The models train on 2 s crops (200 frames) and `analyse()` runs whole files. Hillenbrand words are 0.55 s (median; 0.43–0.68 s), while PTDB-TUG and ARCTIC utterances run for several seconds. On synthetic clips cut to different lengths (`scripts/analysis/len_test.py`; 150 clips, F1 MAE in Hz):

| Input length (frames) | 30 | 50 | 100 | 200 | 400 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GRU v2_s0 | 52.4 | 40.8 | 31.3 | 29.5 | 27.4 |
| Conformer | 74.0 | 44.6 | 31.8 | 26.5 | 24.6 |

Both models degrade on short inputs; the Conformer much faster. The Conformer is better from 200 frames up and worse below about 100. So the Hillenbrand result is a length-generalisation failure, not a worse model. The fix is training on mixed crop lengths (D-021's open item), which the GRU also needs. The synthetic validation set uses 200-frame crops only, which hid this; it needs short-crop cases.

**Mixed crop lengths (2026-10-11; main `fd1adeb`, `25032cf`, `896adde`; `sensitivity/len_test_mixcrop.txt`, `paired/small_mix_*.txt`, `invariance/small_mixcrop.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_small_mixcrop.txt`).** S2, seed 0, v2 recipe, one GRU and one Conformer. Crop lengths come from 8 values spaced geometrically over 32–400 frames, one per batch. The batch size scales so every step holds the same frames (64 s of audio), and short crops take several pieces from one augmented clip. About 37 % of training frames come from crops of 194 frames or more, against 100 % with fixed 200-frame crops.

Synthetic F1 MAE by input length (Hz):

| Model | 30 | 50 | 100 | 200 | 400 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GRU, fixed 200 | 52.3 | 39.4 | 30.9 | 28.5 | 26.6 |
| GRU, mixed | 53.4 | 41.9 | 32.1 | 32.2 | 30.0 |
| Conformer, fixed 200 | 71.8 | 44.4 | 30.7 | 25.7 | 23.8 |
| Conformer, mixed | 49.0 | 32.3 | 28.7 | 27.5 | 26.2 |

The Conformer's short-input failure is fixed (F3 at 30 frames 116 → 62 Hz), at a cost of about 2 Hz on long inputs. The GRU gains nothing on short inputs and loses 3–4 Hz on long ones.

Real voices, against v2_s0 (Hillenbrand MAE, Hz):
- *Mixed Conformer*: F1 all +4.8, women +7.8, children +6.5; F3 −28.1; f0 RPA +0.5; invariance |slope| 15.5. The fixed-200 Conformer was F1 +26.7, F3 +47.8, f0 −2.3, |slope| 28.8.
- *Mixed GRU*: F1 +2.3, women +4.4, children +3.8; F3 −17.6; f0 +0.9; |slope| 14.0.
- Women's Hillenbrand F1 is 47.4 (Conformer) and 44.0 (GRU), against 35.7–41.3 for the three GRU v2 seeds.

Closed-phase F1 (PTDB-TUG): the mixed Conformer has the lowest bias and error of any model so far. Women's bias is +12.7, |error| 36.8; men's bias +10.9, |error| 20.9. The three GRU v2 seeds give women +21 to +39 and 40.7–49.7, men +17 to +39 and 26.2–41.2. The mixed GRU is within the GRU seed range.

The two references disagree on women's F1. Hillenbrand puts the mixed Conformer above the GRU seed range; closed phase puts it below. Input length doesn't explain it: Hillenbrand words are about 55 frames, where the mixed Conformer is the best model on synthetic data. Open: whether the Hillenbrand regression comes from the reduced long-crop exposure (next: a long-weighted mix at the same steps), from the short crops themselves (cut mid-utterance, where Hillenbrand words start and end in silence), or from the references.

**Measures in fp32 (2026-10-11; main `69ccf9a`).** `analyse()` ran under bf16 autocast on the GPU. On the closed phase, bf16 moved the median F1 bias by about 1.7 Hz against fp32, and it is no faster on this GPU for these models. Measures now run in fp32 (`VA_EVAL_BF16=1` for the old behaviour), with cached predictions kept apart by precision. Six S2 models re-measured (`paired/fp32/`): Hillenbrand F1 reads 0.4–1.0 Hz higher in fp32 for every model, F3 −1 to +3 Hz, f0 RPA differences unchanged, so differences between models move by under 0.5 Hz. Reports from here on are fp32; earlier bf16 reports are not mixed with them. Training (and its synthetic validation) stays bf16 autocast. The closed phase also runs on the GPU now (`add_model.py`, `VA_DEVICE=cuda`: fp32, batched by frame count, MIOpen fast find mode), identical to the CPU columns, 1.9 min against 5.2.

**Mixed crops: seed spread and the long-weighted mix (2026-10-11; `paired/fp32/`, `closed_phase/cp_small_crop2.txt`, `sensitivity/len_test_crop2.txt`, `invariance/small_crop2.txt`).** Two more seeds of the mixed-crop Conformer, and one with two-thirds of frames in crops of 194 frames or more (`crop_long_frac`).

| fp32 | Hillenbrand F1 all | F1 women | F3 | closed-phase F1 women: bias (\|err\|) | men |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GRU v2, seeds 0–2 | 34.9–38.3 | 36.3–41.7 | 86.9–107.3 | +21 to +39 (40.7–49.7) | +17 to +39 (26.2–41.2) |
| Conformer mixed, seed 0 | 43.1 | 48.4 | 81.8 | +12.7 (36.8) | +10.9 (20.9) |
| seed 1 | 48.2 | 53.6 | 130.4 | +25.8 (39.8) | +17.5 (22.6) |
| seed 2 | 43.6 | 43.0 | 82.1 | +13.6 (35.4) | +10.0 (19.4) |
| long-weighted, seed 0 | 46.1 | 53.4 | 111.1 | +11.8 (36.1) | +8.4 (19.6) |

On every seed the mixed Conformer is worse than the GRU on Hillenbrand F1 and better on closed-phase F1 error, so the two references disagree consistently, not by chance. The long-weighted mix does not recover Hillenbrand F1 (reduced long-crop exposure is not the cause) and gains nothing on long inputs; it is dropped. Both references agree that every model reads women's and children's F1 high: Hillenbrand median signed F1 error +22 to +42 Hz for women, +14 to +22 for men.

**Shared and model-specific error (`scripts/analysis/ensemble.py`).** Per frame, the error averaged over models is the part they share; per-frame median ensembles remove only the rest. Women's closed-phase F1: 84–90 % of the squared error is shared (any three seeds of one recipe, or all 13 S2 models); men's 53–73 %; Hillenbrand F1 86 %, F3 61–70 %. Median ensembles beat every member where model-specific error is large (Hillenbrand F2, F3; closed-phase |error|), not on women's F1 bias. No recipe or seed change reaches women's F1: every model makes the same error on the same frames.

**Glottal coupling probes (`sensitivity/coupling_check_*.txt`, `coupling_probe2.txt`, `coupling_probe3.txt`; v2_s0 and mix_conformer_s2).** Story's vowels with the D-023 coupling: modal voice (Rd 1.0) without a closed-phase gap moves the teachers' F1 by a median 0–2 Hz for women. With a gap (0.3–0.6 of the peak opening) or a breathier source (Rd 1.6–2.2), +10 to +39 Hz for women and men, the size of the real-voice bias. Breathiness alone (Rd 1.0 → 2.2, no coupling) moves them by under 6 Hz, aspiration noise alone (HNR 30 → 8 dB) by under 4 Hz; Praat moves by up to 126 Hz under either. The Conformer responds more than the GRU (+17 to +28 against +2 to +21).

**What the worst real utterances have in common (`sensitivity/voice_quality.txt`; PTDB-TUG subset, 794 utterances).** Spearman correlation of the shared F1 error (14 models) with Praat measures: CPPS −0.24 (women) / −0.52 (men), EGG contact quotient −0.14 / −0.18, H1–H2 +0.05 / +0.20, whole-cycle minus closed-phase F1 +0.48 / +0.19 (inflated: both differences share the closed-phase F1); HNR and shimmer about 0. The models read worst in breathier, less closed voices; with the probes (noise alone doesn't move them; coupling through a gap does), coupling is the leading hypothesis.

**Test (pre-registered, running): `train_v2c`** = train_v2's settings and seed plus coupling on every tube clip (60 % of clips; per-clip glottis, gap and strength from the sampler); clips without coupling are bit-identical to train_v2. Three S2 mixed-crop Conformer seeds against the three on train_v2. Rule: D-023's (closed-phase F1 bias beyond the seed noise for both sexes; the correlation of F1 error with the EGG contact quotient shrinks), and by size of the women's bias change: 15 Hz or more, coupling is a large part of the problem; 5–15 Hz, three more seeds per arm; under 5 Hz, it is not the main cause. If no hypothesis survives, M3 proceeds with the M2 shortfall recorded here.

**OPENGLOT (2026-10-11; Alku et al. 2019, free for research use with citation; evaluation only; `external/openglot_rep2.txt`, `openglot_rep3.txt`, `openglot_rep3_resonances.json`).** Two physical references, scored with v2_s0 and mix_conformer_s2 (median F1 minus the true first resonance, Hz):
- *Repository II* (Story's physically modelled vowels, source-tract interaction, against resonances calculated from the area functions; 4 vowels × 4 f0 per row): GRU / Conformer, male at vocal-process separation 0.03 / 0.06 / 0.09 cm: +10 / +11 / +22 and +24 / +26 / +40; female at 0.05 / 0.07 / 0.09 cm: +28 / +22 / +60 and +58 / +63 / +87. The overread grows with the gap.
- *Repository III* (LF excitation through a loudspeaker into 3D-printed replicas: real acoustics, no glottis, no coupling; resonances digitised from the measured sweeps, `openglot_sweeps.py`; the pressure signal is the second channel, against the readme): at f0 100–250 Hz, GRU −2 to +12, Conformer −15 to +16 (Praat −34 to −15). No systematic overread. Above 260 Hz both drift, and single replicas scatter (Conformer male [a] +246, an assignment failure); the files are 200 ms, short for the Conformer.

Without a glottis both models read F1 accurately; with one they read it high, more with a wider gap, the Conformer more than the GRU. With the probes and the voice-quality correlations, four independent lines now point to source-tract coupling through the glottal gap as the cause of the shared F1 overread, and suggest the Conformer's Hillenbrand regression is a stronger response to coupling rather than a generally worse reading. The v2c test is the direct check.

**Evaluation tiers (2026-10-11; main `4184813`, `vocal_analysis/eval/tiers.py`, `tiers.json`).** Every decision made by looking at a gold set makes that set's numbers optimistic for the chosen model (test-set reuse), most when the candidates are close. Hillenbrand, PTDB-TUG and ARCTIC have informed dozens of decisions. From here on:
- *dev*: 60 % of each existing gold set's speakers, balanced within groups (Hillenbrand men / women / boys / girls; PTDB-TUG women / men): Hillenbrand 83 of 139, PTDB-TUG 12 of 20, MIR-1K 11 of 19, MDB-stem-synth 18 of 30. ARCTIC, OPENGLOT and the Birkholz audio are dev only (too few speakers to split, or analysis tools). All comparisons and decisions use dev.
- *semi-private*: the other 40 %. It confirms a decision made on dev (does the effect replicate?) and never makes one. Every use is logged here; preferably reported as pass / fail against the dev result within a stated tolerance.
- *lockbox*: sets not yet looked at. MOCHA-TIMIT (3 speakers) whole. Saarbrücken healthy speakers, once their manifest exists: split by speaker 50 / 50 semi-private / lockbox, balanced by sex (rule set now, before any of it is opened). Opened only at milestone acceptance, with the rule fixed beforehand; every opening logged.

The assignment is seeded and committed, and `va tiers` refuses to rewrite it. Earlier full-set reports stay as they are. The v2c comparison, pre-registered and running on the full sets, is read on dev and confirmed on semi-private from its cached predictions; the measure scripts switch to dev by default once it ends. Synthetic checks (the suite, length tests, coupling probes) are regenerated from fresh seeds rather than reused.

**v2c result (2026-10-11 night): coupling as synthesised is not the main cause.** Three S2 mixed-crop Conformer seeds on `train_v2c` against `mix_conformer_s0-2`, read on dev speakers (`reports/analysis/closed_phase/cp_small_v2c_dev.txt`, `paired/fp32/dev_mix_conformer_s*_vs_v2c_conformer_s*.txt`; the synthetic invariance probe, `invariance/small_v2c.txt`, committed 2026-10-22):
- *Women's closed-phase F1 bias change* (the rule's measure), same-seed pairs: +2.5, −9.2, −2.8 Hz (mean −3.2). Men: +7.9, +2.4, −2.0. Under 5 Hz, so by the rule coupling, as `train_v2c` renders it, is not the main cause. The EGG contact-quotient correlation was not recomputed.
- *Semi-private confirmation* (first non-dev use, logged here: PTDB-TUG semi-private speakers, closed phase only, `cp_small_v2c_semi.txt`): women +2.4, −6.6, −5.6 (mean −3.3), men +9.4, 0.0, −3.5. Replicates.
- *Hillenbrand (dev)*: F1 MAE (all) better on every seed (−1.6, −5.9, −3.4 Hz); F2 and F3 mixed (seed 0 F3 +34.5 Hz worse, seed 1 −30.2 better against mix seed 1's outlier, seed 2 +7.8 within noise). PTDB-TUG RPA within ±0.3 points.
- *Tier slip*: the chain's own reports (`paired/fp32/mix_conformer_s*_vs_v2c_conformer_s*.txt`, `paired/fp32/v2_s0_vs_v2c_conformer_s*.txt` and `closed_phase/cp_small_v2c.txt`, never committed) were computed on all speakers, semi-private included, before the measures switched to dev. They were looked at for seed 0 only; nothing was decided on them. Deleted 2026-10-22 (D-032): the `dev_` reports above supersede them.
- *Why the test could not have shown more* (synthetic validation, signed bias, `scratchpad/synth_bias.py`): models trained without coupling read coupled clips only +4 to +6 Hz high in F1 (F2–F4 +5 to +10), against +13 to +28 Hz on real women's voices and +60 Hz on OPENGLOT's widest gap. The v2c models learn to undo those few Hz (F1 +1.4 to +2.0 on coupled clips) at the cost of reading uncoupled F2–F4 4–10 Hz low. So `train_v2c`'s coupling is several times weaker than what the real-voice evidence implies; the test shows that training on it doesn't fix the real overread, not that coupling isn't the cause. Before relying on coupling in M3's resynthesis, check the sampler's gap and coupling-strength distribution against the literature (Holmberg et al.; Barney et al.; Hanson); OPENGLOT II is an evaluation set and may only check the result, never set it.
- *The cascade is not dragging F1*: in-distribution, every model's F1 bias is +0.3 to +1.3 Hz (median signed, synthetic validation), with ordinary shrinkage toward the middle (+11 to +15 Hz below a true F1 of 450, −6 to −9 above 650). A joint (non-cascaded) formant head is deferred (roadmap).
- M3 proceeds with the M2 shortfall recorded here (decided 2026-10-11).

**Coupling strength in `train_v2c` (2026-10-12; `coupling_dist.py`, `sensitivity/coupling_dist_v2c.txt` (rerun 2026-10-22, same numbers), 600 tube targets with v2c's glottal draws).** The physics matches the literature it was built from: median peak area 0.062 cm² (short tracts) / 0.101 (long), closed-phase gap 0.33 of the peak (Holmberg: 0.22–0.52), F1 +9 % at the peak opening (+16 % for F1 < 350 Hz; Barney et al. static +13 %), B1 ×2.1 (cycle-mean increase 53 Hz, below the +77–120 Hz quoted from Hanson). What weakens it is `coupling_scale`, uniform 0–1 per clip on every pole: a hedge for the uncertain F2–F3 shifts (D-023) that F1 and B1 inherited without a reason. Energy follows closure, so the effective F1 shift is roughly the peak shift × the closed-phase gap: about 6 Hz median as rendered (the models read +4–6 Hz on coupled clips), about 16 Hz (upper quartile 25) at scale 1 for short tracts. The real-voice overread (+13 to +28 Hz) is a check on that estimate, not its source.

**Test (pre-registered 2026-10-12, before rendering): `train_v2c2`** = `train_v2c` (seed 2, `p_coupling` 1) with `coupling_full_poles` 1: F1 and B1 take the full open-glottis shift, F2–F5 keep the 0–1 scale. Only the audio of coupled clips changes; draws and labels are identical to v2c. Three S2 mixed-crop Conformer seeds (k35) against `mix_conformer_s0-2`, dev speakers, and against `v2c_conformer_s0-2`. Rule as for v2c, on the mean change in women's closed-phase F1 bias against `mix_conformer` (same seeds): 15 Hz or more, coupling is a large part of the problem and goes into resynthesis; 5–15 Hz, three more seeds per arm; under 5 Hz, coupling as modelled is not the main cause and resynthesis stays uncoupled. Guard-rails as the M3 S2 rule (Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE ≤ +3 / +5 / +10 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1 point); a guard-rail failure is reported, not a veto.

**v2c2 result (2026-10-13): under 5 Hz, so resynthesis stays uncoupled** (`closed_phase/cp_small_v2c2_dev.txt`, `paired/fp32/{mix,v2c}_conformer_s*_vs_v2c2_conformer_s*.txt`; dev speakers).
- *Women's closed-phase F1 bias change against `mix_conformer`* (the rule's measure): +0.4, −12.6, +0.1 Hz (mean −4.0). Seed 1's −12.6 is against `mix_conformer_s1`, the outlier seed (+27.7, the others +12.6 / +13.3). Men: +5.0, −2.0, +0.3. Against `v2c_conformer`: women −1.8, −3.6, +3.8 (mean −0.5), men −2.5, −4.1, +3.2. Full-strength F1 / B1 coupling changed nothing that v2c's didn't.
- *Guard-rails* (means against `mix_conformer`): Hillenbrand F1 MAE −4.2, F2 −2.6, F3 +4.0 Hz (seed 0 +29.9, seed 2 +12.4, seed 1 −30.4); RPA 0.0 points. All pass.
- *The synthetic shift did grow* (`scratchpad/synth_bias.py`, the sets' fixed validation clips, all clips including the 40 % without coupling): models trained without coupling read F1 +4.5 / +5.5 Hz high on `train_v2c` and +7.6 / +8.6 on `train_v2c2` (+12.2 / +13.5 at f0 150–300 Hz); `v2c2_conformer_s0` reads its own set +1.6. So the models learn to undo a synthetic coupling overread close to the real one's lower end, and it doesn't carry over to real voices.
- Reading: coupling as modelled here (quasi-static open-glottis poles interpolated with the opening; Holmberg glottal sizes) is not what makes the real F1 overread, or real coupling acts differently from this model. The four earlier lines of evidence (D-024 above) still say the overread grows with the glottal gap; the next check would be a coupling model that is not quasi-static (a time-domain glottis–tract simulation, as in OPENGLOT II and Birkholz et al.), not more strength.

## D-025 (2026-10-13): M3 with resynthesised VocalSet: S2 rule, full-size Conformer, longer training

**S2** (pre-registered rule, `decide_m3_s2.py`): three mixed-crop Conformer seeds on train_v2 + 30 % resynthesised VocalSet (tracked by teacher_v2_d16k) against `mix_conformer_s0-2`, dev: passed ("m3"). Hillenbrand F1 MAE −6.8 / −13.0 / −9.3 Hz, better for women and men on every seed; F2 +1.0 / −20.4 / −2.8; F3 +11.8 / −41.3 / +12.7; RPA +0.2 / +0.3 / 0.0; women's closed-phase |err| +0.3 / −10.3 / −1.5.

**Full size** (`conformer_m3`, 4.1M parameters, constant to 16000; dev; against the GRU teacher_v2 branches, so architecture and data change together):
- *d06k*: F1 women +5.0 Hz WORSE (40.4 → 45.4), children +3.3 (noise), men −3.0 better; F2 −5.6, F3 −16.7, F4 −48.4 better; RPA unchanged. Closed-phase bias women +7.1 against +13.9, men +7.4 against +15.7.
- *d16k*: better than teacher_v2_d16k on every Hillenbrand measure (F1 women −2.6, children −3.7, men −3.1; F3 −18.8, F4 −77.7), but mainly because the GRU loses F1 from d06k to d16k (women +6.6, children +7.2) while the Conformer barely moves. Closed-phase bias women +12.6 (d06k +7.1), men +8.7. Inference RTF 0.014 against the GRU's 0.051.
- The best women's and children's Hillenbrand F1 is still teacher_v2_d06k's.

**Small M3 to d16k** (`m3_conformer_s2` and `s0`, the best and worst S2 seeds by dev F1 MAE, constant to 16000 then decay 4000, against their own k35): Hillenbrand F1 MAE worse (+4.5, +3.0 Hz; children +7.5, +5.2), F2 −6.2 / −4.3, F3 −18.2 / −20.9, F4 −35.4 / −46.9 better; closed-phase bias lower (women −16.2 / −7.4, men −7.6 / −0.6). Longer training trades Hillenbrand F1 (whose references are LPC formants, D-019) for F2–F4 and, at S2, a lower closed-phase F1 bias; at full size the closed-phase trend went the other way for women (d06k → d16k +4.0).

**Architecture and data separated at d16k** (`conformer_v2_d16k`: full-size mixed-crop Conformer on train_v2 alone, constant to 16000, decay 4000; one seed each; dev; `paired/fp32/teacher_v2_d16k_vs_conformer_v2_d16k.txt`, `conformer_v2_d16k_vs_conformer_m3_d16k.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_conformer_v2_d16k_dev.txt`):
- *Architecture* (GRU → Conformer, same data): Hillenbrand F1 within noise (women −1.1, children −1.6, men −1.4), F2 −1.0 (noise), F3 −11.3 and F4 −69.8 better, F4 DR +3.6; RPA unchanged. Closed-phase bias men −8.8 Hz (all six speakers), women −0.2 (noise).
- *M3 data* (same architecture): F1 all −1.7 (men −1.7 better; women −1.5, children −2.1 within noise), F2 −2.2 better, F3 −7.4 and F4 −7.9 within noise; RPA +0.5 points. Closed-phase bias women −1.9 Hz (every speaker, −1 to −6), men +1.6 (noise).
- Most of conformer_m3's lead over the GRU is the architecture (F3–F4, men's closed phase); resynthesised VocalSet adds small, consistent gains. Its S2 effect (F1 −7 to −13 Hz) did not carry over at this size, so S2 overstated it. The women's F1 regression at d06k is still unattributed (no conformer_v2 d06k branch).

**Teacher v3 candidate: train_v2 + VocalSet + Zeroth Korean (2026-10-16; D-026's fail branch ran days 2–3 on train_v2; `m3_speech_chain.sh v2`).** S2: `v2r2_conformer` against mix passed the M3 rule ("m3"; Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE −11.5 / −13.1 / −8.4, RPA −0.2, women's closed-phase |error| −5.9). Full size, two seeds, each against the train_v2 run with the same seed (`paired/fp32/conformer_v2{,_s1}_d16k_vs_conformer_v2r2{,_s1}_d16k.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_full_seeds_conformer_v2r2_dev.txt`, `sensitivity/resynth_identity_full_seeds.txt`; dev):
- Hillenbrand F1 MAE all −1.5 [−2.7, −0.3] / −0.6 (noise); F2 −1.1 / −0.2; **F3 −12.3 [−20.9, −5.7] / −5.6 [−10.9, −0.5]**; F3 detection +1.1 / +0.8; F4 MAE −8.3 (noise) / −22.4; F4 detection +1.2 / +0.7 (noise); RPA −0.4 [−0.6, −0.2] / 0.0.
- **Women's closed-phase F1 bias −5.7 [−8.2, −2.2] / −5.9 [−7.0, −4.3]** (+15.5 → +9.8, +13.3 → +7.6); men +0.1 / −1.1. F2 / F3 against closed phase: women unchanged (F3 +15.9 / +15.5 against +14.4 / +16.3), men's F3 −7 / −5 → −1 / +3. ARCTIC slt +6 / +8 → −2 / 0.
- Identity resynthesis (VocalSet val): −1.5 / 0.0 overall; at f0 ≥ 300 −0.5 / +0.4, against train_v2's −4.3 / −4.6.
- The two seeds agree in direction everywhere, with one small cost (seed 0's RPA −0.4 points). The S2 F1 gain carries over this time, about half of it. Next: confirm on semi-private (logged), then teacher v3 if it holds.
- *Third seed pair and semi-private confirmation (pre-registered 2026-10-17, with Hannah, before seed 2 ran and before any semi-private use for this candidate; `chains/v3_seed2_chain.sh`, `decide_v3_semi.py`)*. conformer_v2_s2 and conformer_v2r2_s2 (seed 2, both arms so the pair stays matched), dev measures as for seeds 0–1. Then semi-private (a logged use: PTDB-TUG and Hillenbrand semi-private speakers, the three pairs, `VA_TIERS=semi`). Replicates when both headline effects, means over the three pairs, are negative on dev and at least half as large on semi-private: E1 women's closed-phase F1 bias change (dev, seeds 0–1: −4.8 / −5.7 as the script computes it), E2 Hillenbrand F3 MAE change (−12.3 / −5.6). No semi-private guard-rail may fail: F1 MAE all ≤ +2 Hz, F3 / F4 detection ≥ −1 point, RPA ≥ −0.5 point. Replicates → teacher v3 = conformer_v2r2 (seed choice then by dev). Otherwise it stays a candidate and the data question goes back to dev.
- *Seed 2 (dev, 2026-10-17 17:17)*: E1 −4.1 (women's closed-phase F1 bias +13.1 → +8.8), E2 −6.0 (Hillenbrand F3 MAE 53.0 → 47.0), F1 MAE −7.0, F3 / F4 detection +0.6 / +0.2, RPA +0.7. Identity at f0 ≥ 300: −7.7 → −3.5 Hz (seeds 0–1: −4.3 → −0.5, −4.6 → +0.4). Men unchanged (−0.4); ARCTIC slt +9 → 0.
- *Semi-private use, logged (2026-10-17 17:17; PTDB-TUG and Hillenbrand semi-private speakers; the three conformer_v2 / conformer_v2r2 d16k pairs; `paired/fp32_semi/`, `closed_phase/cp_full_seeds3_conformer_v2r2_semi.txt`, `decide_v3_semi.txt`)*. **Replicates.** Means over the pairs, dev → semi: E1 −4.9 → −6.0 (women's closed-phase bias change per pair −5.2 / −9.4 / −7.3), E2 −8.0 → −9.9. Guard-rails on semi: F1 MAE −4.8, F3 detection +0.9, F4 detection −0.0, RPA +0.1; none fails. Men's closed-phase change on semi +0.3 / −2.1 / −1.2.
- *Seed choice (by dev, as pre-registered; criterion stated after the per-pair dev changes had been read but before the seeds were compared with each other)*: rank the three conformer_v2r2_d16k seeds on the two headline measures, absolute: women's closed-phase F1 |bias| 9.8 / 7.6 / 8.8 (s0 / s1 / s2), Hillenbrand F3 MAE 51.2 / 47.7 / 47.0. Rank sums s0 6, s1 3, s2 3: **a tie between seeds 1 and 2**, which the stated tie-break (to seed 0) does not cover. Beyond the rule, s1: RPA 86.8 against 85.6, identity at f0 ≥ 300 +0.4 against −3.5; s2: F1 MAE 37.0 against 38.9, F4 detection 92.0 against 91.6. Left to Hannah.
- *Teacher v3 (Hannah, 2026-10-17)*: **one model: conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k; for labelling, the per-frame mean (in log frequency) of seeds 1 and 2**, the two best on dev. Measured on dev from cached predictions (`ensemble_v3.py`, `ensemble/teacher_v3_seeds_dev.txt`; a measurement, not a decision). Hillenbrand MAE, seed 1 / seed 2 / mean of 1–2 / mean of all three: F1 38.9 / 37.0 / 37.1 / 37.6, women's F1 42.5 / 41.2 / **40.8** / 41.5, F2 47.3 / 45.0 / 45.6 / 45.5, F3 47.7 / 47.0 / **46.3** / 47.2, F4 98.4 / 96.6 / 96.3 / 96.2. Closed phase, median |error| F1 / F2 / F3: women 28.5 / 36.7 / 43.1 (s1), 29.1 / 35.5 / 42.8 (s2), **27.8 / 35.7 / 42.0** (mean 1–2); men 16.0 / 23.4 / 32.7, 15.1 / 22.9 / 33.0, **14.9 / 22.5 / 31.6**. The mean matches or beats the better seed everywhere but F2 (within 0.6 Hz), as D-024's ensemble study predicts: it removes model-specific error; the shared bias stays (women's F1 +8.0, between the seeds' +7.6 and +8.8). The per-frame median of all three (asked for out of interest): Hillenbrand F1 38.0, women's F1 42.1, F2 45.7, F3 46.5, **F4 94.2** (the best of any); closed-phase |error| women 28.5 / 35.6 / 42.7, men 14.7 / 22.6 / 32.3. Between the mean of 1–2 and the single seeds everywhere except F4, where a median ignores one seed's occasional wrong peak.

## D-026 (2026-10-13): why seeds differ on real voices (investigation, not concluded)

S2 seeds that match on synthetic validation (`mix_conformer` F1 MAE 37.9 / 37.4 / 36.5 Hz) differ widely on real voices (Hillenbrand F1 MAE 44.1 / 49.1 / 43.5; women's closed-phase bias +12.6 / +27.7 / +13.3): underspecification (McCoy et al. 2020; D'Amour et al. 2020). No bug found: the Conformer uses LayerNorm / GroupNorm only, so batch composition can't enter.

- *The seed difference on real voices is a near-uniform offset* (PTDB-TUG dev frames): `mix_s1` reads women's F1 a median +13 Hz above s0 / s2, the same for every speaker (+10 to +19), every f0 band, and uncorrelated with the EGG contact quotient, F1 or Praat's error (|rho| ≤ 0.12); men +6.5 Hz, growing with F1. `m3_conformer_s0_d16k` − `s2_d16k`: women +10, men +7, the same pattern.
- *Synthetic probes don't predict it* (`sensitivity/seed_probe_bw.txt`, `seed_probe_coupling.txt`, `probe_vs_real*.txt`; 31 models with a real bias on file). Across all models the coupling response correlates negatively with real bias (rho −0.60), but that is the architecture: within Conformers no bandwidth or coupling sensitivity predicts it (|rho| ≤ 0.3, except men's coupling −0.5); within GRUs, breathiness response +0.56 / +0.71 (n 11). The only feature predictive in both is the plain F1 offset on unperturbed Peterson & Barney vowels at f0 120 (+0.62 Conformers, +0.51 GRUs), and it doesn't separate `mix_s1` from s0 / s2 (+4 / +4 / +3). A channel sweep (`channel_probe.txt`: tilt ±3 / ±6 dB/oct, high-pass, telephone band, low-pass, Rd 0.6–2.4) doesn't reproduce the split either; for women every condition gives the opposite sign.
- *Resynthesised VocalSet* (real source and channel, our resonators, exact labels; `synth_bias.py`, `resynth_alpha.py`, `resynth_mid.py`):
  - Inverse-filtering residue is large: error rises with alpha (labels = tracks / alpha) at +190 to +530 Hz per ln(alpha), so at alpha 0.8 or 1.25 the original voice pulls F1 by about 50–120 Hz. Models trained with resynthesis (`m3_conformer_*_d16k`) are flat in alpha (slope +44): they learnt to ignore it. The §5.2 caveat is real; resynthesised labels carry this noise.
  - At alpha 0.96–1.04 (residue and label together) models trained without resynthesis still read F1 +6 to +13 Hz high (+7 to +19 at f0 150–300). It varies by singer (−11 to +28) and goes with residual ripple (rho +0.18 / +0.27) and lower source H1–H2 (−0.21 / −0.28), not with `tract_left`. So part of it is residue that QC doesn't catch; the rest is unresolved.
  - teacher_v2_d16k, which tracked these clips, reads its own resynthesised tracks +11 Hz high at alpha ≈ 1. Repeated re-track → resynthesise rounds (M4) would drift F1 upward unless the tracker is unbiased on resynthesised audio; check this before any round.
  - The seed offset does not reproduce on resynthesised clips (`mix_s1` at alpha ≈ 1, f0 150–300: +14.0 against +12.3 / +13.4).
- *Reading*: the seed offset needs something present in real recordings and absent from both our synthesis and our resynthesis. Since resynthesis keeps the real source and channel, the remaining candidates are the real tract filter itself (zeros, side branches, non-all-pole spectra, real bandwidths), source–tract interaction, and the closed-phase reference's own behaviour per speaker (common to all models, so it can't make seeds differ, but it sets the absolute bias).
- *OPENGLOT seed contrasts* (2026-10-13; `external/openglot_rep3_seeds.txt`, `openglot_rep2_seeds.txt`; evaluation only, used here to locate a cause, not to tune anything). Five within-family seed pairs, paired per file, files where a contrasted model is over 150 Hz off left out. Real-voice contrasts (PTDB-TUG dev, women): +14.7, +12.2, +11.8, +8.2, +12.7 Hz (`mix_s1` − s0 / s2, `m3_s0_d16k` − `s2_d16k`, `v2_s1` − s0 / s2, `v2c_s1` − s0 / s2, `m3_s0` − `m3_s1`).
  - *Repository III* (recorded 3D-printed tracts, loudspeaker LF source, no glottis): women +8.8, +1.1, +12.0, +2.0, +0.3; men −18.4, +4.9, +25.5, −0.8, −4.9. Only the GRU pair reproduces; across the 10 Conformers the ordering is unrelated to the real one. A real tract and channel alone do not produce the Conformer seed offset (3 female replicas, 200 ms files: weak evidence).
  - *Repository II* (Story's simulated voices with source–tract interaction): all ten contrasts point the real way, women +3.6, +3.9, +7.5, +5.1, +7.6 and men +5.6, +1.3, +7.0, +7.0, +5.2 (about a third to two thirds of the real size), several growing with the glottal gap (women `mix_s1` +0.1 / +3.6 / +8.5, `m3_s0` +5.3 / +9.5 / +12.1 from small to large).
  - Reading: the trigger is most likely source–tract interaction, but not in the form our coupling renders (D-024: training on it doesn't fix the bias, and our coupling probe doesn't separate seeds). Our coupling only moves the poles within the cycle, with a fixed LF flow. In interactive models such as Story's, the glottal flow itself is computed from the glottal area and the tract's acoustic pressure, so it carries formant ripple and skewing (Titze's level-1 interaction). That is the next candidate.
- *Interactive source probe* (2026-10-13; `synth/interactive.py`, `scripts/analysis/interactive_probe.py`, `sensitivity/interactive_probe.txt`; Story's 10 vowels, men 17.5 cm f0 120, women 15 cm f0 210, 31 models; label = closed-glottis fR1; "uncoupled" = the same area waveform and tract without the load's feedback). Median F1 error over models:
  - *Without a closed-phase gap, interaction alone reproduces the real overread's size*: men +13.1 / +13.4 Hz (Rd 1.0 / 2.0) against −0.8 / +0.2 uncoupled; women +21.8 / +14.3 against −5.6 / +1.0. Conformers and GRUs alike (+11 to +23). The real closed-phase bias is +13 to +28 for women. No earlier synthetic condition came near it (quasi-static coupling: +4 to +6, D-024).
  - *Seed ordering: not resolved.* The five within-family contrasts swing by ±20–40 Hz between conditions (10 vowels at one f0 per condition), and no condition's model ranking correlates with the real bias within Conformers. The probe is too coarse to test a ~10 Hz seed offset.
  - *Gap conditions are suspect*: with Holmberg's gap (0.25 / 0.43 of the peak) every model reads F1 low even without the load (women −15 / −24 uncoupled, −41 coupled at Rd 2.0), which real breathy voices don't show. With a large gap the flow pulse rides on a steady leak and its excitation is weak; check the gap's spectral effect against the literature (H1–H2, excitation strength) before a training set uses it.
  - Next: the sampler option (`p_interactive`) and a pre-registered S2 test (`train_v2i`) against `mix_conformer_s0-2`, gaps limited until the gap check is done.
- *Gap check (2026-10-13): the area pulse, not the gap, was wrong.* The sin² opening (borrowed from the aspiration envelope) closes with zero slope: its source spectrum had nulls (H3 −27 to −44 dB) and fell 16–18 dB/oct (LF derivative 10–11). Without a gap the glottal viscous and inertive terms cut the flow off near full closure and hid it; with a gap the flow follows the smooth area and barely excites the tract above H3, so every model read F1 low. Replaced by Rosenberg's (1971) type-C pulse (`interactive.area_pulse`: cosine opening, quarter-cosine closing ending with a finite slope, speed quotient 1.3 here): 7–11 dB/oct, no nulls, and H1–H2 rising with the gap (Rd 1.0: 3.3 → 6 dB; Rd 2.0: 10 → 17 dB), as glottal chinks do (Hanson; Holmberg et al.). Rerun (`sensitivity/interactive_probe.txt`; sin² run kept as `interactive_probe_sin2.txt`): the uncoupled controls are now −2.7 to +2.8 Hz in every condition, and interaction gives men +12.8 / +24.1 (Rd 1.0, gap 0 / 0.25) and +22.5 / +37.9 (Rd 2.0, gap 0 / 0.43), women +17.8 / +8.8 and +18.3 / +26.8. The overread grows with breathiness and the gap, as on real voices (D-024's voice-quality correlations) and OPENGLOT II. Seed contrasts are still too noisy to read (±10–30 Hz per condition).
- *Identity resynthesis (2026-10-13; `scripts/analysis/resynth_identity.py`, `sensitivity/resynth_identity.txt`; 120 VocalSet val recordings re-filtered through their own smoothed teacher_v2_d16k tracks with no perturbation, then with only the bandwidths scaled or only alpha changed).* **Correction:** teacher_v2_d16k does *not* overread its own resynthesis: identity −0.8 Hz (bandwidths ×0.6 / ×1.6: −3.0 / +3.5; alpha 0.9 / 1.1: −0.7 / −3.6). The +11 Hz at alpha 0.96–1.04 above came from the other perturbations of the training renders (per-formant offsets, per-formant bandwidth scales, nasal pairs), not from resynthesis itself, so M4 rounds would not drift for this tracker. **Also corrected:** the seed offset *does* reproduce on resynthesised audio once the perturbations are removed: `mix_conformer_s1` − `s0` = +12.5 Hz on the original recordings and +11.2 on the identity resynthesis (+9.5 / +11.1 with the bandwidths scaled, +6.0 / +7.8 at alpha 0.9 / 1.1). Identity resynthesis keeps the recording's own source (with whatever interaction ripple and tract residue it carries) and replaces its tract by our resonators, so the seed-splitting cue is in the real source, not the real tract filter. That fits the interactive-source hypothesis (and OPENGLOT II); OPENGLOT III's loudspeaker LF source lacks it.
- *Proposed acceptance test (identity resynthesis as a fixed point)*: track training-pool val recordings (VocalSet, Zeroth), resynthesise with no perturbation, re-track; the median F1 (and F2–F3) change must be within the seed noise floor, and repeated rounds must not accumulate. Necessary, not sufficient (a constant output passes), so it sits beside the dev-set checks; it gates M4's re-track rounds. teacher_v2_d16k passes (−0.8 Hz).

**Test (pre-registered 2026-10-13, before rendering): `train_v2i` and the control `train_v2t`.**
- *`train_v2i`* = train_v2 (seed 2, `p_tube` 0.6, every other setting unchanged) with `p_interactive` 1: every tube clip (60 %) gets the interactive source: Rosenberg area pulse (speed quotient 0.9–2.0 per clip) at the open quotient of the clip's Rd, Holmberg glottal sizes and closed-phase gap (`coupling.sample_glottis`), lung pressure 3.6–12 cm H₂O, the subglottal impedance in the load (gain 0.5–2 per clip; the ad hoc subglottal pole-zero pairs are dropped on these clips, nasal pairs kept); keyframed area-function transitions; labels the tube's closed-glottis poles with the tube's own bandwidths; `source_h_db` NaN. Cascade clips (40 %) are bit-identical to train_v2 (checked on 40 clips). One change: the tube clips' source.
- *`train_v2t`* = `train_v2i` with `interactive_coupled` False: the same clips, draws and labels, the load's feedback off.
- Three S2 mixed-crop Conformer seeds (k35) on each, against `mix_conformer_s0-2`, dev speakers.
- *Rule* (as v2c): mean change in women's closed-phase F1 bias, v2i against `mix_conformer` (same seeds): 15 Hz or more, the interactive source fixes a large part of the overread (it becomes the default for tube clips); 5–15 Hz, three more seeds per arm; under 5 Hz, not the main cause as modelled.
- *Attribution*: v2i against v2t (same seeds), same measure: credit goes to the interaction only if v2i lowers the bias by 5 Hz or more against v2t; otherwise to the source shape and bandwidth labels the two share.
- *Guard-rails* (v2i against `mix_conformer`, means): Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE ≤ +3 / +5 / +10 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1 point. Reported, not a veto.
- *Informative*: the seed spread of the women's closed-phase bias (`mix_conformer`: +12.6 / +27.7 / +13.3); the identity-resynthesis test on the new models.

**Interim (2026-10-13 21:00, before any v2t result was read): the three v2i seeds against `mix_conformer`** (`cp_dev`, dev): women's closed-phase bias −14.9 / −11.4 / −9.0 Hz (mean −11.8: the 5–15 band, so three more seeds per arm), every one of 18 per-speaker changes negative; men −9.7 / −3.7 / −2.8. Bias per seed +12.6 / +27.7 / +13.3 → −4.1 / +16.5 / +0.3: the level drops, the seed split stays (s1 high in both). Guard-rails per seed: F1 MAE −9.8 / −12.6 / −7.5 (better); F2 +0.4 / −18.0 / +10.0; F3 +26.1 / −41.4 / +20.2 (fails on s0 and s2); F4 detection −3.7 / −3.1 / −8.1 points; RPA +0.0 / −0.9 / +0.3. ARCTIC slt now reads −9 to −16 (mix −4 to +11).

**Follow-up (pre-registered 2026-10-13, before any v2t result was read; `decide_v2i.py`, `chains/v2i_day1_chain.sh`).**
- *Small d16k* (`chains/v2i_d16k_chain.sh`): the best and worst v2i seeds (s0, s1: lowest / highest Hillenbrand F1 MAE and women's bias) continue to 16000 + decay 4000, as the small M3 d16k runs, against their own k35 and with the identity test. Question: does longer training again trade F1 MAE (+3 to +4.5 Hz on the M3 runs) for F3 / F4 and bias?
- *Six seeds per arm*: seeds 3–5 for `mix_conformer`, `v2i_conformer` and `v2t_conformer`. Pooled over the six same-seed pairs (mean, t interval over seeds), women's closed-phase bias change v2i − mix: −15 Hz or more, large; −15 to −5 with the interval excluding 0, partial; otherwise not shown. Attribution as above (v2i − v2t ≤ −5 Hz credits the load feedback). Guard-rails as above plus, added after the first three v2i seeds, F3 detection ≥ −1 and F4 detection ≥ −2 points.
- *Set carried forward*: v2i if the interaction is credited or v2i fails no more guard-rails than v2t, else v2t. If that set fails an F3 / F4 guard-rail, the high-frequency loss is fixed first (suspects: the dropped ad hoc subglottal pairs, the Rosenberg pulse's high-frequency content) and no full-size run starts. If the effect isn't shown, M3 continues on train_v2.
- *Full size*: on v2i / v2t, one full-size Conformer to d16k against `conformer_v2_d16k` (same architecture, recipe and seed on train_v2), with the identity test. Then M3 speech (Zeroth resynthesis) on the carried set, and a second full-size seed of whichever comparison is closest.
- *Days 2–3* (`chains/m3_speech_chain.sh`, pre-registered with the above). Zeroth-Korean resynthesis, tracked by teacher_v2_d16k like VocalSet: 3,200 train recordings spread through the 105 train speakers and 600 from the 10 val speakers, speech track smoothing (median 5, Gaussian SD 3 frames; VocalSet's 9 / 6 are singing settings). A 40-recording trial: 3.5 clips per recording, 4 of 142 rejected (few voiced), voiced-frame |tract_left| median 0.14 (VocalSet 0.24), ripple 4.8 dB (3.9). About as many clips as resynthesised VocalSet, so the 30 % resynthesised share is about half speech, half singing. On the carried base set, three S2 seeds each of base + VocalSet ("r1") and base + VocalSet + Zeroth ("r2"); `decide_m3_s2.py` (the M3 no-degradation rule) on r2 against the base seeds. If it passes, the teacher v3 candidate: full-size on base + both, to d16k, against the base's full-size run. Then a second full-size seed (seed 1) of `conformer_v2` and of the candidate (v3 if made, otherwise the base's full-size run), both to d16k, paired, with the identity test on all full-size models.

**Result, three seeds per arm (2026-10-13 23:07; `closed_phase/cp_small_v2i_dev.txt`, `sensitivity/resynth_identity_v2i.txt`).** Women's closed-phase bias change, dev:
- v2i − mix −14.9 / −11.4 / −9.0 (mean −11.8: the 5–15 band; the six-seed follow-up above is queued). v2t − mix +5.1 / −8.5 / +4.8 (mean +0.5). v2i − v2t −22.3 / −5.5 / −14.8 (mean −14.2): **the attribution goes to the interaction** (the load's feedback), not to the source shape or labels the two sets share. Men: v2i − v2t −7.8 / +0.6 / −2.3.
- Hillenbrand, v2t − mix: F1 MAE +6.8 / +4.6 / +0.5 (worse; v2i −9.8 / −12.6 / −7.5), F3 +40.2 / −4.2 / +31.8. The F3 losses come with the shared ingredients (Rosenberg pulse, dropped subglottal pairs, keyframed tube clips), with or without feedback; s1 is the exception in both arms.
- ARCTIC slt: v2t +8 / +12 / +1 (like mix), v2i −16 / −11 / −9: the possible overshoot also comes with the feedback.
- Identity resynthesis (VocalSet val, labels teacher_v2_d16k's tracks; d16k itself −0.8): mix +8.6 / +19.8 / +13.1, v2t +7.6 / +14.1 / +8.4, v2i +10.9 / +9.8 / +5.0. All S2 models overread real-source resynthesis by 5–20 Hz, mostly below f0 300; v2i lowers the mean (8.6 against 13.8) and narrows the seed spread (6 Hz against 11). As an acceptance test this separates the full-size tracker from S2 k35 models, so its noise floor has to be set per model size.

**v2i s0 / s1 to d16k (2026-10-14 04:52; `paired/fp32/v2i_conformer_s*_vs_*_d16k.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_small_v2i_d16k_dev.txt`, `sensitivity/resynth_identity_v2i_d16k.txt`).**
- Longer training trades as on the M3 runs: Hillenbrand F1 MAE +5.7 / +0.6 Hz (children +9.3 / +4.7), F3 −30.7 / −14.5, F4 −54 / −80, F4 detection +5 / +6 points; RPA unchanged.
- The bias overshoots: women's closed-phase bias −12.6 / −10.0 (k35 −4.1 / +16.5), men −5.8 / +2.5; ARCTIC slt −34 / −31 (mix about +5). The seed split closes (women 20.6 Hz apart at k35, 2.6 at d16k; the small M3 d16k pair: 12).
- Identity resynthesis (exact labels) agrees: −8.9 / −2.3, and −16 / −12 at f0 ≥ 300, where the small M3 d16k runs read −1.5 and the tracker −0.8. So the underread is real, not the closed-phase reference: trained long on train_v2i, models remove more than the real voices' interaction, most at high f0.
- Reading: the interaction as sampled (glottal sizes, lung pressure, subglottal gain, gap, every tube clip) is probably stronger on average than in these voices. The lower Hillenbrand F1 also fits lower reads, though Hillenbrand's LPC reference reads women and children high (D-012), so part may be the reference. Not acted on before the six-seed decision; candidate follow-ups: interaction on a fraction of tube clips, or Holmberg sizes and pressures narrowed toward modal voice.

**Six seeds per arm: DECISION "fix" (2026-10-14 10:16; `decide_v2i.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_small_v2i_6seed_dev.txt`).**
- Women's closed-phase bias, v2i − mix: −16.2 / −8.8 / −7.8 / −9.5 / +0.1 / −19.8, mean −10.3 [−17.7, −3.0] (partial). v2t − mix +1.7 [−3.7, +7.1]. v2i − v2t −12.7 [−20.6, −4.8]: credited to the interaction.
- Guard-rails, v2i: F1 MAE −7.4, F2 +0.5, F3 +2.7, F3 detection −0.8, RPA +0.4 pass; **F4 detection −6.6 points fails** (every seed −3 to −10). v2t fails F1 (+3.5), F2 (+7.0), F3 (+13.5) and F3 detection; its F4 detection is −0.3. So the F3 MAE losses seen early are the shared source shape (worse without feedback), and the F4 loss comes with the feedback.
- Direction: v2i reads every formant above F1 low. Hillenbrand dev, median signed error over six seeds: F3 −29 / −19 / −32 Hz (women / children / men; mix +18 / +19 / −2), F4 −67 / −38 / −29 (mix −2 / +4 / +23). Against the closed-phase reference (PTDB-TUG dev), women F1 / F2 / F3: mix +18.6 / +15.1 / +23.5, v2t +21.2 / +13.9 / +13.1, v2i +5.3 / −8.7 / −17.9; men mix +12.8 / +12.6 / +6.4, v2i +8.8 / +1.0 / −15.1. v2i shifts F1 / F2 / F3 down by 13 / 24 / 41 Hz (women) and 4 / 12 / 21 (men), so the correction grows with formant number, while the real overread (mix against closed phase) is about level across F1–F3.
- Cause, in the synthesis: the apparent open-phase resonances of h / (Zg + Zin + Zsub) (Story's vowels, linearised glottal impedance at mean flow, 8 cm H₂O) rise far more for the upper formants than F1. Women at 0.044 cm²: F1 +44, F2 +127, F3 +155 Hz; men at 0.06: +39 / +104 / +138 / F4 +199. More glottal inertance (end corrections) narrows this only slowly: ×8 still leaves F3 / F1 about 2 (×1: 2.5–6). The subglottal load barely matters. So the 1-D slit glottis on this tract teaches roughly 2.5 times more upper-formant correction, relative to F1, than these real voices need.
- Done meanwhile (useful whatever the fix): the second full-size seed of the train_v2 baseline (`conformer_v2_s1`, to d16k; `chains/v2s1_chain.sh`). Days 2–3 (`m3_speech_chain.sh`) stood down as designed; Zeroth resynthesis is rendered and waiting.

**Fix, part 1: the dose arm (pre-registered 2026-10-14, before rendering; `decide_v2h.py`, `chains/v2h_chain.sh`).** Decided with Hannah: lower the dose now, look for the physiological cause in parallel; a band-limited (unphysical) interaction is the last resort.
- *`train_v2h`* = train_v2i with `p_interactive` 0.5: half the tube clips interactive, the other half exactly as train_v2's. One change.
- Three S2 seeds (k35) against `mix_conformer_s0-2`; s0 and s1 to d16k (v2i overshot there); identity test.
- *Carried forward* when all hold: k35 means against mix: women's closed-phase bias change ≤ −5 Hz; Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE ≤ +3 / +5 / +10 Hz, F3 detection ≥ −1, F4 detection ≥ −2 points, RPA ≥ −1. d16k (mean of s0 / s1): women's closed-phase F1 bias within ±10 Hz, F3 bias ≥ −10 Hz, identity resynthesis within ±5 Hz. Otherwise "fix" stands.
- If carried: a full-size Conformer on train_v2h to d16k against `conformer_v2_d16k` and `conformer_v2_s1_d16k` (both train_v2 seeds), then days 2–3 (`m3_speech_chain.sh`) on train_v2h.
- *Expectation, written down beforehand*: about half v2i's effect on every formant: k35 F1 bias change about −5, F3 about −20, F4 detection about −3 (so the F4 guard-rail may fail at k35), d16k F1 near 0.

**Dose arm changed before rendering (2026-10-14 15:00): train_v2h → train_v2m.** A probe of the k35 models on Story's vowels through the interactive renderer (Rosenberg pulse, OQ 0.5, peak 0.12 cm² scaled by length, no gap; mean over three seeds; `scratchpad` only), model minus closed-glottis poles, F1 / F2 / F3:
- women, *uncoupled* (the same pulse, no feedback): mix −6 / +19 / −16, v2t +9 / +5 / −14, **v2i −35 / −15 / −22**; *coupled*: mix +8 / +25 / +5, v2t +27 / +31 / +15, v2i −8 / −5 / −7. Men alike, smaller (v2i uncoupled −7 / −3 / −16).
- So v2i reads low even where there is no interaction: it learnt "Rosenberg source → read lower", not to detect the feedback, because every Rosenberg clip in train_v2i had it. Real voices with less interaction than the training average are then over-corrected (the d16k overshoot, the F3 / F4 underread).
- The audio itself shifts much less above F1 than the linearised estimate above suggested. Praat Burg on the same renders, coupled minus uncoupled (median over vowels): men F1 +0 / +32 / +57, F2 +11 / +16 / +21, F3 +23 / +24 / +14 Hz at peak areas 0.05 / 0.1 / 0.2 cm²; women F1 +22 / +42 / +54, F2 +5 / +41 / +41, F3 −22 / +9 / +18 (F3 noisy per vowel). Birkholz et al. (2019, JASA 146, 223), physical resonators with self-oscillating rubber folds and VocalTractLab simulations: F1 rises with peak glottal area, F2 and F3 not systematically affected. Our F1 and F3 agree; women's F2 (+41) may be large.
- train_v2h (p_interactive 0.5) would keep the shortcut: its interactive clips all have the Rosenberg pulse and its other tube clips the LF source. **train_v2m** = train_v2i with `p_interactive_coupled` 0.5 (new sampler option, per clip; at 1.0 nothing is drawn, train_v2i reproduces bit-exactly, checked on 12 clips): the same source on every tube clip, the feedback on for a random half, so only the feedback's own cues predict the correction. The same dose (half the tube clips with feedback), the same criteria (`decide_v2m.py`, `chains/v2m_chain.sh`).

**Full-size seeds (2026-10-14 17:03; `conformer_v2_s1_d16k`, `paired/fp32/conformer_v2_d16k_vs_conformer_v2_s1_d16k.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_conformer_v2_seeds_dev.txt`).** The second full-size train_v2 seed against the first: women's closed-phase bias +15.5 / +13.3 Hz (paired −1.4 [−4.7, +1.1]), men +6.7 / +7.3; Hillenbrand F1 MAE 41.6 / 39.4, F3 63.5 / 53.2, F4 detection 90.6 / 90.8, RPA 86.1 / 86.8. At full size and d16k length the real-voice seed spread is a few Hz, not the 12–20 Hz of the S2 k35 seeds: the single-seed full-size comparisons in D-025 stand, and the seed problem is mostly a small-model, short-schedule one (the small v2i d16k pair also closed to 2.6 Hz).

## D-027 (2026-10-14, in progress): per-formant observability label

Agreed order (with Hannah): the data questions (v2m, days 2–3, the source-variety arm) first with the current head; observability labels and the per-formant head after, before M4, whose re-track rounds need it as a gate. Old measures stay on all frames, with coverage-matched ones added beside them, so no measure improves by abstaining.

- *Definition* (`synth/observability.py`): for each frame and formant, the precision an ideal analyst could reach from the audio's evidence. The log spectrum is sampled at the harmonics (voiced) or on a 40 Hz grid (whispered frames). Each sample's dB scatter comes from its SNR (signal = the tract-filtered excitation; noise = everything else, plus an analysis floor 60 dB below the frame's peak), and its sensitivity is scaled by snr / (1 + snr). Model: smooth envelope (6 cosines in log frequency; nuisance) + all-pole resonances; bandwidths are nuisance too. Weak priors; posterior SD from the Fisher information, pooled over neighbouring frames of the same sonorant run (exp(−|Δt| / 50 ms): formants move slowly while f0 sweeps the harmonics across them). Label o = 1 / (1 + (SD in cents / 100)²): 0.5 at about a semitone. NaN where the formant is absent or the frame isn't sonorant (absent ≠ unobservable). D-021's four cases come out of one calculation (harmonic spacing, SNR, a channel's cut-off, two close formants); tests in `tests/test_observability.py`.
- *First validation* (`scripts/analysis/obs_validate.py`, `reports/analysis/observability/validate_clean_conformer_v2_d16k.txt`; 150 clean train_v2-config clips, conformer_v2_d16k): F1 median error falls steadily with o (41 → 13 cents from o < 0.1 to ≥ 0.9; Spearman 0.42), F2 26 → 9 (0.25); F3 and F4 are flat (6–11 cents). On clean synthetic audio the model's F3 / F4 come from its learnt prior (tract length, F1 / F2), not from evidence, and its own σ (which includes the prior) tracks error better (0.34–0.52). That is what the label is for: evidence alone, so a model can say when it is leaning on synthetic-data correlations.
- *Next*: the channel part at training time (the loader draws the channel: its gain and noise change each sample's SNR; a 3.4 kHz telephone band or a low-pass then removes F4); validate with channels, where F3 / F4 really vanish; store the synthesis-time SNR compactly per frame for the loader; then the head.

**Training-time labels: design (2026-10-17, with Hannah: started alongside the v3 seed-2 chain; the high-f0 F3 finding in D-026 makes it the next fix).**
- *Store* (per synthetic set, a sidecar beside `flat/`, computed by regenerating each clip's components with `generate(i, cfg, seed)`; audio untouched): per frame, the clean signal and noise power on log-frequency grids, 50–8000 Hz. Signal at 1/12 octave (88 points): on voiced frames the harmonic peaks (`component_snr`'s signal read at k·f0) interpolated in log frequency, so reading back at k·f0 is exact up to interpolation; on whispered frames the tract-filtered noise spectrum. Noise (aspiration, whisper, frication; the analysis floor is added later) at 1/6 octave (44 points). uint8 dB relative to the frame's peak (0.5 dB steps, floor −100 dB) plus the frame's level against the clip's voiced mean power (float16): about 134 bytes a frame, 3–4 GB for a 100k-clip set.
- *Loader*: `augment` already draws the channel; it also reports what the spectra need (filter orders, the RIR's direct-to-reverberant ratio; no new draws, audio unchanged). The channel acts on the spectra analytically, in augment's order: the RIR's late part as noise at −DRR; high-pass, low-pass and EQ responses on signal and synthesis noise; added noise at its SNR with its kind's spectrum (white, pink, brown, hum's low lines, babble's 150–4000 Hz shape); the phone band; a codec as its bandwidth plus quantisation noise (a table per codec and bitrate); clipping as flat noise. Then the analysis floor (60 dB under the frame's peak), and `observability` per crop, batched across frames. Resynthesised clips have no separate noise component (their source is inverse-filtered real audio), so they get no observability label (masked) for now.
- *Validation (pre-registered, before any code runs)*: on 300 fresh clips (train_v2's config, a seed no set uses) with a random channel each, the loader's analytic channel against a full recomputation. The full recomputation runs each component through the same drawn channel (linear stages exactly; noise, clipping and codec differences counted as noise) and then `component_snr`. Accepted when the median |o_analytic − o_full| is ≤ 0.05 and Spearman ≥ 0.9 per formant over sonorant frames, and no channel kind has a median |Δo| above 0.1. Otherwise the failing stage is modelled better (or recomputed from the components) before the store is written for a whole set.
