**Built (2026-10-17).** `channel.draw_channel` / `apply_channel` (augment split into its draws and their application; bit-identical on 300 channels without codecs and 20 with; also reports filter orders, the RIR's DRR, hum's mains, babble's bands and the power clipping removed). `obs_store`: `clean_spectra`, `ClipSpectra.channel`, `write_store` (regenerates each clip with `generate(..., keep_components=True)`, checks each shard's first clip against the stored audio). `codec_table.json`, measured per codec and bitrate (`codec_table.py`: band gain and coding noise relative to the input on 12 clips). `SynthCrops(observability=True)` adds "obs" (t, 4) per crop: the same draws and audio (48 of 48 items identical with and without), every sonorant frame with formants labelled, about 64 ms per 200-frame item. That is fine for full-size training (8 workers, about 3 s a step) but would bottleneck the small S2 runs, so `frame_bound` gets vectorised across frames before those use it. The store costs about 1.4 s a clip per worker (about 2 h for 100k clips on 20 workers).
- *Validation, first run: not accepted* (`observability/channel_validate_v1.txt`). Per formant, median |Δo| 0.002 / 0.003 / 0.011 / 0.019, Spearman 0.968 / 0.949 / 0.912 / 0.902; every stage ≤ 0.036 except **phone, 0.102** (13 clips). The analytic labels are slightly pessimistic at F3 / F4 (mean o 0.72 / 0.60 against 0.80 / 0.70), mostly through the RIR (0.036): the full recomputation counts reverberated voicing as signal, the analytic model counts the late part as noise. Fix, as the rule says: the phone path is measured like a codec ("phone@0" in the table: mu-law noise 27–29 dB under the input in band, not the flat 38 dB assumed). Rerun with the same rule.
- *Validation, rerun: accepted* (`observability/channel_validate.txt`; same 300 clips, seed and rule). Per formant unchanged (median |Δo| 0.002 / 0.003 / 0.011 / 0.019, Spearman 0.968 / 0.949 / 0.914 / 0.906); **phone 0.081** (from 0.102); every other stage ≤ 0.036 (RIR 0.036, clip_loss 0.023, white noise 0.019, codec 0.014, the rest ≤ 0.011). The F3 / F4 pessimism through the RIR stays as recorded: which view is right (late reverberation as signal or as noise) is a question about what an analyst can use, not a modelling error, and the head is trained on the analytic view. Next: write the store for train_v2 once the seed-2 chain frees the CPU; then the head's design and pre-registration.
- *Vectorised (2026-10-17).* `frames_bound` takes all of a crop's frames at once (samples padded with zero weight; an absent formant keeps a column with zero sensitivity, so it meets only its prior and decouples); the resonance's derivatives are exact (`resonance_grad`, closed form) instead of central differences; `ClipSpectra.snr_db_rows` reads the log-regular grids by index for all frames. Against the committed per-frame code on 20 channelled clips (18 288 values): the same NaN pattern, largest |Δo| or relative SD difference 2.6e-4 (the old central differences' error). The loader's labels take 13 ms per 200-frame item (from 71); the small runs read 122 s of audio a second on 16 workers (about 61 items: 260 ms per item per worker), so the labels don't bottleneck them.
- *Store for train_v2* (`write_store`, 6 single-threaded workers beside the seed-2 chain: 16 workers cost its training 40 %, 6 cost about 11 %): the shards were consolidated and removed, so each shard's check now reads `flat/audio.npy` (what the loader reads). Done in 3.7 h (14:44): 30 046 416 frames, 4.0 GB in `flat/`; the per-shard parts matched it exactly and were removed (3.8 GB). On 40 clips the loader labels every sonorant frame with formants; mean o 0.48 / 0.77 / 0.76 / 0.68.

**The head: design and test (pre-registered 2026-10-17, before any run; `chains/obs_chain.sh`, `decide_obs_s2.py`).**
- *Head* (`TeacherConfig.obs_head`, off by default, so earlier checkpoints load unchanged): one linear layer from the formant trunk plus the formant heads' expected positions (detached, so the head's loss can't move the formant heads; it does reach the trunk), giving a logit of o per formant. Loss: cross-entropy against the soft label (proper for a value in [0, 1]) on sonorant frames that have one, weight 0.2 (the bandwidth's). Resynthesised items have no label (masked). Inference returns "observability" beside sigma. The head is built after every other module, so a seed's initial weights and data are the same with and without it: the arm differs from its baseline only by the head and its gradient into the trunk.
- *Arm*: obs_conformer_s0–2, mix_conformer_s0–2's command (S2 Conformer, train_v2, crops 32–400, 2000 steps constant then 1500 decay) plus `obs_head=true`, paired with the same seed on dev. First a replica check: 50 steps of mix_conformer_s0's command on current code; if any loss part differs from its log by more than 0.5 %, fresh baselines (mixr_conformer_s0–2) are trained and used instead.
- *Rule*: **A** no degradation: `decide_m3_s2.py`'s rule on the three pairs. **B** the head learns its label: on the synthetic validation set (with channels), means over seeds, every formant F1–F4 has Spearman(o_pred, o) ≥ 0.8 and MAE ≤ 0.1. **C** it transfers to real speech: on PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase frames, means over seeds, for F1 and F2 each, Spearman(o_pred, |error|) ≤ −0.1 and the median |error| in the lowest fifth of o_pred at least 1.5 × the highest fifth's. F3 (the reference's own scatter), sigma's Spearman and women's F3 o at f0 ≥ 250 Hz against < 200 Hz (D-026 predicts lower) are reported, not judged. **Decision**: "gate" (A, B, C: the head goes into the next full-size teacher and may gate M4's re-tracking), "output" (A, B: it ships as an output calibrated on synthetic data, and the gate needs another route), "fix" (A or B fails).
- *Replica check (17:22): no* (`observability/replica_mix_conformer_s0.txt`). Step-50 loss parts against mix_conformer_s0's log: f0 +0.07 %, voicing +0.3 %, formants −0.1 %, sonorant −1.6 %, **bandwidth −7.8 %**. So, as stated, fresh baselines mixr_conformer_s0–2 (GPU about 4 h in all). Open: whether that is GPU nondeterminism after 50 steps or a code change since 69ccf9a touching the bandwidth or sonorant losses or labels; it matters for older arms paired against mix_conformer, and a second replica on the same code would tell (not run: the GPU is busy). *Resolved (2026-10-17 night).* Two 50-step runs on the same code are bit-identical (`replica_pair.txt`), and a clean checkout of 69ccf9a gives exactly today's step-50 numbers, so no committed change moved anything. On dev, the fresh baselines against the old ones (`mix_vs_mixr.txt`, paired files `mix_conformer_sN_vs_mixr_conformer_sN.txt`): **seeds 1 and 2 identical** (every difference +0.0), **seed 0 different** (mixr minus mix: Hillenbrand F1 +3.6, F2 +5.2, F3 +23.3 Hz, RPA −0.5, women's closed-phase |error| +1.4). So mix_conformer_s0 alone, the first mixed-crop run (69ccf9a with uncommitted edits), came from a different training path; seeds 1–5 reproduce. Earlier arms' seed-0 pairs against mix_conformer carry that extra difference, the others none; the check happened to use seed 0. And a change too small to see in the code moved one S2 seed's Hillenbrand F3 by 23 Hz: the recipe screen's rules must allow for that.
- *Order*: after the seed-2 chain and the store; GPU about 2.5 h. The interactive arm on the candidate data waits for this decision (agreed with Hannah).
- *Result (2026-10-17 21:21): **fix*** (`observability/decide_obs_s2.txt`; baselines mixr_conformer_s0–2). **A holds** (`noharm_obs_s2.txt`; means: Hillenbrand F1 −3.0, F2 −3.5, F3 −2.8 Hz, RPA −0.1, women's closed-phase |error| −2.7). **B fails**: Spearman 0.918 / 0.844 / 0.799 / 0.807, MAE 0.102 / 0.101 / 0.167 / 0.192 (F1–F4; a constant guess at the median scores 0.362 / 0.204 / 0.295 / 0.383, so the linear head learns about half the label's spread at F3 / F4). **C fails**: Spearman(o, |error|) F1 −0.235 (ratio 2.06, which alone would pass), F2 −0.086 (1.28), F3 −0.023 (1.08); sigma tracks real error more strongly (+0.31 to +0.36). Women's F3 o at f0 ≥ 250 Hz 0.827, below 200 Hz 0.830: not lower, against D-026's prediction.
- *Diagnosis (after the result; `scratchpad` checks, numbers here).* (1) **The label, not only the head.** On 300 validation clips with channels, the label's median F3 o rises with f0 (0.82 below 150 Hz, 0.93 at 150–250, 0.95–0.96 at 250–400), and F4's is 0.17 below 150 Hz, rising to 0.85 at 250–300: F4 follows the analysis floor (60 dB under the frame's peak), which a low f0 and a steep tilt put F4's harmonics under. That floor is an analysis assumption, not a property of the recording. (2) **On the Rd-sweep renders** (Story's 10 vowels, 15 cm, no channel) the clean label gives F3 a posterior SD of 5.0–6.6 cents at f0 300 for Rd 0.5–2.5 (o ≥ 0.996), where every model reads F3 30–45 cents low (D-026). The VQT resolves those harmonics (bins 44 Hz apart at 3 kHz, 23 ms window). So under the label's assumptions (the tract's all-pole form known, a smooth six-term source envelope, 2 dB scatter per harmonic) the evidence is in the audio and the models are not using it; D-026's "identifiability" reading holds only if real per-harmonic uncertainty is far above 2 dB or real sources have structure at the harmonic scale that a smooth envelope can't absorb. The label as defined can't tell those apart, which is why it neither predicts real F3 error nor marks high-f0 F3 as hard. (3) A single linear layer is also small for what B asks.
- *Next (to agree with Hannah before changing anything)*: what the label should measure. (a) The floor: from the recording's noise rather than 60 dB under each frame's peak. (b) The per-harmonic scatter: calibrated rather than assumed, either from the synthesiser's own source variety (the same tract under LF, Rosenberg and interactive sources: how far harmonic amplitudes move for reasons that aren't the tract) or from real voices (the all-pole fit's residual at the harmonics on closed-phase frames). (b) is what would let the label carry D-026's tilt confound if it is real. (c) Then the head again, with an MLP, under the same A / B / C rule.
- *Source structure, measured (2026-10-18; `source_scatter.py`, `observability/source_scatter.txt`; 60 clips per config regenerated at seed 5151, 16 204 voiced frames).* Each harmonic's level from the clean voiced component, minus the tract's response at k f0 (all labelled poles), minus the label's six-cosine envelope fitted to the rest. SD of the remainder, dB, 50–500 / 500–1000 / 1–2k / 2–3k / 3–4.5k / 4.5–7.6k Hz: train_v2 5.5 / 4.7 / 4.1 / 3.5 / 4.6 / 11.3; train_v2m 6.3 / 5.3 / 4.7 / 4.1 / 5.7 / 12.0; train_v2s with the return phase 6.7 / 5.8 / 5.1 / 4.4 / 6.0 / 12.4: about twice the 2 dB the label assumes, everywhere. Correlation between two harmonics of one frame (below 4.5 kHz) by distance: +0.87 under 0.05 octave, +0.62 at 0.1–0.2, +0.40 at 0.2–0.33, about 0 at 0.33–0.5, −0.34 to −0.61 from 0.5 to 1.5 (partly the six-term fit's own residual). So the source carries a ripple about a quarter octave wide (around 500 Hz at 3 kHz), the width of a formant; at f0 300 neighbouring harmonics near F3 are 0.14 octave apart and move together. The label's independent 2 dB can't represent that. Next: the bound with this as a correlated covariance (per-band SD and the measured correlation, from each set's own config), its validation pre-registered before it runs.
- *Second label definition (built 2026-10-18; pre-registered before running).* (a) **Recording floor**: white noise 45 dB under the clip's mean voiced frame power, constant over the clip (`obs_store.REC_SNR_DB`, `component_snr(rec_snr_db=45)`), instead of 60 dB under each frame's strongest bin. (b) **Source structure**: on voiced frames the per-harmonic covariance is the independent 2 dB plus a correlated part, its SD per band the measured total (train_v2: 5.53 / 4.74 / 4.10 / 3.52 / 4.58 / 11.30 dB) less 2 dB in quadrature, correlated as exp(−d² / (2 × 0.18²)) with d in octaves; the bound uses the full covariance (`ObservabilityConfig.src_sd_db`, the default now; None gives the first label). With the correlated part set to zero it reproduces the independent path to 4e-14; single-frame SDs grow 1.3–1.6× with it. Loader cost about 94 ms a 200-frame item on one thread (from 13 for the label; S2's budget is about 260). **Rule** (`obs_label_check.py`): 300 validation crops of train_v2 (the trainer's draws, channels on), teacher v3's per-frame |error|; "calibrated" if for F3 and F4 Spearman(o_new, |error|) ≤ −0.15 and at least 0.05 below the first label's, and for F1 and F2 at most 0.05 above it; and the channel validation (`obs_channel_validate.py`, its rule unchanged) accepts the new label. Otherwise "prior": even with the measured source structure the evidence doesn't track where the model fails, so D-026's high-f0 F3 underread is the models' prior, not missing evidence; the head waits and M4's gate uses sigma. Reported: floors of 35 and 55 dB; median o by f0 band; the clean F3 SD on the Rd-sweep renders. "calibrated" → the head arm again with a two-layer head, pre-registered then.
- *Result (2026-10-18 02:45): **prior*** (`observability/label_check.txt`). Spearman(o, |error|) F1–F4, first label −0.475 / −0.387 / −0.322 / −0.309, second −0.460 / −0.384 / −0.324 / −0.315 (floors of 35 and 55 dB within 0.01): no better at F3 / F4. On the Rd-sweep renders F3's SD at f0 300, Rd 2.5 goes from 6.6 to 7.8 cents. The channel validation of the second label was stopped unrun (not needed on "prior"; `channel_validate.txt` is still the first label's).
- *A flaw found after the result, in both definitions* (stated here, not used to overturn the result). The bound pools a formant's information over neighbouring frames (exp(−|Δt| / 50 ms)) as if each frame's evidence were independent, which divides the SD by about 3. That holds for the independent scatter, and for the source part when f0 moves the harmonics, but in a steady vowel the source's ripple sits at the same harmonics in every frame, so its evidence is counted about ten times. One frame alone (`context_s = 0`), F3's SD on the Rd-sweep renders: f0 210 Rd 0.5 / 2.5: 13.9 / 17.4 cents; **f0 300 Rd 0.5 / 2.5: 20.6 / 24.5 cents** (first label 15.8 / 19.1; `obs_rd_nopool.py`, `observability/rd_nopool.txt`), against 30–45 cents of model error there (D-026). So the "prior" reading rests on the pooling assumption, and D-026's question (missing evidence or the models' prior) is still open. A third definition would pool only what changes between frames (the source part correlated in time unless f0 moves its harmonics); it would be the third label tried, so the rule would be the same one, stated before it runs, and the result read with that in mind. Decision with Hannah: try it, or stop the label work here and gate M4 on sigma.
- *Decision (Hannah, 2026-10-18)*: **gate M4 on sigma for now**; the label work stops here (the head, the store, the second definition and the source measurement stay built, off by default: `obs_head=False`; `src_sd_db` stays the measured default but nothing trains on it). If sigma proves a poor gate, the third definition (pooling only what changes between frames) is the next step, with the same rule.

**v2m, three S2 seeds (2026-10-14 19:00; d16k pair running).** Women's closed-phase bias change against mix −10.9 / −16.3 / −3.4 (mean −10.2; v2i's first three seeds −11.8); men −6.2 / −6.2 / −2.3. Against closed phase, women F1 / F2 / F3: mix +17.9 / +14.8 / +26.1, v2i +4.2 / −9.5 / −17.9, **v2m +6.6 / −0.8 / −7.1**; men v2m +7.0 / +1.7 / −8.4 (v2i −14.2 at F3). Every k35 criterion of `decide_v2m.py` holds on the paired files (Hillenbrand F1 −5.7, F2 −3.9, F3 +5.8, F3 detection −0.7, F4 detection −1.6, RPA +0.2 to +0.7); ARCTIC slt −7 to −11. Shortcut probe (`sensitivity/shortcut_probe_v2m.txt`; Rosenberg pulse, women, F1 / F2 / F3): without feedback mix −6 / +19 / −16, v2i −35 / −15 / −22, v2m −17 / −13 / −19; with feedback v2m −5 / −6 / −3. The F1 correction applied without feedback is halved (men: gone). The F3 underread without feedback is the Rosenberg pulse on our tube (mix reads it too). Half of v2i's correction is what a model should apply where it can't tell from the audio whether feedback is there, so the rest may be genuine ambiguity rather than a shortcut.

**LF-derived area pulses: built, not used for training (2026-10-14 evening).** Planned: train_v2x, crossing pulse family (Rosenberg / LF-derived) with feedback. `interactive.lf_area_pulse` inverts the static flow law (Bernoulli plus viscous) for the area whose flow without feedback is the LF flow (r 0.996–0.998 with the LF flow; H1–H2 −2 / +3.6 / +11 / +17 dB at Rd 0.5 / 1 / 1.7 / 2.5). But the LF flow is still 27–35 % of its peak at the main excitation (te; then the return phase), so the derived glottis is a third open there, and with feedback the interaction is counted twice: LF describes the flow, whose skew and return phase the interaction itself partly makes (Rothenberg). Probe: mix reads F3 +85 to +113 Hz on LF pulses with feedback (+8 to +10 with Rosenberg's, which excites at closure); Praat loses the formants at Rd 1.0. Birkholz et al. (2019) found F2 / F3 not systematically affected with self-oscillating rubber folds, so this cell is unphysical. train_v2x dropped before rendering; the pulse stays as a probe (shortcut probe, without feedback only), the sampler option `p_lf_pulse` (default 0; v2m reproduces) marked not for training. Variety, if wanted, belongs in area space (closure corner, speed quotient, gap), where the interaction makes the flow's skew itself. The queue stays: v2m d16k pair → decision → full-size v2m → days 2–3.

**Closure sharpness (pre-registered 2026-10-14, before rendering; `select_base.py`, `chains/sharp_chain.sh`).** Rosenberg's type-C pulse closes with one fixed corner, so the interactive source's high-frequency tilt has no range of its own (LF's comes from the return phase). Suspected cause of the Rosenberg-specific F3 behaviour (v2t's F3 losses; mix reads women's F3 −16 Hz on Rosenberg audio without feedback). `interactive.area_pulse` closure p: the closing branch cos(π/2 · u^p) (u the normalised closing time; smooth at the peak, closed at the same instant, final slope p × Rosenberg's; p = 1 is Rosenberg exactly, tested). Uncoupled flow derivative relative to H1, p 1 → 3: 1–2 kHz −23.5 → −14.7 dB, 2–3 kHz −30.1 → −18.9, 3–4 kHz −35.9 → −21.9, H1–H2 +1.6 → +3.7 (about LF Rd 1 → 0.5). In area space, so the interaction makes the flow's skew itself (unlike the LF-derived pulse).
- *`train_v2s`* = train_v2m with `interactive_closure` (1, 3), log-uniform per interactive clip. One change (train_v2m reproduces with the option off).
- The same rule as v2m (`decide_v2m.py ... v2s`: k35 three seeds, s0 / s1 to d16k, identity), then `select_base.py`: if both pass, v2s carries forward if it improves the F3 it targets (Rosenberg-without-feedback F3 error from the shortcut probe at least 3 Hz smaller, or Hillenbrand F3 MAE at least 5 Hz smaller) without giving back the rest (women's bias change no more than 3 Hz weaker, shortcut no more than 2 Hz larger); otherwise v2m. One passing: that one; neither: fix.
- The winner gets the full-size run (against both train_v2 full-size seeds), then days 2–3 (`m3_speech_chain.sh auto`). The v2m chain is stopped before its full-size run and its remaining stages taken over by this chain (its s0 d16k run resumes from its checkpoint).

**v2m decision: fix (2026-10-15 08:56; `decide_v2m.txt`, `closed_phase/cp_small_v2m_dev.txt`, `sensitivity/resynth_identity_v2m_d16k.txt`).** A Windows restart at 01:34 cost about 6.5 h (v2m s1 d16k restarted from step 16000). Every criterion held except one: d16k women's F3 bias −30.5 Hz (limit ≥ −10). The rest: k35 bias change −8.8 (frames where every model including the d16k pair has a value, so slightly different from −10.2 above); d16k women's F1 bias −7.2 (v2i −11.3), identity resynthesis −0.2 (v2i −5.6; f0 ≥ 300 still +4.8 / −11.3 against −16 / −12). Women F1 / F2 / F3 against closed phase, d16k: v2m −6 / −17 / −30, v2i −11 / −28 / −41, train_v2 full size +14 / +9 / +15; men v2m +1 / −1 / −15. So the longer schedule moves v2m's women further down at every formant (k35 → d16k: −14 / −16 / −23), F3 most, and halving the feedback halved v2i's overshoot rather than removing it. F3 underread on Rosenberg audio is what train_v2s targets, so the queue stands: v2s through the same rule, then `select_base.py` (neither passing → fix, and days 2–3 wait).

**Fail branch (pre-registered 2026-10-15 ~11:00, with Hannah, before select_base.py ran and before the probe results below; `chains/fail_chain.sh`).** Two observations shape it. (1) In cents, v2m's d16k underread is uniform: women −19 / −18 / −19 against closed phase (v2i −35 / −27 / −26; full-size train_v2 +43 / +9 / +9), so F3's −30 Hz is the same proportional error as F1's −6 Hz: a tract-scale shift, not an error growing up the cascade. The F3 limit (−10 Hz ≈ 6 cents) was about five times tighter than the F1 one (±10 Hz ≈ ±30 cents); future limits go in cents. Not changed for this rule. (2) The second reference disagrees in size (`sensitivity/hill_signed_v2m.txt`, `hill_signed.py`, signed, not pre-registered as a gate): Hillenbrand women's F3, model minus reference, v2m d16k −7.0 / −17.2 Hz (−4 / −11 cents), full-size train_v2 +19 / +18, v2m k35 −8 to −13. Whole-vowel LPC references sit slightly above closed-glottis poles, so a perfect reader would come out a little negative there.
- *CPU checks, run now whatever the decision* (none changes a pre-registered rule): `render_check.py` (Story's vowels: the standard cascade path against the interactive tube path with the same LF source, models' B − A for mix in cents; the closed-phase method on renders with known poles, area as EGG) and `cascade_probe.py` (PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase frames: F3's slope in cents per cent when the expected bins passed on from F1 and F2 move ±30 cents).
- *Readings fixed now*: render_check "mismatch" if any |mix B − A| ≥ 10 cents at F1–F3 (then no dose arm: the renderer first). cascade_probe "material" if v2m d16k women's F3 slope ≥ 0.33 (a third of a uniform shift carried by the conditioning); then a joint-head-only arm on train_v2m is proposed (not scheduled: GPU time held for it, with Hannah). Closed-phase reference on synthetic renders beyond ±15 Hz at women's F3 → the F3 limit is revisited, any new rule confirmed on semi-private before use.
- *GPU, only on select_base "fix"*: (1) control: mix_conformer_s0 to 16000 and d16k (train_v2; never saw the interactive source): does a small model move down k35 → d16k anyway? (2) dose arm: the better of v2m / v2s (smaller |d16k women's F3 bias|) with `p_interactive_coupled` 0.25 (train_v2q or train_v2sq), the same rule (`decide_v2m.py`). *Expectation, written down beforehand*: on the line through v2i (−41) and v2m (−30), d16k women's F3 about −25 Hz, so the F3 limit probably fails again; F1 near 0. Its value is the dose curve; with the control, whether the F3 limit is reachable at small size at all.

**Render check: "mismatch", on men's F2 only (2026-10-15 ~11:30; `sensitivity/render_check.txt`).** The interactive renderer's transfer peaks sit on the labels (within 0.5 Hz at F1–F3): no render-versus-label mismatch. mix's B − A (cents): men −0.6 / −12.9 / +3.5, women +4.4 / −1.7 / +0.8. The one cell over 10 cents is men's F2, where mix reads the standard cascade path +13 cents high and the tube path at 0: the two renderers differ there as a train_v2 model hears them, and the cascade path is the one further from the poles. Women's F3, the failing measure, agrees across renderers. The closed-phase method on renders with known poles: women with feedback −0.1 / +3.0 / +0.7 Hz, men +1.3 / +2.9 / +2.4 (without feedback within 12 Hz): the reference is unbiased on our physics, so the F3 limit is not revisited. By the reading fixed beforehand the dose arm does not run (the chain runs only the mix control). The 10-cent reading should have been limited to the failing measure; noted, not changed.

**Fail branch extended (2026-10-15 ~13:00, with Hannah, before select_base.py ran; `chains/fail_chain.sh`, `cp_bias.py`, `decide_full.py`).** So the GPU isn't idle after the control, and still on "fix" only:
- *Full-size check*, if mix's women's closed-phase F3 bias falls by 10 Hz or more from k35 to d16k (a small model that never saw the interactive source drifts down too, so the small-model d16k limits can't separate the arms): a full-size Conformer to d16k on the better of v2m / v2s, checked by `decide_full.py` with the same d16k limits on the one model (women's F1 within ±10 Hz, F3 ≥ −10 Hz, identity within ±5 Hz). Exploratory: a pass says the small-model failure was a small-model effect and comes back to Hannah; it does not carry the set forward.
- *Then days 2–3 on train_v2* (`m3_speech_chain.sh v2`, unchanged): the +VocalSet arm exists (m3_conformer); +VocalSet+Zeroth S2 seeds against it and mix (decide_m3_s2.py); on "m3" the teacher v3 candidate at full size and its second seed. Whether real speech helps doesn't depend on the interaction work; if an interactive base is adopted later, the arms are rerun on it.

**Cascade probe: small (2026-10-15 ~13:30; `sensitivity/cascade_probe.txt`).** Every model's F3 slope against the passed-on F1 / F2 bins is 0.000 (median over frames); a full octave moves one utterance's F3 by about 22 cents. The heads read their formants from the encoder; the cascade carries none of the underread.

**v2m's F3 underread is an f0 effect (2026-10-15 ~14:00; PTDB-TUG dev, per speaker, v2m_conformer_s0_d16k).** Women's F3 against full-size train_v2 (cents) by speaker f0: 183 Hz −20, 201 −19, 204 −16, 228 −27, **260 −54, 294 −49**; against closed phase −17, −10, −1, −15, −62, −36. No relation with the closed quotient (Spearman +0.05; quartiles −32 to −25), so incomplete closure biasing the reference is unlikely. Men −4 cents throughout. Two of six dev speakers carry it.
- *Pre-registered before running (`f0_sweep.py`)*: Story's vowels on the women's tract at f0 150–400 Hz, the standard path and the interactive tube with feedback. "Prior shift" if, on both renders, v2m d16k's F3 change from 150 to 300 Hz is at least 15 cents more negative than mix's: the interactive sets moved what the model falls back on where harmonics sample F3 sparsely (then candidates: the length cues the interactive source carries, glottal size and subglottal resonances scaled with tract length; the observability head as the principled fix). Otherwise the f0 effect comes from something real voices have and these renders don't.
- *Result: "prior shift", but the other way round from the hypothesis (`sensitivity/f0_sweep.txt`).* F3 change from 150 to 300 Hz on a fixed tract, cents: standard path mix +26, full-size train_v2 +15, v2m k35 +12, **v2m d16k −22**; interactive with feedback +40, +25, +28, **−3**. Over the whole sweep, v2m d16k's F3 is flat to 350 Hz (noise about ±10), while mix and full-size train_v2 read F3 25–40 cents higher at 300–350 Hz than at 150 on the same tract. So v2m didn't gain a downward fallback; it lost the upward f0 → F3 lean the train_v2 models have (train_v2 draws half its tract lengths from f0, D-020). On a fixed synthetic tract v2m is the more accurate. On real high-f0 women it reads 36–62 cents below closed phase, while full-size train_v2 is near it. Reading: at high f0 something in real voices pulls the evidence for F3 down, and train_v2's f0 lean happened to cancel it. Candidate: the steep spectral tilt of breathier high-f0 voices. With harmonics 260–300 Hz apart, the harmonic below F3 is boosted relative to the one above, which pulls the peak down. The interactive sets lack that tilt: Rosenberg's closure corner is fixed, and v2s added only shallower tilts.

**v2s decision: fix; base: fix (2026-10-15 17:03; `decide_v2s.txt`, `decide_base.txt`, `sensitivity/shortcut_probe_sharp.txt`).** Two failures. At k35, F4 detection was −2.6 (limit −2; seeds −3.7 / −0.4 / −3.6), where v2m passed at −1.6. At d16k, women's F3 bias was −28.3 Hz (v2m −30.5). Everything else held. k35 women's bias change against mix −12.3 (v2m −8.8). Women against closed phase, F1 / F2 / F3: k35 +3.3 / −2.1 / −6.2, d16k −9.3 / −15.0 / −28.3. Identity at d16k −3.0. Shortcut probe: F3 without feedback 19.0 Hz (v2m 16.5), shortcut 8.9 (7.4), coupled 8.5 (4.6). Sharper closures moved F3 by about 2 Hz and cost F4 detection, consistent with training that expects more high-frequency energy than real voices have. The closure-sharpness hypothesis is not supported. The probe's LF Rd 1.7 rows (breathier, at f0 210) show no extra F3 underread for v2m (−1 Hz without feedback), so if tilt matters it does so at high f0, where the f0 sweep found the effect. The fail branch took over at 17:03 (mix control; render_check blocked the dose arm).

**Fail branch overnight (2026-10-15/16).** Control: mix_conformer_s0's women's closed-phase bias, k35 → d16k, F1 / F2 / F3 +12.6 / +15.1 / +20.9 → +17.4 / +11.5 / +14.1 Hz (F3 −6.8; `closed_phase/cp_bias_mix_d16k.txt`). A small model that never saw the interactive source doesn't drift down, so v2m's and v2s's d16k underread (k35 → d16k −14 / −16 / −23) comes from the interactive data and the small-model limits were fair; no full-size check. Days 2–3 on train_v2 followed: +VocalSet+Zeroth (v2r2) against mix, three S2 seeds, DECISION m3 (Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE −11.5 / −13.1 / −8.4, RPA −0.2, women's closed-phase |error| −5.9; `decide_v2r2_s2.txt`); the teacher v3 candidate `conformer_v2r2` is training at full size (slower than train_v2 alone: about 1,150 steps an hour).

**Rd sweep (pre-registered 2026-10-16 ~12:40, before running; `rd_sweep.py`).** Is the high-f0 underread a tilt effect? Story's vowels on the women's tract, standard path, LF Rd 0.5–2.5 at f0 210 and 300. "Tilt" if at f0 300 v2m d16k's F3 change from Rd 0.5 to 2.5 is at least 15 cents more negative than full-size train_v2's. If so, the area-space return phase (built alongside, below) gets an arm; if not, it is kept for M4's source variety and the high-f0 effect needs another explanation.
- *Result: "no tilt" (`sensitivity/rd_sweep.txt`), because tilt affects every model.* F3 change from Rd 0.5 to 2.5, cents: at f0 210 all within ±7 (mix +1, v2m +2, v2m d16k −7, full-size −2); at f0 300 **mix −29, v2m −43, v2m d16k −45, full-size −40**. At high f0 a steep tilt pulls every model's F3 down by 30–45 cents on a fixed tract, including full-size train_v2, whose training had LF up to Rd 2.7 at all f0. So it is not a gap in v2m's data. With harmonics 300 Hz apart, the evidence can't separate F3's position from the tilt, and the prior decides. Full-size train_v2's f0 lean (+30 cents at Rd 0.5, f0 300) offsets most of it (−10 at Rd 2.5); v2m d16k has no lean and lands at −52, about what it reads on the two highest-f0 dev women (−62 / −36). The high-f0 F3 problem is mostly one of identifiability: what D-027's observability label and head are for. *(2026-10-17, D-027: the label as defined finds F3 well determined there, 5–7 cents at f0 300 even at Rd 2.5; so either the label is too optimistic about per-harmonic scatter, or this is the models leaning on their prior, not missing evidence. Open.)* Which prior should fill the gap is a separate question (train_v2's f0 → length link is the population's, D-020 halved it on purpose).
- *Area return phase built, no arm* (`interactive.area_pulse` ret, `return_fraction`, sampler `interactive_return`, default off; tests in `tests/test_interactive.py`). An exponential return in area with LF's Ta at the clip's Rd: from t1 the area's slope decays to exactly 0 at t1 + 4 Ta (or the cycle's end), t1 chosen so the area closes there, as LF's return phase is built in flow. Slope continuous throughout; area derivative harmonics 20–40 relative to H1: Rd 1.0 −51 dB (LF −46, Rosenberg −35), Rd 1.7 −58 (LF −56), Rd 2.5 −61 (LF −63); worst dip between neighbouring harmonics −7.5 dB, like Rosenberg's (−6.9) and LF's (−7.4), against sin²'s nulls. A first version subtracted an offset from the area, which left a 2 % slope step at closure and rippled the spectrum by up to 22 dB at Rd 2.5; replaced. The Rd sweep says it won't fix the high-f0 F3 underread, so it waits for M4's source variety.

## D-028 (2026-10-17, pre-registered): long inputs and context carry-over

Every training set so far draws clips of 2–4 s (`duration_s` (2, 4); train_v2 200–400 frames, median 300) and crops go up to 400 frames, while `analyse()` runs whole files. D-021 item 2 planned crops of 4–16 s from clips up to about 16 s; only the short side was done (D-024). The length test stopped at 400 frames, the longest synthetic clip. Two questions with Hannah: does whole-file analysis beyond 4 s help or hurt the current models, and does one speaker's context leak into another's formants in the same file? (Long clips would make that pooling stronger, and a model that learns "one speaker, one tract" would smooth over a real change.)

- *Measurements* (CPU-built, run on the GPU after the D-027 arm, fp32; `chains/post_obs_chain.sh`). **Synthetic** (`long_test.py`): `data/synth/val_long_v2` (`render_long.py`), 150 clips of 8–16 s, train_v2's config, seed 2718 (used by no set); each model on the whole clip and on 4 s windows (400 frames, hop 200, each frame from the window where it is most central), MAE on sonorant frames by position (0–400, 400–800, 800+). **Real** (`cp_long.py`, PTDB-TUG dev, closed-phase frames): each speaker's utterances joined in fours (0.3 s gaps; parts padded to whole frames so frames align exactly), closed-phase median |error| against each utterance alone; **carry-over**: for each dev speaker B, files A A A B and B A A A with a partner A of the other sex and one of the same sex, B's formants on the frames B alone calls sonorant, median shift in cents signed toward A. Models: teacher v3 (conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k), conformer_v2_s1_d16k, the GRU teacher_v2_d16k.
- *Rule* (`decide_long.py`, judged on teacher v3; the others reported): **"long"** if whole-file MAE beyond frame 400 is at least 3 % above the 4 s windows' (any of F1–F3), or joined same-speaker files raise the closed-phase median |error| by at least 3 % (either sex, any of F1–F3), or cross-sex carry-over moves B's median formant by at least 10 cents (either order, any of F1–F3): a long-clip set is then worth building (roadmap 5: a few per cent with speaker switches, the tract changing independently of the source; long single-speaker clips with sustained larynx-height and lip-protrusion changes). **"pooling"** if none of those, but whole files are at least 3 % better than 4 s windows beyond frame 400, or joined files at least 3 % better: long clips are then worth trying for the evidence they pool (with the VTL head). **"not now"** otherwise.
- *Also in that chain*: the replica pair (mix_conformer_s0's command twice for 50 steps on the same code), to tell GPU nondeterminism from code change in D-027's failed replica check.

- *Result (2026-10-17 night): **long*** (`long/decide_long.txt`). The synthetic part first ran with a windowing bug (each frame's best-window score started at −1, above every window's score, so the 4 s windows left almost every frame empty and the comparison used a few frames per window; `long_test_bug.txt`, `decide_long_bug.txt`, superseded); fixed (start at −∞; a check that every sonorant frame has an estimate) and rerun before the result was used. All three criteria trip for teacher v3. **Carry-over**: after three utterances of the other sex, speaker B's F1 moves 24 cents toward them (22 when B comes first; F2 / F3 4–8 cents); same sex 0–2 cents. **Joined same-speaker files** (about 10 s): closed-phase F1 median |error| +5.1 % (women), +9.4 % (men); F2 / F3 within 3 %. **Synthetic** (one speaker per clip): whole-file MAE beyond frame 400 is 3–4 % above the 4 s windows' on F1 and F2 (F3 within 1.2 %). The other models: conformer_v2 carries more (F1 +37 / +30 cents; joined +13 % / +16 %), the GRU teacher v2 less (+17 / +13; joined about +1 %), as its memory is shorter. Context beyond the training length hurts, and the Conformer's global attention carries one speaker's tract into another's frames, in either order.
- *Next* (roadmap 5): long training clips with speaker switches (the tract changing independently of the source) and sustained larynx-height and lip-protrusion changes, designed and pre-registered with the VTL head. Meanwhile, `analyse()` could run in overlapping 4 s windows (on this evidence no worse anywhere, and better on real joined files): a separate, small decision.
- *Windowed analysis (pre-registered 2026-10-18, before running; `decide_window.py`, `chains/recipe_chain.sh` step 0).* `analyse(..., window_frames=400)` runs files longer than 4 s in windows (hop 2 s, each frame from its most central window; `<ckpt>@w400` in the evaluation scripts, cached apart; tested). Every PTDB-TUG utterance is longer than 4 s (median 7.1 s, maximum 11.2 s), so every PTDB-TUG measure so far ran beyond the training length. Teacher v3 whole against windowed, dev, the same model (no seed noise): **"windows"** (the default from then on) if the closed-phase median |error| (each utterance alone) is no more than 2 % worse for any of F1–F3 in either sex, PTDB-TUG RPA no more than 0.2 point worse, and Hillenbrand (words of about 0.5 s) unchanged (|diff| ≤ 0.1 Hz); otherwise "whole". Joined files and carry-over under windows are reported.
- *Windowed analysis, result (2026-10-18 02:03): **windows*** (`long/decide_window.txt`). Teacher v3, windowed minus whole: Hillenbrand unchanged (+0.0 Hz F1–F3); PTDB-TUG RPA **+0.60 point**; closed-phase median |error| women 28.5 / 36.7 / 43.1 → 28.0 / 35.8 / 42.8 Hz (−1.8 / −2.5 / −0.7 %), men 16.0 / 23.4 / 32.7 → 15.3 / 22.9 / 32.0 (−4.4 / −2.1 / −2.1 %). Joined files under windows: F1 +1.2 % (women), −0.2 % (men), against +5.1 / +9.4 % whole. Carry-over: median shift 0 cents either way (mean |shift| 4–19 cents, frames whose window still holds the other speaker). *Timing*: the D-029 screen (running) compares arms with baselines measured whole (prediction caches, closed-phase frames), so the default changes after it ends, with every reference number re-measured windowed then (the evaluation cache key gets "-w400"; `add_model.py` windowed too). Until then, results stay whole and comparable.

- *Windows made the default (2026-10-27, roadmap 3; commit `736205c`)*: `analyse()` runs files longer than 400 frames in 4 s windows unless told otherwise.
  - Evaluation follows: "<ckpt>@w0" asks for the whole file and keeps the old cache key, so whole-file and windowed predictions are never mixed.
  - Resynthesis analyses go to a `-w400` directory.
  - `add_model.py` windows on the GPU too. Before this change its GPU path batched whole files past `analyse()`.
  - *Re-measured windowed* (`reports/analysis/windowed/`; a new scratch directory with the closed-phase frames stripped of model columns, so no whole-file column is reused): teacher v3, its sibling seeds 0 and 2, labeller v3 and D-031's rA. Teacher v3 reproduces this entry's decision run exactly (closed-phase median |error| women F1 / F2 / F3 28.5 / 36.7 / 43.1 → 28.0 / 35.8 / 42.8 Hz; RPA +0.6).
  - Across the five: Hillenbrand is unchanged (its words are under 4 s), PTDB-TUG RPA rises 0.5–1.0 point, and closed-phase |error| falls slightly everywhere. Signed bias moves by at most 1.6 Hz (sibling seed 0, women's F1, 9.8 → 8.2).
  - Comparisons from now on use these windowed references. Earlier reports stay as they were, measured whole.

## D-029 (2026-10-18, pre-registered): training recipe screen

Roadmap item 3 with Hannah. S2 Conformer on train_v2, three seeds per arm against mixr_conformer_s0–2 (today's code; seeds 1–2 identical to mix_conformer's), the same command otherwise (2000 steps constant, 1500 decay, crops 32–400). Arms: learning rate ×2, ×0.5, ×3 (quality; the best rate at S2 says little about full size, which changes width); warmup 250 (cost); decay over 20 % of the run (2800 + 700; cost); schedule-free AdamW, 3500 steps without decay (quality; D-018 was one run). Muon, Adan, batch 64 and cosine warmup follow once built.
- *Decided on synthetic validation* (`decide_recipe.py`): the six baseline seeds' SDs there are F1 0.50, F2 1.08, F3 1.91, F4 3.62 Hz, RPA 0.07 point; on dev Hillenbrand 3.4 / 7.9 / 17.6 Hz, 7–9 times noisier (and D-027's seed 0 moved dev F3 by 23 Hz while synthetic F3 moved 0.6 Hz). SE of the mean difference over n seeds = sqrt(2 / n) × SD. A measure is better / worse beyond 2 SE, grey between 1 and 2 SE. Real-voice guard-rails catch only large regressions: Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz, RPA ≥ −1.0 point, women's closed-phase |error| ≤ +5 Hz (means over the pairs). **"worse"**: any measure worse or a guard-rail fails. **"grey"**: any measure grey toward worse, or none better and one grey toward better: seeds 3–5 (with mixr_conformer_s3–5), then the same rule at n = 6. **"better"**: one of F1–F4 better, RPA not worse. **"same"**: otherwise. A cost arm is adopted on "same" or "better", a quality arm on "better"; anything adopted is confirmed at full size before it enters a teacher. Dry run on mixr against mix (seeds 0–2): "same".
- *Results, seeds 0–2 (2026-10-18 13:55; `recipe/decide_*_n3.txt`)*, synthetic validation, arm minus baseline, F1 / F2 / F3 / F4 Hz, RPA: **learning rate ×2: better** (−2.3 / −2.5 / −3.3 / −8.0, +0.30); **×3: better** (−3.2 / −4.9 / −5.1 / −9.9, +0.36); ×0.5: worse (+4.3 / +8.9 / +9.7 / +11.1, −0.45; Hillenbrand F2 and F3 guard-rails fail); warmup 250: same (−0.6 / +0.6 / +0.1 / −1.5, +0.11): adopted as a cost change; **decay over 20 %: better** (−1.3 / −2.4 / −0.9 / −4.4, +0.12): adopted (cheaper and better); schedule-free AdamW: worse (+1.9 / +5.8 / +6.7 / +3.7, −0.29; F1–F3 guard-rails fail), which settles D-018's single run. No guard-rail fails for the adopted arms.
- *Next (pre-registered 2026-10-18 14:00, before running)*: the learning rate improves monotonically up to the largest tried, so the sweep continues: ×5 (5e-3) and ×8 (8e-3), the same rule against mixr_conformer. The chosen S2 rate is the largest arm that is "better" and not worse than the next smaller one on any of F1–F4 / RPA beyond 2 SE (the arms' difference, with SE as for a baseline difference). Then one combination arm (that rate, warmup 250, decay over 20 %), the same rule. S2's rate does not carry to full size (width changes the best rate), so full size gets its own check before a teacher uses any of this.
- *Learning-rate sweep continued (2026-10-18 17:57)*: ×5 **better** (−3.3 / −6.0 / −6.3 / −10.1, +0.43), ×8 **better** (−3.5 / −6.9 / −7.5 / −10.8, +0.44); no guard-rail fails. ×8 against ×5: F1 −0.20, F2 −0.90, F3 −1.20, F4 −0.77 Hz, RPA +0.01 (all within 1 SE), so by the rule the S2 rate is **8e-3** (×8). Gains flatten (F1 −3.2 / −3.3 / −3.5 from ×3 to ×8) but haven't turned; training stays stable at ×8 (largest logged loss at step 50, gradient norm ≤ 3.9). Combination arm running: 8e-3, warmup 250, decay over 20 % (2800 + 700), same rule.
- *Combination arm (2026-10-18 19:58)*: 8e-3, warmup 250, decay over 20 %: **worse** by the rule. On synthetic validation it beats mixr (−2.5 / −4.2 / −3.4 / −11.0, +0.43, all beyond 2 SE), but the PTDB-TUG RPA guard-rail fails (per seed −0.2, −1.5, −1.4 points; mean −1.03, limit −1.0; seeds 1 and 2 outside their intervals), and against lr8_conformer alone it is worse on F1–F3 (+0.96 / +2.71 / +4.12 Hz, +2.4 / +3.1 / +2.6 SE; F4 and RPA level; `recipe/combo_vs_lr8.txt`). Neither part failed alone at the old rate (warmup 250 RPA −0.2, decay 20 % −0.0, ×8 +0.4), so at 8e-3 the short warmup or the shorter decay (or both) costs what they saved at 1e-3; not separated. **The S2 recipe stays 8e-3 with warmup 1000, 2000 + 1500** (lr8_conformer), which is D-030's reference. Warmup 250 and decay over 20 % are withdrawn as adopted cost changes at this rate.

## D-030 (2026-10-18, pre-registered): Muon and Adan on the S2 Conformer

Hannah's question: does Muon, Adan, or a hybrid speed training up? `TrainConfig.optimizer`: "adan" (Adan, Xie et al. 2022, `adan-pytorch` 0.1.0, MIT; default betas, proximal weight decay), "muon" (`torch.optim.Muon`, Jordan et al. 2024, for the weights of nn.Linear layers outside the output heads: Conformer attention and feed-forward, projections; AdamW for convolutions, biases, norms and heads), "muon_adan" (Muon there, Adan for the rest). Muon's update is scaled to AdamW's RMS (`adjust_lr_fn="match_rms_adamw"`), so the same learning rate and weight decay apply to every group and the schedule drives both. Tested (train, checkpoint, decay branch).
- *Reference*: the tuned AdamW recipe from D-029, combo_conformer if its decision is "better" and it is not worse than lr8_conformer on synthetic validation (`compare_arms.py`, 2 SE), otherwise lr8_conformer.
- *Arms* (`chains/opt_chain.sh`, seeds 0–2, the reference's schedule): adan, muon, muon_adan, each at the reference rate (8e-3) and at half of it (4e-3; 8e-3 is high and they may want less). **Amended 2026-10-18 18:20, before any arm ran** (Hannah: Adan tolerates larger rates; Xie et al. use 5–10× Adam's): Adan alone at ×1 / ×2 / ×5 of 8e-3 (8e-3, 1.6e-2, 4e-2); Muon (+ AdamW) at 8e-3 and 4e-3; Muon + Adan with Muon at 8e-3 / 4e-3 and its Adan part at Adan-alone's best rate (`rest_lr_scale`, the non-Muon groups' rate as a multiple of Muon's; tested). Best arm per optimiser by `pick_best_arm.py` (decision rank, then the lowest summed SE units over F1–F4). Adan's restart strategy off, as in all but one of the paper's tables. A diverged run (non-finite validation error) counts as worse. `decide_recipe.py` against the reference (its rule; grey counts as not established here, no extra seeds).
- *Speed*: for each optimiser whose better-decided rate comes out "better" or "same" (the reference rate on a tie), a half-budget arm: every step count halved (warmup, comb teacher forcing, constant and decay phases), against the reference at full budget, same rule. "same" or "better" there means it reaches the reference's quality in half the steps. Wall-clock (audio s/s over the constant phase) reported per arm, since Muon's orthogonalisation and Adan's per-parameter loop cost time per step.
- Measured whole, like D-029, so the screens stay comparable; the windowed default (D-028) switches after this screen.
- *Results (2026-10-19 13:36; `recipe/decide_*_n3.txt`, `recipe/opt_speed.txt`)*: reference lr8_conformer (the combination arm was worse, D-029). Synthetic validation, arm minus lr8, F1 / F2 / F3 / F4 Hz, RPA points; Hillenbrand F1 guard-rail (dev) in brackets:
  - Adan 8e-3: **worse** (−1.7 / −2.3 / −1.0 / −4.9, −0.00; PTDB-TUG RPA guard-rail −1.1 fails) [+2.5]. Adan 1.6e-2: **better** (−2.5 / −4.1 / −0.7 / −2.6, +0.07) [+3.0]. Adan 4e-2: **better** (−1.9 / −3.6 / −3.5 / −5.3, +0.07) [+1.9]; Adan's best by `pick_best_arm.py`. Stable at 4e-2 (5× the AdamW rate), as Xie et al. suggest.
  - Muon 8e-3 (+ AdamW): **better** (−2.7 / −5.2 / −3.8 / −12.0, +0.08) [+3.9]. Muon 4e-3: **worse**, by the Hillenbrand F1 guard-rail only (+7.3 > +4), though best of the Muon arms on synthetic (−3.0 / −5.4 / −4.5 / −16.2, +0.06).
  - **Muon 8e-3 + Adan 4e-2: better on all five** (−4.1 / −8.3 / −6.5 / −15.6, +0.13; 10.0 / 9.5 / 4.1 / 5.3 / 2.2 SE) [+3.5]: the best arm. Muon 4e-3 + Adan 4e-2: synthetic level with it (−4.1 / −8.0 / −6.1 / −17.4, +0.11) but **worse** by the Hillenbrand F1 guard-rail (+5.4).
  - *Speed*: no optimiser reaches lr8's quality in half the steps (half-budget arms all **worse**: Adan 4e-2 +5.2 / +8.9 / +12.0 / +19.4; Muon +3.0 / +6.2 / +5.3 / +6.1; Muon + Adan +2.3 / +4.4 / +4.5 / −0.7, the closest). Time per step is unchanged: every arm runs at 116–117 audio s/s (the step is data-bound, so Muon's orthogonalisation and Adan's extra state cost nothing measurable at S2).
  - *Real-voice pattern*: every arm that improves on synthetic validation gives back some of lr8's Hillenbrand F1 gain (+1.9 to +7.3 Hz against lr8; lr8 itself was −6.7 against mixr, the largest of the sweep, so part of this is a lucky reference shared by all arms: the arms are not seven independent observations). Muon + Adan directly against mixr_conformer (`paired/fp32/mixr_conformer_sN_vs_muonadan_conformer_sN.txt`): Hillenbrand F1 −5.2 / −5.9 / +1.5, F2 −9.2 / −24.4 / −6.1, F3 −35.3 / −57.8 / +12.8 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA +0.7 / +0.0 / +0.0: still a real gain overall, seed 2 the weak one, as in the learning-rate sweep. The lower Muon rates, best on synthetic F4, are the ones that fail the F1 rail, so synthetic and real F1 pull apart as synthetic fit improves; watched at full size.
- *Decision*: **the S2 recipe becomes Muon (8e-3) for the non-head linear weights + Adan (4e-2) for the rest**, warmup 1000, 2000 + 1500 (a quality arm, "better"). Answer to Hannah's question: no speed-up per step or a halved budget, but better quality at the same budget, so some step saving in between (not measured). Full size gets its own check (rates and the real-voice F1 pattern) before a teacher uses it.

## D-031 (2026-10-22, pre-registered): the D-030 recipe at full size, a rate bracket from scaling rules

With Hannah. D-030's S2 recipe (Muon 8e-3 for the non-head linear weights, Adan 4e-2 for the rest) can't be carried to full size as is, and a full sweep there costs too much. So published scaling rules give a centre and three runs bracket it at ×0.5 / ×1 / ×2. Nothing here has been run.
- *What changes from S2 to full size*: the Conformer is identical (width 128, 2 layers, 4 heads), and it holds nearly all of Muon's weights. The front end is wider: conv channels 24 / 48 / 64 against 8 / 16 / 32, f0 channels 16 against 8, embedding 384 against 192. The run is about 6× longer (16000 constant + 4000 decay against 2000 + 1500). Batch and crops are unchanged.
- *Rules used for the centre* (sources from transformer language models, so they set the centre, not the answer):
  - *Width* (μP, Yang et al. 2022, *Tensor Programs V*): Adam-like updates want a rate ∝ 1/fan-in for hidden matrices. Muon's rate needs nothing, since its layers keep their width (its update is scaled to AdamW's RMS, Liu et al. 2025). The widened front end would want ½–⅓ of the Adan rate, but **no per-layer scaling is applied in this round** (one change at a time; the widened layers are a minority of the Adan group, and the bracket covers a 2× error). Recorded as a follow-up if the bracket's best is its lowest arm.
  - *Run length* (Bjorck et al. 2024, *Scaling optimal LR across token horizons*): the best rate falls with training length, roughly as a power law. Centre at ½ of S2's rates (a 5.7× longer run with an exponent of about 0.4). The ×2 arm is then exactly S2's rates (no horizon effect), and the ×0.5 arm covers exponents up to about 0.8.
  - *Weight decay* (Wang & Aitchison 2024): AdamW-style decay averages over about 1 / (lr × wd) steps, and that span as a fraction of the run is what should stay fixed. S2 had lr × wd = 8e-3 × 0.01 = 8e-5, a span of 12.5k steps, 3.6 runs. Held at 3.6 runs for 20000 steps: lr × wd = 1.4e-5 for the Muon rate, so **wd = 1.4e-5 / Muon's rate in each arm** (0.007 / 0.0035 / 0.00175). Adan's group uses the same wd, so its lr × wd stays 5× Muon's as at S2. The decayed branches at 5k and 10k then see a longer span (7 and 14 runs) than S2's; accepted, since the span is fixed in steps by the constant phase they share. D-029's ×8 gain also cut the span 8× (100k → 12.5k steps), so part of what the S2 sweep credited to the rate may be decay. The control below separates this at full size.
  - *Batch* unchanged, so no square-root rule. *Warmup* stays 1000 steps (the shorter warmup failed at the high rate, D-029).
- *Arms* (full size, teacher v3's data: train_v2 + resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth Korean, the `v2r2` arguments; seed 1; mixed crops 32–400; comb-truth taper 4000 steps; 8 loader workers; `--optimizer muon_adan`, `rest_lr_scale` 5):
  - **rA**: Muon 2e-3, Adan 1e-2, wd 0.007.
  - **rB** (centre): Muon 4e-3, Adan 2e-2, wd 0.0035.
  - **rC** (S2's rates): Muon 8e-3, Adan 4e-2, wd 0.00175.
  - Each constant to 16000, checkpoints at 4000, 8000 and 16000; a decay branch (20 % of its run) from each: 4000 + 1000, 8000 + 2000, 16000 + 4000, so three horizons (5k, 10k, 20k) from one run.
  - **Weight-decay control** (rB's rates, wd 0.01, everything else rB's): constant to 8000, branches at 4000 + 1000 and 8000 + 2000. About half a run.
  - Reference: **conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k** (teacher v3: AdamW 1e-3, wd 0.01, warmup 1000, 16000 + 4000, seed 1, the same data and command otherwise).
- *Measured*: synthetic validation (each run's last "eval"; train_v2's validation draws). Real-voice dev guard-rails as D-029's against the reference, at 20k only: Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz; PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0 point; women's closed-phase |error| ≤ +5 Hz. Hillenbrand F1 is also reported at every horizon, since longer training traded real F1 for F2–F4 before (D-025) and D-030 showed synthetic and real F1 pulling apart. Real-voice measures whole-file, like D-025 and D-030, so they compare with the numbers on file. Audio s/s reported per arm.
- *Full-size noise* (computed now from the six existing full-size d16k runs, before any arm: conformer_v2 and conformer_v2r2, three seeds each, pooled within family, 4 df): synthetic SD per run F1 0.50, F2 0.65, F3 1.12, F4 1.80 Hz. One run against one reference: SE = √2 × SD = 0.71 / 0.92 / 1.58 / 2.55 Hz. Synthetic f0 RPA is saturated at full size (99.37–99.42 %, SD 0.01–0.02 point), too tight to estimate from three seeds, so RPA counts as worse only below −0.1 point (about 1.5× S2's seed SD).
- *Decision rule*:
  1. *Choice*: at 20k, among rA / rB / rC that pass the guard-rails and are not diverged (non-finite validation error), the one with the lowest summed F1–F4 difference from the reference in SE units.
  2. *Adoption*: that arm becomes the full-size recipe if it is **better** than the reference by D-029's rule with the SEs above (one of F1–F4 beyond −2 SE, none beyond +2 SE, RPA not below −0.1). Otherwise, teacher v3's AdamW recipe stays and the S2 result is recorded as not transferring.
  3. *Weight decay*: at 5k and 10k, rB against its control, in the same SE units. **"decay rule"** if rB is better on at least one of F1–F4 beyond 2 SE at either horizon and worse on none beyond 2 SE at both. **"wd 0.01"** if the reverse holds. Otherwise **"no difference"**, and the scaling rule stays as the default because it follows the literature. If "wd 0.01", the adopted arm's wd is revisited before any teacher uses it.
  4. *Reported, not decided*: the best arm at each horizon, which tests the run-length rule (a lower best rate at 20k than at 5k supports it). The shortest horizon at which the chosen rates are not worse than the reference at 20k (no measure beyond +2 SE), which is the full-size budget the new recipe needs and the speed answer at this size.
- *Cost*: about 5.3 h per 16k constant phase and 2.3 h for the three branches, so about 23 h for rA–rC plus about 4 h for the control; a little over a day of GPU. Approach agreed with Hannah 2026-10-22; it is next on the GPU, ahead of the joint-head trial. Heads that pass at S2 then share one full-size retrain with whatever this adopts (roadmap item 6).

- *Result (2026-10-26, `reports/analysis/d031/decide_d031.txt`, paired reports in `reports/analysis/paired/fp32/`): **rA is adopted as the full-size recipe** (Muon 2e-3, Adan 1e-2, wd 0.007; 16000 constant + 4000 decay). Weight decay: no difference.*
  - *Choice*: at 20k, rA has the lowest summed difference (−10.9 SE units). Against teacher v3: F1 −1.15, F2 −3.42, F3 −2.89, F4 −9.55 Hz (−1.6 / −3.7 / −1.8 / −3.7 SE), RPA +0.09. rB's sum is −5.5 SE units; rC's is +2.6.
  - *Adoption*: better by D-029's rule (F2 and F4 beyond −2 SE, nothing beyond +2 SE).
  - *Guard-rails at 20k*: rA's dev Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 −1.3 / −0.5 / +0.2 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA +0.1, women's closed phase +1.4 Hz; all pass. The only failure in the sweep is rC's 10k branch (RPA −2.0).
  - *Weight decay*: rB against its wd 0.01 control is within 2 SE everywhere (5k: −0.3 / −0.3 / +0.1 / −0.1; 10k: −0.5 / +0.6 / +1.5 / +1.5 SE units). "No difference", so the scaling rule (lr × wd held at 1.4e-5) stays the default.
  - *Reported*: the best arm is rB at 5k, then rA at 10k and 20k. The best rate falls with run length, supporting the run-length rule. Hillenbrand F1 against teacher v3 at 20k: rA −1.3, rB +0.1, rC −1.4 Hz. The shortest horizon at which rA is no worse than teacher v3 at 20k is 20k, so the new recipe needs the same budget and gives no speed saving at this size.
  - *Follow-up recorded at registration*: the bracket's best is its lowest arm, so the optimum may lie below rA. Per-layer (μP) scaling of the widened front end's Adan rate was the planned follow-up for this case. Not run now: rA already passes, and the next full-size run is item 5's shared retrain, which uses rA. A lower-rate arm or μP scaling could ride alongside that retrain if GPU time allows.
  - *Caveats*: one seed per arm, with seed noise inside the SEs (from six existing runs). Synthetic gains are F2 and F4; real-voice F1 moves within noise.

## D-032 (2026-10-22): whole-suite reports respect the tiers; a model register; release tags

Housekeeping with Hannah; no result changes.
- *The CLI's whole-suite reports ignored the tiers.* `va baselines` and `va eval teacher` scored every gold speaker. Since D-024 every decision has gone through the tiered analysis scripts instead, so the CLI fell out of use after 2026-10-09; run on teacher v3 today, it would have been an unlogged semi-private use. Now both take `--tiers` (dev by default; semi and lockbox only when named, with a printed reminder to log the use), filter the manifests before predicting, cache tiered predictions apart (`<baseline>__<corpus>__<tiers>.npz`, the key includes the tiers), and state the tiers in the report header and file name. Token and speaker counts in the Hillenbrand section come from the set scored, no longer hard-coded. The analysis scripts' path is unchanged (no tiers argument = every speaker, the old cache names; they filter with `VA_TIERS`). Tested (`test_tiers.py`); a dev-only Praat run scored 996 tokens from 83 speakers. M3 acceptance needs such a report with the lockbox, so this had to exist before then.
- *Reports layout*: `reports/suite/` for whole-suite CLI reports, `reports/suite/pre_tiers/` for the seven written before D-024 (every gold speaker; five had never been committed), `reports/analysis/` as before (`reports/README.md`). Citations updated.
- *Model register*: `docs/models.md`, one row per named model: run directories, size, training data, recipe, training commit, checkpoint SHA-256 (first 16 hex digits, as the resynthesised sets record their tracker), deciding entry and status, plus the labelling lineage (teacher v2 d16k tracked the resynthesised sets teacher v3 trained on). It also records that `teacher_v3a/v3b_d06k` are D-020 / D-022 data arms, not teacher v3.
- *Release tags*: annotated tags at each released teacher's training commit, `teacher-v1` (`7f89c09`), `teacher-v2` (`e43dfdd`, the d16k decay; trunk `2a173d0`), `teacher-v3` (`8e58a13`), each message naming the run and checkpoint hash. Tags fix the code, the register fixes the weights, the commit log stays the detailed history.
- *Training provenance*: a run's `config.json` recorded "dirty" whenever any file in the checkout was, untracked reports included, so every run since 2026-10-08 but four reads "dirty" and the flag says nothing. Now `dirty` means uncommitted changes to tracked files under `vocal_analysis/` or `configs/`, and `dirty_files` lists them.

## D-033 (2026-10-22, method fixed before running): layer probe of a pretrained ASR encoder (Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3)

Hannah's question: how much of what our models learn can a large pretrained speech encoder already represent, layer by layer? A comparison, not a decision; nothing here changes the pipeline (plan §7.6 argued against frozen pretrained encoders on resolution grounds; this measures it).
- *Model*: NVIDIA `parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3` (FastConformer, 24 layers, width 1024, 128-bin mel front end, 8× subsampling: one frame per 80 ms; CC-BY-4.0, attribution in `ATTRIBUTION.md` if anything is released). Weights fetched over plain HTTPS at revision `541d1f9` (`model.safetensors`, SHA-256 `3a202636…`, checked), no hub client, loaded with `transformers` 5.20.2 from PyPI in a separate worktree and venv (`voice-analyser-parakeet`, branch `parakeet-probe`). Checked: it transcribes two ARCTIC utterances correctly; a click test puts encoder frame e at mel frame 8e (±½). Its training data (Granary; NeMo ASR Set 3.0: LibriSpeech, Fisher, VCTK, Common Voice 7, …) includes none of our evaluation sets.
- *Probe* (low-cost, linear): ridge regression from each layer's features (the subsampling output and layers 1–24), linearly interpolated from the 80 ms frames to our 10 ms frames, standardised, to every target teacher v3 trains on: log F1–F4, log B1–B4, log f0, voicing, sonorant (0/1, threshold 0.5). Fitted **only on teacher v3's training mixture** (train_v2 training clips + 30 % resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean, the saved channel model, 3000 fixed 4 s crops, seed 777, every 2nd frame; masks and weights as the trainer's: formants on sonorant frames weighted by the resynthesised clips' QC weight, f0 / voicing / sonorant / bandwidths on synthetic clips only). Ridge strength per layer and target chosen on the last 20 % of those crops, then refitted on all. No evaluation set is ever fitted.
- *Scored on*: synthetic validation, 512 fixed 4 s crops each (seed 12345): train_v2's validation clips (teacher v3's set) and train_v2s' (the newest rendered set: interactive source with the closure corner, D-026), plus the resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean validation sets (formants). Real voices, the baseline report's gold sets on **dev speakers**: Hillenbrand F1–F4 MAE by group, f0 RPA and voicing on PTDB-TUG, MIR-1K and MDB-stem-synth (whole files). Teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) scored on the same frames, whole-file on the gold sets as in D-025.
- *Reported*: per layer, every measure; the best layer per target; teacher v3 alongside. Expected limits, stated now: 80 ms frames blur fast formant and f0 movement, and a linear probe in log f0 may miss pitch the encoder holds non-linearly, so a weak f0 result says little about the encoder.
- *Part 2 (Hannah, 2026-10-22; fixed before running): a trained head at the best tap, bias analysis, and an ensemble.* The probe's best layer for formants and bandwidths is **layer 5** on every synthetic set (f0 peaks at layer 3, but a linear probe reads almost none). Fine-tuning is too heavy here, so:
  - *Head*: Parakeet frozen and truncated after layer 5 (the subsampling and the first 5 of 24 blocks). On top: LayerNorm and a projection 1024 → 128, linear interpolation from the 80 ms frames to our 10 ms frames (as the probe), two of our Conformer blocks (teacher v3's width 128, 4 heads, kernel 15), then teacher v3's own output heads (f0 and voicing, cascaded F1–F4 heat-maps with the soft mask, bandwidth mean and variance, sonorant). It is trained with `teacher_loss` on teacher v3's training mixture (random 4 s crops and channels, as the trainer draws them): AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 500 warm-up steps, cosine to 0 over 6000 steps, batch 32, seed 0. The step count may be cut to fit about 1.5 h of GPU, before training starts and stated here. `decode_outputs` reads its outputs, so it reports the same values and uncertainties as teacher v3.
  - *Bias analysis* (teacher v3, the layer-5 probe, the head): median signed error and MAE, F1–F3 (F4 where there is a reference), on synthetic validation by f0 band (< 150, 150–250, 250–350, ≥ 350 Hz; train_v2 and train_v2s), on Hillenbrand dev by group, and on **PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase frames** (`ptdb_egg_sub.npz`, women and men; the women's F1 overread is the bias D-026 is about). Also the per-frame correlation of teacher v3's and the head's signed log errors: an ensemble only helps where they differ.
  - *Ensembles, with nothing fitted on evaluation data*: (a) the per-frame mean in log frequency of teacher v3 and the head; (b) a precision-weighted mean, each model weighted by 1 / σ² from its own predicted heat-map spread. Reported on everything above.
  - *Rule for "worth a full arm"* (an ensemble member in the labeller, tested properly later): ensemble (b) cuts women's closed-phase F1 |median signed error| by at least 5 Hz against teacher v3 alone (D-023's threshold), and stays within D-029's guard-rails against teacher v3 on Hillenbrand dev (F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz MAE). Otherwise "no".
  - *Amended 2026-10-22 evening, before the head was trained or any bias number computed* (Hannah): the rule was too harsh for what this architecture can do. Its F2–F4 are limited by the 80 ms frames, and the guard-rails would have measured that, not bias. The interest is bias, because the encoder learned from real speech. That holds most cleanly for the **linear probe**: a trained head fits our synthetic labels with enough capacity to learn the same synthetic-to-real shortcuts teacher v3 did, while the probe can only read out what the encoder already holds. So:
    - *Members*: the layer-5 linear probe as well as the head. The probe gives no per-frame uncertainty, so it enters ensembles (a) and (c) only.
    - *(c) F1 only*: the per-frame mean in log frequency for F1, with teacher v3's F2–F4 unchanged. This is the ensemble the rule is about; (a) and (b) are reported.
    - *Rule for "worth a full arm"*: for the head or the probe, ensemble (c) cuts women's closed-phase F1 |median signed error| by at least 5 Hz against teacher v3 alone, does not raise men's by more than 5 Hz, and keeps Hillenbrand dev F1 MAE (all) within +4 Hz of teacher v3. F2–F4 are unchanged by (c), and for (a) and (b) they are reported, not gated. Otherwise "no".
- *Results, probe (2026-10-22; branch `parakeet-probe`, `reports/analysis/parakeet/probe_summary.txt`)*: formant and bandwidth information peaks at **layers 3–5** on every synthetic set and fades with depth (layer 24 about twice the best layer's error), as expected of an ASR encoder. At the best layer, against teacher v3 (MAE, Hz): train_v2 F1–F4 64 / 164 / 242 / 345 vs 22 / 44 / 60 / 91; bandwidths about 2× worse; Hillenbrand dev F1 51 vs 38.9 (women 49.5 vs 42.5), F2–F4 3–5× worse; Hillenbrand rows are noisy across layers (0.3 s tokens, about 4 encoder frames, unlike the 4 s fitting crops). f0: a linear probe reads almost none (12–16 % RPA vs 87–98 %). Voicing on singing is the exception: MDB-stem-synth 98.5 % (layer 1) vs teacher v3's 91.0 %, MIR-1K 94.9 % vs 94.2 %.
- *Results, part 2 (2026-10-22; `bias.txt`; head: 6000 steps, 44 min, 0.87 M parameters, final loss 5.02)*. Median signed error / MAE, Hz:
  - *Hillenbrand dev F1*: teacher v3 +19.9 / 38.9 (men +13.2 / 22.4, women +23.6 / 42.5, children +32.1 / 51.4). **Head alone +1.1 / 38.6** (men +7.0 / 25.3, women −7.4 / 40.8, children +0.5 / 49.6): teacher v3's MAE with almost none of its F1 overread. Probe alone +12.0 / 51.0. **Ensemble (c), head: +10.7 / 33.2** (men +10.1 / 21.0, women +7.0 / 33.8, children +15.3 / 44.9), F2–F4 unchanged. The head's own F2–F4 are worse (F3 137, F4 240 MAE, F4 −100 signed), so (a) helps F1 and costs F3 / F4; (b) lets teacher v3's tighter σ dominate (F1 +14.1 / 33.4).
  - *PTDB-TUG dev closed phase F1* (9401 frames; teacher v3 recomputed matches the stored frames exactly): teacher v3 women +7.6 / 51.7, men +6.7 / 23.6; head −2.9 / 66.2, +6.2 / 36.3; **(c) head +1.2 / 54.6, +6.3 / 25.8**. The probe fails here (+49.7 / 85.8 and +82.8 / 88.9; F2 / F3 off by hundreds of Hz): on long running-speech files its linear readout of 80 ms frames doesn't hold.
  - *Synthetic*: the head's bias is near zero by f0 band, its F1 MAE about twice teacher v3's; (c) costs 2–11 Hz F1 MAE on synthetic (most at f0 ≥ 350), so it trades synthetic accuracy for real-voice accuracy: the sim-to-real gap, seen from the other side.
  - *Error correlation with teacher v3* (signed log errors): head F1 +0.59 (Hillenbrand), +0.70 (closed phase); probe +0.52, +0.61. Partly shared errors (some of that is the references' own), so averaging gains less than independent errors would, but enough to show.
  - **Rule: head "worth a full arm"** (women's closed-phase |F1 bias| 7.6 → 1.2, men 6.7 → 6.3, Hillenbrand F1 MAE 38.9 → 33.2); **probe "no"** (women 7.6 → 27.7, men 6.7 → 43.7).
  - Reading: Hannah's hypothesis holds for the trained head. A frozen real-speech encoder plus a small head trained on our labels carries much less of teacher v3's F1 overread on real voices, despite seeing only our training data; the linear probe, the purer test, is inconclusive (Hillenbrand women −2.6 Hz bias, but unusable on closed phase). Hillenbrand's reference is LPC-based (D-019), so part of teacher v3's +20 Hz there is the reference's; the closed-phase reference, physical, agrees in direction (7.6 → 1.2). All dev; nothing here is confirmed on semi-private.
- *Part 3 (Hannah, 2026-10-22; fixed before any combination was fitted): every combination of layers 0–6, from cached embeddings.* `cache.py` stores hidden states 0–6 (fp16, 80 ms frames) for every fitting and scoring item above, with labels and teacher v3's outputs on the same frames; everything below runs from that cache.
  - *Probes*: ridge as before (same 3000 fitting crops, every 2nd frame, standardised per layer, strength per combination and target chosen on the last 20 % of the crops, refitted on all), on the concatenation of each of the **127 non-empty subsets of layers 0–6**.
  - *Selection, on training-pool validation only, never on gold*: the mean over the four validation sets (train_v2, train_v2s, resynthesised VocalSet, resynthesised Zeroth-Korean) of each set's sonorant-frame MAE. One winner for **F1** (the bias use); one for **voicing accuracy** (synthetic sets only, the only ones with voicing labels). Rule, Hannah's choice of a light penalty: find the best combination; take every combination within 1 SE of it (paired bootstrap over validation clips, 1000 resamples); choose the shallowest by deepest layer used (what Parakeet must run to at labelling time), then fewest layers, then lowest error.
  - *Reported*: all 127 on every set (F1–F4, bandwidths, f0, voicing, sonorant; the gold sets on dev speakers), the best by deepest layer (the Pareto front), and for the F1 winner the part-2 bias analysis and its rule (ensemble (c) with teacher v3: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| down ≥ 5 Hz, men's up ≤ 5 Hz, Hillenbrand dev F1 MAE within +4 Hz).
  - *Next, if the winner is not layer 5 alone*: the better head on the winning combination, trained on a larger cached set of those layers (about 40,000 crops; 3000 are enough for ridge, not for a head), then the same bias analysis against part 2's head.
- *Part 3 results (2026-10-22; `parakeet/combo/combo_report.txt`, `combo_results.json`; 12 min once the GPU bursts were made short: a first run with 20,000-row chunks hung the machine, see the memory note)*:
  - *Combining layers barely helps a linear probe.* F1 selection metric (mean of the four validation sets' MAE, Hz): best L0+L1+L3+L4+L5+L6 63.5, L5 alone 65.1; 11 combinations within 1 SE. **F1 winner by the rule: L3+L4+L5+L6** (63.7). The front by deepest layer: L0 97.3, L1 81.7, L2 72.2, L3 67.4, L4 64.6, L5 63.8, L6 63.5, so the gain is all in reaching layer 4–5; adding layers beside them is worth about 1–2 Hz.
  - *Voicing winner: L1+L4+L5* (95.8 % on the synthetic sets; teacher v3 99.3 / 94.2 on train_v2 / train_v2s).
  - *Gold (dev)*: the F1 winner is worse than layer 5 alone on real voices (Hillenbrand F1 MAE 59.7 vs 51.0; closed-phase F1 bias women +50.7, men +69.1): more features fitted to synthetic labels read real voices worse, which fits the reading in part 2. **Part-2 rule for the F1 winner: "no"** (women |F1 bias| 7.6 → 28.1, men 6.7 → 36.7). Linear readout is the limit, not the layer choice.
  - *Correction to the probe's voicing finding*: on MDB-stem-synth teacher v3 now scores 98.0 % voicing (98.2 % f0 RPA), not 91.0 %. The difference is analysis mode: its MDB columns are windowed here (4 s windows, D-028), whole-file before. So the "teacher v3 is weak on singing voicing" reading was teacher v3 on minute-long whole files, and D-028's windowed default fixes it. The voicing winner reaches 98.5 % (MDB) and 95.1 % (MIR-1K, teacher whole-file 94.2 %): level with teacher v3, not better.
  - *Next (pre-registered above)*: the better head on L3+L4+L5+L6, trained on a larger cached set of those layers, against part 2's layer-5 head on the same bias analysis.
- *Part 4 (fixed before training; Hannah agreed the plan)*: two heads trained **on the same cache** (`cache_heads.py`: 40,000 fixed 4 s crops of teacher v3's training mixture, seed 999, Parakeet layers 3–6, labels as the trainer's), so only the input differs: **H5** (layer 5, the part-2 architecture) and **H3456** (layers 3–6 concatenated, 4096 → LayerNorm → 128, the rest identical). Each: AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 500 warm-up steps, cosine over 6000 steps, batch 32, seed 0 (192,000 draws, about 5 passes over the cache: less augmentation variety than part 2's on-the-fly head, which is why H5 is retrained here rather than reused). Scored with `bias.py`'s analysis (synthetic by f0 band, Hillenbrand dev by group, closed phase by sex, ensembles (a)–(c)) and the part-2 rule for each. H3456 counts as **better than H5** if its ensemble (c) cuts women's closed-phase |F1 bias| at least 2 Hz more than H5's, or Hillenbrand dev F1 MAE at least 2 Hz more, without being worse by 2 Hz on the other; otherwise layer 5 stays (the shallower tap).
- *Part 4 results (2026-10-22; `parakeet/heads_part4.txt`; each head 8 min)*: training loss H3456 below H5 throughout (step 3000: 4.99 vs 5.26; H5 ends at 4.96, part 2's on-the-fly head 5.08). Synthetic: H3456 better than H5 everywhere (train_v2 F1 MAE by band 18.0 / 20.8 / 34.4 / 65.8 vs 20.2 / 22.5 / 35.0 / 70.4). Real voices, median signed / MAE, Hz:
  - *Hillenbrand dev F1*: teacher v3 +19.9 / 38.9; H5 −4.2 / 40.5; **H3456 +0.5 / 38.3**; (c) with H5 +7.3 / 32.7, with H3456 +9.9 / 33.2. H3456's own F3 / F4 are better than H5's (114 / 216 vs 133 / 257 MAE) but still far behind teacher v3's (48 / 98).
  - *Closed-phase F1*: teacher v3 women +7.6 / 51.7, men +6.7 / 23.6; (c) with H5 −3.8 / 55.5, +3.0 / 26.2; (c) with H3456 +2.7 / 52.6, +4.8 / 25.2.
  - **Part-2 rule: "no" for both**, on the women's threshold alone: cuts of 3.8 (H5) and 4.9 Hz (H3456) against the 5 Hz required, while Hillenbrand F1 MAE improves by 6 Hz with either. Part 2's on-the-fly head cut 6.4 Hz; the cached heads (each crop's augmentation seen about 5 times) cut less. Teacher v3's closed-phase bias is only 7.6 Hz, so the rule asks for two thirds of it to go.
  - **Part-4 rule: layer 5 stays** (H3456's extra cut +1.1 Hz in women's bias, −0.5 Hz in Hillenbrand F1 MAE: within 2 Hz both).
- *Part 4b (Hannah, 2026-10-22, added while part 4 trains, before any part-4 result): the heads on S2's recipe, and S2 itself on the same frames.* Heads are kept consistent with S2 so S2 is the basis for comparison:
  - **H5-S2**: the layer-5 head, same cache, trained with D-030's S2 recipe as a package: Muon 8e-3 for the non-head linear weights, Adan 4e-2 for the rest (`rest_lr_scale` 5, `_optimizer` unchanged: the head's output modules carry the teacher's names), weight decay 0.01, warm-up 1000, WSD 2000 constant + 1500 decay, batch 32, seed 0. Against H5 (AdamW, cosine 6000) on the same analysis. *Amended before it ran (Hannah: S2's 3500 steps against H5's 6000 would confound the comparison): 6000 steps, warm-up 1000, constant to 4500, decay over the last 1500, so the optimiser and schedule shape are the only differences; a negative result still counts for little, since the S2 rates were tuned for a different model.* *And (Hannah, also before it ran) a second arm, **H5-S2s**: the same recipe at S2's own schedule exactly (3500 steps: warm-up 1000, constant to 2000, decay 1500), so H5-S2 is matched 1:1 to H5 and H5-S2s 1:1 to S2.* Reported, no rule: it says whether the heads are under-trained, not whether Parakeet helps. If H5-S2 is clearly better (women's closed-phase |F1 bias| or Hillenbrand F1 MAE in ensemble (c) by 2 Hz or more), heads from here on use the S2 recipe (*trigger loosened before any part-4b result, Hannah, 2026-10-22 night: any improvement counts, i.e. H5-S2's ensemble (c) cuts women's closed-phase |F1 bias| plus Hillenbrand F1 MAE by more in total than H5's (c) does*), H3456 is retrained on it, and part 4's comparison is rerun on that recipe.
  - **S2 on the same frames**: the three S2 Muon + Adan seeds (`muonadan_conformer_s0–2`, k35) scored on exactly the scoring cache's frames (the fixed synthetic crops regenerated from their seeds, the gold files whole), in the same tables (F1–F3 median signed error and MAE by f0 band, by Hillenbrand group, closed phase by sex). Their F1 bias and MAE beside teacher v3's and the heads' say what a 1.9 M-parameter model trained on our data alone does, against a 0.87 M head on a frozen 0.6 B real-speech encoder.
- *Part 4b results (2026-10-22; `parakeet/heads_part4b.txt`)*: training loss is much lower on the S2 recipe (H5-S2 4.63 at step 5000 vs H5 4.96 at 6000), real voices are not better. Ensemble (c), cut in women's closed-phase |F1 bias| + cut in Hillenbrand F1 MAE: H5 3.8 + 6.2 = 10.0 Hz; **H5-S2 (6000 steps) 3.8 + 5.4 = 9.2: the trigger is not met, AdamW stays for heads** (H3456 is not retrained; DAC's head uses AdamW); H5-S2s (S2's own 3500 steps) 4.6 + 6.1 = 10.7, level with H5. Alone, the S2-recipe heads have the same near-zero Hillenbrand F1 bias (−0.5, −3.6 Hz) and slightly higher MAE (42.0, 40.2).
  - **S2 itself on the same frames** (`muonadan_conformer_s0–2`, 1.9 M parameters, our data only): Hillenbrand F1 +23.4 / 42.4, +25.4 / 43.2, +28.5 / 45.0 (teacher v3 +19.9 / 38.9); closed-phase F1 women +20.2, +14.3, +18.8, men +13.2, +11.3, +8.1 (teacher v3 +7.6, +6.7). So the small model trained on our data alone carries the F1 overread at full strength, while a smaller head (0.87 M) on frozen real-speech features, trained on the same labels, carries almost none of it (H5 alone: Hillenbrand −4.2, women's closed phase −11.0, i.e. if anything under). The overread comes with what a model learns from our synthetic audio; a real-speech front end mostly removes it, at the cost of F2–F4 resolution.
- *Seeds (Hannah, 2026-10-22 night, after the seed-0 results of parts 4 and 4b): head training varies by seed at these lengths, so H5, H3456, H5-S2 and H5-S2s each get seeds 1–5 (*six seeds, raised from three before any extra seed ran, Hannah: as for the critical S2 runs*; initialisation and batch order; the same cache). The part-4 rule (layer set) and the part-4b trigger (recipe) are **re-taken on six-seed means** (SDs reported) and replace the seed-0 readings above, which stand as seed 0's. The part-2 rule is also reported on means. If the recipe trigger is met on means, H3456 is retrained on the S2 recipe (six seeds) and the layer-set rule is taken between the two S2-recipe heads. Part 5 then uses the winning recipe, and its Parakeet partner in (d) is the winning layer set on that recipe; the DAC head also gets six seeds, and (d) is reported per seed pair and on the mean over pairs.
