- *Part 5 (Hannah, 2026-10-22; fixed before any DAC result): the same method on a codec.* **DAC** (Descript Audio Codec, Kumar et al. 2023; MIT code and weights; the 16 kHz model, 74 M parameters, 20 ms latent frames, 12 codebooks; weights from the repo's GitHub release 0.0.5, SHA-256 `95ab7176…`; `descript-audio-codec` 1.0.0 from PyPI in its own venv, `.venv-dac`, so the ROCm torch stays untouched). Trained (paper §4.1) on DAPS, DNS Challenge 4 clean speech, Common Voice, VCTK, MUSDB, Jamendo and AudioSet: none of our evaluation sets, but MUSDB contains MedleyDB tracks, so **MDB-stem-synth is "possibly heard"** for DAC and is reported, not used for any reading. Checked: a round trip through the codec keeps the log spectrum within 5.5 dB on two ARCTIC utterances (a 4 kHz low-pass is 10.6–10.9 dB, 20 dB-SNR white noise 12.6–13.5). The question: a model trained to reconstruct sound rather than recognise words should keep spectral-envelope detail that an ASR encoder drops.
  - *Taps*: the outputs of the encoder's four strided blocks (the first three at 8 / 2 / 0.4 kHz frame rates, averaged to our 10 ms frames; the fourth at 20 ms), the encoder's output latent (pre-quantisation), and the quantised latent: six taps, cached as for Parakeet (the same fitting crops and scoring frames, labels and teacher v3's outputs reused from `emb_v1`).
  - *Probes*: ridge on all 63 non-empty combinations of the six taps, the same fitting, selection rule (F1 and voicing winners on training-pool validation; best, then the shallowest within 1 SE, then fewest taps) and reports as part 3.
  - *Head*: the part-2 architecture on the F1 winner's taps (projection to 128, two Conformer blocks, teacher v3's heads), trained on a 40,000-crop cache as part 4, with whichever recipe part 4b settles. Bias analysis and ensembles (a)–(c) with teacher v3 as before, plus **(d) three-way F1**: the mean in log frequency of teacher v3, the Parakeet layer-5 head (H5) and the DAC head, F2–F4 teacher v3's.
  - *Rule*: the part-2 rule for the DAC head's (c) and for (d), the same thresholds. Also reported: the correlation of DAC's and Parakeet's signed F1 errors (a three-way mean only helps if they differ from each other as well as from teacher v3).
- *Deviations in the overnight run (2026-10-22/23, no result read when made):* (i) H5 seed 2's run directory (`runs/h5_s2`) was already the S2-recipe head's seed 0, so the chain skipped H5 seed 2 as done; caught on an interim look before the six-seed evaluation, H5 seed 2 was trained into `runs/h5_seed2` and the six-seed evaluation used it. (ii) All six DAC heads went NaN between steps 1000 and 2000: one of the 40,000 cached head crops (no. 37896) holds 41 frames of DAC activations at or past the fp16 limit (every other crop peaks near 35; the scoring cache has none). That crop is excluded (`cache/dac_heads/exclude.json`; the trainer draws only from the remaining crops, so the Parakeet heads' sampling is unchanged) and the six DAC heads retrained; the NaN runs are kept in `runs/nan_fp16/`. Nothing else changed.
- *Seed-averaged ensemble (Hannah, 2026-10-23, fixed before any part-5 result was read):* six seeds of each head make a seed average the natural ensemble member, and picking the best seed pair on dev would select noise.
  - **(e), decides**: F1 the mean in log frequency of teacher v3, the six-seed mean of the H5 heads and the six-seed mean of the DAC heads (each seed mean taken frame by frame in log frequency); F2–F4 teacher v3's. Reported beside it, the two-way versions (teacher v3 with either six-seed mean). The part-2 rule (same thresholds) on (e), once; it replaces the per-pair and mean-over-pairs readings of (d) as the part-5 decision, which stay reported.
  - **Seed-pair selection, reported only, cross-fitted**: the dev speakers of Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG split into two halves (fixed seed, by sex); on each half the best of the 36 (H5 seed, DAC seed) pairs by (d)'s women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut plus Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut (pairs whose men's |bias| rises by more than 5 Hz excluded), scored on the other half, with (e) scored on the same halves. In-half against held-out shows how much of a best pair's edge is selection. PTDB-TUG's dev halves hold few speakers, so the closed-phase half-scores are noisy; nothing is decided from this.
  - *Noted for later, not run:* a median over seeds before averaging across members (more outlier rejection, e.g. if the seeds' F2–F4 errors are unbiased but noisy and F2–F4 join the ensemble); a separate amendment if taken up.
- *Results, six seeds (2026-10-23; `reports/analysis/parakeet/heads_seeds.txt` on branch `parakeet-probe`)*. Ensemble (c), changes against teacher v3 alone (women's closed-phase |F1 bias| 7.6 Hz, men's 6.7, Hillenbrand F1 MAE 38.9), mean ± SD over six seeds:

  | head | women's bias cut | men's bias change | Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut | part-2 rule on means |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | H5 (AdamW, 6000) | +5.3 ± 1.1 | −2.8 ± 2.1 | +4.1 ± 1.3 | worth a full arm |
  | H3456 (AdamW, 6000) | +5.7 ± 0.9 | −3.4 ± 1.6 | +5.0 ± 1.0 | worth a full arm |
  | H5-S2 (S2 recipe, 6000) | +3.1 ± 0.9 | −4.8 ± 1.1 | +4.2 ± 0.9 | no |
  | H5-S2s (S2 recipe, 3500) | +3.4 ± 1.7 | −5.3 ± 1.6 | +4.6 ± 0.9 | no |

  **Recipe: AdamW stays** (part-4b trigger on means: total cut 7.3 against 9.4 Hz; the S2 recipe cuts men's bias more and women's less in every seed). **Layer set: layer 5 stays** (H3456 ahead by +0.4 / +0.8 Hz). **H5 with teacher v3 (c): worth a full arm** on means. Single seeds disagree (H5: 4 of 6 pass), which is why the decisions are on means.
- *Results, part 5 (DAC)*. Ridge (`dac_combo/`): F1 winner **T1+T3+T4+T5** (91.6 Hz mean MAE on training-pool validation; the Parakeet F1 winner 63.7), voicing winner T1+T4+T5. Heads (taps 1,3,4,5, AdamW, six seeds, after the excluded crop): on real voices **every seed overreads F1** — PTDB-TUG closed phase men +36 to +47 Hz, women +14 to +31 Hz (teacher v3 +6.7 / +7.6); Hillenbrand F1 bias −6 to +24 Hz across seeds, MAE 54–62 Hz. DAC's (c) fails on every seed (women's bias rises 4.9 ± 3.6 Hz, men's 15.9 ± 2.0). **(d) per pair: no** on all six pairs and on their mean (women −2.1, men +7.7, Hillenbrand −3.8 Hz). **(e): no** — teacher v3 with both six-seed means: women 7.6 → 5.6, men 6.7 → 14.0, Hillenbrand 38.9 → 33.9 Hz. Reported beside it: teacher v3 with the six-seed H5 mean, women 7.6 → **1.0**, men 6.7 → 3.4, Hillenbrand 38.9 → 33.4 (passes the part-2 thresholds; better than any single H5 seed, 1.4–3.8), and with the six-seed DAC mean, women 11.5, men 22.9 (fails). DAC's overread is shared across seeds, so averaging keeps it.
  - *Cross-fit (reported only)*: the best of 36 pairs on half 0 (P#3 + DAC#2, in-half +6.8) scored +9.8 on half 1 (median pair +5.0, (e) +6.1); on half 1 no pair keeps men's bias within the 5 Hz exclusion, so nothing could be picked. With about three women per PTDB-TUG half the half-scores are noisy, as expected.
  - *Higher formants of the six-seed H5 mean* (asked, reported only): seed averaging cuts F2–F4 MAE by only 6–11% (Hillenbrand F2 79 → 70, F3 134 → 122, F4 235 → 219 Hz; teacher v3 47 / 48 / 98), so their error is mostly shared across seeds (what layer 5 encodes), not seed noise; F4 is underread (−72 Hz overall, −131 for women). A median over seeds would gain little here; F2–F4 stay teacher v3's.
  - *Reading (not tested)*: the frozen Parakeet heads carry almost none of the synthetic data's F1 overread; the frozen DAC heads carry about as much as S2 (more for men on closed-phase frames). An encoder that keeps raw acoustic detail lets a head learn the synthetic data's shortcut; an ASR encoder normalises it away.
  - *Next (candidates, for an amendment before running)*: the full arm for teacher v3 + six-seed H5 mean (semi-private confirmation once); uncached L5 heads with on-the-fly augmentation (part 2's single uncached seed reached 1.2 Hz), a full-size head, or both, six seeds each.
- *Full arm and bootstrap (Hannah, 2026-10-23; fixed after the six-seed and part-5 results, before any semi-private use for this candidate and before the bootstrap ran)*. The uncached and full-size head arms are parked: with women's closed-phase bias at 1.0 Hz on dev the remaining bias is below what about six dev women can resolve, so they come back only if the confirmation holds and a six-head ensemble proves awkward in use.
  - **The labeller under test, "teacher v3 + H5×6"**: teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) with F1 replaced, frame by frame, by the mean in log frequency of teacher v3's F1 and the six-seed mean of the H5 heads (`runs/h5`, `h5_s1`, `h5_seed2`, `h5_s3`, `h5_s4`, `h5_s5`, each frozen); f0, voicing, F2–F4 and bandwidths teacher v3's. Nothing is retrained or reselected.
  - **Dev, reported (the arm's decision on dev is already the part-2 rule on (c) of the seed mean)**: F1 median signed error and MAE against teacher v3 on Hillenbrand dev by group (all / men / women / children), PTDB-TUG dev closed phase by sex, synthetic validation by f0 band (train_v2, train_v2s), and resynthesised VocalSet / Zeroth-Korean validation.
  - **Bootstrap (dev, reported only)**: speakers resampled with replacement within sex (PTDB-TUG closed phase) and within group (Hillenbrand), 2,000 resamples, seed 20261023, the same resample for every estimate (paired). 95 % percentile intervals for: women's and men's closed-phase |F1 median signed error| of teacher v3, of the arm and of each single-seed (c); the arm minus teacher v3; the arm minus the mean of the single-seed (c)s; Hillenbrand F1 MAE (all) of each and the arm minus teacher v3. Also the share of resamples in which the arm's women's |bias| is below teacher v3's and below the single-seed mean's.
  - **Semi-private confirmation (one logged use)**: PTDB-TUG closed phase and Hillenbrand, semi-private speakers only (Parakeet layer 5 and teacher v3 outputs cached for them on the same terms as dev, in a separate cache). **Replicates** when both effects are in the dev direction and at least half as large: E1 the cut in women's closed-phase |F1 median signed error| (dev 7.6 → 1.0, cut 6.6 Hz; at least 3.3 on semi-private) and E2 the cut in Hillenbrand F1 MAE (all; dev 38.9 → 33.4, cut 5.5 Hz; at least 2.75), and the guard-rail holds: men's closed-phase |F1 bias| rises by at most 5 Hz. The bootstrap is also reported on semi-private. Replicates → teacher v3 + H5×6 becomes the F1 labeller candidate for the next milestone's acceptance (lockbox then, not now). Otherwise it stays a dev finding.
  - *Correction (Hannah, 2026-10-23, minutes after the entry above, before anything of it ran)*: the labeller in use is **labeller v3** (D-025: the per-frame mean in log frequency of `conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k` and `conformer_v2r2_s2_d16k`, which helps F2–F4 against seed 1 alone), not teacher v3 alone, so the arm builds on it and is compared with it.
    - **The arm, "labeller v3 + H5×6"**: labeller v3 for everything, except F1 = the mean in log frequency of labeller v3's F1 and the six-seed H5 mean (the two members 1 : 1, as (c) weighted teacher v3 and the head). Seed 2's outputs are scored on the scoring cache's frames with `s2_score.py` (seed 1 rescored the same way first, and must reproduce the cached teacher v3 columns).
    - **Dev decision, now open again** (labeller v3 on these frames has not been looked at): the part-2 rule against labeller v3 alone (women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut ≥ 5 Hz, men's rise ≤ 5 Hz, Hillenbrand F1 MAE (all) within +4 Hz). Fails → no semi-private use.
    - **Semi-private, if dev passes**: as above, against labeller v3 alone, with E1 and E2 the arm's own dev effects against labeller v3 (at least half as large on semi-private), the same guard-rail.
    - **Bootstrap**: as above, with labeller v3 added as an estimate (and the arm minus labeller v3).
    - Reported beside: the teacher-v3-based version of the entry above (teacher v3 + H5×6 against teacher v3), and F2–F4 of labeller v3 against teacher v3 on these frames. The per-frame median of all three teacher seeds (best F4 in D-025, out of interest) is not included: seed 0 is not part of labeller v3.
    - *Added (Hannah, 2026-10-23, before anything ran)*: the per-frame median of all three teacher seeds (`conformer_v2r2_d16k`, `_s1_d16k`, `_s2_d16k`) **is** reported, for completeness, on every set beside labeller v3 (F1–F4; D-025 found it best at F4). Nothing is decided from it. Resynthesised validation needs the teacher seeds scored on those crops too; `s2_score.py` now covers them, as `cache.py` builds them.
- *Full arm, dev results (2026-10-23; `reports/analysis/parakeet/full_arm_dev.txt` on `parakeet-probe`)*. Seed 1 rescored reproduces the cached teacher v3 columns (max |Δ log2 F| 0 on the crops, 4e-6 Hillenbrand, 0.008 closed phase).
  - **Rule: passes** — labeller v3 + H5×6 against labeller v3: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| 8.0 → 0.2 (E1 +7.7), men's 6.8 → 3.6, Hillenbrand F1 MAE 37.1 → 32.8 (E2 +4.3). Semi-private thresholds therefore E1 ≥ 3.85, E2 ≥ 2.15, men's rise ≤ 5 Hz. (Teacher-v3 version: 7.6 → 1.0, 6.7 → 3.4, 38.9 → 33.4.)
  - *Bootstrap (2,000 paired speaker resamples)*: arm − labeller v3, women's |bias| −4.7 [−8.3, +5.3] Hz (arm lower in 89 % of resamples), men's −3.1 [−5.2, −0.5], Hillenbrand F1 MAE −4.2 [−5.7, −2.8]; arm − single-seed mean: women −0.6 [−2.1, +0.5], Hillenbrand −1.4 [−1.6, −1.2]. The women's cut is not resolved on dev's ~6 PTDB-TUG women (the |median| is also biased upwards under resampling: the arm's 0.2 resamples to 3.3 on average); the men's cut and the Hillenbrand gain are.
  - *Costs, reported*: F1 MAE rises where teacher v3 is already accurate — closed phase women 50.2 → 54.0, men 21.9 → 25.1 (bias falls, scatter rises); synthetic train_v2 / v2s 9.7 → 11.9 / 12.5 → 14.4 (< 150 Hz band); **resynthesised VocalSet 32.6 → 43.2**; Zeroth-Korean 18.6 → 19.3. F2–F4 are labeller v3's by construction.
  - *Labeller v3 against teacher v3 on these frames*: F1 bias unchanged (women 7.6 → 8.0), Hillenbrand MAE F1 38.9 → 37.1, F2 47.3 → 45.6, F3 47.7 → 46.3, F4 98.4 → 96.3; closed phase F2 / F3 MAE −1.5 / −1.7 (women), −1.4 / −2.6 (men); synthetic F2–F4 MAE −2 to −4 Hz. *Three-seed median*: Hillenbrand F4 MAE **94.2** (best, as D-025), F1 38.0, F3 46.5; otherwise between labeller v3 and teacher v3.
- *Semi-private use reassigned (Hannah, 2026-10-26, before any semi-private data for this candidate was cached or seen).* D-034 reserved the one semi-private use for stage 2's deployable form, which needed a student that transfers; v2 and v4 do not (D-034). The labeller runs offline, so Parakeet's size is acceptable there (plan §2, §7.6 note). So the use goes to **labeller v3 + H5×6** as registered above, unchanged: E1 (women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut against labeller v3) ≥ 3.85 Hz, E2 (Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut, all) ≥ 2.15 Hz, men's |bias| rise ≤ 5 Hz; the paired speaker bootstrap reported. Semi-private speakers: PTDB-TUG closed phase (F03, F05, F06, F07, M05, M06, M08, M10) and Hillenbrand's 56. Their cache is built by `cache.py` and `s2_score.py` with `VA_TIER=semi` (gold sets only, the same analysis as dev: whole files, teacher seeds 0–2), in a separate directory (`cache/emb_semi`), and scored by `full_arm.py --semi 7.7 4.3`. **Replicates** → labeller v3 + H5×6 is the F1 labeller candidate for M3's acceptance (the lockbox opens then, once); otherwise it stays a dev finding.
  - *Pooled estimate (Hannah's question; fixed now, before any semi-private result)*: only if it replicates, dev and semi-private are pooled for a **reported estimate**: the same measures and paired speaker bootstrap on the union (PTDB-TUG 10 women, 10 men; Hillenbrand 139 speakers), resampling within each tier and sex / group. It decides nothing and is never used to choose anything later. The dev half was used to choose the arm, so the pooled number inherits some optimism; the semi-private-only estimate stays the unbiased one and is reported beside it. If it does not replicate, no pooled number is computed.
  - *Semi-private use, logged (2026-10-26 17:18; PTDB-TUG closed phase and Hillenbrand, semi-private speakers; `reports/analysis/parakeet/full_arm_semi.txt` on `parakeet-probe`; cache `cache/emb_semi`). **Replicates.*** Seed 1 rescored matches the cached teacher v3 columns (max |Δ log2 F| 3.8e-6).
    - Labeller v3 + H5×6 against labeller v3: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| 10.4 → 5.3 (E1 +5.1, needs ≥ 3.85; dev +7.7); Hillenbrand F1 MAE 34.4 → 30.6 (E2 +3.8, needs ≥ 2.15; dev +4.3); men's 4.7 → 1.4 (a fall, guard-rail ≤ +5 holds).
    - Paired speaker bootstrap (2,000 resamples): arm − labeller v3, women's |bias| −5.0 [−7.2, −2.9] (on dev −4.7 [−8.3, +5.3], unresolved), men's +0.2 [−4.0, +8.0], Hillenbrand F1 MAE −3.8 [−5.0, −2.6]. Against the mean of the single-seed (c)s: women −0.3 [−1.8, +0.7].
    - Reported: teacher v3 with H5×6 (arm_t) against teacher v3, women 9.6 → 5.3 in the bootstrap means; the three-seed median against labeller v3, women 10.4 → 11.3, Hillenbrand 34.4 → 35.5.
    - *So*: labeller v3 + H5×6 is the F1 labeller candidate for M3's acceptance. Semi-private's women read higher than dev's under every model (labeller v3 10.4 against 8.0), and the arm halves it there rather than removing it.
  - *Pooled estimate (as pre-registered: reported only, decides nothing; `full_arm_pooled.txt`, from reruns of `full_arm.py` on both tiers that reproduce their reports exactly)*. PTDB-TUG 20 speakers (10 women), Hillenbrand 139. Labeller v3 → arm: women's |F1 bias| 9.1 → 2.3, men's 5.8 → 2.8, Hillenbrand F1 MAE 36.0 → 31.9. Bootstrap (within tier and sex / group), arm − labeller v3: women −6.2 [−7.8, −2.2], men −2.8 [−4.7, −0.1], Hillenbrand −4.1 [−5.1, −3.0]. Dev was used to choose the arm, so these are a little optimistic; semi-private alone (above) is the unbiased figure.

## D-034 (2026-10-23, stage 1 pre-registered): distilling Parakeet's layer 5 into a small student

**Why.** D-033: a frozen Parakeet layer 5 with a small head trained on our synthetic labels carries almost none of the women's F1 overread that every synthetically trained model shares (D-024: 84–90 % of that error is common to all models), while a frozen codec (DAC) and end-to-end S2 carry it in full. The ensemble that uses it (labeller v3 + H5×6) needs the 0.6 B encoder at inference and adds F1 scatter (D-033 full arm). The question for stage 1: **does the property survive distillation into a model of our own size class?** Alternatives considered (Hannah, 2026-10-23): domain-adversarial training (aligns synthetic and real feature distributions, which differ in their formant distributions, and paired resynthesis inherits the trackers' overread; kept for later) and generic self-supervised pretraining (a reconstruction objective would likely keep the cue, as DAC did).

**Stage 1 (pre-registered, before anything ran).**
- *Student*: our formant encoder's architecture without the comb channel (it is built from the f0 branch's output), scaled to channels (32, 64, 96), embed 512, Conformer width 384, 8 layers, 6 heads, feed-forward ×4: **32.3 M parameters** (33.8 M as a whole teacher). It keeps our 10 ms frames; a linear projection to 1024 dimensions, used for the loss and kept as the head's input. Size (Hannah): measured on the GPU (bf16, 32 × 4 s), this size trains at 25 audio s/s and infers at 133 against 37 / 195 for teacher v3's size, a factor of about 1.5 despite 8× the parameters (the VQT and full-resolution convolutions dominate the small model), so only the large student is tried.
- *Targets*: Parakeet layer 5 (D-033's loading; weights unchanged), computed on each crop with up to 1 s of context either side, standardised per dimension (statistics from the targets). Student frames are averaged in eights centred on each Parakeet frame (frame e at our frame 8e) for the loss; the head later reads the 10 ms frames.
- *Loss*: L1 plus (1 − cosine similarity) per Parakeet frame (DistilHuBERT's), on the standardised targets.
- *Data* (fixed 4 s crops, cached targets; training pools only, no evaluation corpus):
  - real, 30,000 crops: Zeroth-Korean train 15,000 (CC BY 4.0), VocalSet train 7,500 (CC BY 4.0), Common Voice 27.0 Korean 7,500 (CC0; speaker ids stay opaque hashes). M4Singer stays out (CC BY-NC-SA would carry to the weights).
  - synthetic, 30,000 crops: teacher v3's training mixture as `cache.py`'s fit set builds it (train_v2 with resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean, fixed channels).
  - held out for fidelity: 512 crops each from Zeroth-Korean val, VocalSet val and the scoring cache's synthetic validation sets.
  - *Caveat*: Parakeet v3 covers 25 European languages; our real speech is Korean (plus VocalSet's singing). D-033's heads already ran on this mixture. English Common Voice (CC0) would be the fix if it matters.
- *Training*: AdamW, lr 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, warm-up 1000 then cosine to 0, **12,000 steps of 32 crops** (about 6 passes), seed 0, bf16 autocast. Fidelity (per-dimension R², mean cosine) on the held-out crops every 2,000 steps.
- *Heads*: the student frozen; the D-033 H5 recipe on its 1024-dimensional output at 10 ms (head ratio 1, no interpolation): a 40,000-crop head cache built from the same crops and labels as `cache_heads.py`, AdamW, 6,000 steps, **six seeds** (0–5). Scored on D-033's scoring sets (synthetic and resynthesised validation crops, Hillenbrand dev, PTDB-TUG dev closed phase) as `eval_heads.py`.
- *Control R*: the same student architecture, randomly initialised (seed 0) and frozen, with the same heads (six seeds). It tells whether distillation is what matters or any frozen non-formant encoder would do (DAC says not any, but DAC is a different architecture).
- **Rule (stage 1)**, on six-seed means, ensemble (c) with teacher v3 (D-033's part-2 thresholds, as for H5): the student head's (c) cuts women's closed-phase |F1 median signed error| by ≥ 5 Hz, raises men's by ≤ 5 Hz, and keeps Hillenbrand dev F1 MAE (all) within +4 Hz of teacher v3. **"transfers"** if it passes and control R fails; **"frozen encoder suffices"** if both pass; **"does not transfer"** if the student fails. Reported beside: H5×6 (D-033) and the student's ×6 with the paired speaker bootstrap of `full_arm.py` (student − H5, student − teacher v3), the heads alone (F1 bias and MAE), labeller v3 + student×6 against labeller v3 (the full-arm form), fidelity at every checkpoint and on the dev gold sets' audio, and the student's training loss.
- *Then*: "transfers" → stage 2 (sketched, to be pre-registered separately): one network, the distilled trunk feeding the F1 head through a stop-gradient, a teacher branch for f0, voicing, F2–F4 and bandwidths trained on the synthetic labels and distilled from labeller v3 (F1 left out of that loss), judged against the two-stage result and labeller v3 + H5×6. "does not transfer" → the student's fidelity decides between too small (low fidelity: a larger student) and not transferable (high fidelity, bias kept: domain-adversarial training next).
- *Licence*: weights trained on Parakeet's outputs (CC-BY-4.0), CC BY / CC0 audio and synthetic data are CC BY 4.0, with attribution to NVIDIA's Parakeet and the corpora (`ATTRIBUTION.md` if released).
- *Cost*: targets about 1–2 h; distillation about 12–20 h; head caches and twelve heads about 3 h; evaluation about 1 h. GPU work in short chunks (D-033's limits).
- *Stage 2, revised sketch (Hannah, 2026-10-23, before stage 1 ran; replaces the "Then" sketch above; full pre-registration after stage 1)*: per-output routing, nothing learnt in the fusion.
  - *Student branch*: the stage-1 student frozen; its six F1 heads distilled into one head on the frozen features (ensemble distillation), so inference runs one head.
  - *Teacher branch*: labeller v3 (seeds 1 and 2) distilled into one network, trained on the synthetic labels and labeller v3's soft targets; it never sees Parakeet targets or the student's gradients. Separate convolutional front ends (only the VQT is shared); sharing the front end is a later, separate test.
  - *Routing, from D-033's evidence*: **F1 joint** (the fixed 1 : 1 mean in log frequency of the two branches); **F2–F4, f0, voicing teacher-only** (Parakeet heads 1.5–2.5× worse on F2–F4 MAE even as six-seed means; layer-5 linear f0 near nil; teacher v3's windowed voicing 98 %); **bandwidths and sonorant teacher-only** by default. *Exception rule, fixed now*: an output moves from teacher-only to joint only if, in stage 1's dev evaluation of the student heads, the joint mean beats teacher-only on its synthetic validation measure by at least 2 SE and on its real dev measure with a bootstrap interval excluding 0 (B1 is the plausible candidate: D-023's teacher reads damping as higher F1). No learnt fusion weights: fitted on synthetic data, where the teacher wins, they would undo the F1 correction.
  - *Judged*: against labeller v3 + H5×6 (should match it) and labeller v3 alone (part-2 rule); the one semi-private use then goes to this deployable form.
- *Implementation note (2026-10-23, before the run)*: Parakeet's feature extractor normalises each utterance by its own statistics, so zero padding is counted as signal (checked: it moved a crop's features by about their own scale; attention-mask batch padding left them identical to 2e-6). Real crops therefore take context only from their source file (up to 0.96 s either side, the leading part a whole number of encoder frames) and synthetic crops are run alone, with no zeros added; targets with and without context correlate 0.993 at zero shift and 0.44 at ±1 frame (alignment checked). Smoke run: 38 audio s/s, so about 11 h for 12,000 steps. Code: `distil_cache.py`, `student.py`, `distil_train.py` on `parakeet-probe`.
- *Fidelity by language and singing task (exploratory, 2026-10-23, step 2000; adopted as reported measures)*. R² per dimension / variance-weighted / cosine: **English (LibriTTS-R dev-clean, 512 crops; no English in the student's training) 0.21 / 0.24 / 0.57**, Korean (Zeroth val) 0.42 / 0.44 / 0.69, singing (VocalSet val) 0.30 / 0.30 / 0.64; within VocalSet, excerpts (sung words) 0.34, scales 0.32, arpeggios and long tones 0.25, vibrato and fast passages 0.33–0.35, breathy and vocal fry about 0.22, messa di voce 0.03, lip trill −0.13 (small groups). Reading: layer 5 may be close to language-neutral for Parakeet, but the student copies it best where it was trained; our real-voice evaluation sets are English, so the student is probably least faithful where the bias is measured. From now on, English held-out fidelity (LibriTTS-R dev-clean, `fid_en.npz`; LibriTTS-R is CC BY 4.0, downloaded 2026-10-23 from OpenSLR 141 and its ELDA mirror, sizes checked) and the VocalSet task / technique split are reported for every checkpoint; no rule changes.
- *Amendment: blended restart, "student v2" (Hannah, 2026-10-23, before any bias result; decided on fidelity alone)*. At step 2000 the first student copies Parakeet best on what it was trained on and about equally worse on every unseen speech population: Korean (in training) R² 0.42; singing (in training) 0.30; unseen English adults (LibriTTS-R dev-clean, speechocean762) 0.21–0.24, English children ≤ 12 (speechocean762) 0.23, Icelandic children ≤ 12 and teens (Samrómur) 0.23–0.24, Italian / Portuguese / Polish (MLS dev) 0.26–0.28 (`fid_breakdown.py`). The gap is coverage, not one language, and our real-voice tests are English with the largest bias in children. Training on the blend from the start (rather than continuing on new pools, which risks drift) gives the student one target. The first run (`student_v1`) is stopped at its next checkpoint and kept, reported only.
  - *Training crops* (90,000 fixed 4 s crops; training splits only; pools of short utterances are cut from each speaker's utterances joined in a seeded order, so short utterances are not dropped): synthetic 45,000 (teacher v3's mixture, as before); English adults LibriTTS-R train-clean-100 + 360 15,000; speechocean762 train speakers (children and adults) 3,000; Samrómur Children train 6,000; Korean 10,000 (Zeroth-Korean 7,500, Common Voice 2,500); VocalSet train 8,000; Multilingual LibriSpeech train, Italian, Portuguese, Polish 1,000 each. VCTK stays out (still downloading). New pools: CC BY 4.0 (`ATTRIBUTION.md`); children's corpora used under the same rules as every corpus (Hannah: the models analyse and cannot synthesise).
  - *Held out*: as before (Zeroth val, VocalSet val, synthetic validation) plus `fid_breakdown.py`'s sets (LibriTTS-R dev-clean; speechocean762 test speakers ≤ 12 and ≥ 19; Samrómur test ≤ 12 and 13–17; MLS dev), reported at every checkpoint.
  - *Unchanged*: student, loss, standardisation (statistics recomputed on the new training targets), schedule (12,000 steps of 32, seed 0, so the same initial weights: control R is unchanged), heads, control R and the rule.
  - *Head pipeline check* (no decision): one head seed on `student_v1` step 2000, to test head caching, 10 ms heads and scoring before the real head stage; its numbers are logged as a pipeline test.
- *Candidate after stage 1 (Hannah, 2026-10-23; not pre-registered)*: **denoising distillation**: the student's input crops passed through the existing channel model on the fly (`channel.draw_channel` / `apply_channel`: noise, RIRs, filters, level, codecs; CPU-side in loader workers, as in teacher training), the target staying Parakeet's layer 5 on the clean crop. The student then learns Parakeet's clean features from degraded input (more robust than Parakeet itself; mainly for real-world recordings such as phone audio, less for the clean gold sets). It changes what is distilled, so it is an arm of its own, beside the English / VCTK continuation.
- *Student v2, step 2000 (reported; `fid_breakdown.py`)*. R² per dimension, v1 → v2 at the same step: English adults LibriTTS-R dev-clean 0.21 → **0.48**, speechocean762 children ≤ 12 0.23 → 0.38 and adults 0.24 → 0.37, Samrómur children ≤ 12 0.24 → 0.35 and teens 0.23 → 0.33, MLS Italian / Portuguese / Polish 0.26–0.28 → 0.35–0.37, Korean (Zeroth val) 0.42 → 0.39 (its share of the real crops fell from 75 % to 22 %), singing 0.30 → 0.31 (long tones 0.26, arpeggios 0.28, sung excerpts 0.40), synthetic train_v2 / v2s 0.47 / 0.42 → 0.50 / 0.45. Every speech population is now within 0.33–0.48 (v1: 0.21–0.42). The held-out speech sets are new speakers from corpora v2 trains on, so part of the gain is corpus coverage; the real-voice gold sets are separate corpora, tested only by the heads.
- *Student v2 to step 6000 (reported)*. Checkpoints and fidelity every 1,000 steps from step 4000 (Hannah; no effect on training: batches are seeded by step and the optimiser state is restored). R² per dimension at steps 2000 / 4000 / 6000: English adults LibriTTS-R 0.48 / 0.56 / 0.59; speechocean762 children 0.38 / 0.46 / 0.51; Samrómur children 0.35 / 0.43 / 0.48; Korean 0.39 / 0.48 / 0.52; singing 0.31 / 0.36 / 0.41; synthetic train_v2 0.50 / 0.54 / 0.59. Still rising at every checkpoint (`reports/analysis/parakeet/d034_fidelity.md`). VocalSet groups v1 → v2 at step 2000, paired bootstrap over crops (`fid_paired.py`): sung excerpts, forte and trill better on all three metrics; vibrato, fast forte, breathy, pianissimo, trillo and arpeggios on cosine only.
- *Head stage, implementation (2026-10-23, before any head result)*. The student's 10 ms output is cached like D-033's DAC taps (`student_cache.py`): the scoring sets' items in `emb_v1`'s order (asserted), and a 40,000-crop head cache from `cache_heads.py`'s seed-999 crops. Crop frame j sits at mel position j + 4, so heads use ratio 1, offset 4; whole files run in one pass and are stored from frame 4 on, so the same offset reads them (a file's first 40 ms clamp to its frame 4). bf16 autocast as in training; fp16 storage. Heads: `train_cached.py` unchanged in recipe (AdamW 1e-3, 6,000 steps, seeds 0–5, layer argument unused). `eval_heads.py` reads student features with `--feat=NAME=DIR`. H5×6 is scored in the same run, as the pre-registration reports it beside. The student is step 12,000 (the last; no checkpoint choice). Control R is `student_v2/ckpt_init.pt`. *Deviation*: the pipeline check (student v1, step 2000) uses a 4,000-crop head cache instead of 40,000, to save time and disk; no decision rests on it. Head caches (about 33 GB each) are deleted once their six heads are trained, except student v2's and v3's (more disk freed, 2026-10-23, no results seen): stage 2's ensemble distillation of the F1 heads reads the same features. Code: `chains/d034_heads_1b.sh`.

**Stage 1b (pre-registered 2026-10-23, before any head result; Hannah asked for it queued with criteria chosen by Claude).** Runs only if stage 1's verdict is "transfers". *Question*: does scaling the distillation data, at the same number of passes, give a better student for stage 2?
- *Student v3*: v2 with every training pool ×3 (270,000 crops: synthetic 135,000; LibriTTS-R 45,000; speechocean762 9,000; Samrómur 18,000; Zeroth-Korean 22,500; Common Voice Korean 7,500; VocalSet 24,000; MLS Italian, Portuguese, Polish 3,000 each), the same seeds (so v2's real crops are among them) and 36,000 steps (4.3 passes, as v2), checkpoints and fidelity every 2,000 steps. Everything else as v2: architecture, loss, schedule shape (warm-up 1000, cosine to 0), seed 0, so the same initial weights. Statistics are recomputed on v3's targets. One change: data and steps scaled together at constant passes, because more distinct audio needs more steps to be seen.
  - *Known limit*: small pools can't supply three times as much distinct audio. VocalSet (6 h, about 5,400 distinct 4 s crops) and speechocean762 train (about 3 h) give overlapping crops, so for them the scale-up acts like more passes. New corpora (VCTK, once verified) and noise in the student's input (the denoising candidate) are separate later arms.
- *Heads*: six on v3's step 36,000, as stage 1, scored in one run with S2×6 and H5×6.
- **Rule (`decide_1b.py`)**: v3 replaces v2 as the stage-1 student (stage 2's trunk) if all three hold:
  1. v3's heads pass the stage-1 rule on seed means;
  2. they are not worse than v2's on its measures: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut ≥ v2's − 2 Hz, men's rise ≤ v2's + 2 Hz, Hillenbrand dev F1 MAE cut ≥ v2's − 2 Hz;
  3. fidelity: mean R² over the nine held-out speech populations ≥ v2's at step 12,000 + 0.02, and no reported row (the nine, singing, and the two synthetic sets) below v2's by more than 0.01.
  - Otherwise v2 stays. "No gain from scale" if 1 and 2 hold but not 3.
  - Thresholds: D-033's head measures vary by 1–2 Hz SD across seeds, so the difference of two six-seed means has an SE of about 1 Hz and 2 Hz is about 2 SE. 0.02 R² is about one 1,000-step checkpoint of v2's gains at steps 4,000–6,000.
  - Dev only; semi-private stays for the deployable stage-2 form.
- *Cost*: targets about 2 h (about 63 GB; the chain checks for 100 GB free first); training about 36–40 h at v2's rate; heads and evaluation about 3 h.
- *Then, in either case*: D-031's chain resumes from where it was paused (rA's trunk at 16,000), so the GPU keeps working on it in the gaps.
- **Stage 1 result (2026-10-24, dev; `reports/analysis/parakeet/d034_heads.txt`): "does not transfer".** Six-seed means, ensemble (c) with teacher v3: student v2 (step 12,000) women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut −8.9 ± 4.9 Hz (worse), men's rise +5.4 ± 1.1, Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut −2.7 ± 3.4; control R −0.9 / −3.9 / −16.7; H5×6 in the same run +5.3 / −2.8 / +4.1 (its D-033 result, reproduced). Neither student nor control passes.
  - *The pipeline is sound*: on synthetic validation the student's heads match H5's (train_v2, f0 < 150 Hz, F1 MAE 19.8 vs 20.2 Hz; R's 47.0), and on the gold audio the student's frames align with Parakeet's (R² peaks at zero shift on both sets, `gold_fidelity.py`). The pipeline check (student v1 step 2000, one head) also fails the rule; it decides nothing.
  - *Where it fails*: real voices. Heads alone, Hillenbrand F1 MAE 53.7 Hz (six-seed mean) against H5's 42.5. PTDB-TUG women's closed-phase F1 median error +14.6 Hz (seed 0) against H5's −11.0, the same direction as our own teacher's overread.
  - *Fidelity on the gold audio* (reported measure): PTDB-TUG R² 0.49 (cosine 0.76), like held-out speech; Hillenbrand 0.10 (cosine 0.60). Hillenbrand's isolated words of under half a second, with silence around them, are unlike the 4 s crops of running speech the student trained on.
  - *Reading*: at this fidelity the cue Parakeet's heads use is not copied. On PTDB-TUG the student's heads overread F1 even at R² 0.49, so the cue is probably in the variance it doesn't reproduce. The pre-registered "then" says the student's fidelity decides between "too small" (a larger or longer student) and "not transferable" (domain-adversarial training next). No fidelity threshold was fixed. 0.5–0.6 on speech is moderate and 0.10 on Hillenbrand is low, so this is a judgement for Hannah, not a rule.
  - Stage 1b did not run (gated on "transfers"). D-031 resumed at 06:08 as queued.
- *Diagnostic after the result (2026-10-24, reported, no decision; `student_as_parakeet.py`)*. D-033's H5 heads (trained on real Parakeet) fed the student's output in Parakeet's place: destandardised, averaged in eights onto the 80 ms frames as in the distillation loss. Control: real layer 5 plus Gaussian noise of the student's per-dimension error variance on each set (fidelity matched, R² 0.48 on PTDB-TUG, about 0 on Hillenbrand).
  - Six-seed means, ensemble (c): on the student, women's cut +5.6 ± 0.8 Hz, men's rise −5.0, Hillenbrand MAE cut +1.6, which passes the stage-1 rule. On noise +4.9 / −1.4 / +2.2. On real Parakeet +5.3 / −2.8 / +4.1.
  - Heads alone (seed 0), women's closed-phase F1 median error on PTDB-TUG: Parakeet −11.0, noise −13.4, student −7.9 Hz. Hillenbrand, all: −4.2 / −7.4 / **+13.5**.
  - *Reading*: on running speech the student's 80 ms output carries the cue H5 uses; on Hillenbrand's isolated words it does not (no fidelity there). Matched random error keeps the cue everywhere, so the student's errors are structured, not just large. Stage 1's own heads read the student's 10 ms frames. Distillation constrained only their 80 ms means, so the variation within each eight is free to carry what our architecture learns from synthetic data.

**Stage 1c (pre-registered 2026-10-24, after the stage-1 result and the diagnostic above): heads on the student's 80 ms means.** One change from stage 1: the heads read student v2's output (step 12,000) averaged in eights onto Parakeet's frames (encoder frame e = the mean of the student's frames for mels 8e − 4 .. 8e + 3, as in the distillation loss), destandardised to Parakeet's scale, i.e. exactly what distillation constrained. Built from the kept 10 ms head cache, the same 40,000 crops and labels; heads as H5 (ratio 8, offset 0, `train_cached.py` AdamW recipe, 6,000 steps, seeds 0–5); scored on the scoring sets with the same averaging (the diagnostic's stand-in cache), H5×6 beside.
- **Rule**: stage 1's (six-seed means, ensemble (c) with teacher v3: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut ≥ 5 Hz, men's rise ≤ 5 Hz, Hillenbrand F1 MAE within +4 Hz). Control R is not rerun: it failed at 10 ms with fidelity about −0.8, and averaging can't add the cue.
  - **"Transfers at 80 ms"** if it passes. Stage 2's student branch then reads 80 ms means (as H5 does), and the next student targets short isolated words, which the Hillenbrand result shows uncovered.
  - Otherwise, **"heads on the student fail"**, and the reported alternative is H5's heads trained on real Parakeet and run on the student (the diagnostic's form), to be pre-registered separately.
- Reported: Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG separately (heads alone and (c)), synthetic validation, and the H5-on-student numbers beside.
- Dev only. Cost about 1 h.
- *Diagnostic: the within-window part (2026-10-24, reported, no decision; `free_part.py`, before stage 1c's result)*. Hypothesis: the student's 10 ms frames carry, beyond the 80 ms means that distillation constrained, a free part that the stage-1 heads read.
  - *Spectrum*: no excess power at 12.5 / 25 / 37.5 / 50 Hz, the frequencies a shift-equivariant student could fill at no cost (peak ratios 0.97–1.11, as control R's). 15–20 % of the trajectory's energy lies outside a centred sliding mean of eight (control R 8–11 %), spread broadly rather than at those frequencies.
  - *Probe* (ridge, log2 F1, fit train_v2, scored train_v2s): smooth part R² 0.77, whole frame 0.76, residual 0.09. On synthetic speech, the F1 information is in the smooth part.
  - *Ablation*: stage 1's heads fed the sliding mean instead of the frames (a distribution shift, so suggestive only). Women's closed-phase cut −8.9 → −2.4 Hz, men's rise +5.4 → −1.8 (six-seed means); seed 0's women's median error on PTDB-TUG +14.6 → +4.7 Hz, against H5's −11.0; Hillenbrand unchanged. Still fails the rule.
  - *Reading*: the within-window part accounts for some of the overread but not most, and not through the null-space route. It is not the main cause on its own. Stage 1c, with heads trained on the means, is the clean test.
- **Stage 1c result (2026-10-24, dev; `d034_1c_heads.txt`): "heads on the student fail"**, narrowly. Six-seed means, ensemble (c): women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut +3.6 ± 1.9 Hz (needs 5), men's rise −5.1 ± 2.1, Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut +2.4 ± 1.6. Stage 1's 10 ms heads gave −8.9 / +5.4 / −2.7; H5 on the same averaged features gave +5.6 / −5.0 / +1.6.
  - *PTDB-TUG*: heads trained on the means carry the correction. Seed 0's women's F1 median error is −13.0 Hz alone and −4.7 in (c), against stage 1's +14.6 and Parakeet-trained H5's −11.0. The (c) mean now overshoots below zero, which the |bias| measure counts against it.
  - *Hillenbrand*: still overread (seed 0 alone +23.8 Hz, all speakers), where the student barely reproduces Parakeet.
  - *Correction to the within-window diagnostic's reading*: feeding smoothed features to 10 ms-trained heads understated the effect. Heads trained on the means flip PTDB-TUG's women's error from +14.6 to −13.0 Hz, so the 10 ms detail was a large part of stage 1's failure on running speech. What remains is mainly Hillenbrand: isolated short words, where the student has no fidelity.
  - Per the pre-registration, the reported alternative is H5's heads on the student. It passed in the diagnostic, but that was measured before being pre-registered, so it can't be confirmed on dev blind.

**Student v4 (pre-registered 2026-10-24, after stages 1 and 1c and their diagnostics; agreed with Hannah).** One arm with three changes from student v2, each aimed at a failure that stage 1, stage 1c and the diagnostics located. The parts are not separable within this arm (accepted: the question is whether the combination yields a student whose heads transfer).
1. *Per-frame targets at random 10 ms phases* (Hannah's proposal): each of v2's 90,000 crops (the same files, offsets and seeds) gets a phase φ ∈ 0..7 from its own seeded generator. Parakeet sees the crop shifted by 10φ ms, so its frame e sits at the crop's mel 8e + φ. The loss matches the student's frame 8e + φ − 4 to it, for e = 1..49, instead of the means of eights. Phase 0 reproduces v2's targets. The student is close to shift-equivariant at 10 ms, so one phase per crop should constrain every phase. Checked with all eight phases on the held-out sets.
2. *Isolated words* (`words.py`): one MFA-aligned word (english_us_mfa / english_mfa, MFA 3.4.2) of 0.2–0.9 s, with 10 ms fades, and 0.15–0.5 s of the same recording's own aligned pauses either side (room tone). Each is a short whole file of L = 48–192 frames (a whole number of eights), shown to the student as at inference (the file with the VQT's zero padding) and to Parakeet alone, at a random phase.
   - Training items: 24,000 from LibriTTS-R train-clean-100 (2,778 aligned utterances, 12 per speaker) and 12,000 from speechocean762 train (2,500 utterances, children and adults).
   - Each step adds a batch of 16 words of one length (lengths drawn in proportion to their counts), and its loss is added to the crops'.
   - English only. Held-out words in Korean (Zeroth-Korean val, korean_mfa) are built as a reported transfer check, if ready before the first decision.
3. *WSD schedule* (Hannah): warm-up 1,000, then constant at 1e-3 to step 16,000, with checkpoints every 1,000. Two decay branches (linear to 0): 8,000 + 2,000 (10k) and 16,000 + 4,000 (20k). The peak rate is unchanged (Hannah: a higher-rate bracket later, not mixed in here).
- *Unchanged*: architecture, seed 0 (v2's initial weights, so control R is unchanged), loss form (L1 + 1 − cosine on standardised targets; statistics recomputed on v4's training targets), batch 32 crops, AdamW, weight decay, clipping, bf16.
- *Held out*: Zeroth val, VocalSet val, synthetic train_v2 / v2s (512 each) at all eight phases; words from LibriTTS-R dev-clean and speechocean762 test (512 each, all eight phases); `fid_breakdown.py`'s populations (v2's averaged measure).
- *Heads* on the 20k branch: six on its 10 ms frames (stage 1's form) and six on its 80 ms means (stage 1c's form); the recipe, crops and scoring as before.
- **Rule** (stage 1's thresholds on six-seed means, tested in this order):
  1. **"transfers"** if the 10 ms heads pass; stage 2 then reads 10 ms frames.
  2. Otherwise **"transfers at 80 ms"** if the 80 ms heads pass.
  3. Otherwise **"does not transfer"**. The reported comparisons then guide what's next (domain-adversarial training, or H5's heads on the student, each to be pre-registered).
- *Reported*:
  - fidelity per phase (flat across phases is the equivariance check);
  - held-out word fidelity, and the student's fidelity on the gold audio (`gold_fidelity.py`; Hillenbrand was 0.10);
  - Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG separately;
  - the 10k branch's fidelity;
  - comparisons with stage 1 (v2, 10 ms) and stage 1c (v2, 80 ms);
  - the within-window spectrum (`free_part.py`).
- *Cost*: targets about 1.5 h (about 30 GB); training 22,000 steps, about 25 h; head caches and 12 heads about 4 h. Dev only.
- *Student v4, amendment before any training (2026-10-24; Hannah listened to sample word items)*. Two faults heard: a loud artefact in one item's silence (MFA had labelled speech or noise as a pause), and boundary bleed from a neighbouring word in running speech ("as leader", and /tə/ before "this"). Changes, made before anything trained:
  - **Words need an aligned pause of at least 0.1 s on both sides**, and the 60 ms of pause next to the word on each side must be at least 20 dB below the word. Otherwise the word is dropped, not trimmed. About half of the pause-bounded candidates pass: speechocean762 train 549 of 1,019, dev-clean 113 of 196, speechocean762 test 503 of 883.
  - **Room tone is gated by level**: only 20 ms frames of the recording's pauses within 10 dB of their 10th percentile and at least 20 dB below the word, in runs of at least 40 ms.
  - **More aligned audio**: all of LibriTTS-R train-clean-100 and dev-clean (2–12 s utterances), plus train-clean-360 (already in v2's crops), since pause-bounded words are rare in read speech.
  - Item counts unchanged (24,000 LibriTTS-R, 12,000 speechocean762, 512 per held-out set). The few distinct child words (about 550) are repeated with different silences, lengths and phases.
  - Run as `chains/d034_v4.sh`, which builds the crop targets first and the word sets once alignment finishes.
- *Student v4, second amendment before any training (Hannah, 2026-10-24: "relax as much as possible without learning actively wrong things for the children's samples")*.
  - The targets are always Parakeet's own output on the audio the student hears, so a little bleed from a neighbouring word is not a wrong label. The limit is items that stop being a word against silence.
  - **speechocean762** (training and held-out alike): a pause on at least one side. That side's 60 ms edge must be at least 6 dB below the word. The other side is cut at MFA's boundary with a 25 ms fade (a soft onset, not a chop).
  - **LibriTTS-R**: pauses on both sides, edge relaxed from 20 to 10 dB.
  - The room-tone gate is unchanged.
  - Passing words: speechocean762 train 6,039 (children ≤ 12: 2,542 words from 52 speakers; was 549 in all); speechocean762 test 5,950; LibriTTS-R train-clean-100 1,278 (train-clean-360 to come); dev-clean 166.
- *Student v4, reported diagnostic queued (Hannah, 2026-10-24, at training step ~1,500, before any bias result for v4)*. On the 10k branch, the first fully decayed checkpoint: D-033's H5 heads fed the student's 80 ms means in Parakeet's place, with the matched-noise control (as the stage-1 diagnostic), and the student's fidelity on the gold audio (`chains/d034_v4_h5diag.sh`). Reported only. The verdict stays on the 20k branch's own heads, and no checkpoint or recipe choice is made from intermediate bias looks.
  - *Reported fidelity check at step 1,000* (gold audio, means of eights): Hillenbrand 0.145 (v2 at step 12,000: 0.100); PTDB-TUG 0.140 (v2: 0.485). Held-out word fidelity at the same step was 0.43 / 0.40, partly inflated by easy silent frames; a speech-frames-only measure is to be added before any change to the word share.
- *Student v4, reported comparison (Hannah, 2026-10-24, at v4 step 5,000)*. v4's held-out fidelity measure run unchanged on every v2 checkpoint (`fid_v4_on.py`, v2's outputs restandardised to v4's statistics; `reports/analysis/parakeet/fid_v4_on_v2.jsonl`). Per-frame R² (mean over the eight phases) at v2's 12k against v4's 5k: synthetic v2 0.537 / 0.563, v2s 0.507 / 0.524, VocalSet 0.309 / 0.366, Zeroth 0.284 / 0.489; held-out words 0.037 / 0.602 (LibriTTS-R), 0.110 / 0.572 (speechocean762). On v2's own pooled measure v2 still leads (0.639 / 0.550 synthetic, 0.566 / 0.454 Zeroth), as expected for the objective it trained on. v2's per-frame R² is flat across phases (range ≤ 0.015) and on Zeroth flat across training (0.29 → 0.28 while its pooled R² rose 0.40 → 0.57): its gains were in the 80 ms means only, consistent with the unconstrained within-window part found by the diagnostics. No decision.
- *Reported analyses of the students' per-frame fidelity (Hannah, 2026-10-24, v2 step 12,000 / v4 step 5,000; no decision)*. Per-crop R² (`phase_spread.py`): the per-phase means hide a phase-0 preference in v2 on synthetic audio (117 of 512 crops best at phase 0, 64 expected) and larger per-crop swings across phases than v4's. An 8-frame moving average lifts v2 at every phase almost to its pooled R² (Zeroth 0.28 → 0.57), so v2's shortfall is noise around the right 80 ms trend (`smooth_spectrum.py`). Parakeet's interleaved 10 ms trajectory has a 12.5 Hz comb from its 8× grid (up to +12 dB, VocalSet), carrying about 1 % of its variance; Parakeet smoothed over 80 ms scores 0.87–0.92 against itself (`spectrum_vs_teacher.py`, `teacher_ceiling.py`). At 2 ms offsets (`subhop.py`, 128 crops per set) R² is flat for both students, whose 10 ms grid shows only as 100 / 200 Hz comb lines. On the synthetic crops (`crop_factors.py`; held_synth_v2 and v2s are the same voices) both students agree on which crops are hard (Spearman 0.90–0.91): crops with median voiced f0 above about 400 Hz (mean R² 0.44 against 0.57–0.65 below), where Parakeet's own grid pattern doubles its share; a hinge on log f0 explains 19–42 % of the crop spread, and F1 / f0 explains nearly the same variance (shared), so the two cannot be separated in these sets.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-24 (Hannah), before rendering: controlled f0 × F1 grid (reported only, no training decision).* Question: is the students' (and Parakeet's) loss of fidelity on high voices driven by F1 / f0 (harmonics sampling F1 sparsely: the mechanism behind the F1 overread) or by f0 alone (a "voice too high" limit of Parakeet's layer 5)?
  - *Stimuli* (`fxf1_grid.py`): sustained vowels through the standard path (LF Rd 1.0, pole cascade, no channel, peak 0.5), f0 ∈ {150, 250, 400, 600, 800} Hz crossed with F1 ∈ {300, 450, 600, 800, 1000} Hz; F2–F5 fixed at 1700 / 2800 / 3800 / 4800 Hz, bandwidths 80 / 100 / 140 / 180 / 220 Hz; a slow ±3 % f0 drift (0.5 Hz sinusoid, random phase) so no clip is a pure line spectrum. Four variants per cell (drift phase and the synthesiser's seed), 100 clips of 4 s plus the eight-phase margin.
  - *Measure*: as the held-out crop sets: Parakeet layer 5 at the eight 10 ms phases (audio shifted by 10q ms), each student (v2 step 12,000, v4 step 5,000) at frame 8e + q − 4, e = 1..49. Per cell, the normalised error NE = mean over dimensions of the squared error divided by held_synth_v2's per-dimension target variance (1 − R² with a fixed reference, since a sustained vowel has almost no variance of its own); also Parakeet's grid-pattern share per cell.
  - *Rule*, per student, on the 25 cell means: fit NE = a + β_f log2 f0 + β_1 log2 F1 (equivalently in log2 f0 and log2 F1 / f0). If the range of cell NE is under 0.05: "no effect on controlled vowels". Otherwise ρ = −β_1 / β_f: ρ ≥ 0.67 "F1 / f0 drives it" (NE depends on the ratio), ρ ≤ 0.33 "f0 drives it", in between "both". Reported with a bootstrap interval over variants (1,000 resamples). The reading needs both students to agree; if they differ, "students disagree". Reported only; it decides nothing about training.
  - *Result (2026-10-24, `fxf1_grid_v2_12k_v4_5k.txt`)*: **"students disagree"** under the rule. v2: NE rises 0.245 per octave of f0 and is flat in F1 (−0.010), ρ +0.04 [−0.01, +0.09] → "f0 drives it". v4: +0.131 per octave of f0, −0.044 per octave of F1, ρ +0.34 [+0.26, +0.42] → "both", by 0.01 over the boundary. Two things qualify any reading. (1) Both students are poor on these stimuli everywhere: NE is 0.79–1.59 (v2) and 1.14–1.81 (v4), so even at 150 Hz the error exceeds the held-out set's whole target variance; sustained, channel-free vowels are far from what either student saw, and v4 is the worse of the two at low f0 (1.15–1.49 against 0.79–0.88), the opposite of the held-out sets. (2) Parakeet's own output on a steady vowel varies about five times more at high f0 (variation 0.09–0.14 at 150 Hz, 0.37–0.51 at 800 Hz, in the same units) and hardly with F1 at a given f0: the teacher itself becomes unstable as f0 rises, whatever F1 does. So the controlled grid supports f0 (Parakeet's "voice too high" instability) over F1 / f0 for the teacher, and is inconclusive for the students, whose errors here are dominated by being off-distribution.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-24 (Hannah's prediction), before rendering: fine F1 sweep at fixed f0 (reported only, no training decision).* Prompted by a post-hoc observation on the grid above: in every f0 row both students' worst cell sits at or next to F1 = f0 or F1 = 2f0, and at F1 = f0 (600 and 800 Hz rows) their error runs opposite to Parakeet's own variation.
  - *Stimuli* (`f1_sweep.py`): as the grid (standard path, LF Rd 1.0, F2–F5 1700 / 2800 / 3800 / 4800 Hz, bandwidths 80 / 100 / 140 / 180 / 220, no channel, peak 0.5) but with a ±1 % drift so coincidences stay sharp; f0 ∈ {220, 300, 450, 600} Hz; F1 / f0 from 0.6 to 3.4 in steps of 0.1, kept where 150 ≤ F1 ≤ 1500 Hz; four variants per cell.
  - *A, teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, whole-file `analyse`)*: signed F1 error in cents (median over frames 30–370, mean over variants) against the cascade's F1. Hannah's prediction (harmonic attraction): at each coincidence F1 = k f0 (k = 1, 2, 3 where the window fits) the signed error crosses zero from positive to negative (error at k − 0.2 above 0 and at k + 0.2 below 0, the interpolated crossing within ±0.1 of k) and |error| at the cell nearest k is below both cells at k ± 0.3. Reading over the (f0, k) pairs: ≥ 75 % meet both → "harmonic attraction"; ≤ 25 % → "no attraction"; otherwise "mixed". Also reported: the same per k.
  - *B, students (v2 step 12,000, v4 step 5,000), post-hoc hypothesis*: normalised error against Parakeet (as the grid) peaks at coincidences: at the cell nearest k f0 it exceeds the mean of the cells at k ± 0.3 by more than twice the across-variant SD. ≥ 75 % of pairs for both students → "coincidence peaks confirmed"; ≤ 25 % → "not confirmed"; otherwise "mixed". Parakeet's own variation reported alongside.
  - *Result (2026-10-24, `f1_sweep_v3_v2_12k_v4_5k.txt`)*: **A "mixed"** (4 of 11 coincidences meet both parts; k = 1: 1 of 4, k = 2: 2 of 4, k = 3: 1 of 3). **B "students differ"**: v2 1 of 11 ("not confirmed"), v4 3 of 11 ("mixed"); the students' coincidence pattern in the grid does not hold up at fine resolution. *Seen after the readings (post hoc, not part of the rule)*: on these steady vowels teacher v3 hardly tracks F1 at all; its read F1 (`_teacher_reads.csv`) is a staircase locked near harmonics. At f0 220 Hz it reads 2.06–2.12 f0 for every true F1 from 0.7 to 2.4 f0, then 2.99–3.01 f0 from 2.5 to 3.4; at 300 Hz 1.17–1.21 f0 below 1.25, 1.87–1.96 f0 from 1.3 to 2.8, then 2.97–3.02 f0; at 450 and 600 Hz plateaus at about 1.07–1.28 f0 and 1.8 f0, erratic above 2.5 f0 at 450 Hz. So the attraction is total within each step, which is why the pre-registered test (a zero crossing and an |error| minimum near k) is met only where a plateau happens to sit on k. With a steady f0 the harmonics sample the envelope at fixed points and the model reports the strongest one; on real speech its errors are far smaller (closed-phase women's F1 bias +8 Hz, D-025), presumably because f0 movement sweeps the harmonics through the envelope. This is the harmonic-sampling mechanism of the F1 overread in its extreme form: it predicts that the overread grows as f0 movement shrinks (sustained or sung vowels, steady-pitch speakers), which is checkable on real data (Hillenbrand / PTDB-TUG bias against each token's f0 range), and it argues for steady and sustained voicing in training (teacher and students).
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-24 (Hannah's question), before rendering: the F1 sweep with breathy voices (reported only).* The sweep above (`f1_sweep.py`, the "modal" arm, Rd 1.0, no aspiration) re-run unchanged but for the source, two arms: **B1** Rd 2.7 (the LF maximum, steep tilt, H1 dominant), no aspiration; **B2** Rd 2.7 with aspiration at 0 dB HNR (`hnr_db = 0`, pulsed noise through the same tract; training draws 5–40 dB, so just past its edge). F1 bandwidths stay fixed, so only the source changes.
  - *Measure*, per f0, for teacher v3: the tracking slope (least-squares slope of log2 read F1 on log2 true F1 over the sweep's cells: 1 follows F1, 0 is a flat staircase) and the mean |F1 error| in cents. Students and Parakeet reported as before.
  - *Reading per arm against modal*: tracking slope higher by ≥ 0.2 at ≥ 3 of the 4 f0 values → "tracks better"; lower by ≥ 0.2 at ≥ 3 → "tracks worse"; otherwise "no clear change". *Expectation written beforehand*: B2 tracks better (noise samples the envelope between harmonics); B1 no clear change or worse.
  - *Result (2026-10-24, `breathy_f1_sweep.txt`)*: **B1 "no clear change"**, **B2 "no clear change"** under the rule (slope changes against modal at f0 220 / 300 / 450 / 600 Hz: B1 +0.49 / +0.05 / −0.23 / −0.56; B2 −0.88 / −0.50 / +0.51 / +0.42). *Seen after the readings (post hoc)*: the whole-sweep slope mixes two opposite effects in B2. Where true F1 ≥ 350 Hz, aspiration removes the staircase at every f0 (slope +1.01 / +0.95 / +0.73 / +0.96, mean |error| 41 / 32 / 120 / 38 cents, against modal's +0.70 / +0.52 / +0.01 / +0.54 and 171 / 296 / 714 / 317 cents): the read follows F1 almost exactly. Below about 300 Hz, at f0 220 and 300 Hz, the teacher loses F1 under the noise and reports F2 (≈ 1,670 Hz) instead, which is what sends the whole-sweep slope negative. B1 (tilt alone) keeps the staircase (plateaus near 1.2, 2.1 and 3 f0 at 220 Hz). So on these stimuli the snapping comes from the excitation sampling the envelope only at harmonics, not from the model being unable to read an envelope: given noise that excites it between harmonics, teacher v3 reads F1 to within a few tens of cents. Students: v2 0.06 worse with aspiration than with Rd alone, v4 0.10 better; Parakeet's own variation rises (0.39 → 0.64), as noise would.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-24 (Hannah), before running: harmonic locking on real singing, by VocalSet technique (reported only).* The sweeps found teacher v3 reading F1 as a harmonic staircase on steady synthetic vowels, removed by aspiration noise. Does it happen on real voices, and do vibrato and breathiness reduce it?
  - *Data*: VocalSet's **val** split (5 singers: female2, female8, male3, male5, male10; a training pool's held-out part, not an evaluation set), arpeggios and scales, techniques straight, vibrato and breathy, matched on (singer, exercise, vowel) where all three exist (the paired unit). Teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`), whole-file `analyse` (as the resynthesis' own analysis). Voiced frames: voiced_p > 0.5 with finite f0 and F1.
  - *Part 1, original recordings (no ground truth)*: (a) **locking excess**: share of voiced frames with F1 / f0 within 0.1 of a whole number ≥ 1, minus the same share with F1 shuffled against f0 within the file (20 shuffles), per file; (b) **following slope**: per voiced run ≥ 50 frames, both log2 f0 and log2 F1 detrended by a centred 31-frame moving average, the least-squares slope of F1's residual on f0's, kept where the f0 residual's SD ≥ 15 cents (1 = F1 moves with f0, as a locked read would; 0 = F1 holds while f0 moves); median over runs per technique. *Readings*: R1 (vibrato files): median following slope ≥ 0.5 → "F1 follows the vibrato", ≤ 0.2 → "F1 holds", otherwise "partly". R2: locking excess straight − vibrato, and straight − breathy, mean over matched triples with a 95 % bootstrap interval over triples: above 0 → "vibrato (breathy) locks less", below 0 → "locks more", otherwise "no difference". Expectation: vibrato and breathy lock less; R1 "partly" or "follows".
  - *Part 2, fresh resynthesis of the same files (ground truth)*: `resynth.render.source_from_audio` on teacher v3's analysis, then one `render_variant` per file (default `ResynthConfig`, seed fixed per file; the perturbation moves every pole by a tract factor 0.8–1.25 and per-formant offsets, so the new F1 does not sit where the analysis locked); teacher v3 reads each clip; |F1 error| in cents on label-voiced frames with finite labels, median per file. *Reading* R3: |error| straight − vibrato and straight − breathy over matched triples, bootstrap as R2: above 0 → "vibrato (breathy) reads F1 better", below 0 → "worse", otherwise "no difference". *Confound check*: each file's median |tract_left| reported by technique; R3 repeated on clips with median |tract_left| < 0.2 and reported beside it.
  - *Result (2026-10-24, `reports/analysis/locking/vocalset_locking_v3.txt`; 150 files, 50 matched triples, no resynthesis failures)*: **R1 "F1 holds"** (vibrato files' median following slope −0.00). **R2: straight − vibrato "no difference"** (+0.009 [−0.011, +0.029]); **straight − breathy "breathy locks less"** (+0.068 [+0.046, +0.092]). **R3 "no difference"** for both (straight − vibrato +12 cents [−18, +40], straight − breathy +14 [−10, +38]); the |tract_left| < 0.2 check agrees (+25 [−2, +53], +15 [−16, +46]); median |tract_left| 0.08–0.14 in every group. The context matters more than the contrasts: on these real recordings teacher v3 shows **no harmonic locking at all**. The locking excess is at or below zero in every technique (straight −0.024, vibrato −0.033, breathy −0.092: reads fall on harmonics no more often than chance, breathy less), F1 holds still through vibrato, and on the fresh resynthesis its median |F1 error| is 29–39 cents. *Post hoc, by file median f0*: below 250 Hz |error| 25–36 cents in every technique; 250–400 Hz straight 69 against vibrato 39 and breathy 38 cents (15–18 files each); above 400 Hz only 2–5 files per technique. So the staircase is a property of the perfectly steady, noise-free synthetic vowels, not of real singing at these pitches (median 175–187 Hz, up to about 600 Hz): real sources carry enough aperiodicity, aspiration and pitch movement, even on "straight" notes, for the envelope to be sampled between harmonics. Harmonic locking is therefore unlikely to be the main driver of the women's F1 overread on real speech; the 250–400 Hz straight-note difference is the one place a smaller version may remain, and it is a post-hoc look at 15 files.
- *Student v4, 10k branch results and the reported analyses re-run on it (2026-10-25; reported only, no decision; the verdict stays on the 20k branch's heads).*
  - *Fidelity* (per-frame, mean over phases) at the branch's end, step 10,000, against the constant run at 16,000: synthetic v2 0.626 / 0.609, v2s 0.590 / 0.580, VocalSet 0.456 / 0.428, Zeroth 0.557 / 0.554, held-out words 0.660 / 0.651 (LibriTTS-R) and 0.636 / 0.626 (speechocean762). The decay beats six more constant-rate steps on every set. Gold audio (means of eights): Hillenbrand 0.438 (v2 at step 12,000: 0.100), PTDB-TUG 0.422 (v2: 0.485).
  - *By population* (`fid_breakdown.py`, v2's pooled measure; fixed 2026-10-25 to read v4's eight-phase held-out caches at phase 0, after its first run stopped at Korean): VocalSet's weakest techniques are lip trill 0.12, messa di voce 0.20, vocal fry 0.32 and pianissimo 0.35, and long tones 0.37 the weakest task, against 0.50 for vibrato and fast forte.
  - *H5 on the student* (the diagnostic queued above, 6 seeds): women's F1 bias cut −0.5 ± 1.9 Hz (on student v2 at step 12,000 +5.6 ± 0.8; on Parakeet itself +5.3), men's bias rise −4.1 ± 1.5, Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut +3.1 ± 1.4; the head alone reads Hillenbrand F1 with +6.5 Hz bias, MAE 41.1 (on student v2 +20.6 / 43.3). Matched-noise control +4.8 / −1.3 / +3.1. Part-2 rule on the means: no. *Corrected 2026-10-25*: this entry first gave v2's numbers as Parakeet's.
  - *Re-run analyses* (`*_v2_12k_v4_10k*`; v2 unchanged at 12,000). Controlled f0 × F1 grid: under the same rule both students now read "f0 drives it" (v4 ρ +0.02 [−0.02, +0.06], +0.37 per octave of f0; at step 5,000 +0.34, "both"); v4's error at 150 Hz fell from 1.15–1.49 to 0.88–0.96. Fine F1 sweep part B: v4 1 of 11 coincidence peaks (3 of 11 at step 5,000), so both students "not confirmed". The registered readings stay those made at step 5,000. Per-crop: v4's phase-0 excess at step 5,000 is gone (VocalSet 88 → 67 of 512), a mild excess at phase 7 remains on synthetic audio; its 100 / 200 Hz comb lines at 2 ms rose from 7–10 to 8–12 dB, with R² across 2 ms offsets still flat (spread 0.002–0.019).
- *Student v4, amendment to the reported checks before any result for it (Hannah, 2026-10-25): the Korean held-out words.* Pre-registered above as "built as a reported transfer check, if ready before the first decision" (Zeroth-Korean val, korean_mfa). Not yet built; nothing about Korean words has been seen. Results seen before this entry: v4's fidelity through the 10k branch (including Zeroth crops), and H5 on the 10k branch.
  - *Source*: Zeroth-Korean val (`test_data_01`, 10 speakers, in no training set). Alignments are the MFA ones minimal-pairs has already cached: korean_mfa acoustic model with its patched dictionary, 454 of the 457 val utterances. Only word boundaries and pauses are used. The cached phone labels have known errors (stops before a glide; hannahsq/minimal-pairs#1), but the targets are Parakeet's own output on the audio, so phone labels don't enter.
  - *Words*: space-delimited Korean words (eojeol, particles included), 0.2–0.9 s, built as `words.py` items. The pauses are the gaps between aligned words. Read news speech has almost no pauses on both sides (20 candidates), so the rule is speechocean762's: a pause on at least one side with a 6 dB edge, the other side cut at the boundary with a 25 ms fade. That gives 1,354 candidates (1,018 distinct words) from all 10 speakers; 98 % build. 512 items, seed 12345, all eight phases, as the other held-out word sets.
  - *Kept apart*: built into `cache/distil_v4_korean` (`distil_cache_v4.py`, `VA_PART=korean`), so no training run reads it.
  - *Measure*: v4's held-out word measure (`fidelity()` from `distil_train_v4.py`, unchanged: per-frame R² over the masked frames at all eight phases, v4's statistics; `fid_v4_on.py` with `VA_ONLY_SETS`). Scored on every v2 checkpoint (restandardised as before), every v4 constant-rate checkpoint, the 10k branch, and the 20k branch once it exists.
  - *Reported* (no rule, no decision):
    - Korean word R² beside the English word sets at the same checkpoints; speechocean762 is the like-for-like set, since it uses the same one-sided rule.
    - The transfer ratio at each branch: (v4 − v2) on Korean words over (v4 − v2) on English words, both against v2 at step 12,000.
- *Reported sweep, pre-registered before running (Hannah, 2026-10-25): H5 on the student by checkpoint.* Seen: H5 on student v2 at step 12,000 and on the 10k branch. Question (curiosity, no decision; explicitly not used for any v4 checkpoint or recipe choice): does the H5 diagnostic trend with training?
  - *Checkpoints*:
    - v2 at 4,000 and 8,000 (its cosine schedule, so not decayed until 12,000);
    - v4's constant-rate run at 4,000, 8,000, 12,000 and 16,000 (not decayed);
    - the 20k branch at its end.
  - *Measure*: as the 10k-branch diagnostic (`chains/d034_v4_h5diag.sh`): D-033's six H5 heads fed the student's 80 ms means, with the matched-noise control, plus gold fidelity. Reported as the six-seed means, and as the student's margin over its own noise control.
  - *When*: after every pre-registered 20k-branch step (heads, rule, gold fidelity, free part), before D-031 resumes. Each checkpoint's caches are deleted once its reports are written.
  - *Korean words, result (2026-10-25; `korean_words_v2.jsonl`, `korean_words_v4.jsonl`; reported only)*. Per-frame R² on the 512 Korean held-out words:
    - v2 never learns them: 0.07–0.10 at every checkpoint, as on the English words (LibriTTS-R 0.04 and speechocean762 0.11 at step 12,000).
    - v4 learns them from English words alone: 0.21 at step 1,000, 0.45 at 8,000, 0.48 at 16,000, and 0.492 at the end of the 10k branch. At the same checkpoints, LibriTTS-R scores 0.660 and speechocean762 0.636; Zeroth crops score 0.557.
    - Transfer ratio at the 10k branch: (0.492 − 0.096) / (0.660 − 0.037) = 0.64 against LibriTTS-R, and 0.75 against speechocean762, the like-for-like set (one-sided pauses).
    - On the constant-rate run from 8,000 to 16,000, Korean gains +0.037 against +0.026 / +0.028 for the English sets. The decay adds +0.035 at 10k (English +0.028 / +0.031).
- **Student v4 result (2026-10-25, dev; `d034_v4_heads_10ms.txt`, `d034_v4_heads_80ms.txt`): "does not transfer".** Six-seed means, ensemble (c) with teacher v3, on the 20k branch (step 20,000):
  - *10 ms heads*: women's closed-phase |F1 bias| cut −1.1 ± 2.6 Hz (needs ≥ 5), men's rise +7.1 ± 1.6 (limit 5), Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut +5.0 ± 1.3. Fails on both bias parts. Stage 1 (v2, 10 ms) gave −8.9 / +5.4 / −2.7.
  - *80 ms heads*: +1.3 ± 2.6 / −0.6 ± 2.5 / +5.7 ± 0.6. Fails on the women's cut. Stage 1c (v2, 80 ms) gave +3.6 / −5.1 / +2.4. One seed of six passes alone (S4x80#2: women 7.6 → 1.9 Hz); the rule is on means.
  - *Beside the rule*: both head sets improve Hillenbrand F1 MAE more than H5×6 does on Parakeet (+4.1), and more than either v2 stage, so the students' heads add accuracy but not the women's bias correction.
  - *Gold fidelity* (means of eights): Hillenbrand 0.474 (10k branch 0.438; v2 at 12,000 0.100), PTDB-TUG 0.419 (0.422; v2 0.485).
  - *Free part* (`d034_v4_free_part.txt`; the file's "student v2" label is a fixed string in `free_part.py`, the rows are the 20k branch): energy share outside the 80 ms sliding mean 0.038 (synthetic) and 0.048 (PTDB-TUG), against control R's 0.083 / 0.114. A ridge probe for log2 F1 on synth_train_v2s gives R² 0.767 on whole frames, 0.769 on the 80 ms smooth part and 0.172 on the residual.
  - *Fidelity by population* (`fid_v4_b20k.txt`): every population up 0.016–0.027 on the 10k branch. Per-frame held-out R²: synthetic v2 0.646, v2s 0.612, VocalSet 0.479, Zeroth 0.583, words 0.677 (LibriTTS-R) / 0.657 (speechocean762). Weakest VocalSet techniques: lip trill 0.14, messa di voce 0.22, vocal fry 0.34; long tones 0.40.
  - *Per the rule*, what's next (domain-adversarial training, or H5's heads on the student) is to be pre-registered separately. Higher fidelity has not brought the bias correction with it (Hillenbrand gold fidelity 0.10 → 0.47 from v2 to v4, women's cut at 80 ms +3.6 → +1.3), which argues against "too small" as the explanation.
- *H5-by-checkpoint sweep, result (2026-10-25; `d034_h5sweep_<tag>_*.txt`; reported only, no decision)*. Six-seed means of D-033's H5 heads on each student's 80 ms means; women's closed-phase F1 bias cut, then the margin over the matched-noise control (noise alone: +4.6 to +4.9 at every checkpoint):

  | Checkpoint | Women's cut | Over noise | Men's rise | Hillenbrand F1 MAE cut | Gold fidelity Hillenbrand / PTDB-TUG |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | v2 4k | −0.7 ± 2.1 | −5.5 | −5.4 | +3.0 | 0.087 / 0.386 |
  | v2 8k | +5.5 ± 0.8 | +0.8 | −5.5 | +1.7 | 0.092 / 0.382 |
  | v2 12k (earlier) | +5.6 ± 0.8 | +0.7 | −5.0 | +1.6 | 0.100 / 0.485 |
  | v4 4k | −9.0 ± 2.0 | −13.6 | −0.5 | +3.5 | 0.330 / 0.359 |
  | v4 8k | +3.0 ± 2.1 | −1.7 | −5.5 | +2.7 | 0.402 / 0.374 |
  | v4 12k | +0.0 ± 2.1 | −4.9 | −5.1 | +4.1 | 0.376 / 0.461 |
  | v4 16k | −2.1 ± 1.9 | −6.9 | −2.6 | +3.3 | 0.430 / 0.406 |
  | v4 10k branch (earlier) | −0.5 ± 1.9 | −5.3 | −4.1 | +3.1 | 0.438 / 0.422 |
  | v4 20k branch | +0.4 ± 2.4 | −4.4 | −4.5 | +4.1 | 0.474 / 0.419 |

  - No student beats its own noise control by more than 1 Hz at any checkpoint. v2 reaches the noise level by 8,000 and stays there. v4 peaks near it at 8,000 and then falls back, while its Hillenbrand gold fidelity keeps rising. More training doesn't move v4 toward Parakeet's H5 behaviour, so a longer run is not the lever for the bias.
  - *Korean words at the 20k branch*: 0.516 (10k branch 0.492). Transfer ratio (against v2 at 12,000) 0.66 against LibriTTS-R ((0.516 − 0.096) / (0.677 − 0.037)) and 0.77 against speechocean762 ((0.516 − 0.096) / (0.657 − 0.110)), up from 0.64 / 0.75.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah's observation), before running: the 10 ms ripple in R² at 2 ms offsets on synthetic audio (reported only, no decision).* Seen before this entry (`subhop_2ms_v2_12k_v4_10k_r2.csv` and the f0 500–700 Hz rerun, `a6038af`): on the synthetic held-out crops, R² across the forty 2 ms offsets ripples with the students' 10 ms hop, and on the real sets it doesn't. Share of the variation at the 10 ms period: synthetic v2 0.14, v2s 0.48–0.53, VocalSet and Zeroth-Korean 0.01–0.15. Within each hop R² is highest at 2–4 ms and lowest at 6–8 ms, and the trained alignment (0 ms) is slightly below average. Both students show the same shape, at v4 steps 5,000 and 10,000. On crops with median voiced f0 500–700 Hz the range roughly triples (0.035–0.048 against 0.013–0.019).
  - *Question*: is the ripple (1) an artefact of `subhop.py`'s synthetic path, (2) structure in the synthetic audio locked to the clip's 10 ms frame clock, or (3) neither?
    - (1): synthetic crops are used alone and zero-padded, so a student shifted by 2s ms ends on up to 8 ms of digital silence. Real crops are cut from a longer file and get real audio there.
    - (2): `SynthCrops._crop` starts every crop on a whole 10 ms frame of its clip.
  - *Arms* (`subhop.py` with `VA_ARM`): the first 256 crops of each synthetic held-out set (v2 and v2s), drawn by `SynthCrops` with three frames (30 ms) of extra clip audio after the crop (`n_frames` = 403; same seed, so the draws differ from the cached sets'). Same crops in every arm:
    - **base**: the crop alone (its first 400 frames), zero-padded as now. This reproduces the ripple on these draws.
    - **edge**: the same crop, with the real audio after it in place of the padding (`subhop.py`'s real-crop path, start 0).
    - **clock**: as edge, with the crop's start moved by u ms, u drawn per crop from 0–9 (seeded), so any clip-locked structure lands at a different place in each crop.
    - Students and measure as before: v2 at step 12,000 and v4's 10k branch; R² per crop at each 2 ms offset against Parakeet's layer 5 at the same offset.
  - *Ripple measure* P: the peak-to-peak of R² averaged by position inside the 10 ms hop (the five sub-offsets 0–8 ms, each averaged over the eight hops), over crops. Also reported: the share of variation at the 10 ms period, and P by median voiced f0 (< 250, 250–400, ≥ 400 Hz).
  - *Readings*, per student and set, on ratios with 95 % paired bootstrap intervals over crops (1,000 resamples):
    - edge / base: upper bound < 0.5 → "the padding accounts for most of it"; lower bound > 0.75 → "not the padding"; otherwise "partly".
    - clock / edge: upper bound < 0.5 → "locked to the clip's clock"; lower bound > 0.75 → "not clip-locked"; otherwise "partly".
    - Overall, if both students and both sets agree: (1), (2), or (3) if neither ratio falls below 0.5. Otherwise "mixed".
  - *Expectation written beforehand*: "partly" for edge (the padding can't explain why the 0 ms position isn't best), and clip-locked for whatever remains, more on v2s.
  - *Result (2026-10-26, `ripple_v2_12k_v4_10k.txt`): **(3) neither** under the rule.*
    - *edge / base*: 1.00 [1.00, 1.00] for both students on both sets, "not the padding". The arms are identical to the last digit. The padding can't reach the measured frames: the student frames compared (up to 395) have VQT windows inside the crop at every sub-offset, so (1) was ruled out by construction. That should have been checked before registering it.
    - *clock / edge*: 0.80–0.94, with intervals of about [0.25, 3.0] in every case, "partly". Ripple P on base is 0.0037–0.0046.
    - *Seen after the readings (post hoc)*:
      - Moving the crop starts changes the ripple's shape (lowest at 6–8 ms on base, at 0–2 ms under clock) without shrinking it. So it is tied to each crop's content, not to the clip's clock and not to the crop's edges.
      - It sits in the high-f0 crops. P on base is 0.010–0.011 for crops with median f0 ≥ 400 Hz on synthetic v2, against 0.001–0.002 below 400 Hz. On v2s it is 0.013–0.015 at 250–400 Hz.
      - A few crops carry it, which is why the intervals are wide.
      - One reading that fits: a steady synthetic f0 keeps a fixed relation between the glottal pulses and the 10 ms hop across a crop. R² then depends on where the hop lands relative to the pulses, differently per crop. Real voices' jitter and vibrato break that relation. This is a hypothesis and wasn't tested here.
    - *Not covered by these arms*: the step between the 60 and 62 ms offsets (synthetic sets and VocalSet, both students, not Zeroth-Korean). Parakeet sees the same span in every arm.
- *Pre-registered 2026-10-26 (Hannah: jitter first, then the step and context length, v4 only to halve the compute), before running: three follow-ups to the ripple check (reported only, no decision).* Student: v4's 10k branch, as the ripple check (the effects are clearer on v4). Sets: the first 256 crops of synthetic held-out v2 and v2s, drawn as in the ripple check unless stated.
  1. *Jitter (hop–pulse locking)*. The synthesiser draws each clip's jitter uniformly from 0–2 % (SD of the per-period relative period perturbation), and clips regenerate exactly from their index and seed (labels match). Arms (`subhop.py`, `VA_ARM`, as base: the crop alone, zero-padded):
     - **regen**: the clip regenerated as drawn, a check that it reproduces base;
     - **jit0**: regenerated with jitter fixed at 1e-6 (none; the random stream is unchanged, so only the period perturbations differ);
     - **jit2**: fixed at 0.02.
