  - *Prediction*: stage 1's median of (QCP-FB − closed-phase) F1 over the same renders, in the women's and men's regions, with its vowel-bootstrap interval. It says how far the two references should disagree if real voices behave like the renders.
  - *Agrees* if, for both sexes, the speaker-bootstrap (2,000) 95 % interval of the real-voice median difference overlaps the prediction's interval. Then each reference's synthetic bias carries over, and QCP-FB is adopted.
  - *Precision*: an interval can overlap simply by being wide (6 speakers per sex). So the real-voice interval's half-width must be at most **4 Hz**, the same reasoning as D-043's bound: a reference must resolve half the women's F1 bias it exists to measure (about +8 Hz). If it is wider, the result is **inconclusive**, not agreement, and QCP-FB is not adopted on this evidence.
  - *Disagrees* (precise, no overlap): real voices hold something the renders lack (GCI errors, breathiness); QCP-FB is not adopted, and the difference is investigated.
  - F2 / F3 differences and the frame-level spread are reported.
  - *Amendment, reported only (Hannah's question, 2026-10-29 00:35; stage 1 running, nothing seen)*: arm A also runs on PTDB-TUG dev. Its real-voice difference from arm B, (A − B) F1 by sex with a speaker bootstrap, is set beside stage 1's synthetic (A − B). A real gap clearly smaller than the synthetic one would say our renderer's subglottal coupling is too strong (D-026's coupling check already found Praat's F3 rising with the opening more than Birkholz et al. did); a similar gap, that it is realistic. Reported, no rule; a finding there would be a synthesiser entry of its own.
- **Adoption**: if both stages pass, QCP-FB becomes the formant reference for voices without an EGG, inside its valid f0 range: Hillenbrand dev (all four groups) and D-045's children's arm, reported **beside** the existing labels (Hillenbrand's own labels stay; every comparison is shown against both). On EGG sets the closed-phase reference stays primary and QCP-FB is reported beside it. Adoption changes no earlier decision; using the new reference for a decision needs that decision's own entry.
- *Before registering, done*: `vocal_analysis/eval/qcp.py`, 9 tests (`tests/test_qcp.py`: the weights match `qcp_wt`'s construction on a worked example; known poles recovered from free decay; the QCP-weighted fit returns exactly four formants despite open-phase noise; SEDREAMS finds ≥ 95 % of closures at f0 100 / 200 / 300 Hz; the end-to-end analysis reads the poles with ideal GCIs); commit `31e0387`. Smoke runs on development renders only (above). SEDREAMS is implemented from the published method; GLOAT's code (GPL-3.0) was consulted, not copied. The gate grid has not been run.
- *Limits*: one physical renderer; SEDREAMS assumes a reasonably clear excitation, so breathy or noisy voices may lose GCIs (the coverage and the 30 dB check report it); 8 kHz analysis limits F3 to well below 4 kHz (the paper's choice, kept).
- **Stage 1 result (2026-10-29 00:29; `reports/analysis/d046/d046_gate.txt`, `d046_stage1.txt`, `d046_stage1_high.txt`; 1,080 renders, plus 540 at 400–800 Hz): no arm passes, so QCP-FB is not adopted and stage 2 does not run** (nor arm A's coupling check, which was part of it).
  - *GCIs*: SEDREAMS found 99.4 % of the lag-shifted closures (97.0 % at 330 Hz); the gate passes.
  - *Arm A (as published)*: women's-region F1 **+16.6** Hz, men's +10.3; leaky cells +20.3 / +29.0; fails every criterion but coverage. As expected from the development renders.
  - *Arm B*: unbiased on F1 at every rate (women's region +2.1 / +3.6 / +3.1 Hz at 8 / 10 / 16 kHz; men's +0.1 / −0.0 / +0.8), and better than the reference with a leaky closure (gap 0.10: +4.5 / +6.4 / +6.6 against +8.8). Each rate misses one or two paired criteria, narrowly: 8 kHz F3 −17.1 (bound 8.6) and precision (+4.7 against 3.3); **10 kHz precision only** (paired upper bound +4.0 against the reference's half-width 3.1); **16 kHz F3 only** (+10.1 against 8.6). The reference is very consistent over vowels on F3 (half-width 1.1 Hz), which makes that bound tight.
  - *Forward-only QCP (duration quotient 0.3)* collapses (F3 −438 Hz): with a short weighted span the backward half of the fit is essential, as Gowda et al. argue.
  - *400–800 Hz*: every method fails, the reference included (F1 median |error| 63–111 Hz), as D-043's addendum.
  - *Not done*: changing a setting until a rate passes would fit the gate. A second attempt needs its own entry and fresh validation material not used here (other tract shapes, a child's tract among them, other vowels and source seeds), with the rate fixed in advance.


## D-047 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): does aspiration level move teacher v3's F1 bias on synthetic renders?

D-045's addendum: on PTDB-TUG dev, teacher v3's F1 overread rises with Praat HNR against all three references (women −0.1 → +14.7 Hz from the least to the most harmonic quartile, men +3.1 → +10.0), and the references' differences don't track HNR. Hannah: test it on renders, where the noise level can be changed alone. Earlier findings (D-034's sweeps, on the fidelity dashboard): on *steady* synthetic vowels with no aspiration, teacher v3 reads F1 as a staircase locked near harmonics, and 0 dB aspiration removes it; on real VocalSet singing there is no locking at all. Those sweeps compared no noise against an extreme one on unnatural vowels. This entry asks whether the noise level within the training range shifts the *signed bias* on natural renders.

- *Renders*: fresh clips from train_v2's synthesis config (teacher v3's synthetic training pool), seed 4547 (train_v2 is seed 2, so never trained on), no channel. Each clip is regenerated once per arm with `hnr_db` fixed. The generator still draws `hnr_db` from a one-point range, so its random stream is unchanged. *Checked*: on two clips, the voiced component and every label are identical between 5 and 40 dB, and the aspiration's RMS ratio is 56.2, as set.
- *Clips*: chosen by labels alone (arm-independent): not whole-clip whispered, median label-voiced f0 165–300 Hz (**women's range**, the first 150) or 90–160 Hz (**men's range**, the first 100), in index order.
- *Arms*: `hnr_db` 5, 10, 20, 30, 40 dB (training draws 5–40) and 60 dB (cleaner than any training clip).
- *Measure*: teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, whole-file `analyse`) against the label F1 on label-voiced, non-whispered frames with finite F1, 5 frames trimmed at each end; per clip the median signed error (Hz). Also the median |error| and Praat HNR (cc, as D-045) per clip and arm, to place the arms on the real voices' scale (PTDB-TUG women's Praat HNR quartiles 12.5 / 16.4 / 19.9 dB; synthetic training frames at f0 165–400 Hz 8.1 / 13.8 / 18.6).
- **Rule** (women's range): the paired difference in per-clip median signed error, 40 dB − 5 dB, mean over clips with a 95 % clip bootstrap (2,000).
  - Lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"cleaner excitation raises F1 on renders"**: the noise level reaches the bias directly, which supports a source-noise part in the real-voice link. Next: compare the training HNR distribution with real voices' (its own entry).
  - Interval inside ±4 Hz → **"no HNR effect on renders"**: the real-voice link goes through something that correlates with HNR (vowel nucleus, level, closure), not the noise level itself.
  - Upper bound ≤ −4 Hz → "opposite"; otherwise "inconclusive".
  - ±4 Hz is half of the women's +8 Hz closed-phase bias, as in D-046.
- *Reported*: every arm's mean and median signed error, |error| and Praat HNR; paired 20 − 5, 40 − 20 and 60 − 40 dB (the last outside the training range); the men's range likewise (reported only).
- *Noise floor, checked before registering* (`pilot`: the 20 dB arm only; seen: the per-clip spread and one clip's error, no contrast): per-clip median error SD 5.0 Hz (women, 150 clips), 3.2 Hz (men, 100), so a paired interval's half-width is at most 0.8 / 0.6 Hz, well inside ±4.
- *Limits*: one synthesiser, whose noise has a fixed spectral shape relative to the harmonics (pulsed, differentiated white noise through the tract). Real aperiodicity rises with frequency in a way that differs by phone class (SoFilNet's band aperiodicities, `docs/papers/literature.md`), so a null here doesn't rule out a noise-*shape* effect. No channel, so the channel augmentation's own noise is absent.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d047_hnr_renders.py` (`check`, `pilot`, `run`). The 20 dB rows of the pilot are reused, since rendering is deterministic.
- **Result (2026-10-29 11:47; `reports/analysis/d047/d047.txt`): "no HNR effect on renders".** Women's range, paired 40 − 5 dB: +0.7 [+0.1, +1.3] Hz, inside ±4.
  - *The whole curve is flat*: women's mean signed error −0.1, +0.1, +0.4, +0.6, +0.6, +0.6 Hz from 5 to 60 dB; men −0.3 to −0.8 Hz. Mean |error| rises slightly with cleaner excitation (women 7.3 → 8.5 Hz).
  - *The arms reach the real voices' range*: Praat HNR rises from 7.7 to 21.1 dB (women's range) and saturates there, where jitter, shimmer and vibrato set the limit, so 60 dB adds nothing (60 − 40: +0.0 Hz). PTDB-TUG women's top HNR quartile starts at 19.9 dB, so the renders do cover it.
  - *Reading*: on renders, teacher v3 is unbiased at every aspiration level, including real voices' most harmonic range, where on PTDB-TUG it overreads by +11 to +15 Hz. The noise level does not reach the bias. The real-voice HNR link goes through something that moves with HNR and that the renders lack or don't vary with it (vowel nucleus against transitions, level, closure, the noise's spectral shape: this entry's limits).


## D-048 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): does an aspiration shape matching real voices move teacher v3's F1 bias on renders?

D-045's follow-ups: renders are too periodic below 2 kHz (women's 1–2 kHz band periodic fraction 0.68 against real 0.53) and agree above 2 kHz; whatever smears their harmonic detail makes them look more real, while more of our aspiration makes them look less real. D-047 varied only the aspiration *level* at our fixed shape (the first difference of white noise, about +6 dB/octave before the tract) and found no effect on F1; its 5 dB arm reached the real 1–2 kHz value only by making the bands above 2 kHz far too aperiodic. Hannah: test the shape.

- *Synthesiser change* (no effect on any existing set): `SynthConfig.asp_tilt_db_oct` (fixed, no random draw; default None = the difference, as before) and `synthesize(..., asp_tilt_db_oct)`, which shapes the *same* white-noise draw by a tilt in dB per octave (re 1 kHz, flat below 50 Hz). Level is still set by `hnr_db` over voiced samples. The interactive source refuses it. *Checked*: tests (`test_aspiration_tilt_changes_only_the_aspiration`): voiced component and labels identical, aspiration energy equal, more of it below 1 kHz at −6 dB/octave; all 36 synthesis tests pass.
- *Calibration, before registering* (`reports/analysis/d048/calib.txt`; band profile only, no F1 measured): 30 separate women's-range clips (seed 4550, never in the test), clean, tilt {difference, 0, −6, −12 dB/octave} × HNR {5, 10, 15, 20, 30 dB}; distance = RMS over the five bands to real women's mean profile (follow-up A, Zeroth and speechocean762 averaged: 0.908 / 0.527 / 0.286 / 0.127 / 0.042). Best: **−6 dB/octave at 10 dB** (0.899 / 0.531 / 0.264 / 0.090 / 0.005; distance 0.025); best with today's shape: the difference at 15 dB (0.970 / 0.610 / 0.168 / 0.026 / −0.007; 0.086); no other combination below 0.067.
- *Arms* (fixed by the calibration): **reference** = the difference at 20 dB (D-047's arm, re-rendered here for its band profile; its errors must reproduce D-047's 20 dB rows, checked in the report); **level_matched** = the difference at 15 dB; **shape_matched** = −6 dB/octave at 10 dB.
- *Renders, clips, measure*: exactly D-047's (fresh train_v2 renders, seed 4547, no channel, the same 150 women's-range and 100 men's-range clips; teacher v3 whole-file `analyse` against label F1, label-voiced non-whispered frames, 5 trimmed each end; per clip the median signed error). Band periodic fraction per clip and arm reported, to confirm the match on the test clips.
- **Rule** (women's range): paired shape_matched − reference, mean over clips, 95 % clip bootstrap (2,000). Lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"matched noise shape raises F1 on renders"**: real voices' noise shape reaches the bias, a candidate cause of part of the overread and a synthesiser change to test in training (its own entry). Interval inside ±4 → **"no noise-shape effect on renders"**. Upper bound ≤ −4 → "lowers"; otherwise "inconclusive". ±4 Hz as D-046 and D-047.
- *Reported*: level_matched − reference and shape_matched − level_matched (shape at a matched fit against level alone); |error|; men's range likewise.
- *Noise floor*: D-047's pilot on these clips (per-clip SD 5.0 / 3.2 Hz; paired half-width at most 0.8 / 0.6 Hz).
- *Limits*: one tilt for the whole noise; the timing (a closure peak) and wander within a clip unchanged; women's profile used for both sexes (men's real profile is less periodic still at 0–2 kHz); clean renders matched to real recordings, whose recording chain also lowers periodicity, so the shape_matched arm may slightly overshoot the source's true aperiodicity.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d048_noise_tilt.py` (`calib`, `run`).
- **Result (2026-10-29 14:27; `reports/analysis/d048/d048.txt`): "no noise-shape effect on renders".** Women's range, paired shape_matched − reference: −2.0 [−2.7, −1.4] Hz, inside ±4. The re-rendered reference reproduces D-047's 20 dB rows (max |difference| 0.001 Hz).
  - *The match held on the test clips*: shape_matched's band periodic fraction 0.890 / 0.551 / 0.258 / 0.088 / 0.025 against real women's 0.907 / 0.527 / 0.286 / 0.127 / 0.042 (reference 0.971 / 0.719 / 0.266 / 0.045 / 0.014).
  - *Level alone does nothing* (level_matched − reference −0.1 [−0.2, +0.1]); the shape lowers F1 slightly (shape_matched − level_matched −2.0 [−2.5, −1.4]), the opposite of the real-voice overread. Men: +0.6 [−0.1, +1.3]. Mean |error| unchanged (women 7.7 → 7.3 Hz).
  - *Reading*: with real voices' aperiodicity profile, teacher v3 stays unbiased on renders. Neither the level (D-047) nor the shape (here) of the source noise produces the overread, so it lies in something else that renders lack: the dynamics resynthesis brings (D-045 follow-up B3), the source pulse and closure, or the definition of F1 itself (the effective, coupled resonance in real voices, against the closed-glottis one in our labels, D-023). The tilt stays in the synthesiser as an option for realism (follow-up A), with no claim on the bias.


## D-049 (2026-10-29, pre-registered): teacher v3's F1 bias on resynthesised real speech, by sex

`docs/f1_bias_register.md`, "Dynamics": synthetic contours are unnatural (Hannah), and D-045's probes found resynthesised clips much more real-looking than renders. Teacher v3 is unbiased on identity resynthesis of real *singing* (VocalSet val, sexes pooled: +0.0 Hz); real *speech* by sex has not been measured. Resynthesis keeps a recording's real source (inverse-filtered) and real f0, level and articulation dynamics, and replaces the tract with a perturbed synthetic one whose labels are exact (D-016, D-019).

- *Clips*: `data/resynth/zeroth_korean_val_d16k` (10 held-out val speakers, recorded sex 6 women / 4 men; numeric ids only), audio as stored, no channel. The store keeps no clip-to-file map, so only clips from shards whose 8 files share one speaker are used: 1,880 of 2,084 clips (52 of 58 shards). The flat store follows the sorted shards (`consolidate`), checked against the index's clip count.
- *Measure*: whole-clip `analyse`; per clip the median signed F1 error (model − label) on label-voiced frames with finite F1, 5 frames trimmed each end, clips with at least 20 such frames. Per sex, the mean over speakers of each speaker's mean clip error (speakers weighted equally), with a two-level bootstrap (speakers, then clips within them; 2,000).
- *Models*: **teacher v3** (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`, trained with 30 % Zeroth-train and VocalSet resynthesis) and, reported beside it, **no_resynth** (`conformer_v2_s1_d16k`: same seed and recipe, train_v2 only). The second separates "real speech dynamics cause the bias" from "teacher v3 learned this domain from its resynthesised training data".
- **Rule** (teacher v3, women): lower bound ≥ +4 Hz → **"bias appears on resynthesised speech"** (real dynamics and source with exact labels reproduce the overread: dynamics or the real source are implicated); interval inside ±4 → **"no bias on resynthesised speech"** (with real source and dynamics, teacher v3 reads the labelled tract correctly, which leaves the real tract and F1's definition); upper bound ≤ −4 → "underread"; otherwise "inconclusive".
- *Noise floor, before registering* (pilot on the **men** only; seen: teacher v3's men's result): +0.7 [−0.6, +3.1] Hz, speakers −0.4 to +3.1, within-speaker clip SD 6.1 Hz. With 6 women's speakers the interval should be about ±2 Hz, inside the ±4 band.
- *Limits*: teacher v3 trained on Zeroth *train* resynthesis, the same method and corpus, so it may have learned this domain; no_resynth is the control for that. The resynthesis tract is perturbed (all poles × 0.8–1.25, per-formant offsets), so it is not the speaker's own; only source and dynamics are real. Korean read speech.
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d049_resynth_speech.py` (`pilot`, `run`; teacher v3's pilot clips are cached and reused).
- **Result (2026-10-29 15:13; `reports/analysis/d049/d049.txt`): "no bias on resynthesised speech".** Teacher v3, women: −0.0 [−0.8, +0.7] Hz (speakers −1.3 to +1.2); men +0.7 [−0.6, +3.3].
  - *Control*: no_resynth **underreads**: women −5.4 [−6.9, −3.8], men −4.1 [−5.7, −2.9], with a wider spread (mean |error| 21.5 against 12.8 Hz). The same model overreads real women's voices against the closed-phase reference (D-025: about +13 to +15 Hz at d16k). So real source and real speech dynamics do not produce an overread in either model; resynthesis training removed an *underread* on this domain, not the real-voice overread.
  - *Reading*: with the real source and dynamics and an exactly labelled synthetic tract, the bias is absent. The overread therefore lies in what resynthesis replaces: the real vocal tract (real bandwidths, wall vibration, coupling to the subglottal system and the open-phase shift) or F1's definition on real voices (`docs/f1_bias_register.md`; `considering.md`, Heads: a whole-cycle effective F1 head).
  - *Caveat*: the resynthesis source is the recording inverse-filtered with a tracker's formants, so it carries what the inverse filter left of the real tract (clips with |tract_left| > 0.5 were rejected at build); the real tract is mostly, not wholly, removed.

## D-050 (2026-10-30, design agreed; thresholds registered per probe): roadmap item 7, the invariance and equivariance probe suite: design

*Status*: **design agreed with Hannah (2026-10-30)**; nothing had run when she agreed it (only a renderer smoke test checking that the label formants and f0 move by exactly the imposed factors). It fixes what the suite measures and how it is built. Each probe's decision thresholds are registered separately, after its noise floor is measured (item 7's rule), and before the lockbox opens. 5c waits for this suite (roadmap, Hannah 2026-10-30).

- *Why one suite*: the probes so far are one-off scripts (`invariance_run.py`, `channel_probe.py`, `d037_cache.py`'s probe and shift parts, `d038_score.py`, `d041_invariance.py`), each with its own clips and measures. Item 7 needs every model scored on identical, cached inputs with the same measures and one report, so v3, v3.1 and 5c can be compared in one go.
- *Structure*: `vocal_analysis/eval/probes/`. A probe is a family of **paired conditions**: each source item is rendered under every condition, and each condition carries the imposed change, as a log factor for each output it should move (0 for invariance). Rendered audio is cached per probe with a fixed seed (`data/probes/<probe>/`), so every model hears the same samples. A runner scores a checkpoint (`va probes <ckpt>`) into `reports/probes/<run>.md/.json`, as the suite does.
- *Measures, per output* (F1–F4, B1–B4, VTL; f0 and voicing where they apply): per source item, the slope of log output against the imposed log change across its conditions (equivariance: ideal 1; invariance: the slope or the change against the reference condition, ideal 0); the median over items with a bootstrap over speakers or source recordings; a **shuffle null** (conditions permuted within item) for every slope; and **σ growth**: the change in the model's own σ against the reference condition, so an output that loses evidence should widen rather than drift. On real audio with a reference (channel probes on gold sets) the change in error against the reference is reported too.
- *Noise floor*: the shuffle null per measure, and seed spread from teacher v3's three seeds (`conformer_v2r2_{,s1_,s2_}d16k`) and v3.1's two (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`). Thresholds come from these; none is set from the models' probe scores.
- *Probes, in build order*:
  1. **Counterfactual resynthesis** (non-gold held-out pools: Zeroth-Korean val, VocalSet val; teacher v2's cached analyses): the real inverse-filtered source re-filtered through a deterministic tract (smoothed tracks, no random offsets or bandwidth draws). **Length**: α ∈ {0.85, 0.92, 1, 1.09, 1.18} (formants × 1/α; VTL × α); **f0**: source resampled by r ∈ {0.84, 0.92, 1, 1.09, 1.19}, tracks time-warped to match (this also changes the speaking rate by 1/r, which the timing probe separates); **both**, crossed at the outer values. *Checks before use*: α = r = 1 against the original recording (resynthesis artefacts); and the **source residue**: what the inverse filter left of the real tract (`tract_left` β) does not scale with α, so length equivariance is biased towards 0 by the residue's share. The probe reports its slopes against β and on clips with |β| < 0.2, which also gives the held tract_left dose test its natural home.
  2. **Single-formant shift on resynthesis**: D-038's probe with the real source instead of the synthetic pulse.
  3. **Channel** (dev gold audio only, Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG dev; paired against the clean file): codecs from `synth/codecs.py`'s table at fixed bitrates; telephone band; microphone responses (`synth/channel.py`'s peaking-filter EQ at fixed settings); reverberation, T60 ∈ {0.2, 0.4, 0.8} s; additive noise at SNR ∈ {30, 20, 10, 5} dB (white, pink, babble). Invariance with σ growth; error against the reference.
  4. **Timing**: sub-hop offsets (k/8 hop), polarity inversion, crop position and context length, time-stretch without pitch change.
  5. **Time reversal with a change of speaker** (Hannah): A-then-B joins, forward against reversed per frame, by distance from the join; dry audio, whole-hop padding, symmetric windows (`considering.md`).
  6. **Voice quality and effort** (synthetic renders with exact truth): Rd, aspiration (D-047's level and D-048's shape), jitter and shimmer, spectral tilt.
  The existing synthetic f0 probe (D-020) and shift probe (D-038) move into the suite unchanged, so their history carries over.
- *Data rules*: gold audio is used only for evaluation and only dev speakers; nothing here is trained on or tunes a parameter. Zeroth: numeric ids only.
- *Cost*: rendering is CPU; scoring one model on every probe should take minutes on the GPU.
- *Amendment (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~01:30; seen beforehand: probe 1's exploratory scores for v3 and v3.1, committed in `dc8b41b`)*: four more probes, built in this order before the rest of the list:
  7. **Bandwidth counterfactual** (probe 1's renderer, bandwidths × s ∈ {0.7, 0.85, 1.2, 1.45, 2.0}, formants fixed): F1–F4, VTL and f0 should not move (ideal slope 0 against log2 s); bandwidths should follow the labels (ideal 1). Prompted by probe 1's side finding that the models read real voices' B1 and B2 1.4–1.8 × wider than the synthesis prior: if F1 rises with bandwidth, that is a mechanism for the women's overread.
  8. **Extra pole-zero pairs**: each item re-rendered with a nasal pair and, separately, subglottal pairs, drawn from the training distribution (`synth.tract.sample_extras` with p = 1, seeded per item). F1–F4 should not move (change against the reference, ideal 0), σ may grow.
  9. **Signal chain** (on the original clips): gain (× 0.1, × 0.5, × 2, no clipping), DC offset, hard clipping at half the peak, and a 16 → 44.1 → 16 kHz round trip. Every output should be unchanged (ideal 0).
  10. **Instantaneous against anatomical length**: larynx height and lip protrusion move the formants while the anatomical length stays (needs 5c's `pg_cm` / `pm_cm` labels; built with 5c's preparation, so 5c can be scored on it).

- *First measurements, exploratory (2026-10-30 night and morning; nothing is decided from them; thresholds come from the noise floors, registered per probe)*. Seven models: v3.1 (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`) and its sibling seed (`_s1_`), v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) and its sibling seeds (`conformer_v2r2_d16k`, `_s2_`), v1, v2; 60 items (Zeroth-Korean val, 10 speakers; probe 10: 60 synthetic tube clips). Reports in `reports/probes/<probe>/`.
  - **Probe 1, counterfactual**: length equivariance 0.97–0.99 for F1–F4 in every Conformer, and VTL 0.99 / 0.99 (v3.1's two seeds; nulls ±0.16). f0 arm, F1 against log2 f0: v3 seeds +0.021, +0.024, +0.023; v3.1 seeds +0.007, +0.021; v1 −0.010, v2 +0.033; every interval includes 0. So v3.1's +0.007 is within seed spread. σ widens where evidence thins (lower F1, higher f0, F4 near the band edge). The original recording against its α = r = 1 resynthesis: B1 / B2 read 1.4–1.8 × the synthesis prior's bandwidths (men more than women), while the resynthesis reads at 0.95–0.97 ×: real bandwidths are wider than the prior, or read so (a lead for `f1_bias_register.md`, not sex-specific in the overread's direction).
  - **Probe 2, single-formant shift on resynthesised speech** (D-038's factors 0.9, 0.95, 1.05, 1.1; frames where the moved formant comes within 1.10 of a neighbour left out): the moved formant follows its label change with slopes 0.92–0.98 in every model, and the unmoved ones drag by at most 0.03. VTL leans on the upper formants: an F4-only shift moves it with slopes −0.27 and −0.34 (v3.1's two seeds). Reports `reports/probes/shift/`.
  - **Probe 3, channel** (Hillenbrand dev, 996 files; PTDB-TUG dev, 240 files; 25 conditions paired against the clean file; medians with a speaker bootstrap; reports `reports/probes/channel/`). Codecs, the 4 kHz low-pass and 30–20 dB noise move F1–F4 by a few cents at most. Exceptions:
    - **A ±6 dB peak at 1 kHz moves F1 by +11 to +13 / −12 to −14 cents in every model**, and cutting 1 kHz lowers Hillenbrand F1 error by 2–6 Hz while boosting it raises the error by 4–6. Energy between F1 and F2 pulls F1 up; this is a lead for the women's F1 overread (register row), not a test of it.
    - Noise at 10–5 dB lowers F1 by up to 12 cents (16 for v2) and also lowers its error (−3 to −7 Hz), the same direction as the 1 kHz cut. F3 and F4 errors grow with noise (F3 +90 to +117 Hz at 5 dB, every model), but the Conformers' F3 median stays within 7 cents where v1 drifts −30 and v2 −19. The models widen σ rather than drift: F1 σ log2 +0.2 to +0.5 at 5 dB, F2–F4 up to +1.5.
    - Reverberation costs f0 RPA 2–7 points in every model alike (the largest f0 effect here); otherwise RPA moves ≤ 1.7 points. Under reverberation v3.1's two seeds read F1 8–10 cents low, while v3's three seeds scatter (−7 to +6); the F2 error grows 5–10 Hz in every Conformer.
    - AMR-NB at 7.4 kbit/s and the telephone band raise F1 by 3–10 cents in the Conformers (not v1 or v2) and lower F3 by 7–11 (v1 −33).
    - Clean errors match the suite (v3.1 F1–F4 39/42/43/97 Hz, PTDB RPA 87.1).
  - **Probe 4, timing** (probe 1's 60 originals; sub-hop offsets of 20, 40 and 80 samples, polarity inversion, 2 s of silence on both sides; time-stretch left out pending a method choice; reports `reports/probes/timing/`. The first render wrote every item to one file, a variable reused for the pad length; re-rendered and re-scored 2026-10-30 ~10:00). Sub-hop offsets move F1–F4 by 1.5 cents or less in the Conformers (v1 F1 up to −4) and f0 by about +1 cent at half a hop. Polarity changes nothing in any model, exactly: the VQT log power cannot see the sign, so this arm only checks the plumbing. Two seconds of silence each side move the formants by 3 cents or less in the Conformers (v1 F1 −6) and VTL by +2 (v3.1's seeds), but **widen B1 by +8 to +30 cents** (v2 −73): bandwidth depends on how much silence surrounds the clip.
  - **Probe 5, time reversal across a speaker change** (90 cross-speaker and 10 same-speaker joins of probe 1's originals, whole file, no windows; reports `reports/probes/reversal/`). The frame mapping was checked on the features: forward and mapped-reversed VQT frames agree to 0.03 % (a one-frame shift gives 3 %). **The models are not time-symmetric**: far from any join, forward and reversed F1 differ by a median 40–60 cents in the Conformers (v3.1 about 42) and 80–100 in v1 / v2, F2 about 20, F3 about 10. The leak (cross-speaker minus same-speaker) beyond 10 frames from the join stays within ±15 cents with no consistent sign in any model. Within 10 frames the same-speaker control has only 12 frames, too few to read (the 0–10 bin's large negative leaks come from it). VTL (v3.1's seeds) shows a small, steady cross-speaker excess at every distance (+2 to +8 cents): a whole-file length estimate partly carries the other speaker. More same-speaker joins are needed before this probe's thresholds can be set.
  - **Probe 6, voice quality** (60 synthetic clips, train_v2's settings, each condition with only the source changed: Rd 0.5–2.5, HNR 10–40 dB, jitter 0.02, shimmer 1 dB; reports `reports/probes/voice/`): F1–F4 move by 2 cents or less in every model, f0 by 2 or less, VTL by 4 or less. Bandwidths widen under jitter (B2 +17 / +24 cents for v3.1's seeds, +24 to +39 for v3's, +63 for v1), though the labels do not move.
  - **Probe 7, bandwidth**: F1–F4 do not move with bandwidth in any model (slopes within ±0.003, intervals about ±0.007): **the bandwidth mechanism for the women's overread is ruled out on resynthesised speech**. Bandwidth outputs follow only half the imposed change (B1–B4 slopes 0.42–0.63 for the Conformers, 0.32–0.42 for v1): bandwidth estimates are compressed towards the prior.
  - **Probe 8, pole-zero pairs**: nasal pairs move F1 by +0 to +6 cents, subglottal pairs F2 by −3 to −8 cents (v1 the most); small against D-033's 5 Hz bars.
  - **Probe 9, signal chain**: DC offset, clipping at half the peak and a 44.1 kHz round trip move nothing beyond ±2 cents. Gain (× 0.1 to × 2): Conformers within ±3 cents; **v1 F1 −21 cents and v2 −5**: the older GRU teachers were not gain invariant.
  - **Probe 10, articulation**: lip changes move F1–F4 with the labels (slopes 0.98–1.07); larynx changes move F2–F4 with the labels, while F1's slope is unstable (intervals about ±0.6: a larynx change barely moves F1, so its x is tiny). **The anatomical VTL head follows the instantaneous length: larynx slope +1.01 and +1.11, lips +0.89 and +0.87 (v3.1's two seeds; ideal 0).** Performed shortening currently reads as a different anatomy; this is 5c's baseline.
- *Amendment 2 (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~11:00; seen beforehand: every first measurement above)*:
  - **Thresholds**: for each measure, the larger of (a) the shuffle null's 97.5 % point of |value| (slope measures only) and (b) the largest absolute difference between any two of the five Conformer seeds (v3's three, v3.1's two). A measure beyond its threshold is **flagged, not failed**: no probe blocks a release yet. The one exception is a measure a decision registers as a rule (D-051 uses probe 10). The thresholds are computed once, from the seven models already scored, and written to `reports/probes/thresholds.json` before 5c is scored on the suite.
  - **Probe 4's time-stretch arm, by resynthesis**: probe 1's items, stretch s ∈ {0.8, 0.9, 1.1, 1.25}. The tract tracks are time-warped by s. The real source is lengthened or shortened without a pitch change by repeating or dropping whole glottal cycles (pitch-synchronous overlap-add on the source alone, cycles placed from teacher v2's cached f0, the same analyses probe 1 uses). Labels: formants and f0 unchanged, so the ideal change is 0 for every output (medians over scored frames, which do not depend on the rate).
  - **Probe 5, more same-speaker joins** (more data; no design change): every ordered pair of a speaker's cached clips (30 per speaker, 300 in all) instead of one, so the control near the join has as many frames as the cross-speaker joins.
  - **Probe 11, uniform scale ("tape speed")** (Hannah): a clip resampled to 16000 / r Hz and then read as 16 kHz. Every frequency scales by r (f0, formants, bandwidths) and time by 1 / r, so the ideal slope against log2 r is 1 for f0, F1–F4 and B1–B4 and −1 for VTL. r ∈ {0.85, 0.92, 1.09, 1.18} (probe 1's length factors). The same scaling, applied to CQT frames rather than audio, is the transposition that SPICE and PESTO train their pitch heads on.
    - *Sources at a higher rate give exact deltas by construction* (Hannah): PTDB-TUG is stored at 48 kHz, so its dev files (probe 3's 240) can be scaled either way with the full 0–8 kHz band kept. Their reference f0 scales by r exactly, its frame times by 1 / r: f0 slope and RPA against the scaled reference.
    - *Hillenbrand dev* is 16 kHz: r > 1 only (r < 1 would leave the band above 8000 · r empty). Formant references scale by r exactly: formant slopes, and |error| against the scaled references.
    - *Caveat*: a uniformly scaled voice is not a physically plausible speaker (bandwidths and the source spectrum scale with it). This is an equivariance check, not a claim about real smaller vocal tracts, so VTL's −1 is the ideal only in this probe's sense.
  - *Implementation (2026-10-30 ~12:00, before any scoring)*: probe 11 is `probes/scale.py`. It resamples from the stored file with `load_audio(item, sr=round(16000 / r))`, so the exact r is 16000 / R. A one-file check with v3.1 tracked the scaled PTDB reference f0 within 1–3 cents at every r. The time-stretch arm is its own cache (`run.py` probe `stretch`, `counterfactual._stretch`) with the resynthesised clip at s = 1 as its reference, so the arm is not read against the unprocessed original. A two-item check: lengths exactly × s, model f0 within 0.5 Hz, label formants within a few Hz (rounding in the warp). Reversal: 300 same-speaker joins.
- *Amendment 3 (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~13:00; seen beforehand: probe 5's first measurements)*: **probe 5, direction arm**. Probe 5 measures |forward − reversed| without ground truth, so it cannot say which direction is right. Data: probe 1's 60 reference resynthesis clips (exact formant labels), whole file, no windows, trimmed to k · HOP + 1 samples so frame i maps to reversed frame k − i. Measure: per item and formant, the median |error| against the labels (cents) over the scored frames, forward and reversed; the paired difference reversed − forward, median over items with a speaker bootstrap; and the median |forward − reversed| on these clips, to compare with the real-audio joins. Reading: reversed clearly worse means the asymmetry is context helping in the trained direction; both equally far from the labels means the context acts more like noise. Exploratory and flag-only, as the rest of the suite, with thresholds from the five-seed spread (amendment 2).
- *Measurements under amendments 2 and 3 (2026-10-30 afternoon; exploratory, seven models)*:
  - **Probe 11, uniform scale** (`reports/probes/scale/`). f0 follows almost exactly in every model (PTDB-TUG slope 1.002–1.005; RPA against the exactly scaled reference changes by ≤ 1.5 points at any r). F2 and F3 follow within 1 % everywhere; F4 within 4 %. **F1 departs in opposite directions on the two sets**: on Hillenbrand (scaled up only) the Conformers under-follow (0.975–0.985; v2 0.966, v1 0.998), and the F1 error grows by 3 Hz at r = 1.09 and 5–8 Hz at r = 1.18, as if pulled towards the prior where F1 lands high; on PTDB-TUG (both directions) they over-follow (1.037–1.043; v1 0.992, v2 0.961). f0 scales with everything here, so probe 1's f0 coupling (about +0.02) explains part of the PTDB-TUG excess at most. **Bandwidths follow 0.3–0.8** (Hillenbrand B1 0.69–0.76 for the Conformers, PTDB-TUG 0.29–0.45; v1 0.57 / 0.13), the compression of probes 4, 6 and 7, now on gold audio. VTL (v3.1's seeds) −0.97 / −0.98 on Hillenbrand, −0.90 / −0.91 on PTDB-TUG (wider intervals).
  - **Probe 5, re-run with 300 same-speaker joins** (`reports/probes/reversal/`): the control now has frames at the join, and the earlier large negative leaks there have gone. Cross minus same scatters both ways (mostly within ±20 cents near the join, ±3 beyond 100 frames) with no consistent sign across models: no evidence of formant leakage across speakers. v3.1's VTL keeps a small cross-speaker excess (+1 to +5 cents seed 2, +4 to +13 seed 1).
  - **Probe 5, direction arm**: on probe 1's reference resynthesis, **reversed F1 is clearly less accurate than forward** in every model: Conformers +11.5 to +12.8 cents (forward error 30–32, reversed 41–44; intervals about [+8, +16]), v1 +16.0, v2 +19.1. F2 +0.2 to +1.7, F3 and F4 about 0. So the time asymmetry is context helping in the trained direction, and almost entirely for F1, the formant a single frame pins down least.
  - **Probe 4, time-stretch arm** (`reports/probes/stretch/`; slopes against log2 s, ideal 0): every output is close to invariant. F1 −0.005 to −0.010 in the Conformers (−0.012 / −0.014 for v1 / v2), about −4 cents at s = 1.25 at most; F2–F4, VTL and f0 within ±0.005; B1 +0.03 to +0.06 (v1 about 0).
- *Amendment 4 (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~13:30; seen beforehand: every first measurement above, including probe 11's bandwidth slopes)*: **probe 12, bandwidth evidence**. Do the bandwidth heads measure bandwidth, or lean on a prior, and on which one? Shrinkage towards a prior is the right behaviour where evidence is weak, so the probe separates three questions.
  - **Arm A, formants move with bandwidths held** (probe 1's renderer, its 60 items): length α ∈ {0.85, 0.92, 1.09, 1.18}, with every bandwidth held at the α = 1 values in Hz (the renderer's bandwidths are exactly the conditional prior `bandwidth_prior(F, f0)`, so normally they move with the formants). Ideal: formants follow their labels (slope 1), VTL slope 1 against log2 α, **bandwidth slope 0** against log2 α. Reliance on the conditional prior = the model's bandwidth slope here divided by the prior's own bandwidth slope in probe 1's length arm (labels), per formant: 0 means it measures, 1 means it reads B off F.
  - **Arm B, shrinkage against evidence** (no new rendering): probe 7's per-item bandwidth slopes split into tertiles of the item's f0 (the model's reference-condition f0). Justified shrinkage shows slopes rising towards 1 where f0 is low (dense harmonics); slopes flat across tertiles mean the evidence is barely used.
  - **Arm C, prior against truth on synthetic speech** (exact labels): 150 new cascade clips (train_v2's settings, `p_tube` 0, seed 5054, used by no other set). There the label bandwidth is the prior times an independent log-uniform factor (0.6–1.6), so the two can be separated. Per formant, over sonorant voiced frames: least squares of the model's log2 B on the label's log2 B and the prior's log2 B (`bandwidth_prior` at the label formants and the clip's median voiced f0, standing in for the sampler's centre f0), with a clip bootstrap; and the ratio of output to label variance (a ratio near 0 means the output has collapsed to the mean).
  - Exploratory and flag-only, as the rest of the suite; seven models.
- *Probe 12 measurements (2026-10-30 ~14:10; exploratory, seven models; `reports/probes/bw_evidence/`, `reports/probes/bwhold/`)*: **the bandwidth heads mostly read bandwidth off the formants and f0.**
  - **Arm C** (synthetic cascade clips, 31,608 frames): the model's log2 B puts weight 0.11–0.42 on the true bandwidth and 0.54–0.85 on the conditional prior, in every model (v3.1 B1 0.20–0.21 against 0.75; B2–B4 0.33–0.42 against 0.54–0.65; v3 and v2 lean further on the prior). Output variance is 0.77–0.94 of the labels', so the output has not collapsed to a mean: it varies, but mostly as the prior does.
  - **Arm A** (formants moved, bandwidths held; ideal 0): B2–B4 move with the formants at 0.05–0.55 of the prior's own slope in the Conformers (v1 0.66–0.77). B1's prior slope is close to 0 (+0.06), so its ratio is not meaningful; B1 itself moves +0.18 to +0.45 per log2 α where it should not move at all.
  - **Arm B** (probe 7's slopes by f0 tertile; edges 131 / 222 Hz for the v3.1 items): slopes do not rise where f0 is low and the harmonics are dense. Conformer B1 0.35–0.52 in the lowest tertile against 0.43–0.46 in the highest; v1 and v2 rise with f0, the opposite of evidence-driven shrinkage. The shrinkage does not track the evidence.
  - Reading: widening the synthesis bandwidth prior alone would move what the heads output without making them measure. Decoupling bandwidth from the formants in the training data (a wider independent factor than today's log-uniform 0.6–1.6) is the more direct fix (`considering.md`, Synthesiser).
- *Amendment 5 (2026-10-30 ~19:00; Hannah approved; seen beforehand: probe 9's results, and a singing recording peaking at -36 dBFS where 76 % of the feature bins sat at the -100 dB floor and B1 moved +174 cents per frame between raw and peak-normalised input)*: **probe 13, gain**. Probe 9's gain arm held ×0.1, ×0.5 and ×2 in one arm, and the runner reports an arm without a slope by its first condition only, so probe 9's gain result is ×0.1 alone (the others were scored, never reported; its committed reports stay as they are). Probe 13 gives each gain its own arm: ×0.01, ×0.03, ×0.1, ×0.3 and ×2 on probe 9's 60 original clips (peaks median -14 dBFS, so about -54, -44, -34, -24 and -8 dBFS), no clipping, change against the reference for every output (F1-F4, B1-B4, VTL, f0) and sigma growth. Training's channel puts peaks at -6 to -26 dBFS (`ChannelConfig.gain_db`), so ×0.03 and ×0.01 are outside it. Exploratory and flag-only, as the rest of the suite.
- *Probe 13 measurements (2026-10-30 ~20:45; exploratory, seven models; `reports/probes/gain/`; gains ×0.3 / ×0.1 / ×0.03 / ×0.01 put the median peak near -24 / -34 / -44 / -54 dBFS)*:
  - **f0 is level-invariant** everywhere: within 4 cents at every gain in every model.
  - **Formants hold to about -34 dBFS**: Conformers' F1-F3 within about ±3 cents at ×0.1. Below training's range they drift: at ×0.03 up to ±10 cents, at ×0.01 F1 -14 to +18 and F2 up to +20 cents (intervals about ±20). v1's F1 was not gain-invariant (-21 cents at ×0.1, as probe 9 found, -68 at ×0.01).
  - **Bandwidths narrow as level drops, even inside the trained range**: Conformer B1 -8 to -28 cents at ×0.3 (about -24 dBFS, inside training's -6 to -26), -22 to -68 at ×0.1, -19 to -115 at ×0.03 and -64 to -186 at ×0.01 (about ×0.3-0.7); B2 -97 to -216 cents at ×0.01. v1 moves the other way. This is the direction seen on Hannah's quiet singing recording (narrower B1 raw than normalised), so the bandwidth heads read level as evidence: a fifth context effect on bandwidth after probes 4, 6, 7 and 12.
  - **VTL** (v3.1's seeds): within 1.3 cents down to ×0.03; -1 and -16 cents at ×0.01.
  - Reading: for analysis, peak normalisation (now `va analyse`'s default) removes the level effect; for training, the bandwidth heads' level dependence joins the decoupling question (`considering.md`, Synthesiser) and the level-range note (roadmap, Later's AGC item).
  - *5c's seeds (2026-10-31 02:53, after D-051 was decided; `reports/probes/gain/d051_5c_*`)*: same pattern. f0 within 1 cent; F1 within 3 cents at every gain (×0.01: +3 / +2, against 5b's -14 / -13 in the same report column); VTL -26 / -15 cents at ×0.01 and within 4 cents above it; bandwidths still erratic below training's range (×0.01 B1 -68 / +35, B2 -102 / -6 cents) with no consistent sign between seeds.

## D-051 (2026-10-30, registered with Hannah's choices before anything was built): roadmap 5c, long clips with sustained larynx and lip movement, and an instantaneous-length head

*Status*: **decided 2026-10-31: 5b stays** (length reliability fails on both seeds; see Result). Registered (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~02:00): her answers fixed the head, the clip and crop lengths, the mix share, the movement ranges and the rule's form; the movement trajectories below are mine, within her ranges. Nothing had been generated or trained. 5c starts after D-050's probes 7–10 (Hannah: "work through the new probes, then start 5c").

- *Question*: does training on long clips where larynx height and lip protrusion move slowly, with the anatomical length fixed, plus a head that reads the instantaneous length, cost anything on what we already measure? Gains are reported, not required (Hannah: the 4 s crops may be what limits it, so no improvement is demanded).
- *Arms*: **5c** = teacher v3.1's recipe (5b: rA + joint VTL head) with the new data and head, seeds 1 and 2, against **5b** seeds 1 and 2 (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`), seeds paired. One addition: the long articulation clips and the instantaneous head together (neither does anything without the other).
- *Data*: **30 %** of training items (as resynthesis's share in v3, D-025) drawn from a new long-clip set; the other 70 % as 5b's mix in the same proportions. Long clips are **8–16 s**; **crops stay 4 s** (400 frames), as now and as the windowed analysis (Hannah: one change at a time). Each long clip keeps one speaker and one anatomical length; larynx height `pg_cm` and lip protrusion `pm_cm` follow **sustained trajectories within the current tube ranges** (`tube.sample_target`: pg uniform ± 0.5 cm × size, pm −0.3 to +1.0 cm × size): piecewise holds of 1–4 s joined by 0.2–1 s cosine transitions, each new level drawn from those ranges, with the vowel targets changing underneath as now. Wider ranges are a later arm (5d, Hannah) after this one, with sources for the ranges checked first; longer crops likewise.
- *Labels*: instantaneous length per frame = anatomical length + pg + pm (tier III adds length at the glottis and lips); the existing VTL head keeps the anatomical length (fixed per clip) as its target. **New head**: per-frame log2 instantaneous length with σ (Gaussian NLL), built like the VTL head (D-036: gate, weight 0.0125, own warm-up), on sonorant frames. On the other 70 %, instantaneous = anatomical + that target's pg + pm.
- *Measures*: everything D-040 measured, against 5b (synthetic F1–F4 and RPA; dev guard-rails; women's closed phase; Hillenbrand length reliability, w/m, probe excess); D-050's probes (counterfactual, bandwidth, pole-zero, signal chain, and probe 10, which this set enables); the new head's validation error and coverage on held-out long clips.
- *Rule* (**non-inferiority**, seed means, seeds paired; D-040's margins): 5c is adopted if (1) no formant cost (synthetic F1–F4 no worse than +2 SE, RPA not below −0.1; dev Hillenbrand F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA ≥ −1.0, women's closed-phase F1 ≤ +5 Hz); (2) length not worse: Hillenbrand reliability − 5b's paired interval lower bound above −0.02 (D-040's check margin), w/m in [0.85, 0.92]; (3) f0 probe excess ≤ 10 c/oct. Otherwise 5b stays. Seeds disagreeing → "not robust to the seed", as D-040. Probe results and the instantaneous head are reported; they decide nothing here.
- *Cost*: CPU generation of the long set; two runs of about 8 h each on the GPU.
- *Implementation (2026-10-30 ~03:00; no 5c run had started, nothing scored)*: `SynthConfig.articulation="sustained"` (`sampler._sustained`; pg and pm on independent timelines, levels uniform over `tube.PG_RANGE` / `PM_RANGE` × size, imposed on each tube target through `sample_target(pg_cm=, pm_cm=)`; no draws when off, and train_v2 clips were checked to reproduce bit for bit). Labels `length_inst_cm` from `generate`, and the same values without rendering from `sampler.clip_length_inst` (tested equal), written for any set by `scripts/analysis/inst_labels.py`. train_v2's tube clips already move: their per-target pg / pm draws give instantaneous lengths varying by up to about 1.5 cm within a clip, so the new head learns from the 70 % too. **The long set is all tube** (`p_tube` 1: the movement is the tube's tier III, so a cascade clip would carry none); otherwise train_v2's settings (`scripts/analysis/make_long_set.py`: 25,000 clips of 8–16 s, about train_v2's audio hours, seed 5051, `data/synth/train_long_v1`). The instantaneous head is a second `vtl_head_module` (`TeacherConfig.vtl_inst_head`) on the same gated input, in the VTL parameter group, initialised with `vtl_seed + 1`; `TrainConfig.vtl_inst`, `synth_extra`, `synth_extra_frac` (the rest scaled by 1 − 0.3, so train_v2 49 %, resynthesis 21 %). The long set's last 2 % is held out as `eval_extra:train_long_v1`. `va train --train-set` now parses bools and tuples (no earlier chain passed either). Chain: `chains/d051.sh`.
- *Chain restart (2026-10-30 ~06:00; nothing trained)*: the chain's first step reran `make_long_set.py` on the finished set and `write_set` refused it: the set's `index.json` records `artic_pg_cm` / `artic_pm_cm` as `None`, the default when generation started, and the default became 0.0 before the commit. Only `articulation="fixed"` reads those fields. Clips from shards 0, 40 and 97, regenerated with the committed code, matched the stored int16 audio and `length_inst_cm` labels exactly, so the set stands as written. The step now skips when the last shard exists.
- *Result (2026-10-31 02:50; `reports/analysis/d051/decide_d051.txt`, `vtl_joint.txt`, `paired/w400/*5c*`, `reports/probes/*/d051_5c_*`)*: **5b stays item 5's model**: criterion (2) fails on both seeds; (1) and (3) pass on both, so the result is robust to the seed. (1) Seed means against 5b: synthetic F1–F4 −0.9 / +0.8 / +1.0 / +0.8 SE (seed 1's F3 +1.9, inside +2), RPA +0.01; dev Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 −0.9 / +0.3 / −3.1 Hz, RPA −0.1, women's closed-phase F1 +0.6 Hz. (2) Hillenbrand length reliability 5c − 5b **−0.042 [−0.066, −0.017]** (seed 1 −0.021 [−0.050, +0.010], seed 2 −0.063 [−0.090, −0.036]); lower bound below −0.02. Reliability 5b 0.228 / 0.261, 5c 0.207 / 0.198; the drop is mostly men's (5b 0.254 / 0.328, 5c 0.228 / 0.212; women 0.201 / 0.194 against 0.186 / 0.183). w/m 0.878 (in range). (3) f0 probe excess +3.9 c/oct (5b +0.6; seed 1 +5.5, P(null ≥ obs) 0.014; seed 2 +2.2), under 10. Reported, deciding nothing: held-out long clips, instantaneous head MAE 1.40 / 1.38 %, coverage at 1σ 0.71 / 0.70 (anatomical head 2.03 / 1.99 %, 0.62 / 0.61); probe 10's VTLi slopes larynx 1.07 / 1.13 and lips 0.93 / 0.92 (ideal 1), anatomical VTL 0.67 and 0.76–0.77 (ideal 0, so it still follows larynx and lips about two-thirds of the way). Probe 12 on seed 1: bandwidths rely slightly more on the prior than 5b's (arm C w_label B3 0.37 against 0.42, B4 0.33 against 0.38). Probe 13 (gain) was not in the chain; it runs on both seeds separately.
- *Follow-up (2026-10-31, after the decision; Hannah asked)*: is the anatomical head's leakage onto larynx and lips new in 5c? No: 5b's probe 10 (already in `reports/probes/articulation/d040_5b_*`, D-050's first measurements; a re-score reproduced the slopes) is larger, larynx 1.11 / 1.01 and lips 0.87 / 0.89 against 5c's 0.67 / 0.67 and 0.76 / 0.77 (ideal 0). 5c separated anatomical from instantaneous length partly on synthetic clips, and Hillenbrand reliability still fell, so the head did not take over per-speaker signal it had before. The cause stays open between the mix (resynthesised real voices 30 % → 21 %, the long set all tube) and the cost of the separation itself; D-051 bundled them, so this result cannot tell them apart.

## D-052 (2026-10-30, registered before anything was built; descriptive, no decision rule): how well does the synthetic glottal source cover real voices?

*Status*: **registered (Hannah, 2026-10-30 ~14:30: "a better synthesiser probably can't hurt")**. Descriptive: it decides nothing. Any change to the synthesiser it motivates is registered separately. CPU only.

- *Question*: do the synthesiser's source settings cover the source measures of real voices, by sex and f0 band? And how accurately does our inverse filtering recover a source, which limits what the comparison can show?
- *Part 1, recovery accuracy* (OPENGLOT Repository II, evaluation only: 96 files, Story's physically modelled vowels with source-tract interaction; channel 2 is the true glottal flow). Our `source_from_audio` pipeline (teacher v2's analysis, as the resynthesis stores use) on channel 1 against the true flow's derivative: per measure, the recovered minus true value, by sex, f0 and adduction. OPENGLOT tunes nothing: it only gives the error bars of each measure.
- *Part 2, coverage*. Real: the non-gold resynthesis stores' recovered sources (Zeroth-Korean val, recorded sex only, numeric ids; VocalSet val), voiced frames passing the stores' source check. Synthetic: 300 new clips with train_v2's settings (seed 5055, used by no other set), analysed with teacher v2 and inverse filtered by the **same** pipeline, so both sides carry the same instrument error. Groups: sex × f0 band (< 150, 150–250, > 250 Hz).
- *Measures*: from the harmonic levels of the recovered flow derivative (`source_qc`'s per-frame `h_db`): H1–H2, H1–H4 and the source slope (least squares of harmonic level on log2 harmonic number, H1–H10). From the flow (the integrated derivative, per cycle): NAQ and QOQ. Noise: HNR and CPP on the source.
- *Report*: per group and measure, real and synthetic medians and 5–95 % ranges, and the share of real frames outside the synthetic 5–95 % range, read against Part 1's error for that measure. A gap smaller than the recovery error is not read. Reports in `reports/analysis/source_coverage/`.
- *Data rules*: OPENGLOT is evaluation only and fits nothing; Zeroth numeric ids and recorded sex only; nothing here is trained on.
- *Amendment (2026-10-30 ~16:00; Part 1 seen, Part 2 not: its report came out empty)*: synthetic voices have no sex. `length_group` is unset whenever the length comes from f0, which was every one of the 300 clips, and the source settings are drawn by f0, not sex. So real frames are grouped by sex × f0 band and synthetic frames by f0 band alone.
- *Results (2026-10-30 ~16:10; `reports/analysis/source_coverage/`)*:
  - **Part 1, recovery error** (recovered minus true, median over 96 files [IQR]): H1–H2 −0.3 dB [−1.2, +0.2], H1–H4 −0.5 [−1.9, +1.5], slope +0.4 dB/oct [−0.2, +1.2], NAQ −0.010 [−0.020, −0.002], QOQ +0.011 [0.000, +0.037], CPP −0.3 dB [−0.6, −0.1], **HNR −5.6 dB [−7.2, −3.4]**. Inverse filtering adds noise, so recovered HNR is not read below gaps of about 6 dB.
  - **Speech (Zeroth val) is covered well** in the bands where most frames lie (women 150–250 and > 250 Hz, men < 150 and 150–250 Hz): medians agree within a few dB or hundredths, and 1–15 % of real frames fall outside the synthetic 5–95 % range for H1–H2, H1–H4, slope and NAQ. The gaps are larger than the recovery error:
    - **QOQ: real voices are more open.** Men < 150 Hz 0.51 against 0.43 synthetic (37 % outside); women 150–250 Hz 0.51 against 0.45 (13 %). The synthetic 95th percentile is 0.54–0.62, the real 0.67–0.80. Long open phases are where coupling is strongest, so this bears on the F1 register's coupling item.
    - **CPP higher in real women above 250 Hz** (2.4 against 1.6, 36 % outside).
    - Women below 150 Hz (186 frames): H1–H2 −16 dB, far outside the synthetic range. That is creak or vocal fry, which the synthesiser has no mechanism for. Few frames.
  - **Singing (VocalSet val) is covered less well**: lower H1–H2 and H1–H4 (men < 250 Hz 1.6–2.2 against 7.2–8.4 dB), lower NAQ (0.07–0.10 against 0.13–0.17) and much higher CPP (2.5–5.0 against 1.2–1.8; 43–89 % outside). Singers are more adducted (pressed) and more periodic than anything the synthesiser draws; low-f0 women (65–83 frames) again show creak-like values.
  - Reading: the synthetic source covers ordinary speech; it lacks long open phases (high QOQ), pressed or adducted and very regular phonation (low NAQ and H1–H2, high CPP, mostly singing), and creak. Candidate synthesiser changes (each its own decision): a wider Rd or open-quotient range towards the open end, a pressed tail, less aperiodicity in some voices, and a creak mode.

## D-053 (2026-10-31, registered after the label-only floor and before any model was scored; descriptive, with one flag): Iseli–Alwan validation with predicted F / B, by f0 band

*Status*: **done 2026-10-31: flag not raised** (see Result). Registered; Hannah asked for it (2026-10-31) after D-051, to size the bandwidth problem the probes found (D-050 probes 7, 11, 12, 13) on M3's own use of bandwidths before any GPU time goes on decoupling them. It is plan §8.2's validation and M3's checklist item ("Iseli–Alwan validation with predicted F/B, by f0 band"). Measurement only; no training.

- *Question*: how well do the corrected harmonic measures H1\*–H2\* and H2\*–H4\* recover the source's own values when the correction uses teacher v3.1's formants and bandwidths, and how much of the error is the bandwidths'?
- *Data* (clean stored audio, no channel; never gold): synthetic validation (`train_v2`'s held-out 2 %; the LF source's own H_k are exact), resynthesised Zeroth-Korean val and VocalSet val (H_k measured on the recovered source each clip was rendered from, by the same estimator; formants and bandwidths are the re-filter's poles, so exact). 400 / 600 / 600 clips drawn with seed 5053, up to 40 voiced sonorant frames each with finite F1–F4, B1–B4 and source H1–H4, three frames from each end dropped; clips from resynthesis shards whose files span two speakers are left out (they can't be grouped). Harmonics by `backend.harmonics` (least squares at k·f0, label f0).
- *Arms* (all correct F1–F4 plus upper poles from the apparent VTL, `correct(upper_from_vtl=True)`): raw (uncorrected); true F + true B (the estimator's floor); **predicted F + predicted B** (the deliverable); predicted F + Hawks–Miller B (common practice); predicted F + true B; true F + predicted B; and "full", the deliverable with the model's own f0 for the harmonic fits.
- *Models*: teacher v3.1 (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`) and its sibling seed (`d040_5b_s1_b20k`), for seed noise.
- *Measures*: per f0 band (`f0_band_labels`: < 150, 150–300, 300–500, > 500 Hz) and overall, median |error| and median error (dB, corrected − source), with 95 % bootstrap intervals over clips (synthetic) or speakers (resynthesised: 10 Zeroth, 5 VocalSet speakers, so those intervals are crude); paired shares, the median |error| of the deliverable minus that with the true bandwidths put back (bandwidth share), with the true formants put back (formant share), and with Hawks–Miller bandwidths instead (model against Hawks–Miller). Bands under 500 frames are flagged.
- *Floor, seen before registering* (labels only, no model output; `reports/analysis/iseli_alwan/iseli_alwan.txt` as first written): true F + B recovers H1\*–H2\* to a median 0.10 / 0.19 / 0.34 / 0.91 dB by band on synthetic clips (0.06–0.55 dB on the resynthesised sets), and H2\*–H4\* to 0.20 / 0.68 / 1.17 / 3.18 dB; uncorrected errors are 1–10 dB. So the measure resolves effects of about 1 dB below 500 Hz on H1\*–H2\*.
- *Flag* (decides the next GPU run's candidate, not a model): **bandwidth decoupling is flagged as the next full-size candidate** if, on synthetic validation for H1\*–H2\*, in any band of at least 500 frames, on both seeds, the bandwidth share is at least 1 dB with its interval above 0, or the model's bandwidths are no better than Hawks–Miller's (model against Hawks–Miller ≥ 0) in two or more bands on both seeds. The resynthesised sets are reported as the real-source check. The 1 dB is my choice: above the floor below 500 Hz, and small against the 5–10 dB between phonation types.
- *Script*: `scripts/analysis/d053_iseli_alwan.py` (`floor`, `score <ckpt> ...`, `report`); reports in `reports/analysis/iseli_alwan/`.
- *Result (2026-10-31 11:13; `reports/analysis/iseli_alwan/iseli_alwan.txt`, `.json`; both seeds agree to about 0.05 dB)*: **the flag is not raised**: bandwidths are not what limits the correction.
  - **Bandwidth share** of H1\*–H2\*'s error on synthetic clips: at most 0.08 dB in any band (intervals above 0, far under 1 dB); on the resynthesised sets at most 0.25 dB (VocalSet > 500 Hz). H2\*–H4\*: at most 0.06 dB synthetic, 0.21 dB resynthesised.
  - **Model against Hawks–Miller**: the model's bandwidths are better on synthetic clips in every band above 150 Hz (H1\*–H2\* −0.11 to −0.22 dB; H2\*–H4\* up to −0.86 dB above 500 Hz), and only about 0.01–0.05 dB better on the resynthesised sets: on real sources they add little over the formula, consistent with probe 12's prior reliance, but the correction barely depends on them.
  - **Formant share** carries the error: H1\*–H2\* 0.14 / 0.68 / 0.85–0.91 dB (150–300 / 300–500 / > 500 Hz) on synthetic clips, and 0.4–0.5 / 1.5–1.9 / 2.0–2.1 dB on the resynthesised sets. Deliverable median |error| H1\*–H2\*: synthetic 0.11 / 0.42 / 1.15 / 1.87 dB by band (floor 0.10 / 0.19 / 0.34 / 0.91), Zeroth 0.23 / 0.98 / 2.5, VocalSet 0.13 / 0.73 / 1.86 / 2.69; uncorrected 1–10 dB. Bias stays within ±0.2 dB except 300–500 Hz on the resynthesised sets (+0.2 to +0.6 dB).
  - The model's own f0 ("full") changes nothing (≤ 0.05 dB).
  - Reading: for M3's corrected measures the bandwidth heads' known defects (D-050) cost almost nothing, because H1 and H2 rarely sit on a formant's peak, where the bandwidth matters; F1's error at f0 above 300 Hz is the limit. Bandwidth decoupling is not justified by this use; it stays a candidate for the bandwidth outputs themselves.

## D-054 (2026-10-31, registered before any arm trained; a 60-step smoke run per arm was seen, for speed and memory only): a frequency-resolved read-out on teacher v3.1's frozen trunk

*Status*: **done 2026-10-31: not supported (futility stop)**; see Result. Hannah's choice (2026-10-31): test the trunk's bottleneck cheaply on a frozen trunk first, with FiLM. The formant heads read the pooled per-frame vector h (128-d), which reaches them only through one non-residual flatten (64 channels × 91 bins → 384 → 128); the heat maps are linear in h and the bandwidth head is one shared linear layer of 1,032 parameters. Probes 3, 11 and 12 (D-050) are consistent with a read-out that has lost local spectral detail: F1 follows the envelope peak, F1 under- or over-follows a uniform scaling, and the bandwidths mostly return their prior.

- *Question*: do teacher v3.1's frozen conv maps carry formant and bandwidth evidence that the flatten discards, so that a frequency-resolved read-out does better than heads on h?
- *Trunk* (frozen, all arms): `d040_5b_s2_b20k` (teacher v3.1): f0 network, comb, encoder, sonorant and VTL heads; only the formant and bandwidth heads train.
- *Arms* (`TeacherConfig.readout`; `scripts/analysis/d054_readout.py`):
  - **flat**: today's heads (cascaded linear F1–F4 heat maps, linear bandwidth head), fresh: 193 k parameters.
  - **mlp**: the same with one hidden layer of 512 (GELU): 1.09 M parameters, the capacity control.
  - **film** (`FreqReadout`): the coarsest conv map FiLM-modulated by h (a scale and shift per channel and frame, the same at every bin), upsampled along frequency to the VQT's 363 bins with U-Net skips from the finer conv maps (1×1 convs, a residual conv Block per resolution), one logit per bin per formant interpolated onto the formant axis, the cascade's soft mask as the flat heads'; bandwidth n read from the full-resolution features at formant n's expected position, beside h: 91 k parameters. More compute than flat or mlp, fewer parameters than either.
- *Training*: 5b's data mix and channel (train_v2 + 30 % resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth), 5b's mixed crop lengths and batch; AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 300 warm-up steps, cosine over **8,000 steps**; comb from the frozen f0 network throughout; head seeds 1 and 2 per arm (initialisation and data order). Six runs, about 7 h.
- *Measures* (each run's final checkpoint):
  1. Synthetic validation F1–F4 MAE (Hz; the trainer's `eval`), and resynthesised validation F1–F3 MAE (Zeroth, VocalSet; `eval_resynth`).
  2. Bandwidth error: median |cents| of predicted against true B1–B4 on D-053's frames (synthetic, Zeroth, VocalSet; `d053_iseli_alwan.py score`), by f0 band; D-053's corrected-measure arms come with it.
  3. D-050 probe 12 (bandwidth evidence: arm C's weight on the label) and probe 11 (tape speed: F1–F4 slopes, ideal 1).
  4. Dev: Hillenbrand F1 / F2 / F3 MAE (windowed, paired) and women's closed-phase F1 bias (PTDB-TUG); f0 and voicing are the frozen trunk's, identical in every arm.
  - Context, reported: film and mlp against flat, flat against v3.1's own (jointly trained) heads, and the spread between head seeds within each arm.
- *Rule* (film against **mlp**, seed means of the two head seeds): the frequency-resolved read-out is **supported** if (A) or (B) holds and no guard fails.
  - (A) formants: synthetic F1–F4 MAE lower by at least 5 % (mean over F1–F4 of the relative change), with both film seeds better than both mlp seeds on that mean;
  - (B) bandwidths: synthetic bandwidth error lower by at least 10 % (mean over B1–B4) and probe 12's weight on the label up by at least 0.05 (mean over B1–B4), both on both seeds;
  - guards (film − mlp): Hillenbrand dev F1 ≤ +4, F2 ≤ +9, F3 ≤ +20 Hz; women's closed-phase F1 |error| ≤ +5 Hz (D-040's margins, as `decide_d051.py` measures them); resynthesised F1–F3 MAE no more than 5 % worse.
  - Supported: a full-size registration follows (readout "film" trained end to end, against 5b). Not supported: no full-size run on this evidence; since the frozen conv maps were trained for the flat read-out, that is "not shown on v3.1's trunk", not "the bottleneck doesn't matter". The thresholds are mine; amendable before any arm's results are seen.
- *Amendment 1 (2026-10-31 ~16:00; Hannah agreed; seen beforehand: training losses of mlp seed 1 (finished) and film seed 1 to step 2,500, and their step-2,000 validation: synthetic F1 / F2 / F3 / F4 MAE mlp 22 / 42 / 60 / 90 Hz, film 52 / 101 / 146 / 186; formant loss film 2.73 against mlp 2.45, still falling slowly)*: **a futility stop**. When film seed 1 finishes, if its synthetic F1–F4 MAE (mean of the four) is more than 1.5 × mlp seed 1's, film seed 2 is not trained; the decision is then "not supported (futility stop)", and film seed 1 is still scored for the diagnosis (whether its error is formant-identity swaps or scatter). flat and mlp keep both seeds. Below 1.5 ×, the design runs as registered.
- *Code*: `TeacherConfig.readout` ("flat" default: earlier checkpoints load unchanged), `FreqReadout`, `Encoder.forward(maps=True)`; `TrainConfig.init_from` / `freeze` / `reinit` (a new run from another run's weights without its step or optimiser).
- *Result (2026-10-31 21:58; `reports/analysis/d054/decide_d054.txt`, `paired/w400/d054_*`, `reports/probes/*/d054_*`)*: **not supported (futility stop)**: film seed 1's synthetic F1–F4 MAE 42.9 / 78.9 / 116.4 / 160.1 Hz against mlp seed 1's 21.4 / 40.7 / 58.4 / 85.9 (1.93 ×); film seed 2 not trained. Scoring stopped once on probe 12's missing inputs (it reads the bwhold, counterfactual and bandwidth probes' reports; the chain now runs them first) and restarted before any result; seed 2's per-run scoring ran in parallel helpers (`chains/d054_helper.sh`).
  - **Heads on h are saturated**: flat retrained on the frozen trunk matches v3.1's own jointly trained heads within 1–2 % (synthetic F1–F4 22.0 / 41.3 / 59.8 / 86.9 against 21.7 / 40.7 / 58.7 / 86.3, both seeds alike); mlp, 5.6 × the parameters, gains about 1 % (21.4 / 40.7 / 58.4 / 85.9; Hillenbrand dev F1 −0.3 to −0.4, F3 +0.9 to +1.3 Hz flat against mlp). Whatever limits the formants is in h or before it, not in the heads.
  - **Bandwidths do not depend on the read-out at all**: synthetic B1–B4 error 133–155 cents, Zeroth 159–232, VocalSet 284–334, and probe 12's weight on the label 0.21 / 0.35 / 0.39 / 0.33, the same to within a few cents or 0.01 in every arm, film's position-gathered head included, and as v3.1's own head.
  - **Film's error is a clip-level tail, not swaps** (descriptive, D-053's frames): median errors only 10–40 % above mlp's (synthetic F1 30 against 21 cents), but 6.2 % of F1 frames off by more than 300 cents (mlp 1.1 %), clustered by clip (the worst clips 25–30 of 40 frames); fewer than a third of the gross errors are assignments to a neighbouring formant (1.0–1.7 % of frames). Errors correlate with mlp's at r 0.45–0.6 (F1–F2) on frames without swaps.
  - **On real voices film is closer than its synthetic error suggests**: Hillenbrand dev F1 +0.9 [−0.9, +2.9] Hz against mlp (within noise), F2 +6.3, F3 +17.2 (worse); women's closed-phase F1 bias +2.8 Hz against mlp's +4.2 (one seed; within the ±5 Hz power of dev); probe 11's Hillenbrand F1 scale slope 0.999 against 0.977–0.979 (ideal 1), PTDB-TUG 1.030 against 1.043–1.046: the per-bin read-out is nearer the equivariance it was built for.
  - Reading: the frozen-trunk test cannot show the bottleneck, since the heads already read h as well as heads can and film failed on clip-level misreads it was never given an anchor against. Candidates (`considering.md`, trunk structure): an absolute anchor (a log-frequency position channel, or the per-bin read-out as a residual on the flat logits), which keeps film's equivariance and real-voice F1 where the flat heads place the clip; or training the read-out end to end. Bandwidths need something other than a read-out change.
- *Follow-up (registered 2026-10-31 ~23:00, after the result; Hannah asked; reported only, the decision stands)*: film seed 2 on the frozen trunk after all, to see whether film seed 1's probe 11 F1 scale slopes (Hillenbrand 0.999, PTDB-TUG 1.030, against mlp's 0.977-0.979 and 1.043-1.046) are a property of the per-bin read-out or of one seed; scored as the other D-054 runs (`chains/d056.sh`).

## D-055 (2026-10-31, registered before rendering; descriptive, with one flag): D-050 probe 14, energy in the F1–F2 valley

*Status*: **done 2026-10-31: flag raised** (see Result). Hannah (2026-10-31) chose it as the next F1 lead (`f1_bias_register.md`, Open: "Energy between F1 and F2"; `considering.md`, Heads). Probe 3 found a ±6 dB peak at a fixed 1 kHz moves F1 by about ±12 cents in every model; a fixed frequency sits in different places relative to each vowel's formants, so this probe places the energy per frame from the exact F1 and F2.

- *Question*: do the models read energy in the F1–F2 valley as F1 evidence, with the formant peaks themselves unchanged? If they do, real voices with a fuller valley (breathier, less damped) would read F1 high, the direction of the women's overread.
- *Items*: probe 1's 60 resynthesised Zeroth-Korean val clips (10 speakers × 6; 6 recorded women, 4 men; numeric ids and recorded sex only), each the reference resynthesis (exact F and B labels) with a per-frame spectral gain (`counterfactual.valley`: zero-phase, 40 ms Hann STFT at the label hop; frames without F1 and F2 unchanged). The labels don't move, so every output should be unchanged (ideal 0).
- *Conditions* (one arm each): **peak** at F1 + p (F2 − F1) in log frequency, p 0.33 / 0.5 / 0.67, a Gaussian with SD 0.1 of the F1–F2 distance, ±6 dB at its centre; **fill**, +3 / +6 / −6 dB flat over the middle of the valley (from F1 + 0.25 d to F2 − 0.25 d, raised-cosine ramps of 0.15 d), 0.0 dB at F1 and F2 themselves (checked on noise); **null**, the same STFT path at 0 dB (checked: reconstructs exactly, so it should read exactly 0).
- *Models*: teacher v3.1 (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`) and its sibling seed (`d040_5b_s1_b20k`) decide the flag; reported: teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) and D-054's film seed 1 (the per-bin read-out).
- *Measures*: per arm, every output's change against the reference (cents; median over each clip's scored frames, then the 95 % speaker bootstrap over clips, as the other change arms); F1 also by recorded sex (6 / 4 speakers, so crude).
- *Noise floor*: the null arm (exact) and the speaker-bootstrap interval; probe 3's fixed 1 kHz ±6 dB gives the scale (about ±12 cents).
- *Flag*: **"valley read as F1 evidence"** if, on both 5b seeds, any +6 dB arm moves F1 by at least 10 cents (about 3–5 Hz at women's F1) with its interval excluding 0, in the same direction on both seeds. If raised, a follow-up (its own entry) compares the valley level of real women's voices (dev) with the synthesis prior's, and a training arm would only follow from that.
- *Probe*: `run.py`'s "valley" (render, score, the usual reports in `reports/probes/valley/`); cache `data/probes/valley_v1`.
- *Result (2026-10-31 22:28; `reports/probes/valley/`)*: **flag raised: the valley is read as F1 evidence**, from energy just above F1 rather than mid-valley. F1 change, cents (95 % speaker bootstrap), seeds 2 / 1:
  - **fill +6 dB: +11.0 [+7.1, +13.3] / +11.9 [+7.7, +15.5]** (the flag's arm); fill +3: +3.2 / +3.2; fill −6: −2.6 / −2.3 (smaller: taking energy out moves F1 less than adding it).
  - Peak a third of the way from F1 to F2: +6 dB +8.9 / +9.6, −6 dB −2.9 / −1.9; at half way −0.8 / −1.4 and two-thirds −0.5 / −0.8 (F2 moves −1.8 / −2.0 there). So it is energy on F1's upper side, not the valley's middle, that pulls F1 up; F2 is nearly untouched (≤ 2 cents in every arm).
  - By recorded sex (fill +6): women +12.4 / +13.7, men +7.5 / +8.1 (6 / 4 speakers; intervals overlap). About 4–5 Hz at women's F1, the size of today's women's closed-phase overread (+4.1 Hz for v3.1).
  - Reported: teacher v3 the same (fill +6 +11.8, peak33 +6 +8.8); D-054's film seed 1 too (fill +6 +8.6 [+4.2, +20.9], peak33 +6 +7.2): the per-bin read-out does not remove it. Null exactly 0 in every model.
  - Next, per the registration (its own entry): compare the level just above F1 (the lower third of the valley) in real women's voices on dev with the synthesis prior's and with the resynthesised training clips'. A fuller lower valley in real voices would make this a candidate mechanism for the overread; only then a training arm (e.g. wider bandwidths, aspiration or spectral detail in that region in synthesis).

## D-056 (2026-10-31, registered before any phase ran; a 40-step smoke run of each phase was seen, for function only): the anchored per-bin read-out, trained on the frozen branch point and then end to end in 5b's decay

*Status*: **done 2026-11-01: not a candidate** (non-inferior, no gain; see Result). Hannah's design (2026-10-31): the frozen-trunk schedule first, then unfreeze for the decay. D-054 found the heads on h saturated and the per-bin read-out ("film") failing on clip-level scale misreads, yet nearer probe 11's scale equivariance and reading women's closed-phase F1 lower (one seed). Anchoring it on the flat heads keeps their absolute frequency; training it end to end lets the trunk adapt to it.

- *Read-out* (`TeacherConfig.readout = "film_residual"`): the flat heads plus D-054's `FreqReadout`, whose formant logits and bandwidths are added to the flat heads'; its output layers start at zero, so the model starts exactly at the flat heads' outputs (checked: maximum difference 0.0).
- *Phase 1* (`scripts/analysis/d056_anchored.py`): on 5b seed s's frozen branch point (`runs/d040_5b_s{s}/ckpt_016000.pt`; trunk and flat heads frozen), only the read-out trains: AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 1,000 warm-up steps, then 3,000 at the full rate (D-054's recipe and data mix), head seed s.
- *Phase 2* (`va train teacher --branch-from runs/d040_5b_s{s}/ckpt_016000.pt --decay-steps 4000`, `--train-set readout=film_residual add_from=<phase 1> readout_lr=0.001`): 5b's own decay branch, everything unfrozen, with phase 1's read-out added in its own AdamW group at 1e-3 times the model's decay (outside Muon / Adan, as D-040's VTL head). The trunk at the branch is 5b's 16k trunk, and the branch has 5b's seed and step, so its paired control is **`d040_5b_s{s}_b20k`**: the same start, schedule and data seeds; the read-out is the only difference.
- *Seeds*: 2, then 1. About 1 h (phase 1) + 1.5-2 h (phase 2) each.
- *Measures* (against `d040_5b_s{s}_b20k`, per seed and seed means): synthetic F1-F4 MAE (last `eval`); resynthesised validation F1-F3 MAE; Hillenbrand dev F1 / F2 / F3 (windowed, paired); women's closed-phase F1 |error| and signed bias (PTDB-TUG dev); probe 11's F1 scale slopes (ideal 1) on Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG; probe 14's fill +6 dB F1 change (D-055); probe 12's weight on the label; f0 RPA. Phase 1 alone is reported as a frozen-trunk arm beside D-054's.
- *Rule* (seed means, both seeds in the same direction): the anchored read-out is **a candidate** (worth a full-size run or the learned-query decoder next) if it is non-inferior on D-040's margins (synthetic F1-F4 no worse than +2 SE, dev Hillenbrand F1 <= +4, F2 <= +9, F3 <= +20 Hz, women's closed-phase F1 |error| <= +5 Hz, RPA >= -0.1 point) **and** gains on at least one of: probe 11's F1 scale error |1 - slope| at least halved on both Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG; synthetic F1-F4 MAE lower by at least 3 %; women's closed-phase F1 |error| lower by at least 2 Hz (power-limited; reported with the paired speaker interval). Otherwise not a candidate. Seeds disagreeing: "not robust to the seed". It is not a teacher adoption: that would be its own decision. The thresholds are mine; amendable before any result is seen.
- *Code*: `readout="film_residual"`; `TrainConfig.readout` / `add_from` / `readout_lr` (a branch that changes the read-out, loads its new modules from another checkpoint and gives them their own AdamW group); chain `chains/d056.sh`.
- *Chain note*: the decision step first failed (07:29) because D-054's mlp seed 2, a reported-only row, had never had probe 14 (it predates D-055); the probe was run for it and the unchanged decision script rerun (07:31).
- *Result* (`reports/analysis/d056/decide_d056.txt`): **not a candidate.** Non-inferior on every margin (seed means: synthetic F1-F4 -0.5 / -0.7 / -0.7 / -0.8 SE, RPA +0.00, Hillenbrand dev F1 / F2 / F3 +0.3 / +0.2 / -0.1 Hz, women's closed-phase |error| +0.7 Hz), but no gain on either seed: synthetic F1-F4 MAE -1.9 % / -2.0 % (3 % needed); probe 11's F1 slopes unchanged (0.976 / 1.041 and 0.975 / 1.042 against 5b's 0.978 / 1.043 and 0.975 / 1.039); women's closed-phase |error| +0.7 / +0.6 Hz (worse, not lower); signed bias +3.7 / +4.1 against +4.3 / +4.1.
  - *Reported*: probe 14's fill +6 dB F1 change falls from +11.9 / +11.0 cents to +8.5 / +6.9 (less valley sensitivity); probe 12's weight on the label rises slightly (B1 0.22 / 0.24 against 0.20 / 0.21). Resynthesised F1-F3 within 1-2 Hz. Phase 1 (frozen trunk, read-out only): synthetic F1-F4 about 15-20 % worse than 5b's branch (24.8-25.0 Hz F1), women's closed-phase bias +4.4 / +6.5, F1 slopes 0.979 / 0.986 and 0.974 / 1.045: anchoring removes the per-bin read-out's clip-level misreads but also the scale equivariance and lower closed-phase bias D-054's unanchored arm showed.
  - *D-054 follow-up (film seed 2)*: as seed 1, synthetic F1-F4 about twice the flat heads' (40.9 / 79.8 / 114.5 / 160.7 Hz), F1 slopes near 1 (1.005 / 1.014), and women's closed-phase F1 bias **+0.1 Hz** against mlp seed 2's +4.2 (seed 1: +2.8 against +4.2); Hillenbrand dev against mlp seed 2 F1 -0.1, F2 +2.7, F3 +15.7 Hz.
