Both seeds now read the closed phase lower, but with the misreads that doubled synthetic error.
  - *Reading*: trained end to end on the flat heads, the read-out converges to the flat heads' behaviour (same slopes, same closed-phase bias) with a small synthetic gain. Whatever made D-054's free per-bin read-out read women's closed phase lower comes with its scale misreads, and anchoring removes both.

## D-057 (2026-10-31, registered before the synthetic and resynthesised sets were measured; seen beforehand: Hillenbrand dev women's median "low" level, −12.8 dB over 312 frames, while testing the loader; descriptive, with one flag): is the region just above F1 fuller in real voices than in our synthesis?

*Status*: **done 2026-10-31: flag not raised** (see Result). D-055's next step (Hannah, 2026-10-31: run it beside the GPU queue). D-055 showed v3.1 reads energy just above F1 as F1 evidence (+11 to +12 cents for +6 dB); it becomes a candidate cause of the women's overread only if real voices carry more there than training does.

- *Measure* (`scripts/analysis/d057_valley_level.py`; CPU): per voiced frame, the envelope of the harmonic amplitudes (`backend.harmonics`, 40 harmonics, interpolated linearly in log frequency); F1's level is the envelope's own peak within ±15 % of the frame's reference F1, so a misplaced reference cannot lower it; relative to that peak, **low** = the level a third of the way from F1 to F2 (log frequency; D-055's sensitive peak position) and **fill** = the mean over D-055's fill region (F1 + 0.25 d to F2 − 0.25 d).
- *Sets*: Hillenbrand dev (steady-state frames with their own f0, F1, F2; women and men); synthetic validation (`train_v2`'s held-out clips, exact labels, the clean stored audio; 600 clips, up to 20 frames each, seed 5057); resynthesised Zeroth val (exact labels, real source; recorded sex from AUDIO_INFO, numeric ids; 600 clips, shards spanning two speakers left out). All frames matched on f0 band (women 150–300 Hz, men 80–150 Hz), F1 300–1000 Hz, F2 / F1 ≥ 1.6. Bootstrap over speakers (Hillenbrand, Zeroth) or clips (synthetic), 2,000 resamples.
- *Reported*: medians per set, band and measure; real − synthetic and real − resynthesised differences with intervals, and the F1 shift they would imply at D-055's sensitivity (about 1.5 c/dB low, 1.9 c/dB fill; linear, rough). Resynthesis is the useful contrast: it has a real source and no overread (D-049).
- *Flag*: **"real lower valley fuller"** if, for women, the low measure's real − synthetic **and** real − resynthesised differences are both at least 2 dB with intervals above 0. Raised: a synthesis change in that region becomes a training candidate (its own entry), with D-055's sensitivity saying how much of the overread it could carry (about 6 dB would be all of it). Not raised: the valley mechanism is real but not what separates real voices from training.
- *Caveats*: Hillenbrand's references are LPC-based (the peak anchor limits their effect on F1's level, not on F2's position); the envelope between harmonics is interpolated, so at women's f0 the low point sits between one or two harmonics; recording channels differ (Hillenbrand's lab microphone, clean synthetic audio).
- *Result (2026-10-31 23:02; `reports/analysis/valley_level/`)*: **flag not raised**. Women (f0 150–300 Hz), level just above F1 relative to F1's peak: real (Hillenbrand dev) −12.8 [−13.8, −12.0] dB, synthetic −13.4 [−14.3, −12.7], resynthesised Zeroth −14.6 [−15.3, −13.5]; real − synthetic **+0.7 [−0.7, +1.8] dB**, real − resynthesised +1.8 [+0.2, +2.9]. The fill measure agrees (+0.7 [−1.2, +2.0] and +2.3 [+0.2, +3.6]). Men the same pattern (+0.6 and +1.3 dB low). At D-055's sensitivity these imply F1 shifts of about +1 cent against synthesis and +3 to +4 against resynthesis, against an overread of about 12 cents (+4 Hz): the valley mechanism is real (D-055) but real voices' valley is about as full as training's, so it carries at most a small part of the overread. The resynthesised clips' valley is the emptiest of the three, which fits D-049 (no overread on them) without explaining real voices' overread.

## D-058 (2026-11-01, registered before any model read a KL render; seen beforehand: KL's labels for Story's vowels against OPENGLOT II's Table 2, and one 220 Hz KL /a/ render's harmonic levels across gaps, both without a model): does the KL waveguide reproduce OPENGLOT Repository II's interaction effect?

*Status*: **done 2026-11-01: does not reproduce, by the registered rule** (women's paired growth +9.7 Hz against the +10 needed, interval through 0; see Result). The last step of making the KL waveguide a reference (`docs/considering.md`, KL entry; Hannah, 2026-11-01: "proceed with all the items"). OPENGLOT II (Story's physically modelled vowels with source-tract interaction) is the one external set where the F1 overread grows with the glottal gap (D-024 era: v2 / Conformer, male +10 / +11 / +22 and +24 / +26 / +40 Hz, female +28 / +22 / +60 and +58 / +63 / +87 at increasing vocal-process separation). If KL renders show the same growth for the same models, KL can stand in for it with labels, variety and the gap under our control; if not, KL is not yet used to judge interaction.

- *Seen before registering (no model)*: (1) KL's closed-glottis labels for `tube.STORY_VOWELS` a / i / u / ae at Story's size differ from OPENGLOT II's Table 2 by -10 to +19 % (/a/ F2 1304 against 1095 Hz), in both directions, while KL and `tube.poles` agree within 1 %: OPENGLOT II's vowels are other area functions (probably Story's MRI set), so resonances cannot be compared across the sets, only each set's error against its own labels. (2) At f0 220 Hz, a female-size /a/ (F1 898 Hz): gap 0 -> 0.3 raises H5 (just above F1) from -15.6 to -3.5 dB re H4, so the gap reaches the measured region.
- *KL arms* (`scripts/analysis/d058_kl_openglot.py`, CPU render): Story's vowels a, i, u, ae; male at Story's size (17.46 cm), female at 15.6 cm (areas scaled by size^2); OPENGLOT II's f0 grids (male 82 / 110 / 156 / 220, female 175 / 196 / 220 / 294 Hz), steady, 1 s; closed-phase gap 0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3 of the peak (`kl.glottal_area`; Rosenberg oq 0.6, sq 1.3; peak 0.12 cm^2 male, 0.07 female; pressure 8000 dyn/cm^2; glottis length and depth scaled with size); coupled, subglottal system on, no nose, no jitter or noise. 64 renders per sex. Labels: `kl.frame_labels` (the training definition).
- *Readers*: teacher v3.1 and its sibling (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`, `d040_5b_s1_b20k`), the seed mean reported. Also, in the same run, both on OPENGLOT II itself (dev only, evaluation only, fits nothing), so the comparison is like for like.
- *Measure*: per file, the median F1 over the middle (30-80 %) minus the label F1 (KL: its label; OPENGLOT: Table 2 fR1); per sex and gap arm, the median over files with a bootstrap interval over files (2000 resamples). Files where a model is more than 150 Hz off are left out (as `openglot2_seeds.py`), and counted.
- *Decision rule*: KL **reproduces** the effect if, for the seed mean, (1) in both sexes the F1 error at gap 0.3 minus gap 0 is at least +10 Hz with its paired interval above 0, and (2) for women the gap-0.3 error is within a factor of two of OPENGLOT II's widest-gap (large) error for the same models (ratio 0.5-2). Reproduces: KL counts as a reference for interaction (its own sets, with labels, can test F1-bias fixes); the KL entry moves out of considering. Partly (only 1): the direction is there but not the size; noted, and the gap mapping is the suspect (vocal-process separation is not a closed-phase area fraction). Neither: KL is not used to judge interaction until the difference is understood.
- *Caveats*: different area functions and glottis models (Story's kinematic folds against a Rosenberg area pulse with a gap), so only the trend and rough size are comparable; one speaker per sex.
- *Amendment 1 (2026-11-01 01:10, before any D-058 result was seen; Hannah wanted the tube clips beside KL and OPENGLOT)*: a third set, **reported only, outside the decision rule**: the same arms (vowels, sizes, f0 grids, gaps 0-0.3, Rosenberg oq 0.6 / sq 1.3, peak areas, pressure, glottis size) rendered by the training synthesiser's interactive source (`interactive.render` through `tract_responses` of the same Story shape, its own subglottal load, coupled), labelled `tube.poles` (F1 of the continuous shape), read by the same two models (`scripts/analysis/d058_tube.py`, `reports/analysis/d058/tube.txt`). It says whether the training-side interaction already shows the gap growth, or only the time-domain physics does.
- *Result (2026-11-01 01:04; `reports/analysis/d058/`: `d058.txt`, `per_file.tsv`, `tube.txt`, `tube_per_file.tsv`)*: **does not reproduce** by the rule. F1 error (seed mean of v3.1 s2 / s1, median over files), gap 0 / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3:
  - KL: men +13 / +15 / +39 / +52, women +26 / +25 / +55 / +66 Hz. Paired gap 0.3 minus 0: men **+10.3 [+4.3, +57.1]** (meets 1), women **+9.7 [-1.7, +33.8]** (misses 1). Women's widest error +66 against OPENGLOT II's +40, ratio 1.65 (meets 2). 3 of 224 files left out.
  - OPENGLOT II (same models): men +8 / +9 / +25, women +26 / +36 / +40 Hz at increasing separation: the earlier models' growth holds for v3.1.
  - Tube clips (amendment 1, reported only): men +12 / +14 / +31 / +40, women +33 / +27 / +43 / +61 Hz; paired men +21.5 [+6.9, +45.6], women +3.8 [-0.3, +34.7]. 6 of 128 left out.
  - Reading: the arm medians grow with the gap in all three sets, by similar amounts, and the gap-0 levels match OPENGLOT's narrowest (men about +10, women about +26). What fails is the women's paired median: at women's f0 several files read 80-120 Hz high at every gap (/i/ at 220 Hz, /ae/ at 294: F1 captured by a harmonic on a steady vowel), so their pairs change little and dilute the median, in KL and the tube clips alike; /a/ grows by +44 to +83 Hz at 175, 220 and 294 Hz. So KL is not yet admitted as an interaction reference under this rule; the KL entry stays in considering. Two things the rule did not ask but the data show: (1) teacher v3.1 overreads the training synthesiser's own interactive renders as the gap widens, within the training gap range (0-0.38 of the peak at oq 0.6), as much as it does KL; (2) KL adds nothing over the interactive tube for this effect (the same direction and size). A re-test that avoids harmonic capture (f0 varied or jittered within each file, so no harmonic sits on F1 throughout) would be its own entry.
- *Correction (2026-11-01, Hannah's question)*: v3.1 (and teacher v3) trained on `train_v2`, rendered before the interactive source existed (`p_interactive` absent, so 0): its tube clips are the tube's poles driven by LF through the cascade. The interactive source was only in the D-026 test sets (v2i / v2t / v2m / v2s / v2q). So the interactive tube clips here are not "the training synthesiser's own" renders, and the gap range 0-0.38 is the interactive sampler's, not one v3.1 saw. Reading (1) becomes: v3.1 overreads interactive-source renders as the gap widens, as on KL and OPENGLOT II, none of which it trained on. `interactive_return` (area return phase) was built 2026-10-16 but never rendered into any set.

## D-059 (2026-11-01, registered after a 2-clip smoke run of each stage, for function; seen: the measure medians of that smoke run, not any realness score): how real do the KL clips look to D-045's discriminators, beside the training synthesisers and OPENGLOT?

*Status*: done (2026-11-01): flag not raised; result below. Hannah (2026-11-01, after D-058): are the KL clips classified as more real, and how real does OPENGLOT look? D-045's discriminators separate train_v2 from real voices perfectly (AUC 1.0) and D_h's realness rises with f0 and breathiness; a synthesiser closer to real voices in what the encoder sees would be a training candidate.

- *Sets* (`scripts/analysis/d059_realness.py`; train_v2's synth config, 160 clips each, seed 5059): **kl** (`kl_set.generate`), **tube** (interactive source, p_interactive 1), **cascade** (p_tube 0); each scored **clean** and through teacher v3's channel augmentation (**channel**, fixed per clip, as D-045's cached train_v2 frames were). **OPENGLOT II** (96 files, channel 1) and **III** (channel 2, the radiated pressure) as they are: evaluation only, they fit nothing (dev tier). Up to 60 voiced, sonorant frames per clip or file (teacher v3's probabilities > 0.5).
- *Discriminators*: D-045's D_h (teacher v3's encoder output) and D_a (its interpretable measures), refitted from D-045's cached features with its own code (`cache/d045`: train_v2 against Zeroth test and speechocean762 adults, per sex, f0-reweighted); nothing scored here is fitted.
- *Measure*: realness = the discriminator's decision function (log-odds of real). Per set, sex and corpus: the median over frames, synthetic sets weighted to that sex's real f0 distribution (D-045's weights), with a 95 % bootstrap over clips / files (1000). OPENGLOT by its files' sex. Real and train_v2 rows are out-of-fold (5-fold, grouped), for scale.
- *Flag*: **"KL closer to real than the training synthesisers"** if, for women and D_h in both corpora, kl_channel's median realness exceeds both tube_channel's and cascade_channel's by at least 1 (log-odds) with the bootstrap interval of the difference above 0. Raised: KL clips as training data become a candidate (their own entry), with the measures saying in what. Not raised: reported.
- *Reported*: all rows for D_h and D_a; the 11 measures' medians per set (H1-H2, H1-A1, CPP, HNR, tilt, HF, jitter, shimmer, B1-B3), beside real and train_v2.
- *Caveats*: perfect separation means every synthetic set will sit on the synthetic side; only the shifts between sets are informative. Praat's HNR is -200 dB where undefined (as in D-045); medians are robust to a minority of those. Teacher v3 (not v3.1) provides h, as in D-045.
- *Result* (`reports/analysis/d059/d059.txt`): **flag not raised.** Women, D_h, kl_channel minus tube_channel: Zeroth +0.45 [-2.21, +3.96] (fails), speechocean762 +3.92 [+0.07, +7.70]; minus cascade_channel +2.82 [+0.06, +5.32] and +5.02 [+2.74, +6.73]. KL beats the cascade in both corpora but not the interactive tube on Zeroth.
  - *D_h, channel sets (log-odds; real out-of-fold +7 to +15, train_v2 -17 to -23)*: KL is the least synthetic-looking set in all four sex x corpus fits (women -16.9 Zeroth / -8.7 speechocean762; men -13.0 / -11.9), then the interactive tube (-17.3 / -12.8; -14.9 / -18.5), then the cascade (-19.6 / -13.7; -18.6 / -17.1). Every synthetic set stays deep on the synthetic side, as the caveat expected: the shifts are 2-7 log-odds against a real-synthetic gap of about 25-35.
  - *OPENGLOT*: on D_h's **real** side in every fit but one interval (II: women +3.7 / +9.2, men +0.8 [-0.9, +2.3] / +5.5; III: +2.6 to +4.7), and on D_a's real side except Zeroth men's. It is out of distribution for both classes (sustained vowels, HNR 27-40 dB, jitter and shimmer near 0), so this says the encoder's synthetic direction is not "physically modelled" as such, but something our synthesisers share and Story's model with its own source does not; not that OPENGLOT is realistic.
  - *Measures*: KL's spectra are flatter than real voices' (tilt -2.4 against -6.7 to -7.2 dB/oct; HF -17 against -28 to -35 dB; the interactive tube flatter still, -10), and its B1 is wider (96 Hz against 67-73; the tube's 88). The cascade is closer in tilt (-5.1) and HF (-37) but has B3 at 207 Hz. H1-H2 is 5-8 dB in every synthetic set against -0.5 to +1.1 real. (H1-A1's median is 0 wherever F1 is nearest H1 in most frames, by D-045's definition, not a fault.)
  - *Reading*: KL clips are somewhat more real to the encoder than both training synthesisers in most fits, but not reliably more than the interactive tube, and far from real. The measures point at the source (H1-H2, a too-flat spectrum on the physically coupled renders; whether the flow's closure, the waveguide's high-frequency losses or the radiation is untraced), more than the tract's resonances. No training entry from this.
- *Correction (2026-11-01)*: "the training synthesisers" in the flag and reading is wrong for the tube set: `tube` here is the interactive source (`p_interactive` 1), which no teacher trained on; train_v2's own tube path (tube poles, LF through the cascade) was not scored as its own set (it is part of the train_v2 out-of-fold row). The comparison is KL against the interactive tube (a D-026 test synthesiser) and the cascade. D-061 adds that the flat spectra are on the interactive paths only, so they are not in train_v2.

## D-060 (2026-11-01, registered after a noise pilot, seen and reported below, whose files are not reused): does the F1 overread grow with the glottal gap on KL renders once harmonic capture is broken?

*Status*: done (2026-11-01): **grows** (the corrected run, amendment 1); KL admitted as an interaction reference. D-058's follow-up (Hannah, 2026-11-01: would a higher-powered probe decide it?). D-058's women's paired test (+9.7 [-1.7, +33.8] Hz) was diluted by steady-vowel files whose F1 sat on a harmonic at every gap; its arm medians grew by about +40 Hz.

- *Renders* (`scripts/analysis/d060_kl_gap.py main`): as D-058 (male at Story's size, female 15.6 cm, Rosenberg oq 0.6 / sq 1.3, peak 0.12 / 0.07 cm^2, 8000 dyn/cm^2, coupled with the subglottal system, no nose or noise; labels `kl.frame_labels`) with vibrato of 1 semitone at 5 Hz, so harmonics sweep across F1; all ten of Story's vowels; f0 male 85 / 98 / 110 / 124 / 139 / 156 / 175 / 196, female 175 / 185 / 196 / 208 / 220 / 247 / 262 / 294 Hz; gaps 0 / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3. 80 files per sex and gap.
- *Readers*: teacher v3.1 seeds 2 and 1 (`d040_5b_s{2,1}_b20k`), seed mean.
- *Measure*: per file, the median over frames in the middle 30-80 % that the model calls voiced of F1 minus the label; paired gap 0.3 minus gap 0 per (vowel, f0); median over files with a 2000-resample bootstrap.
- *Rule*: **grows** if in both sexes the paired median is at least +10 Hz with its interval above 0; **does not grow** if in both sexes the interval's upper end is below +10; otherwise **undecided**. Grows: KL (and, by D-058's amendment, the interactive tube) carries OPENGLOT II's interaction effect, and KL is admitted as an interaction reference despite D-058's narrow miss; with D-058 this also says v3.1 overreads its own synthesiser's interactive renders as the gap widens, a lead for the register. Does not grow: D-058's arm-median growth was carried by a few vowels; reported. Undecided: reported, no further power without a new entry.
- *Pilot (seen; `reports/analysis/d060/pilot.txt`)*: vowels A, i, u at f0 92 / 131 (male) and 180 / 233 Hz (female), gaps 0 and 0.3. Paired gap 0.3 - 0: men +13.8 [-1.6, +38.4] Hz (half the files within 5 Hz), women +14.0 [+6.1, +31.0] (17 % within 5 Hz). With 80 files instead of 6, the interval should narrow to roughly +-10 Hz, enough to separate the outcomes unless the effect sits near the threshold.
- *Amendment 1 (2026-11-01, after the main result was seen)*: the main run (`reports/analysis/d060/main.txt`, kept as the record) said GROWS, but its render cache named files by vowel and Windows names ignore case, so I / i, O / o and U / u shared one file each (whichever rendered first: i, O, U, each with its own label). It covered 7 distinct vowels, 3 of them counted twice: 56 distinct pairs per sex, not 80. Without the duplicates the rule still holds (men +30.4 [+13.4, +37.0], women +17.5 [+10.1, +23.5] Hz), but the registered design is ten vowels. Fix: case-safe cache names in a fresh cache (`runs/d060_main_cs`); everything re-rendered and rerun into `reports/analysis/d060/fixed/`, rule unchanged. The rerun decides. D-058 and the pilot used no colliding names.
- *Result* (`reports/analysis/d060/fixed/main.txt`; 640 distinct renders): **GROWS.** Paired gap 0.3 - 0: men +12.9 [+7.8, +21.5] Hz, women +12.0 [+6.8, +18.8] (about a fifth of files within 5 Hz, against half in D-058). Medians by gap 0 / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3: men +23 / +24 / +35 / +41, women +30 / +31 / +41 / +50 Hz; flat to 0.1, then rising. By vowel (mean paired, women / men): open and back-open vowels carry it (A +44 / +37, V +45 / +48, O +37 / +51, ae +20 / +33); front and close vowels move little (i +6 / +7, I +2 / 0, E +2 / +11, u +12 / +10, o +12 / +4) and U falls (-15 / -9). The flawed run's larger numbers came from counting O twice.
  - *Consequences*: KL is admitted as an interaction reference (`docs/considering.md`). With D-058, v3.1 overreads F1 more as the closed-phase gap widens, on physically coupled renders of known resonances, most where F1 is high; the women's overread on real voices could include this if their closed phases leak more than the training clips' (D-052's longer open phases point the same way). Not yet tested: whether real women's gaps are wide enough, and whether training on gapped coupled renders removes it; each its own entry. F1 register row.

## D-061 (2026-11-01, registered before anything ran): H1-H2 with and without formant correction, by source parameter, and the real recordings' low-frequency roll-off

*Status*: registered; descriptive, with pre-stated readings. Hannah (2026-11-01, after D-059): does our source read as pressed? D-059's measures are mixed: H1-H2 is 5-8 dB on every synthetic set against -0.5 to +1.1 real (breathy side), while KL's and the interactive tube's spectra are much flatter than real voices' (pressed side). Two confounds before reading it as a source fault: H1-H2 is not formant-corrected (F1 near H2, as at women's f0, lowers it), and real recordings' microphones and pipelines may roll H1 off.

- *Synthetic frames* (`scripts/analysis/d061_source.py labels`, then `measure`): D-059's 160 clips per set (kl, tube, cascade) regenerated with their labels and meta (same seed and calls; audio checked equal to D-059's saved clips, any mismatch reported). Up to 60 frames per clip where the labels say voiced, not whispered, f0 > 0 and F1-F3 / B1-B3 finite and not mixed; 20 ms clear of the ends. Clean and through teacher v3's channel augmentation (D-059's fixed per-clip draws).
- *Measures*: H1-H2 and tilt from D-045's `measures` (unchanged), at those frames with the label f0 and F1. **H1\*-H2\*** (Iseli & Alwan 2004): H1-H2 minus the all-pole tract's level difference between f0 and 2 f0, summed over F1-F3 with their bandwidths (each resonance normalised to 0 dB at DC). Two versions: **exact** (label F, B) and **teacher** (teacher v3's F1-F3, B1-B3 at the nearest frame), the latter being all that real voices allow.
- *Real frames*: D-045's cached Zeroth test and speechocean762 adult frames (`cache/d045`), which carry H1-H2 with teacher v3's f0, F and B: teacher-corrected H1\*-H2\* per sex.
- *Ground truth*: cascade clips' LF source is labelled per frame (`source_h_db`, the flow derivative's harmonics), so on clean cascade frames exact H1\*-H2\* can be checked against the true H1-H2 of the source.
- *Roll-off* (`rolloff`): long-term spectra of the quietest 10 % of 32 ms frames (pauses: the room and the recording chain) in up to 3 files per speaker, Zeroth test and speechocean762 adults, and in the synthetic channel clips; levels in 30-60, 60-100 and 100-150 Hz relative to 300-600 Hz. Also the same ratios over the loudest 50 % of frames.
- *Readings (pre-stated)*:
  1. *Correction valid*: on clean cascade frames, median |exact H1\*-H2\* minus source truth| <= 1.5 dB. If not, the difference is traced before anything else is read.
  2. *Teacher correction usable*: on synthetic channel frames, per f0 bin (80-130, 130-180, 180-250, 250-350 Hz), the median of teacher minus exact H1\*-H2\*; real voices' H1\*-H2\* is read only in bins where its magnitude is <= 2 dB.
  3. *Source gap after correction*: in readable bins, if the synthetic channel sets' teacher-corrected H1\*-H2\* exceeds real voices' by >= 3 dB in both corpora, our source's H1 is too strong after formant correction (a source fault, not the measure).
  4. *Roll-off*: if the real pause spectra's 60-100 Hz level (re 300-600 Hz) is >= 10 dB below the synthetic channel clips' in both corpora, the channel model lacks the real recordings' low-end roll-off (a channel candidate, its own entry).
  5. *Parameter split* (reported): H1-H2, exact H1\*-H2\* and tilt by Rd tertile per set, and on the tube and KL clips by pulse type (LF-derived or Rosenberg) and area speed-quotient tertile.
- *Caveats*: the pause spectrum shows the chain only where the room noise reaches low frequencies; files with digital silence give nothing (reported as counts). Iseli-Alwan's correction ignores nasal zeros and extra poles / zeros; KL's nasalised frames with mixed labels are already excluded.
- *Smoke run (2026-11-01, seen, before the full run)*: two clips per set through every stage, for function. Its roll-off stage read every real file the full run will (same seed and files), so reading 4's real side is seen: pause spectra 60-100 Hz re 300-600 Hz, Zeroth -0.6 / +2.0 dB (women / men), speechocean762 +6.2 / +5.1, against about -20 on two synthetic channel clips per set. Nothing in the design or readings changed.
- *Result (2026-11-01; `reports/analysis/d061/d061.txt`)*: regenerated audio identical to D-059's clips in all three sets.
  1. *Correction valid*: on clean cascade frames exact H1\*-H2\* is within 0.61 dB (median |difference|) of the labelled source's H1-H2, median -0.29 [-0.39, -0.22].
  2. *Teacher correction usable* in every f0 bin: teacher minus exact -0.03 / -0.02 / -0.12 / -0.21 dB (80-130 ... 250-350 Hz); worst KL at 250-350 Hz, -0.66.
  3. *No source gap after correction*: in every bin the lowest synthetic set's teacher-corrected H1\*-H2\* is within -3.5 to +4.2 dB of real voices', with mixed signs (180-250 Hz: synthetic +7.6 to +8.6, real Zeroth women +8.9, speechocean762 women +8.1). Even the uncorrected H1-H2 differs by only about 1-4 dB within an f0 bin. D-059's 5-8 dB synthetic H1-H2 against about 0 real was mostly the sets' different f0 distributions (unweighted medians), not the source.
  4. *No roll-off*: real pause spectra at 60-100 Hz are +2.8 (Zeroth) and +7.2 dB (speechocean762) above the synthetic channel clips', not below. (The full run's synthetic pause level, -1.4 dB, differs from the two smoke clips' -20: many clips have no pause, so their quietest frames are voiced; 32-35 per set are digitally silent.)
  5. *Parameter split* (clean): H1\*-H2\* follows Rd in every set (lowest to highest tertile: KL +0.4 to +11.9, tube -0.7 to +10.3, cascade -0.3 to +14.5 dB); the area speed quotient matters little. Tilt does not follow everywhere: the cascade steepens with Rd (-3.9 to -5.9 dB/oct), KL somewhat (-0.8 to -4.0; its LF-derived pulses -0.1 against Rosenberg -3.2), the interactive tube not at all (-0.3, -0.4, +0.6).
  - *Reading*: H1-H2 is not a source fault. The flat spectra are, on the interactive sources only: their closure sharpness does not follow Rd as LF's does in the cascade, so breathy (high-Rd) interactive frames keep a sharp closure. That is the decoupling Hannah suspected, but on the interactive path, not the open phase. A candidate for its own entry: an interactive return phase tied to Rd (`interactive_return` exists, off in train_v2).

## D-062 (2026-11-01, registered after an 8-clip pilot per engine, seen and reported below; probe, descriptive): the area return phase and the aspiration's spectrum on the interactive paths, 2 x 2 per engine

*Status*: done (2026-11-01): the return phase does not tie tilt to Rd (reading 1: no, every cell); D-048's aspiration steepens tilt and raises realness; only KL return + D-048 matches real tilt. Result below. D-061 found the interactive paths' spectra flat and not following Rd (the interactive tube's tilt -0.3 / -0.4 / +0.6 dB/oct by Rd tertile; KL's partly), against the cascade's LF (-3.9 to -5.9). Hannah (2026-11-01): test the area return phase with Rosenberg-C pulses only, all four arms per synthesiser since it is a probe. No training follows from it directly.

- *Pilot (seen; 8 clips per engine, seed 7062, every 5th voiced frame; tilt dB/oct by Rd tertile 0.4 / 0.95 / 2.3, corner -> return)*: with the aspiration as it is, KL -0.7 -> -0.8, -2.6 -> -3.8, -5.4 -> -6.0, the tube +1.3 -> +1.2, +3.7 -> +3.6, +1.0 -> +0.8; with no aspiration at all, KL -1.1 -> -1.2, -2.6 -> -4.5, -5.9 -> -11.1, the tube -0.6 -> -1.4, +0.1 -> -0.9, -5.9 -> -10.8. So the return phase steepens breathy frames by about 5 dB/oct, and the aspiration hides it. Tracing the paths: the cascade's aspiration is the first difference of white noise as its (radiated) source, +6 dB/oct before the tract; the interactive tube's is the same noise as unradiated flow into U_lips / U_glottis (equivalent, +6); KL's is that noise as flow, radiated by the waveguide (+12 dB/oct, one difference brighter; its whisper too). D-048 matched real women's aspiration profile at -6 dB/oct.
- *Code* (defaults reproduce every earlier set; checked bit-identical on D-059's kl, tube and cascade clips, synthesis tests pass): `KLSetConfig.area_return` (Rosenberg pulses close by the exponential area return at LF's Ta for the clip's Rd, `interactive.area_pulse` ret; no draw); `asp_tilt_db_oct` now reaches the interactive tube (`interactive.synthesize`) and KL (`kl_set`) with the cascade's meaning, the radiated source's tilt before the tract (0 = white; KL's flow noise gets t - 6 since the waveguide radiates it); `voice.tilted_noise` factored out of `voice.synthesize` (cascade unchanged).
- *Arms* (`scripts/analysis/d062_source.py`; train_v2's synth config as D-059, 160 clips per arm, seed 5062): per engine, **KL** (`kl_set.generate`, `p_lf_pulse` 0: Rosenberg-C only, p_tube 1) and **tube** (`sampler.generate`, p_tube 1, p_interactive 1; its pulses are already Rosenberg-C only), closure **corner** (today's) or **return**, aspiration **as is** (`asp_tilt_db_oct` None) or **D-048** (-6). Eight arms; within an engine the four share every draw (the options add none), so clips and frames pair across arms.
- *Measures*: as D-061 on clean audio at label frames (voiced, not whispered, F1-F3 / B1-B3 finite and unmixed; up to 60 per clip, the same frames in every arm of an engine): tilt, H1-H2 and exact H1\*-H2\*, by Rd tertile (tertile edges from the engine's corner / as-is arm). Through teacher v3's channel augmentation (D-059's fixed per-clip draws): tilt and teacher-corrected H1\*-H2\* at those frames, against D-045's real frames (Zeroth test, speechocean762 adults; tilt median over all their frames, per sex); and D-059's realness (D-045's D_h and D_a refitted, frames by teacher v3, median f0-weighted per real sex).
- *Readings (pre-stated; paired 1000-resample bootstrap over clips)*:
  1. *Return ties tilt to Rd*: per engine and aspiration level, the return arm's tilt slope across Rd (top minus bottom tertile median, clean) minus the corner arm's is <= -2 dB/oct with its interval below 0.
  2. *Aspiration*: per engine and closure, the D-048 arm's clean tilt median minus the as-is arm's, overall and by Rd tertile: reported.
  3. *Tilt against real voices*: each arm's channel tilt median against real voices' per corpus; an arm "matches" if within 1.5 dB/oct of both corpora's median (both sexes pooled; per sex reported).
  4. *Realness*: each arm's D_h (women, both corpora) minus its engine's corner / as-is arm; "closer to real" if >= 1 log-odds with the interval above 0 in both corpora. Reported; a training set built from any arm would be its own entry.
- *Caveats*: tilt is a regression over 100 Hz-4 kHz and carries the formants; the tube's output here has no extra poles and zeros either way. One aspiration tilt (D-048's) is tried, not a fit. KL renders about 5 s of CPU per second of audio.
- *Smoke run (seen, for function)*: two clips per arm through every stage.
- *Result (2026-11-01; `reports/analysis/d062/d062.txt`; Rd tertile edges 1.17 / 1.83)*:
  1. *Return phase and Rd*: **no** in every cell. Return minus corner, tilt slope across Rd (T3 - T1, clean): KL -0.59 [-1.18, -0.11] (as is), -1.44 [-2.08, -0.92] (D-048); tube +0.78 [+0.15, +1.13], -0.54 [-0.91, -0.03]. The pilot's -5 dB/oct at high Rd (no aspiration, 8 clips, wider Rd range) does not survive the sampled aspiration even at D-048's level. The return phase does steepen KL overall (about -0.8 to -2 dB/oct by tertile), little on the tube.
  2. *Aspiration*: D-048 minus as-is, clean tilt: KL -1.0 [-1.3, -0.8] (corner) and -2.0 [-2.4, -1.6] (return), against an 18 dB/oct change of the noise; tube -4.3 [-5.2, -3.3] and -4.6 [-5.7, -3.6] for a 12 dB/oct change, most at high Rd (-6 to -7). With D-048 the tube's tilt follows Rd no worse than KL's (T3 - T1 -0.7 / -1.2).
  3. *Against real voices* (channel; real Zeroth -7.2, speechocean762 -6.7): only **KL return + D-048** matches (-5.8 [-6.6, -5.1]); the tube's best is return + D-048, -5.3 (1.9 short of Zeroth). Every as-is arm is -2.1 to -4.5.
  4. *Realness* (D_h, minus the engine's corner / as-is arm): D-048's aspiration raises it in both corpora for women, **closer to real** by the rule for KL corner (+2.7 [+1.6, +4.1] Zeroth, +3.0 [+1.4, +5.0] speechocean762), KL return (+2.3, +2.0) and tube corner (+2.3, +3.8); tube return + D-048 not (+0.4, +0.6). For men D-048 helps with the corner (KL +3.7 / +7.5, tube +1.8 / +5.3) but not with the return. The return phase alone **lowers** realness everywhere (-1.0 to -4.8), and D_a agrees in direction (smaller).
  - *Reading*: the aspiration's spectrum, not the closure, is what made the interactive paths flat and less real; D-048's -6 dB/oct helps on both engines and both measures. The return phase brings KL's tilt to real voices' level but reads as less real to the encoder, so what it changes is not what separates synthetic from real; it is not a candidate as is. KL's own aspiration (+12) costs it least in tilt (1 dB/oct) but realness as much as the tube's. D-063 measures the noise's slope from real /h/ instead of taking D-048's.

- *Notes (2026-11-01, written with D-064, as D-063 promised)*: (1) D-048's aspiration shape boosts the low frequencies, so after HNR normalisation its high-frequency aspiration is lower too - part of what moved the slope; (2) KL's default flow noise is the first difference as flow, so +12 dB/oct radiated against +6 elsewhere (it was a copy of the tube's noise line), and the tilt switch has a below-500 Hz mismatch: the whisper floor is 500 Hz, the aspiration's is 50 Hz, so one number gives two different low-frequency shapes.

## D-063 (2026-11-01, registered before any /h/ spectrum was measured; seen: MFA label counts of word-initial ㅎ / h in the Zeroth cache and speechocean762, for feasibility): the spectral slope of /h/ noise in real speech, and the noise tilt that reproduces it in each engine

*Status*: done (2026-11-01); see Result: the mapping holds only for the cascade (channel), and the synthetic side was not stratified by tract size, so the per-sex tilts are not yet usable. Hannah (2026-11-01): instead of fitting the noise tilt so the composite spectrum matches real voices, measure the noise itself on aligned /h/, stratified by age and gender, and let the final slope come out as it does. D-062 showed the aspiration's spectrum drives the interactive paths' tilt and realness; D-048's -6 dB/oct was matched on a voiced-frame profile, not on the noise directly.

- *Real segments* (`scripts/analysis/d063_h_noise.py audit`; the label `h` on a word spelled with initial ㅎ / h (not wh), word-initial, >= 60 ms, before a non-high vowel; **strict**: after >= 100 ms with no word; **relaxed**: without that, reported): **Zeroth-Korean train** speakers (the minimal-pairs MFA cache, korean_mfa; D-045's ten test speakers excluded; recorded sex from AUDIO_INFO's SEX column, numeric ids, never names); **Common Voice 27.0 Korean** validated (1,773 clips, 114 speakers as numeric indices, no client ids kept; aligned here with MFA 3.4.2, korean_mfa and minimal-pairs' patched dictionary, `--no_tokenization` as minimal-pairs does; age band and gender); **speechocean762 children** (< 18, train and test splits; the existing english_mfa alignments; age bands 5-10 and 11-17). speechocean762 adults are D-045's real side and are not used. The label restriction keeps ㅎ's other allophones out (MFA labels them `ç`, `ɸʷ`, `ɸ`, `x`, `ɦ`: palatal, bilabial, velar and voiced), and the minimal-pairs dictionary audit (issue #1) has not yet checked ㅎ, so the audit stage counts every label of spelled ㅎ / h.
- *Measure* (identical for real and synthetic): the mean power spectrum of a segment's middle 60 % (32 ms Hann frames, 8 ms hop; frames teacher v3 calls voiced at p >= 0.2, or within 15 dB of the file's pause floor, dropped); corrected by the all-pole tract of the following vowel (teacher v3's F1-F3 / B1-B3, median over voiced frames in the 60 ms after the segment; D-061's correction, which held to 0.6 dB on voiced frames); slope = least-squares dB per octave over **1-5 kHz** (below 1 kHz the open glottis's coupling makes the vowel's tract a poor model). Also the uncorrected slope, and the residual peak (smoothed corrected spectrum above its line, 1-3 kHz).
- *Per stratum*: the median over speakers of speaker medians, a 1000-resample bootstrap over speakers, and the SD across speakers (for a sampler draw). Strata: Zeroth by sex; Common Voice by gender x age band; speechocean762 children by sex x age band; pooled Korean adults by sex.
- *Synthetic* (`synth`): train_v2's synth config with `p_h_onset` 1, 48 clips per engine and tilt, seed 5063; engines **cascade** (p_tube 0), **tube** (interactive, corner) and **KL** (Rosenberg-C, corner); noise tilt `asp_tilt_db_oct` = `whisper_tilt_db_oct` (new switch, all three engines, defaults unchanged) as is, 0, -6, -12. Each clip's `h_onset` segments (meta) >= 60 ms, clean and through D-059's channel draws, measured as above.
- *Readings (pre-stated)*:
  1. *Label audit*: the share of spelled ㅎ / h labelled `h`, and each other label; reported. Errors traceable to the dictionary go to minimal-pairs issue #1 as a comment.
  2. *Mapping valid*: per engine, the channel slope of the tilts 0 / -6 / -12 is linear in the tilt with gradient 0.7-1.3 and R^2 >= 0.9; otherwise the tract correction does not isolate the noise in that engine and its fitted tilt is not read.
  3. *Fitted tilt*: per valid engine and stratum, the tilt whose synthetic channel slope equals the stratum's strict median (and at its interval's ends), with the speaker SD alongside.
  4. *Children*: their /h/ is used only if their median residual peak is within 3 dB of the Korean adults' (Mandarin L1 speakers may produce /h/ as velar [x], whose noise source is above the glottis); otherwise reported, not used.
  5. Reported: Zeroth against Common Voice adults of the same sex (corpus and channel effect); the fitted tilts against D-048's -6.
- *Next* (its own entry): render the engines with the fitted tilts (per sampler speaker group, with the speaker SD as a draw) and read the composite tilt against real voices as an untuned check, with D-062's measures and realness.
- *Caveats*: Korean and L2-English /h/; an /h/'s glottis is wider than voiced speech's aspiration and its noise may be brighter; the correction uses the following vowel's tract (an /h/ anticipates it, not exactly); real channels are not removed (the synthetic side gets the channel model); Common Voice women are nearly all in their twenties.
- *Run notes*: the first real stage was stopped after 25 min (MIOpen searched kernels for every file length, ~6 s per file); rerun with the teacher's input padded to whole seconds and `MIOPEN_FIND_MODE=FAST` (same segments on the smoke files). Smoke run of every stage seen, for function.
- *Result (2026-11-01; `reports/analysis/d063/`)*:
  1. *Labels*: spelled ㅎ word-initial is `h` in 62 % (Zeroth) and 67 % (Common Voice), the rest the expected allophones (`ɸʷ`, `ç`, `x`, `ɸ`, `ɦ`); 5 stop labels in 22,500 words. speechocean762 children's spelled h: `h` 57 %, `ç` 40 %. Nothing new for minimal-pairs issue #1 from the word-initial audit; a dictionary check of the word-medial ㅎ rules found 283 words with no correct variant (posted to the issue).
  - *Real slopes (strict, tract-corrected)*: Zeroth women +13.5 [+12.4, +14.6] (63 speakers), men +21.5 [+19.6, +22.7] (40); speechocean762 children +21.0 [+19.1, +22.6] (60); speaker SD 3.6-5.2. Common Voice strata have 2-7 speakers (cv women twenties +18.1, men twenties +25.6, thirties +27.4) and do not support an age split. Uncorrected slopes are -10.6 (Zeroth women), -11.2 (men), -4.3 (children). The corrected values are far above any plausible source slope (+6 for radiated white flow noise): the three-formant all-pole correction subtracts too much at high frequencies (F4 / F5 not modelled, an /h/'s formants damped by the open glottis), so the absolute values only mean anything against the synthetic side through the same pipeline.
  2. *Mapping*: valid only for the **cascade** with the channel (gradient 0.85, R^2 0.974), though its -6 and -12 points rest on 8 and 1 segments (steep-tilt /h/ falls under the pause-floor criterion). Tube 0.83 (R^2 0.859) and KL 0.69 (0.813): **not 1:1** with the channel; both valid clean (1.04, 0.87).
  3. *Fitted tilt* (cascade, channel): women -1.0 [-2.3, +0.2], men +9.1 [+7.0, +10.8], children +7.8 [+5.6, +9.6] dB/oct, against the as-is first difference (about +6) and D-048's -6.
  4. *Children*: residual peak +14.7 against the Korean adults' +15.8 dB: glottal-like, used.
  - *Flaw found in reading*: the synthetic clips mix every tract size (train_v2's length groups), while the real targets are per sex. The correction's error depends on where F4 / F5 fall, so it differs between men's, women's and children's tracts, and the pooled mapping cannot remove it per stratum: the 10 dB/oct gap between the women's and men's fitted tilts is at least partly the correction, not the source. Before a rendering entry: an amendment (its own registration) mapping per synthetic length group, with more clips and a pause floor that keeps steep-tilt /h/.
- *State of the follow-up (D-064, registered below; note 2026-11-01, paused for D-065)*:
  - *Planned design (Hannah agreed)*: synthetic clips with `length_dist="population"` and `p_length_from_f0=0`, mapped per length group (women / men / children to Zeroth women / Zeroth men / speechocean762 children; "cover" left out); about 150 clips per engine x tilt; tilts as-is, +6, 0, -6, -12; primary band 1-3.5 kHz (less of F4 / F5 in the correction), 1-5 kHz reported; D-063's validity rule (gradient 0.7-1.3, R^2 >= 0.9) per engine x group, channel primary; a new script `d064_h_noise.py` reusing D-063's helpers.
  - *Done (7c77891)*: `whisper_tilt_db_oct` redefined as a shape-only switch: one `voice.whisper_noise` for all three engines, flat below 500 Hz (`WHISPER_FLAT_BELOW_HZ`; `tilted_noise` gained `flat_below_hz`, aspiration keeps 50 Hz) and power-normalised to the default noise before the tract. D-063's -6 / -12 points used the old definition (50 Hz floor, no normalisation), so the steep-tilt /h/ was louder than the vowel and read as voiced: the cause of the drop-outs. Defaults bit-identical (D-059's clips reproduce).
  - *Open problem*: normalising at the source is not enough. /h/ level against the clip's loudest 5 % (8 clips each, `scripts/analysis/d064_level.py`), as-is / -12 / +6 dB/oct: cascade -23 / -4 / -25, tube -32 / -36 / -33, KL -31 / -47 / -31 dB. Each tract weights a low-frequency-heavy noise differently (the cascade's filter boosts the F1 region; KL radiates flow noise at t - 6, losing low frequencies). Next step: set the /h/ (and whisper) level after the tract, matched to the as-is noise's output level in the measurement band per clip, recheck with `scripts/analysis/d064_level.py`, then register D-064.
  - *Notes still to add when D-064 is registered*: under D-062, D-048's aspiration shape boosts the low frequencies, so after HNR normalisation its high-frequency aspiration is lower too (part of what moved the slope); under KL, its default flow noise is +12 dB/oct radiated against +6 elsewhere (a copy of the tube's noise line), and the switch's below-500 Hz +6 mismatch.
  - Korean MFA problems found on the way go to minimal-pairs issue #1 (word-medial ㅎ rules already posted).
  - *Registered 2026-11-01 (below, `## D-064`)*: the level policy is fixed (after the tract, broadband, the anchor the HNR holds) and the noise path is one module; the tilt -> real-slope arm this note planned is still unregistered, and it now has a known floor problem at steep tilts (the cascade loses 14.6 dB in 1-3.5 kHz at -12 dB/oct).

## D-065 (2026-11-01, registered before any arm trained; seen: a 20-step smoke run of each option, for function, and the decision script's check on D-051's files): two full-size arms against 5b, bandwidths decoupled from the formants, and a wider training level range

*Status*: registered; chains `scripts/analysis/chains/d065.sh` (training) and `d065_score.sh` (scoring and decision). Queued by me while Hannah is away for about a day (she asked for about 36 h of GPU work from the open decisions and the roadmap); she has not seen the design. Both arms are the roadmap's: `considering.md`'s "Widen the bandwidth prior towards real voices" (decoupling, its own arm after 5c, which is done), and Later's level-range note under the AGC item (one augmentation arm, decided after probe 13, which is done). D-064 (the /h/ follow-up to D-063) is not registered yet: the whisper switch's level still differs by engine at steep tilts.

- *Arms*: each is 5b exactly (chains/d040.sh: rA + joint VTL head, 16,000 constant steps and the 4,000-step decay branch, seeds 1 and 2, paired to `d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`) with one change.
  - **bw**: `data/synth/train_bw_v1`, train_v2 re-rendered at its own seed and settings with one difference: each formant's bandwidth gets a per-formant log-uniform factor of 0.4-2.5 independent of the formants, on the cascade's Hawks & Miller prior (in place of plan §5.1's 0.6-1.6) and on the tube's own bandwidths (which had none; the per-speaker `bw_scale` stays). The factor's geometric mean is 1, so the prior's centre does not move (decoupled, not widened; widening needs a target calibrated on non-gold real voices, its own arm). The tube's factor comes from a generator of its own, so every other draw is the clip's own: on six clips checked (cascade and tube) the defaults reproduce train_v2's labels and the arm changes only the bandwidth labels. The set takes train_v2's share of the mix (`synth_extra_replace`: 70 % beside the resynthesised 30 %, same position and seed); train_v2 gives only the synthetic validation, so validation is 5b's. The arm's own held-out 2 % is reported as `eval_extra:train_bw_v1`.
  - **gain**: `channel_gain_db = (-40, 0)` for training crops instead of (-20, 0) (peaks after the channel's 0.5 x peak normalisation: 5 / 50 / 95 % at -45 / -24 / -6 dBFS against -26 / -15 / -6; the clean 15 % unchanged). Validation crops keep (-20, 0). Resynthesised crops get the same channel as before, so they widen too.
- *Measures and rule* (`scripts/analysis/decide_d065.py`): an arm joins the recipe if on seed means its primary criterion and D-051's three guard-rails hold:
  - *(P bw)*: probe 12 arm C (D-050 amendment 4; synthetic cascade clips with exact labels, seed 5054): the weight on the true bandwidth, mean over B1-B4, rises by at least +0.10, **and** probe 7's bandwidth slope (resynthesis, bandwidths x 0.7-2.0), mean over B1-B4, rises by at least +0.10. 5b: 0.317 / 0.336 and 0.571 / 0.574 (seeds 2 / 1). Noise check: 5c against 5b, with no bandwidth change, moved them by -0.010 and -0.021 on seed means (per seed at most 0.047).
  - *(P gain)*: probe 13 at x0.03 (median peak about -44 dBFS, inside the arm's range): mean |change| over B1-B4 at most half of 5b's (the ratio's seed mean <= 0.5 and each seed's <= 0.75), and mean |change| over F1-F4 no more than 5b's + 3 cents. 5b: 30 / 28 cents. Noise check: 5c against 5b gave ratios 0.51 and 1.19 (seed mean 0.85), hence the per-seed bound.
  - *(1) no formant cost, (2) length not worse, (3) f0 guard*: D-051's, unchanged (synthetic F1-F4 within +2 SE, RPA >= -0.1; Hillenbrand F1 <= +4, F2 <= +9, F3 <= +20 Hz, PTDB-TUG RPA >= -1.0, women's closed-phase F1 |error| <= +5 Hz; Hillenbrand length reliability lower bound above -0.02 with w/m 0.85-0.92; f0 probe excess <= 10 c/oct).
  - Each seed is evaluated too; disagreement is reported as NOT ROBUST TO THE SEED. The two arms are decided separately against 5b; combining them would be its own entry.
  - *Reported, deciding nothing*: women's signed closed-phase F1 bias per seed (the register's 1 kHz lead: wider training bandwidths make the envelope peak and the pole disagree more often); probe 13 at x0.3, x0.1 and x0.01; every other probe in the suite.
- *Order and cost*: gain s1, gain s2, then bw s1, bw s2, about 8.7 h each; the bw set renders on 6 CPU workers during gain's training (about 100,000 clips; D-051's long set, the same amount of audio, took about 2 h on 12), which may slow that training by the 15-36 % seen under CPU load. Gain's scoring overlaps bw's training. About 37 h in all.
- *Restart (2026-11-02)*: gain s1's 16,000 steps finished (2026-11-01 18:04), but its decay branch segfaulted at start-up, as did every GPU process at that time (another agent's sandbox, run after a permissions elevation; not this entry's code). The bw set had finished cleanly at 16:30, before the D-064 synthesis changes, which leave default renders byte-identical anyway. Both chains restarted from the decay step, unchanged; the scoring chain now reads the closed-phase cache at `cache/m3w` (moved from the session scratchpad, which keeps a junction to it). No result had been seen. The first restart (2026-11-02 20:22) segfaulted the same way: every torch GPU call failed with an access violation. Running after a reboot, with the sandbox's explicit low-integrity labels on `C:\github` and the repos set back to medium, the GPU test passed and the decay branch ran (from 20:33).
- *Result (2026-11-03; `reports/analysis/d065/decide_d065_{gain,bw}.txt`, `vtl_joint_{gain,bw}.txt`)*: **neither arm
  joins; 5b's recipe stays. Hannah: logged, to revisit later.** Both decisions are flagged NOT ROBUST TO THE SEED.
  - *gain*: the primary holds clearly (probe 13 at x0.03, mean |B| ratio 0.30 on seed means, 0.43 / 0.17 by seed;
    mean |F| excess -3.9 cents; at x0.01 B falls from about 110 to 20 cents), formants and f0 hold, but **(2) length
    fails narrowly**: Hillenbrand reliability against 5b [-0.030, +0.002] (bar -0.02), carried by seed 2 (s1
    [-0.019, +0.032], s2 [-0.056, -0.014]); the seeds also disagree on (1).
  - *bw*: the guards hold (length [-0.015, +0.020], w/m 0.857; formants within margins), but **the primary fails on
    one half**: probe 12's weight on the true bandwidth more than doubles (0.33 -> 0.70, +0.37; bar +0.10), while probe
    7's bandwidth slope rises only +0.065 (bar +0.10). Seed 2 also fails (2) alone.
  - *Seed spread, found after the decision (Hannah asked whether gain's length failure is real)*: 5b's own seeds differ
    by +0.034 [+0.005, +0.060] in Hillenbrand reliability (s2 - s1, `vtl_joint_gain.txt`), the size of gain s2's
    "failure" against 5b s2 (-0.035); both gain seeds sit close to 5b s1 (+0.008, -0.002), and gain's seeds agree with
    each other within 0.01. So the paired design matched gain s2 with 5b's good draw; the result stands under the rule.
  - *Reported*: women's signed closed-phase F1 bias, 5b -> arm, s1 / s2: gain +4.3 -> +6.2 / +4.1 -> +3.5 Hz; bw +4.3
    -> +7.1 / +4.1 -> +4.8 Hz (no consistent direction for gain; bw up on both seeds, a weak lead for the register's
    bandwidth idea).
  - *For a revisit (not registered)*: a third seed of 5b and of gain, decided on three-seed means with the same rule
    (5b s3 would also tighten every comparison against 5b); for bw, whether probe 7's bar was set too high for a
    resynthesis probe whose slope moves less than probe 12's on the same change.

## D-064 (2026-11-01, registered after the fact: a code and level-policy change, checked against the goldens, D-059's clips and D-063's tilt grid; no experiment arm runs yet): the whisper and /h/ level is anchored to the as-is render after the tract, so a spectral tilt moves only the shape

*Status*: in, two commits - `ff9faae` (the extraction, no behaviour change) and `7c8ded9` (the level policy). This entry registers the code and its checks. The arm D-063 planned (the tilt -> real /h/ slope mapping) is still unregistered, and it now has a known floor problem at steep tilts (below).

- *Why*: 7c77891 (D-063) made `whisper_tilt_db_oct` shape-only at the source - one noise for all engines, flat below 500 Hz, power-normalised to the default. But the component's level was never anchored after the tract: it was the fixed `* 0.05` shared with frication, so the tract still weighted the redistributed spectrum differently per engine and the /h/ grew or shrank with the tilt. The aspiration next to it has no such problem: D-048's HNR sets its level after the tract, and the /h/ is the same kind of noise. So the rule is one noise source, one tract, one explicit level policy; aspiration is the case "match a reference component's power at a stated ratio", /h/ is "match the as-is render's power".
- *What changed*: `vocal_analysis/synth/noise.py` now holds `tilted_noise`, `noise_source`, `whisper_noise`, `band_energy`, `level_gain`, `aspiration_component` and `whisper_component`; the six copies in `voice.py`, `interactive.py` and `kl_set.py` are calls, each passing its tract as a closure and its source domain as a fixed offset (KL's flow noise is -6 dB/oct, since the waveguide radiates it). New `SynthConfig.whisper_level` ("as-is", the default; "legacy" reproduces the old policy), pinned to "legacy" in `V1_SETTINGS` so an older `index.json` still resumes.
- *Which anchor*: broadband (the time-domain power the HNR holds), not a band. (`scripts/analysis/d064_level_choice.py`.) Measured on three clips per engine with the 1-3.5 kHz anchor, the tilted component's total power at -12 dB/oct differed from the as-is render by +16.1 (cascade), +6.7 (tube) and +2.2 dB (KL) - the same objection in another form, a level that moves with the tilt. With the broadband anchor the component's power is the as-is render's by construction at every tilt and engine.
- *Before and after* (`scripts/analysis/d064_level.py --serial`, 8 clips per cell, train_v2's config, /h/ level against the clip's loudest 5 %, as-is / -12 / +6 dB/oct): cascade -23.3 / -4.1 / -25.0 -> -23.3 / -19.0 / -23.6; tube -32.4 / -36.3 / -32.5 -> -32.4 / -32.3 / -32.1; KL -31.1 / -47.2 / -31.2 -> -31.1 / -29.3 / -30.8. The engine spread at -12 falls from 43 to 13 dB. In 1-3.5 kHz after the fix: cascade -17.5 / -32.1 / -17.6, tube -40.2 / -43.1 / -42.0, KL -37.6 / -35.5 / -34.0.
- *Residual, and why it is not a level bug*: the cascade still reads +4.3 dB at -12. The component's power is invariant; the measure divides by the clip's loudest frames after the clip gain, and low-frequency-weighted noise has a higher peak factor, so the ratio moves. The tube (+0.1) and KL (+1.8) are flat.
- *Regression evidence*: (`scripts/analysis/d064_check.py`: `clips` for D-059's grid, `tilt capture|check` for the 12-clip datum) `ff9faae` changed nothing - the golden matrix (21 cases by then: every engine, every noise branch, both interactive paths, the tilt paths, draw and length breadth) passed unchanged at the pre-fix manifest, and a 12-clip tilted-component datum (audio, labels, every component) was byte-identical. D-059's full grid also reproduces: all 480 clips (160 per engine, 2-4 s, every tract length and both tract types) are byte-identical to `cache/d059/clips`, so the chain code at `ff9faae` == the code before 7c77891 == the record the training data was rendered from. In `7c8ded9` exactly one golden moves, `cascade_whisper_tilt`, in its audio, whisper, total and meta gain - nothing else - and a `whisper_level="legacy"` case pins the old policy. New tests: the tilt holds the component's power, moves its energy below 1 kHz and leaves every other component and label alone; legacy still moves the level. `test_whispered_spectrum_is_the_labelled_tract` is unchanged (it uses no tilt).
- *Not fixed on purpose*: the in-band level still moves with the shape (-14.6 dB at -12 on the cascade in 1-3.5 kHz) - that is what a constant-power tilt is; the ~9 dB as-is spread between engines (pre-existing); the `* 0.05` constant itself and frication, which now share a module but not a policy; and the 0.5 clip gain normalisation, which the residual above goes through.
- *Consequences for the D-063 follow-up*: at -12 dB/oct the cascade's /h/ is 14.6 dB down in 1-3.5 kHz, which is the band D-063 fits the slope in. The arm must therefore floor each /h/ segment against the vowel's tail (or report the bands it can still fit), or its steepest tilt will be measuring the vowel.
- *Notes on D-062*: recorded under D-062 (D-048's shape and the aspiration's high-frequency level; KL's default flow noise and the tilt floors).


## D-066 (2026-11-02, Hannah; seen: vtl-mri's model lengths for 34 of the 59 speakers, as aggregates by sex): tiers for the USC 75-speaker MRI set's other 59 speakers

D-035 says the 59 speakers without Annot-16 contours get their tiers "before any of them is measured". That has
slipped: vtl-mri's V-014 ran its contour model over 32 frames each of 34 of them and recorded length aggregates by
sex (no per-speaker lengths were looked at for choosing anything). Set now, before any per-speaker use.

- *Assignment* (`tiers.USC75_SEMI`, `tiers.json`): **semi-private** are the 25 speakers vtl-mri's V-012 keeps test-only
  (sub002, 003, 004, 005, 007, 020, 021, 025, 027, 031, 032, 035, 036, 038, 041, 045, 046, 048, 049, 050, 053, 062,
  069, 073, 075; 11 men, 14 women); **dev** are the other 34; the 16 Annot-16 speakers stay **lockbox** (D-035).
- *Why V-012's split and not a fresh draw*: V-012 drew it with a seed, stratified by sex, and committed it (vtl-mri
  `4b4582a`, 2026-11-01) before any of these speakers was labelled or measured, so it was not chosen knowing any
  length. Reusing it gives each tier its references by how well the lengths are known (Hannah): the lockbox's 16
  have the densest hand labels; the 25 semi-private speakers are vtl-mri's evaluation set and will be densely
  hand-labelled for its acceptance, so their lengths are measured largely by hand; the 34 dev speakers have sparse
  labels, with lengths measured mostly by the contour model. Whether the contour model trained on a speaker's images
  does not matter here: it makes that speaker's length no worse, and "held out" only matters for vtl-mri's own
  accuracy estimate. 34 / 59 is 0.58, close to `DEV_FRAC`.
- *Not changed*: the audio rule. No voice model has been run on any of these speakers.

## D-067 (2026-11-02, draft, not registered; nothing measured): the MRI length reference: per-frame and per-speaker lengths, posture adjustment and masking

*Status*: **draft** (Hannah asked for it; thresholds wait for vtl-mri's evaluation set and its label-noise floor). It replaces D-035's Annot-16-based reference for every tier: vtl-mri is dropping Annot-16's contours (its "arytenoid" labels often close the airway against the epiglottis), and D-066 gave dev and semi-private MRI speakers. Requirements on vtl-mri's export are listed in vtl-mri's `docs/considering.md`.

- *Why both levels*: whether to add an effective (instantaneous) length output beside the anatomical one is undecided. The MRI's synchronised audio makes per-frame lengths the only real-voice reference for it (probe 10 and D-051 tested it on synthetic clips only); the anatomical reference is a per-speaker summary of the same frames.
- *Per frame* (from vtl-mri's export): midline length, glottis height and lip protrusion against fixed landmarks, timestamp and audio offset, QC values. Scored against a model's instantaneous output on sonorant voiced 10 ms frames within each MRI frame's span.
- *Per speaker, anatomical*: each frame's length adjusted to a reference posture, L_adj = L - b_g (g - g_ref) - b_l (p - p_ref), with g glottis height and p lip protrusion, g_ref / p_ref per-sex medians, and b_g / b_l fitted within speaker on **dev** only (pooled over speakers, so the slope isn't any one speaker's); then the median of L_adj over frames that pass QC. This matches the synthetic labels' definition (anatomical length fixed while `pg_cm` / `pm_cm` move the instantaneous length). The plain median of L is reported beside it.
- *Masking*, fixed before any voice model is run on these speakers, and never from a voice model's output (that would bias the reference towards the model): vtl-mri's QC (shape checks, jumps against neighbouring frames, the contour model's own spread), thresholds chosen on vtl-mri's hand-labelled frames by errors caught against good frames dropped; then here, frames more than 3 robust SDs from the speaker's L_adj median. The fraction masked is reported per speaker; a speaker who loses more than half their frames is excluded and named.
- *Use by tier* (revised 2026-11-03, after D-069 amendment 1 and D-070; replaces the line first drafted here):
  - *Dev* (USC's 34, ArtSpeech P1 and P7): build the methods (frame matching, masking, posture adjustment, effective
    against anatomical, per frame against speaker medians), each change logged. Frame-level methods are checked on P1
    and P7 first (in-sample reference, the most accurate per frame); between-speaker figures (women / men ratio,
    within-sex correlation, calibration slope) come from USC dev. Once settled, a dev length score joins the probe
    suite as a guard-rail for training arms; MRI audio is never trained on and never fits synthesis priors.
  - *Semi-private* (USC's 25, vtl-mri's densely hand-labelled eval set): one logged confirmation per candidate model
    or settled method version, never a choice between arms; scored against the hand labels and vtl-mri's model, which
    also measures what the model reference costs.
  - *Lockbox*, once per milestone, rule set beforehand, two claims reported separately: same lab (USC's 16; say
    whether their reference avoids Annot-16's labels) and across labs and languages (ArtSpeech's 8, D-069; D-070 found
    the audio usable).
  - *Order*: vtl-mri's eval set and reference error; this entry registered with thresholds from it; dev work; the dev
    probe; semi at a release candidate; lockbox at the milestone.
- *Pooling across tiers* (2026-11-03): dev is where the methods are tuned, so dev figures are optimistic for any
  measure that depends on those choices.
  - The primary result is the untouched tier alone (semi at semi use, lockbox at lockbox use); pass or fail is decided
    there.
  - Pooled figures ({dev, semi} at semi use, {dev, semi, lockbox} at lockbox use) are secondary and descriptive, for
    precision, labelled as pooled with each tier's speaker count.
  - Each pooled figure is reported beside a tier-difference check; if the held-out tier is clearly worse than dev (its
    interval excludes dev's estimate), the pooled figure isn't quoted and the gap is the finding.
  - Exception: a measure fully specified before dev was looked at, with nothing tuned against it (e.g. the
    reference's error from hand labels), may pool freely, if its registration says so.
  - *Power first*: before registration, estimate what each tier can detect given the reference noise (about 17
    speakers per sex in USC dev and 12 in semi: a within-sex correlation of about 0.5 or more). A test that can't
    reach its effect size even pooled is descriptive, not a criterion. More labs (each its own claim, most likely
    lockbox, after a D-070-style audio check) add independence; pooling only adds speakers from the same setups.
- *Criteria, to set before semi-private is opened* (shapes only; values against the reference's noise floor): Spearman correlation within sex above a floor; calibrated median absolute error per speaker below a fraction of the between-speaker SD; women / men ratio inside the MRI's interval; interval coverage near nominal; and, if an instantaneous output is proposed, per-frame tracking of L - L_adj (larynx and lips) with a slope near 1. Clean-speech checks alongside, since the scanner audio is noise-cancelled AAC.

## D-068 (2026-11-02, registered before any of this audio was extracted or analysed; descriptive, with one verdict): is the USC 75-speaker MRI audio usable for checking vocal-tract length?

*Status*: registered. Hannah's question: is the scanner audio good enough for a length check, before another 9 hours of hand-labelling for vtl-mri's evaluation set? The MRI recordings' audio is noise-cancelled scanner audio in AAC, so a hard domain; if a voice model can't give stable lengths on it, the MRI reference has no use here however good the contours are.

- *Exception to D-067's order* (Hannah asked, 2026-11-02): this is the first voice model run on these speakers. It is allowed because nothing here is compared with any MRI length, no per-speaker length is printed, reported or committed (per-file values stay in `cache/d068/`, uncommitted), and nothing feeds D-067's masking or posture rules. Dev speakers only (34: 19 women, 15 men; `tiers`, D-066).
- *Data*: per dev speaker, the five read passages (rainbow, grandfather 1 and 2, north wind 1 and 2), both repetitions, audio from the MP4s with ffmpeg to 16 kHz mono, peak-normalised; files with fewer than 50 sonorant voiced frames left out. Reference: PTDB-TUG dev (studio microphone, read English), 10 files per speaker spread over each speaker's list.
- *Analysis* (`scripts/analysis/d068_usc_audio.py`): teacher v3.1 seeds 2 and 1 (`d040_5b_s{2,1}_b20k`), windowed `analyse`, seed mean per file; sonorant voiced frames (voiced and sonorant probability > 0.5). Per file: that fraction, the median anatomical length and its sigma, median formant sigma F1-F4.
- *Measures*, per corpus and sex: within-speaker SD of per-file log length (%), between-speaker SD, reliability (between over between + within variance), speaker bootstrap; USC only, the median |r1 - r2| of the same passage.
- *Verdict*, per sex: **usable** if USC's within-speaker SD is at most 1.5 x PTDB-TUG's, USC's reliability is at least 0.5, and USC's median length sigma at most 1.5 x PTDB-TUG's; **not usable** if the within-speaker SD is over 2 x PTDB-TUG's or reliability under 0.3; otherwise doubtful. Overall usable only if both sexes are. Caveat: PTDB-TUG dev has few speakers per sex, so its within-speaker SD is the firmer comparison, and reliability depends on the speakers' spread of lengths as well as on the audio.
- *Amendment 1 (2026-11-02, Hannah, while the analysis ran and before any result was seen)*: the absolute reliability bars (0.5, 0.3) test the model as much as the audio (teacher v3.1's Hillenbrand reliability is 0.23-0.26, D-051) and depend on the speakers' spread of true lengths. The verdict now uses only the comparisons against PTDB-TUG, for the same model: **usable** if USC's within-speaker SD is at most 1.5 x PTDB-TUG's and its median length sigma at most 1.5 x; **not usable** if the within-speaker SD is over 2 x; otherwise doubtful. Reliability is reported, deciding nothing.
- *Result (2026-11-02; `reports/analysis/d068/d068.txt`; 332 USC files, all with >= 50 sonorant voiced frames; 120 PTDB-TUG)*: **doubtful by the rule, carried by one criterion: the women's length sigma.**
  - *Stability is studio-like in both sexes*: within-speaker SD of per-file length, USC / PTDB-TUG, women 2.17 % [1.77, 2.58] / 2.45 % (ratio 0.89), men 2.58 % [0.97, 4.07] / 2.70 % (0.96). The two repetitions of the same passage agree to a median 1.13 % (women) and 0.73 % (men). Reliability (reported only): USC women 0.78 [0.58, 0.88], men 0.45 [0.14, 0.88]; PTDB-TUG 0.73 / 0.61 on 6 speakers each.
  - *Uncertainty is higher on the scanner audio, mostly in women*: median length sigma ratio 1.67 (women; over the 1.5 bar) and 1.09 (men). Women's formant sigma F1-F4 172 / 83 / 64 / 118 cents against 111 / 50 / 35 / 35 in the studio; men's 131 / 76 / 75 / 65 against 83 / 44 / 39 / 47. So the model sees less evidence in the upper formants (the denoiser and AAC most likely), says so, and its lengths still don't wander more than on studio speech.
  - The sonorant voiced fraction is higher on USC (0.50-0.54 against 0.21), because the passages are continuous read speech while PTDB-TUG's files carry long silences.
  - *Reading*: for speaker-level length checks, the MRI audio is about as repeatable as studio speech; its cost is wider intervals, so any criterion on it should use the model's sigma (coverage) or speaker medians over many files, not single frames. Whether its lengths are *right* is untested here by design (D-067 decides that against MRI).

## D-069 (2026-11-03, Hannah; nothing of ArtSpeech measured here): ArtSpeech's 10 speakers are all lockbox

ArtSpeech (Isaieva et al. 2021, figshare collection 5270387, CC0: 10 French speakers, 5 men and 5 women, real-time
MRI with denoised audio and alignments) is the second lab and language in the paper outline's length claim ("an
independent check"). Too small to split into dev and semi, so all 10 go to the lockbox (`tiers.LOCKBOX`): no voice
model is run on their audio, and no length is compared with their MRI, before a milestone acceptance with its rule
set beforehand. vtl-mri may train its contour model on the *images* of two of them (its V-023: P1 and P7, drawn
with a seed, one per sex), as it does with the USC lockbox speakers' images (D-035). Before ArtSpeech audio is used
here it goes into `ATTRIBUTION.md`.

- *Amendment 1 (2026-11-03, Hannah and Claude; still nothing of ArtSpeech measured here: no audio decoded, no model
  run, no length compared)*: **P1 and P7, the two speakers whose images vtl-mri trains on, move to dev; the other
  eight stay lockbox** (`tiers.ARTSPEECH_DEV`). vtl-mri never sees audio, and all ten speakers will get hand-labelled
  frames, so what differs is the per-frame model output: in-sample for P1 and P7, held-out for the eight. The lockbox
  should be the pessimistic case, like the general use at acceptance: a held-out reference whose error vtl-mri's eval
  labels measure, so the slope correction for reference error applies as estimated. Dev, where masking, posture
  adjustment and frame matching get built, gains the most accurate per-frame reference, and absorbs any selection in
  the training pair. Two dev speakers suffice for within-speaker checks (a D-068-style repeatability and sigma check)
  and method building, not for between-speaker figures (those stay with USC dev). Supersedes D-069's first rule for
  these two speakers; ArtSpeech goes into `ATTRIBUTION.md` before its audio is used.


## D-070 (registered 2026-11-03, Hannah and Claude; nothing of ArtSpeech measured here): is ArtSpeech's MRI audio usable for checking vocal-tract length? (dev speakers P1 and P7)

*Why*: D-068 asked this of USC's scanner audio (repeatable, wider sigma for women: "doubtful"). ArtSpeech is a second
lab, scanner, denoiser and language (French), and its eight lockbox speakers are the length claim's independent check
(D-069). Before anyone spends labelling time on that check, the two dev speakers (D-069 amendment 1) can say whether
the audio holds up. Label-free: no length is compared with MRI, and no per-speaker length is printed or committed.

- *Data*: P1 (man) and P7 (woman), all 16 sessions each (`OTHER/S<k>/DENOISED_SOUND_P<n>_S<k>.wav`, 16 kHz mono,
  about 44 s of read French; different sentences per session, the same texts for both speakers), read from the zips
  without unpacking. Seen before registration: file names, sizes, wav headers and the transcripts' text only.
- *Measures* (as D-068, `scripts/analysis/d070_artspeech_audio.py`): per session, sonorant voiced frames
  (voiced_p and sonorant_p > 0.5) of teacher v3.1 seeds 2 and 1 (seed mean): median anatomical length, its median
  sigma, median formant sigma F1-F4. Per speaker: within-speaker SD of per-session log length (%), with a bootstrap
  over sessions, and the median sigma. References: PTDB-TUG dev of the same sex, and USC dev of the same sex, from
  D-068's per-file cache (same models).
- *Rule* (per speaker, against PTDB-TUG dev of the same sex, as D-068 amendment 1): **usable** if within-speaker SD
  <= 1.5x and median length sigma <= 1.5x; **not usable** if within-speaker SD > 2x; otherwise **doubtful**. Overall:
  usable if both are; not usable if either is. The USC ratios are reported beside it.
- *Caveats, written beforehand*: ArtSpeech sessions are longer than PTDB-TUG's files, which lowers a per-file SD and
  flatters the within-speaker ratio (USC's passages share this; the USC ratio is the like-for-like one). One speaker
  per sex, so a "doubtful" carried by one speaker is not a sex difference. Language and recording setup are
  confounded. Between-speaker figures are not computed (two speakers).
- *Result (2026-11-03; `reports/analysis/d070/d070.txt`; all 32 sessions kept)*: **usable by the rule, for both
  speakers.**
  - *Stability*: within-speaker SD across sessions, P1 (man) 0.90 % [0.48, 1.16], P7 (woman) 1.80 % [1.10, 2.26];
    ratios against PTDB-TUG dev 0.33 / 0.73, against USC dev 0.35 / 0.83. Part of that is the longer files (the
    caveat above), but the USC ratio, like for like in length, is under 1 too.
  - *Uncertainty*: median length sigma ratio against PTDB-TUG 1.07 (P1) and 1.42 (P7, under the 1.5 bar, close to
    it); against USC 0.97 / 0.85. Formant sigma F1-F4 131 / 66 / 73 / 65 cents (P1) and 149 / 56 / 53 / 91 (P7):
    between the studio and USC, with F1 the widest, as on USC.
  - *Reading*: the woman's wider sigma recurs on a second scanner and denoiser, a little smaller than on USC; with one
    speaker per sex it may be the speaker, not the sex. ArtSpeech's audio is at least as repeatable as USC's, so the
    lockbox check across labs and languages (D-069) is worth its labels. Whether the lengths are *right* is still
    untested by design.

## D-071 (registered 2026-11-03, stage A; seen beforehand: a 2-clip smoke run per set, for function only): roadmap item 10, uncertainty calibration of teacher v3.1's non-f0 heads

*Why*: item 10 is due before M3's acceptance. D-068 and D-070 already judged audio partly by sigma ratios, M4's
sigma gating (D-027) and the back end's Monte Carlo corrections assume the bands mean what they say, and a smoke run on
high-pitched Korean speech (item 10's note) showed F2's sigma at +-40-50 cents where the spectrum allowed a peak
200 Hz away. Descriptive, with flags; no training.

- *Models*: teacher v3.1 (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`) decides the flags; `d040_5b_s1_b20k` reported beside it (a flag carried
  by one seed only is marked).
- *Heads*: formants F1-F4 (`formant_sigma_cents`), bandwidths B1-B4 (`bandwidth_log_sd`), anatomical length
  (`vtl_sigma`, log2), and the probabilities voicing (`voiced_p`) and sonorant (`sonorant_p`). (v3.1 has no
  observability head.) f0's sigma reported only.
- *Data, stage A (exact labels; decides the flags)*: train_v2's held-out validation clips (the training run's own
  split, 512 clips), the hard-case suite `suite_v1` (each case separately), and the resynthesised validation sets
  (`vocalset_val_d16k`, `zeroth_korean_val_d16k`: real sources, exact tracts). Each item is the trainer's fixed
  validation crop (its seed and the run's validation channel) at 400 frames, D-028's window, so train_v2's 2-4 s
  clips are whole, run through `infer.analyse` as `va analyse` would. Formants and bandwidths on sonorant frames
  with a label (as the trainer's eval; the whisper case has no voiced frames; resynthesised clips with a formant
  weight below 0.5 left out); length on train_v2's frames with D-035's label (the suite's lengths are not masked
  that way); voicing and sonorant on every frame of the synthetic sets only (the resynthesised sets' f0 and voicing
  are the tracker's).
- *Data, stage B (dev gold, descriptive only; registered in detail before it runs, after stage A)*: Hillenbrand dev (formants), PTDB-TUG dev (formants against the
  closed-phase reference, voicing against the laryngograph). The references have their own error, so instead of
  assuming one, each cell reports the **reference SD that would make 1-sigma coverage nominal** (solving
  coverage(sqrt(sigma^2 + s_ref^2)) = 0.683), to set beside what is known of the reference (D-043, D-046).
  Windowed analysis, as everywhere.
- *Measures*: for the Gaussian heads, the scale factor s (below), 1-sigma and 2-sigma interval coverage (ideal
  0.683 / 0.954) and the RMS of z; for probabilities, expected calibration error (10 equal-width bins). Every cell
  is a set (each suite case apart) by f0 band (`f0_band_labels` on the label's f0: < 150, 150-300, 300-500,
  > 500 Hz, "unvoiced" for whispered sonorant frames) plus "all"; on the gold sets also by sex. Intervals by a
  bootstrap over clips (1,000 resamples; frames within a clip are correlated).
- *Flags* (stage A, v3.1; revised in draft 2026-11-03, before anything was measured): the threshold is stated in
  the quantity it is about, the **sigma scale factor** s per head and f0 band: the multiplier on the model's sigma
  that makes 1-sigma coverage nominal (s = the 0.683 quantile of |z|; s > 1: sigma too narrow), the RMS of z as a
  cross-check. A Gaussian head is **miscalibrated** in a cell if s is outside [0.87, 1.15] (more than 15 % too wide
  or too narrow, symmetric in log), with the clip-bootstrap interval excluding 1 and at least 20 clips in the cell;
  a probability head if its ECE exceeds 0.05.
  - Coverage at 1 and 2 sigma is reported with the bounds +-15 % implies, 0.615-0.750 and 0.918-0.979. They are
    asymmetric about nominal (0.683 / 0.954) because coverage saturates towards 1, not by choice.
  - A 2-sigma coverage that disagrees with the one s implies (2 Phi(2 / s) - 1; the clip-bootstrap interval of the
    difference excludes 0 and the difference exceeds 0.01) is reported as **non-Gaussian tails**, its own flag: expected where a heat map is multimodal (a competing peak, as in item 10's Korean F2
    case), and it means one scale factor per cell cannot fix that cell.
  - Training minimises a proper scoring rule on train_v2, so near-nominal coverage there is expected; the hard
    cases and resynthesised sets are the real tests.
- *What follows*: a flagged head gets a post-hoc variance scale per head and f0 band, fitted on synthetic or
  resynthesised data only (never gold), as its own entry. D-068's and D-070's sigma comparisons are re-read in the
  light of the stage A result (a note under each, not a rerun).
- *Code*: `scripts/analysis/d071_calibration.py` (inference cached per model and set; CPU aggregation). GPU about
  an hour, alongside D-065's scoring (Hannah, 2026-11-03: overlapping is fine).
- *Result, stage A (2026-11-03; `reports/analysis/d071/d071_stage_a.txt`; both seeds agree on every pattern below)*:
  **formant sigma flagged almost everywhere, as too wide in the core and too narrow in the tails; bandwidths, length's
  shape and the probabilities are close to calibrated.**
  - *Formants*: s below the band in nearly every set, train_v2's own validation included (v3.1, all bands: F1-F4
    0.85 / 0.75 / 0.70 / 0.71; the suite's reference case 0.74 / 0.63 / 0.67 / 0.72; resynthesised Zeroth 0.90 /
    0.79 / 0.82 / 0.88, VocalSet 0.87 / 0.72 / 0.92 / 0.88), so 1-sigma intervals are 15-40 % wider than they need to
    be. Yet 2-sigma coverage sits below what that s implies (train_v2 F1-F4 0.96 against 0.98-1.00): **non-Gaussian
