    tails** in nearly every cell. Errors are mostly well inside sigma, with a heavier tail of misses than a Gaussian
    of that width; one scale factor per cell cannot fix that.
  - *The exception is F1 near a harmonic*: `f1_near_h1` F1 s 1.92 (seed 1: 2.44) and B1 1.46, `f1_near_h2` F1 1.67:
    there sigma is far too narrow, so the model is confidently wrong where F1 sits on a harmonic (the overread's
    regime). Also `f2_f3_close`: F4 0.56, B3 1.50.
  - *Bandwidths*: in distribution calibrated (train_v2 B1-B4 0.97-1.00, no flags); on the hard cases and VocalSet
    slightly too narrow (s 1.03-1.15, a few cells just over 1.15).
  - *Length*: s 0.79 (seed 1: 0.75), 2-sigma coverage consistent with it (no tails flag): vtl_sigma about 25 % too
    wide, Gaussian in shape.
  - *Voicing and sonorant*: ECE 0.000-0.030 in every synthetic set; calibrated.
  - *Why the formants differ from what training implies*: the heat maps are trained with a proper scoring rule, but
    `formant_sigma_cents` is a summary of them (`bins.decode`: the RMS distance of the whole distribution from the
    decoded value, the targets' blur removed in quadrature). Small mass far from the mode inflates an RMS, so the
    summary is wide where the distribution is sharp with thin far tails, which is what the errors show.
  - *Reading for D-068 / D-070*: their sigma comparisons were ratios between corpora under the same model, so a common
    scale cancels; the length head's Gaussian shape means those ratios read as intended. The formant sigma ratios
    there (reported, not ruled on) inherit the summary's shape.
  - *What follows (not decided here)*: a per-cell variance scale would not fix the formants. Candidates, each its own
    entry: report formant intervals as heat-map quantiles (the central 68 % / 95 % mass) instead of an RMS-based
    sigma, scored the same way; a scale for vtl_sigma (about 0.77); stage B on the gold sets, registered in detail
    first.

## D-072 (registered 2026-11-03; seen beforehand: the zero-start check and a 40-step smoke run of each arm, for function only): an attention retrieve beside the trunk's flatten, with a factorised control, in 5b's decay branch

*Why*: D-054 found the formant heads saturated on h (mlp at 5.6 x the parameters gains about 1 %), and D-056's
anchored read-out converged to the flat heads, so the limit sits in h or before it. The main encoder's flatten
(64 channels x 91 bins -> 384, then GELU) is the one non-residual layer between the conv stack and the Conformer,
holds 53 % of the parameters, and is the only place the frequency axis is collapsed (considering.md, "Trunk
structure"). Question: **do the conv maps hold information h is missing, that a content-dependent route can fetch?**

- *Why these paths*: a plain skip around the flatten would have to be a second full projection (5,824 -> 384; the
  sizes differ, so there is no identity), testing only the GELU and adding capacity of the same kind. A factorised
  path is a restricted linear map the flatten can already express (apart from its GELU), so in a branch from a
  trained flatten it is mostly structured extra capacity. Attention adds what the trunk lacks: a selection of bins
  by their content, which can follow a formant peak that moves from frame to frame.
- *Arm B, the arm of interest* (`TeacherConfig.proj_side = "retrieve"`): per frame, 8 learned queries (dimension 64)
  cross-attend over the 91 bins of the last conv map; keys and values are 1x1 projections of the 64 channels, the
  keys carrying a learned absolute position per bin (D-054's free read-out showed what is lost without an absolute
  anchor; a rotary encoding would be relative only if the queries had positions, and these don't). The 8 outputs
  are concatenated (512) and projected to 384 by a Linear initialised at zero, added after the flatten's GELU:
  h = GELU(proj(x)) + retrieve(x). 211,648 parameters; multiply-adds about +3 % per frame.
- *Arm A, its control* (`proj_side = "factorised"`): each channel compressed along frequency by one Linear shared
  across channels (91 -> 9 bins), GELU, mixed to 384 by a Linear initialised at zero and added in the same place:
  222,396 parameters (arm B + 5.1 %; 8 bins would be - 6.6 %). Same capacity and the same late-added route, no
  content-dependent selection.
- Both start exactly at 5b (checked on `d040_5b_s1/ckpt_016000.pt`: maximum output difference 0.0 in every output).
  The f0 network's flatten is unchanged. Code: `TeacherConfig.proj_side` (`ProjSide`), `TrainConfig.proj_side` /
  `side_lr`.
- *Training*: `va train teacher --branch-from runs/d040_5b_s{s}/ckpt_016000.pt --decay-steps 4000 --train-set
  proj_side=<arm> --train-set side_lr=0.001`: the new path in its own AdamW group at 1e-3 x the model's decay
  (weight decay 0.01), as D-056's read-out and D-040's VTL head (it cannot join the trunk's Muon / Adan groups,
  whose saved state at the branch point does not include it); everything else 5b's; paired control `d040_5b_s{s}_b20k`
  (same start, schedule and data seeds). Seeds 2, then 1, for B then A; about 1.5-2 h each, about 7 h in all.
- *Measures and rule* (arm B against `d040_5b_s{s}_b20k`): D-056's, unchanged (non-inferior on D-040's margins, and
  a gain on at least one of: synthetic F1-F4 MAE lower by >= 3 %; women's closed-phase F1 |error| lower by >= 2 Hz;
  probe 11's F1 scale error halved on Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG), plus D-051's length guard (2) and f0 guard (3), as
  5b has the VTL head. Seed means, both seeds in the same direction; otherwise "not robust to the seed". The length
  guard's paired seed means carry 5b s2's good draw (D-065's note), reported beside the guard.
- *Attribution* (reported, decides nothing): arm A against the same control, and B against A. A gain in B and not
  in A is the content-dependent selection's; a gain in both is the late-added capacity's.
- *Reported only*: where arm B's queries attend (attention over the 91 bins by f0 band and sex; whether any query
  tracks a formant across frames).
- *Reading*: a gain in B makes the retrieve a from-scratch candidate, and a write-back update (h's context placed
  back into the conv map before a second retrieve) the next screen; none makes the flatten unlikely to be the limit
  (the Conformer's width of 128 after `t_in` is the other narrow point). Either way it does not gate D-073, which
  tests the read-out's behaviour rather than missing information.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d072/decide_d072.txt`)*: **arm B is not a candidate.** Both arms land on 5b to the decimal: synthetic F1-F4 within 0.2 SE on every formant, RPA within 0.01, Hillenbrand within 0.1 Hz, women's closed-phase |error| +0.1 Hz, probe 11 slopes 0.976 / 1.045 and 1.035 against 5b's 0.978 / 1.043 and 1.039; no gain on either seed. Non-inferior and both guards met (reliability interval [-0.001, +0.001], w/m 0.864; f0 +1.0 c/oct). Arm A (factorised) the same; B against A within 0.1 Hz and 0.1 %.  - *Reading*: the paths did learn (output weights' norms 3.9-4.0 for retrieve, 7.4 for factorised, from zero), but what they add doesn't change any reading: the flatten's output already carries what these paths could give it, at this scale and in a 4k-step decay branch. A late-added path beside the bottleneck is not where the F1 error lives; with D-073, read-out and bottleneck changes on this trunk are exhausted as F1-bias fixes. Where arm B's queries attend (registered as reported) was not run, and is moot.

## D-073 (registered 2026-11-03; seen beforehand: a 40-step smoke run of each arm, for function only): learned formant queries as the read-out, on teacher v3.1's frozen trunk

*Why*: D-054's free per-bin read-out ("film") read women's closed-phase F1 lower (+0.1 / +2.8 Hz bias against mlp's
+4.2) with F1 scale slopes near 1 (1.005 / 1.014), but misread scale clip by clip (synthetic F1-F4 about 1.9 x mlp's),
because the same read-out at every bin has no absolute frequency; anchored on the flat heads (D-056) it converged to
them. Learned queries split the jobs (considering.md, "Learned formant queries"): the bins give equivariance, the
queries formant identity, and the keys can carry an absolute anchor without the flat heads' per-bin weights.
Question: **can a query read-out keep film's equivariance and lower closed-phase bias without its scale misreads?**
Independent of D-072 (the read-out's behaviour, not missing information); the two may run at once.

- *Trunk* (frozen, as D-054): `d040_5b_s2_b20k`; only the formant and bandwidth read-out trains.
- *Read-out* (`TeacherConfig.readout = "queries"`): D-054's `FreqReadout` maps unchanged (FiLM by h, upsampled to the
  VQT's 363 bins with U-Net skips), so film is the exact comparison and the read-out the one change. Four formant
  queries (dimension 32), each a learned vector plus a projection of h; one self-attention layer among the four;
  formant n's logits are query n's scaled dot product with the keys at every bin (a 1x1 projection of the maps plus
  the position encoding), interpolated onto the formant axis as film's. The cascade's soft mask is kept (dropping it
  would be a second change; self-attention among the queries is part of the read-out). Bandwidths as film's (read at
  each formant's position).
- *Arms* (corrected at registration: the queries have no position, so a rotary encoding on the keys alone would be
  an absolute one, not relative): **queries** (content-only keys: the read-out is shift-equivariant along frequency,
  and identity must come from the queries and h's context) and **queries_abs** (keys plus a learned absolute position
  per bin). Head seeds 1 and 2 each; four runs. `TeacherConfig.readout`, `QueryReadout`;
  `scripts/analysis/d073_queries.py`; chain `chains/d073.sh`; read-out parameters about 0.1 M (film's 91 k plus the
  queries, less its 1x1 output: 112,616 and 124,232 against film's 90,764).
- *Training*: D-054's exactly (5b's data mix and channel, AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 0.01, 300 warm-up steps, cosine
  over 8,000 steps; comb from the frozen f0 network). About 1.5 h a run.
- *Measures*: D-054's (synthetic F1-F4 and resynthesised F1-F3 MAE; bandwidth error and probe 12; probe 11's slopes;
  Hillenbrand dev F1-F3, paired; women's closed-phase F1 bias and |error|), against D-054's existing mlp and film
  runs (same trunk, data and head seeds).
- *Rule* (per arm, seed means, both seeds in the same direction; against mlp): **supported** if (1) synthetic F1-F4
  MAE (mean of the four) is at most 1.15 x mlp's (film was 1.93 x) and (2) probe 11's F1 scale error |1 - slope| is
  at most half mlp's on both Hillenbrand and PTDB-TUG, with D-054's guards (Hillenbrand dev F1 <= +4, F2 <= +9,
  F3 <= +20 Hz against mlp; resynthesised F1-F3 MAE no more than 5 % worse). Women's closed-phase F1 bias reported
  with its paired speaker interval (power-limited, not a criterion). Futility stop as D-054 amendment 1: if an arm's
  seed 1 synthetic F1-F4 MAE exceeds 1.5 x mlp seed 1's, its seed 2 is not trained.
- *Reading*: supported makes a query read-out a full-size candidate (end to end, and combined with D-072's retrieve
  if that gains: retrieve, Conformer, query read-out, as in Perceiver IO). queries against queries_abs says how much absolute
  frequency the identity needs. Not supported, with queries_abs near mlp and no equivariance, says the anchor costs
  the equivariance in this form too.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d073/decide_d073.txt`)*: **neither arm supported.** queries: futility stop (seed 1 synthetic F1-F4 MAE 86.0 Hz, 1.67 x mlp's 51.6; seed 2 not trained). queries_abs: (1) met (MAE 1.00 x mlp's, 51.8 / 52.0 Hz) but (2) not: probe 11's F1 slope 0.975 / 1.036 (Hillenbrand / PTDB-TUG) against mlp's 0.978 / 1.045, nowhere near halving the scale error; guards pass (Hillenbrand -0.6 / -0.5 / -1.1 Hz, resynthesised worst -0.4 %). Reported: women's closed-phase F1 bias, arm - mlp, -1.5 [-2.8, -0.6] (seed 1) and -1.0 [-2.7, -0.2] Hz (seed 2), small but on both seeds; queries s1 read -1.3 Hz absolute (film +0.1 to +2.8) at a large synthetic cost.  - *Reading*: with an absolute position per bin the query read-out is an mlp head by another route: same synthetic error, same scale, about 1 Hz less women's bias. Without it (equivariant), identity can't be recovered well enough from content and context alone, the same cost film paid (D-054). A query read-out is not a candidate on this trunk; D-072's retrieve decides whether the Perceiver combination is worth anything.

## D-074 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: D-060's results, and the candidate shifts below for D-060's ten vowels and glottis without any model): which resonance does teacher v3.1's gap growth on KL renders follow?

*Status*: done (2026-11-04): the sexes disagree, no reading; the cycle-mean pole accounts in both (see Result). From an outside review of the F1 register (Hannah, 2026-11-04): D-060 shows v3.1 reading F1 higher as the closed-phase gap widens, against the static closed-glottis label (`kl.frame_labels`: the tube's poles with the glottis rigid). With a gap the glottis never closes, so two quantities other than the label are physically present: the resonance of the leaky "closed" phase, and the pole's mean over the cycle. If the teacher's growth follows one of them, the gap effect is the teacher reading a resonance that is really there, and the question becomes which F1 we want (the label's definition), not how to remove an error.

- *Candidates* per (sex, vowel, gap), quasi-static (`coupling.open_poles`, the pole with the glottis held open at a fixed area, D-023's physics with its Barney check), on D-060's Story shapes and glottis (male 17.46 cm / peak 0.12 cm^2, female 15.6 / 0.07; length 1.4, depth 0.3 cm scaled by size; 8000 dyn/cm^2; coupling's subglottal resonances scaled by size):
  - **C0**, static: 0 (the label).
  - **CL**, leaky closed phase: F1 at the gap's area (gap x peak) minus the label. Energy follows closure (D-023's note): each cycle's main excitation is at closure, so the ringing heard is mostly the closed phase's.
  - **CM**, cycle mean: the mean over one Rosenberg cycle (`kl.glottal_area`, oq 0.6, sq 1.3, the gap) of F1 at each sample's area minus the label, F1 interpolated over 25 areas from 0.02 to 1 x peak (the reviewer's mean-pole definition).
- *Seen beforehand (feasibility; scratch script, no model)*: gap 0 -> 0.3 growth of CL, women: A +30, V +20, O +20, ae +15, U +14, E +10, I +9, o +8, u +6, i +5 Hz; men larger (A +39, V +36, O +38). CM is already +10 to +50 Hz at gap 0.1 and grows less (women A +17, V +14, ae +11). D-060's teacher growth is largest for the same open vowels, so the test can come out either way. F1's continuation to the peak opening switches branch for V and O (peak shift negative); their CL values at gaps up to 0.3 look continuous.
- *Data*: D-060's per-file errors (`reports/analysis/d060/fixed/main_per_file.tsv`; seed mean of v3.1 s2 and s1; 80 files per sex and gap). No new renders, no model run.
- *Measures*, per sex: (a) **growth residual**: per (vowel, f0) pair, the teacher's paired growth (gap 0.3 - 0) minus the candidate's growth for that vowel; median over pairs, 2000-resample bootstrap over pairs. (b) **level residual**, reported: per file, teacher error minus candidate, median by gap. (c) Reported: Spearman correlation over the ten vowels between the teacher's mean growth and each candidate's.
- *Rule*: a candidate **accounts for the growth** in a sex if its growth residual's interval contains 0 and its median is within +-5 Hz. Both sexes: **CL accounts and CM does not** -> "the gap growth is the leaky closed phase's resonance"; **CM and not CL** -> "the cycle-mean pole"; **both** -> not separable here; **neither** (C0 is D-060's own result, already rejected) -> the growth is not the quasi-static pole movement: bandwidth or spectral shape next (a gap x bandwidth entry). Sexes disagreeing: reported as such, no reading.
- *Reading*: CL or CM accounting makes the gap part of the overread a definition question (D-075 sizes it on the training side; `considering.md`'s effective-F1 head takes the winning definition, not OQ x df). It does not say real women's gaps are this wide (an EGG closure entry on PTDB-TUG dev, not yet registered).
- *Caveats*: quasi-static poles, not the time-varying system; the tube's poles stand in for KL's (they agree within 1 %, D-058) and coupling's subglottal model for KL's (`branches.subglottal`); one area function per vowel and size. The rule is computed on all ten vowels; without V and O reported beside it.
- Script `scripts/analysis/d074_gap_candidates.py` (CPU); report `reports/analysis/d074/`.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d074/d074.txt`)*: **the sexes disagree; no reading by the rule.** Growth residual (teacher gap 0.3 - 0 minus the candidate's): men C0 +12.9 [+8.0, +21.7], CL -3.4 [-11.1, -0.5], **CM +1.9 [-3.5, +7.0]** (CM only accounts); women C0 +12.0 [+7.4, +18.7], CL -1.5 [-4.3, +5.8], CM +2.2 [-3.6, +6.9] (both account). Without V and O the same pattern (men CL -7.8, CM -3.5; women both). Reported, deciding nothing: the level residual against CM is small at every gap, men +3.1 / +2.4 / +7.1 / +2.5 Hz and women +11.2 / +9.7 / +14.4 / +16.7 (against the label: men +23 to +41, women +30 to +50); Spearman over vowels, CL +0.58 / +0.60, CM +0.55 / +0.71 (men / women).
  - *Reading (not a decision)*: CM is the one candidate that accounts for the growth in both sexes, and it also absorbs most of the overread's level on KL, all but about 3 Hz for men and 10-15 Hz for women. So v3.1 on KL renders reads close to the cycle-mean quasi-static pole, not the closed-glottis label; what remains for women is the part this physics doesn't explain. The rule needed both sexes to exclude CL, and women's data can't separate the two. Not shown: that real voices carry the same shift (the EGG closure entry), or that the time-varying system's own spectral peak is CM (quasi-static poles stand in for it).

## D-075 (registered 2026-11-04, with D-074; nothing measured): how large is the coupling shift as the training sampler draws it, under each definition?

*Status*: done (2026-11-04; see Result). Teacher v3.1 trained on `train_v2`, which has no coupling (`p_coupling` 0); D-023's `train_v2c` test did, and its "about 6 Hz median" effective shift was an estimate (peak shift x gap). The cascade moves each pole linearly with the opening, F(t) = F (1 + df sc a(t)) with a(t) = g + (1 - g) sin^2 over the open phase (`voice.glottal_opening`), while the quasi-static physics is far from linear in the area (D-074's feasibility: /A/'s gap-0.3 shift is +30 Hz against 0.3 x its peak +62 = +19; /i/'s +5 against +17). This entry measures the shifts the sampler implies.

- *Clips*: 2000 tube targets drawn with `train_v2c`'s settings (its seed; glottis, gap offset, Rd and `coupling_scale` as sampled), split by tract length as D-023 (short / long).
- *Per clip, F1, Hz*: (1) cascade leak shift F1 df sc g; (2) cascade cycle-mean shift F1 df sc <a>, from the rendered opening (te from Rd, g from `gap_from_open_quotient`); (3) quasi-static leak shift (`open_poles` at g x peak) x sc; (4) quasi-static cycle mean, as D-074's CM. Medians and quartiles per group; (1) against (3) per clip.
- *Rule*: (i) the effective-F1 label in `considering.md` becomes D-074's winning definition computed as here (if D-074 says neither, the head is set aside). (ii) If the cascade's leak shift (1) differs from the quasi-static (3) by more than 30 % in median ratio, the cascade's linear interpolation is recorded as misstating the closed-phase shift (a fix is its own entry). (iii) If the short-tract median of the winning definition at scale 1 is under 3 Hz, the gap mechanism as sampled cannot reach the women's +6 Hz without wider real gaps than the sampler's; reported in the register.
- Script `scripts/analysis/d075_train_shifts.py` (CPU); report `reports/analysis/d075/`.
- *Amendment 1 (2026-11-04, before the 2000-clip result; seen: an 8-clip function check)*: the clips are drawn as `coupling_dist.py` (D-023) draws them, with the sampler's own speaker, glottis and target functions and train_v2c's glottal settings, but tract length (13-18.5 cm) and Rd (0.3-2.7) uniform and seed 5075, not replayed from `train_v2c`'s own clip pipeline and seed. The quasi-static F1 is interpolated over 12 areas (0 to 1 x peak), not 25.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d075/d075.txt`, per-clip `clips.npy`)*: F1 shift medians, Hz, at scale 1 (as train_v2c, x sc), short / long tracts: (1) cascade leak +14.9 / +11.5 (+5.3 / +3.6); (2) cascade mean +29.2 / +22.4 (+11.7 / +8.2); (3) quasi-static leak +13.1 / +11.1 (+4.1 / +3.6); (4) quasi-static mean +29.6 / +22.8 (+12.0 / +9.1). Median gap 0.32, <a> 0.57. No continuation failed.
  - (i) D-074 named no winner (the sexes disagreed), a case the rule didn't foresee: `considering.md`'s effective-F1 label is left undecided, with both definitions computed here; OQ x df is dropped (the mean opening is g + (1 - g) te / 2, about 0.57 here, not OQ). (ii) Cascade / quasi-static leak, median ratio per clip: 0.94 (short), 0.78 (long): **within 30 %**, so the cascade's linear interpolation is not recorded as misstating the closed-phase shift; per vowel it can (D-074's feasibility: /A/ and /i/ in opposite directions), and the medians hide it. (iii) Both definitions' short-tract medians at scale 1 are far above 3 Hz (13 and 30 Hz): the sampler's gaps can reach the women's +6 Hz under either. As train_v2c rendered it (x sc), the median is 4-12 Hz, consistent with D-023's estimate of about 6.

## D-076 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: dev frame counts, the EGG closed quotient's spread, and a shuffle null of the measure, none of the error's relation to it): does v3.1's real-voice F1 error track EGG closure within speakers? (PTDB-TUG dev)

*Status*: done (2026-11-04): not detected (see Result). The real-voice side of D-074 (Hannah, 2026-11-04). D-074 found v3.1 reads KL renders close to the cycle-mean quasi-static pole. If real voices carry the same shift, a frame with a longer open phase should read higher against the closed-phase reference. That reference is LPC over the closed phase, so it carries the leaky closed phase's own shift. Under D-074's physics the error against it is about CM - CL, the open-phase excursion F1 df (1 - g) OQ / 2, so it falls as the closed quotient rises: about -2 Hz per 0.1 of CQ at F1 600-700 Hz, df 0.09, g 0.3 (D-075's medians).

- *Data*: `cache/m3w/ptdb_egg_sub.npz` (D-053's windowed closed-phase frames), dev speakers only (6 women, 3867 frames; 6 men, 3876), frames with a finite EGG closed quotient (`cq`, `closed_phase.py`), closed-phase F1 and model F1. Error: the seed mean of v3.1's F1 (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`) minus the closed-phase F1.
- *Seen beforehand*: CQ 10 / 50 / 90 % women 0.31 / 0.43 / 0.55, men 0.37 / 0.47 / 0.58; within-speaker SD about 0.08; speaker medians 0.40-0.50 for women and 0.41-0.57 for men, too narrow to compare speakers. Shuffle null (CQ permuted within speaker, 200 draws) of the per-sex median slope: 95 % range women [-3.2, +2.8], men [-1.2, +1.3] Hz per 0.1 CQ. Women alone cannot detect -2.
- *Measure*: per speaker, ordinary least squares of the error on CQ, closed-phase F1 and log f0 (so vowel and pitch don't stand in for closure); the CQ coefficient in Hz per 0.1 CQ. **Primary**: the mean of the 12 dev speakers' slopes. Null: 2000 permutations of CQ within each speaker, one-sided (negative).
- *Rule*: **tracks** if the primary slope is at most -1 Hz per 0.1 CQ with permutation p < 0.05; **not detected** otherwise. The null's SD is reported beside it, so "not detected" says whether -2 was within reach.
- *Reported, deciding nothing*: each sex's mean and median slope with its own permutation p; the same with each seed alone; the slope with closed-phase F1 binned (below and above 500 Hz); teacher v3 (`conformer_v2r2_s1_d16k`) beside v3.1.
- *Reading*: tracks: real frames with longer open phases read higher in the direction and roughly the size D-074's physics predicts, so the cycle-mean shift is present in real voices and part of the real overread is the definition (as on KL). Not detected with -2 within reach: the mechanism isn't visible at this size on PTDB-TUG's dev closure range. Either way the between-speaker question (are women's gaps wider?) stays open: 6 speakers per sex can't answer it.
- *Caveats*: the EGG contact quotient measures vocal-fold contact, not the glottal area or a leaky gap (a posterior chink can leak at full contact); CQ also moves with loudness and register, partly controlled by log f0. The closed-phase reference itself depends on the closed phase's length (shorter windows, noisier LPC). Dev only; semi-private would confirm.
- Script `scripts/analysis/d076_egg_closure.py` (CPU); report `reports/analysis/d076/`.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d076/d076.txt`)*: **not detected.** Mean within-speaker slope over the 12 dev speakers +0.31 Hz per 0.1 CQ, permutation p 0.63 (one-sided); null SD 0.91, so the predicted -2 was 2.2 null SDs away (power roughly 70 %). Reported: women -1.48 (median -1.17; p 0.20, null SD 1.71), men +2.11 (p 0.996 one-sided: if anything, the opposite direction); each seed alone and teacher v3 near 0 (+0.75, -0.12, +0.34); closed-phase F1 below 500 Hz -1.21 (p 0.047, one of five reported splits, not a finding), above +0.72. Per speaker from -10.1 to +6.7.
  - *Reading*: within speakers, frames with longer open phases do not read higher against the closed-phase reference at the size D-074's physics predicts, pooled over sexes. Women's slope points the predicted way and men's the other, but neither sex alone has the power to say. So D-074's cycle-mean account of the KL overread has no support yet on real voices; it isn't refuted either: the EGG contact quotient is not the glottal area (a posterior chink leaks at full contact), and within-speaker CQ variation (SD 0.08) is small. Whether women's gaps are wider than men's remains open.
- *Addendum (2026-11-04, after the result; reported, deciding nothing; the outside review's speaker-level request; `reports/analysis/d076/speakers.txt`, `scripts/analysis/d076_speakers.py`)*: per-speaker slopes with OLS SEs (too small: frames aren't independent). Women split 3 / 3 in sign (inverse-variance mean -0.87 +- 1.49, heterogeneity Q 10.0 on 5 df); only F09 stands out (-10.1, t -2.2). Men 2 / 4 (mean +1.28 +- 0.56, Q 27.3 on 5 df: genuinely heterogeneous, M07 +6.1 at t 4.5 against M09 -3.3). The reference explains much of it: women's median closed phase is 1.3-2.7 ms against men's 3.3-6.1, and the per-frame error SD against the closed-phase reference is 70-160 Hz for women against 20-26 Hz for men. Within a woman, a -2 Hz effect sits under a reference noise five times men's; the test was limited more by the reference than by the speaker count.

## D-077 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: the estimator on three KL renders, listed below, no model): does the cycle-mean pole predict KL's whole-cycle acoustic response, and does v3.1 follow that response?

*Status*: done (2026-11-04): both narrowly not, see Result. The outside review's experiment A (Hannah, 2026-11-04): D-074 compared the teacher with quasi-static poles, not with what the time-varying system actually does to the sound.

- *What the response is*: the transfer from the glottal flow to the lip flow is the closed-glottis tract's at every gap (seen: /A/ female 887 Hz coupled and rigid alike, 889 at gap 0.3), because U_lips / U_g depends only on the tract. Coupling acts by reshaping the flow itself. So the whole-cycle response an analyser sees is defined against a source without the tract's feedback: **H_eff = S(U_g uncoupled, out coupled) / S(U_g uncoupled)**, the coupled render's output against the flow the same glottal area gives with no acoustic load (`kl.render(..., coupled=False)`, which also drops the subglottal tract). Cross-spectra over the whole 1 s render (Welch, 4096 points), bins with coherence > 0.95 between 0.55 and 1.6 x the label F1, the radiation's d/dt divided out, one resonance plus a spectral slope fitted to log |H|. **F_eff** is its F1.
- *Estimator check, per file*: the same fit on the same flow through a rigid glottis (`flow=`, an LTI system with the label's poles) gives **F_rig**. The shift **dE = F_eff - F_rig** cancels the estimator's own bias (seen: F_rig 1 % below the label at /A/, 2 % above at /i/).
- *Seen beforehand* (female /A/ 220 Hz, female /i/ 196, male /A/ 110; gap 0 / 0.3): F_eff 892 / 976, 359 / 375, 791 / 853 Hz; F_rig 887, 343, 793. dE grows +84, +16, +62 Hz from gap 0 to 0.3, against CM's growth of +17, +6, +22 and the teacher's mean growth for those vowels of +44, +6, +37.
- *Renders*: D-060's grid exactly (ten Story vowels, 8 f0 per sex with 1-semitone 5 Hz vibrato, gaps 0 / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3; 640 files), each rendered three ways: coupled (D-060's render), uncoupled, rigid with the uncoupled flow. Files with fewer than 15 usable bins or a fit at its bounds are left out and counted.
- *Measures*, per sex, as D-074 (growth residual per (vowel, f0) pair, gap 0.3 - 0, median over pairs with a 2000-resample bootstrap; level residual per file, median by gap): (1) **CM against the response**: dE - CM; (2) **the teacher against the response**: teacher error (D-060's per-file seed mean of v3.1) - dE. Reported: CL against dE; the fitted bandwidth's change with the gap; Spearman over vowels.
- *Rule*: (1) **CM predicts the response** if in both sexes the growth residual's interval contains 0, its median is within +-5 Hz, and the level residual's median over all gaps is within +-10 Hz; otherwise **does not** (and CL is reported in its place). (2) **v3.1 follows the response** if in both sexes the teacher's growth residual against dE meets the same +-5 Hz test; otherwise **does not**, with the residual's sign saying over or under.
- *Reading*: (1) yes: the cycle-mean quasi-static pole is a physically real description of what KL sounds like, and can serve as a label. No: it predicts the teacher (D-074) without being the acoustic response; the response is then the better candidate. (2) yes: v3.1 reads the acoustic F1 of coupled phonation; its KL overread against the closed-glottis label is the definition. No, under: it partly sees through coupling (towards the label). No, over: it overshoots even the response.
- *Caveats*: a periodically time-varying system has no single transfer function; H_eff is its cycle-averaged linear part, sampled at harmonics swept by the vibrato. The uncoupled source is one choice of "the source without feedback" (inverse filtering makes the same choice implicitly). KL only; the interactive tube is not part of this entry.
- Script `scripts/analysis/d077_kl_response.py` (CPU); report `reports/analysis/d077/`.
- *Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d077/d077.txt`)*: **(1) CM does not predict the response; (2) v3.1 does not follow it (under)**, both narrowly. 36 of 640 files left out (mostly close vowels at high f0: too few coherent bins near F1); the estimator check reads the rigid tract within 1 Hz of the label (median -0.4 men, -0.6 women).
  - (1) dE - CM: growth men +2.1 [-6.6, +18.8], women -1.9 [-6.1, +0.9] (both within +-5); level men -1.0, women **+13.0** (over the +-10 allowed: the only failing part). CL against dE: growth -4.9 / -4.9, level +16 / +33.
  - (2) teacher - dE: growth men **-5.6 [-12.1, +2.8]** (median 0.6 Hz past the +-5), women -0.7 [-4.5, +3.2] (within); level men +6.9, women +17.3.
  - Reported: Spearman over vowels of the teacher's growth against dE's, **men +0.93, women +0.73**; dE's growth against CM's +0.65 / +0.79. H_eff's fitted B1 widens with the gap (men 50 -> 124, women 96 -> 126 Hz; rigid 72 / 75).
  - *Reading*: the measured acoustic response of coupled KL phonation grows with the gap about as CM does (growth residuals within 2 Hz in both sexes), and v3.1's growth follows the response closely across vowels (rho +0.93 / +0.73); each rule fails on one part, by a small margin. So the gap growth is close to the acoustic F1 of coupled phonation, which the cycle-mean quasi-static pole approximates. What the response does not explain is a level: v3.1 reads women's KL renders about 17 Hz above even the acoustic response at every gap (men about 7). That is the error part of the KL overread, consistent with D-074's 10-15 Hz, now measured against the sound itself. My expectation before the run, from three files (CM far below the response, teacher under it), was wrong across the grid: /A/ is the exception, not the pattern.
- *Note (2026-11-04, after an outside review; no new results)*: the reading above says v3.1's growth "follows the response closely", and D-078's status says "D-077 shows it follows the acoustic response". Both say more than the result. Under the registered rule v3.1 **does not follow** the response: the men's growth residual is -5.6 Hz, just past the +-5 allowed. The Spearman correlations (+0.93 / +0.73) show the growth ranks the vowels as the response does, not that the two agree in size: v3.1 grows slightly less than the response in men, while its level sits above it in both sexes. Read both passages as "tracks the response's variation across vowels; agreement not shown". Likewise for CM: it predicts the gap-dependent growth, not the absolute response (women's level +13 Hz), so D-077 does not make CM a complete acoustic label. That leaves open which acoustic label to use, D-078's cycle-mean pole included. F_eff is an operational single-resonance fit to a time-varying system; whether it is stable under the fitting choices is in `considering.md` (Evaluation).

## D-078 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: the cascade response check below, no model): can the closed-glottis F1 be recovered from coupled phonation? Two frozen-feature heads on teacher v3.1's trunk

*Status*: registered (the data to render, the heads waiting for the GPU). Hannah, 2026-11-04, after D-077: the acoustic F1 is probably the more tractable target, so can a model learn the closed-glottis number at all? v3.1 has never been asked to: in `train_v2` there is no coupling, so the closed-glottis label and the acoustic F1 are the same number on every clip, and D-077 shows it follows the acoustic response. D-023's `train_v2c` models partly undid a weak coupling (median 4-12 Hz) at a cost to F2-F4; a realistic shift (D-075: +23 to +30 Hz median at scale 1) was never tried. This is `considering.md`'s effective-F1 head, step 1, with D-075's label in place of OQ x df.

- *Check before registering* (`scripts/analysis/d078_cascade_response.py`, 120 coupled cascade clips drawn as D-075, steady, D-060's vibrato, scale 1): D-077's response estimator against the cycle-mean pole. Rigid check -0.6 Hz. Response shift dE - CM median **+4.4** Hz (IQR -2.0 / +12.0), correlation +0.92, slope 1.19 through 0; dE - CL +14.2. So within the cascade the cycle-mean pole is close to the sound's own response (about a fifth low, probably the bandwidth's movement). Label gap |closed - CM|: median **27 Hz**, against v3.1's synthetic F1 MAE of about 21 Hz.
- *Data*: a new set **`train_v2k`**: `train_v2`'s settings with every clip a tube clip (`p_tube` 1) and D-023 coupling on every clip at scale 1 (`p_coupling` 1, `coupling_scale` (1, 1)); about 6000 training clips and 1000 held out, separate seeds; no resynthesised clips. CPU render.
- *Labels per frame*: **closed**, the closed-glottis poles (as every set); **acoustic**, the cycle-mean pole F (1 + df <a>), <a> the mean of the rendered opening over the frame's cycles (the cascade's own trajectory, so its true mean). F1 only differs between the two in the decision; F2-F4 carry their own cycle means and are reported.
- *Arms*: two heads of identical form (D-054's mlp formant head) on v3.1's frozen trunk (`d040_5b_s2_b20k`), one per label; D-054's recipe (AdamW 1e-3, 300 warm-up, cosine over 8000 steps) on `train_v2k` only; head seeds 1 and 2; four runs.
- *Measures*: on held-out `train_v2k`: each head's F1 MAE against its own label and against the other's. Reported: both heads on `train_v2`'s uncoupled validation clips and on the resynthesised validation set (does the closed head see through coupling, or subtract a coupling-sized offset everywhere?); PTDB-TUG dev, Hillenbrand dev and KL (D-060) F1 bias for each head, against the closed-phase reference, the label and D-077's response; F2-F4 likewise.
- *Rule*, seed means, both seeds the same way: let A = the acoustic head's F1 MAE against the closed label, C = the closed head's. **Separable** if C <= 0.7 A (the trunk carries information that moves the reading back towards the closed-glottis pole); **not separable** if C >= 0.9 A (the number is not recoverable from these features: the acoustic target by necessity as well as tractability); between, **partly**: reported, and an end-to-end arm is the next entry.
- *Reading*: the frozen trunk never saw coupling, so "not separable" is about these features, not about what end-to-end training could learn; "separable" makes a closed-glottis output possible in principle and puts the generality question (`train_v2`'s full mix, end to end, F2-F4 cost as in D-023) in the next entry. The uncoupled validation clips say whether a closed head generalises or merely shifts.
- *Caveats*: the coupling is the cascade's (linear in the opening, D-075: right in median, wrong per vowel in both directions; KL's response sits 13 Hz above CM for women, D-077); the acoustic label runs about a fifth below the cascade's own measured response.
- Scripts: render `scripts/analysis/d078_render.py`, heads `scripts/analysis/d078_heads.py`, decision `scripts/analysis/decide_d078.py`; chain `chains/d078.sh` after D-072 and D-073; report `reports/analysis/d078/`.

*Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d078/decide_d078.txt`, KL in `reports/analysis/d078/kl/`)*: **separable** by the rule, on both seeds alike: C = 20.5 Hz, A = 34.7 Hz, C / A = **0.591** (seeds 0.592, 0.590). Each head fits its own label equally well (closed 20.5 Hz against the closed label, acoustic 19.6 Hz against the acoustic one, signed medians within 1.5 Hz of zero), and each sits about 24-26 Hz from the other's label, close to the label gap (median 28.9 Hz on val_v2k). But the reported measures say the closed head mostly **shifts** rather than sees through coupling, which is the question the registration put to the uncoupled clips:

- *Uncoupled audio* (labels equal there): the heads still disagree by 15-19 Hz, in the same direction as on coupled audio (closed / acoustic signed median: `train_v2` val -7.5 / +11.8; VocalSet val -3.1 / +12.2; Zeroth val -1.6 / +16.7), against 24.4 Hz on val_v2k. So about two thirds to four fifths of the separation is a learned offset that does not depend on whether the audio is coupled; at most a third is read from the sound.
- *PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase bias* (women / men): closed head -7.0 / +1.7, acoustic head +13.9 / +15.5, v3.1 +4.2 / +5.7. Same pattern: a level moved down or up, women's and men's together.
- *KL* (D-060's files): against the closed label the closed head's error still grows with the gap (men +10.4 to +37.0, women +22.4 to +46.6 from gap 0 to 0.3), as v3.1's did; against D-077's measured response it is nearly flat (men +1.3 to -7.2, women +6.1 to +8.6). So on KL's physical coupling the closed head follows the acoustic response minus an offset, not the closed-glottis pole. (KL's coupling is not the cascade's, a registered caveat; but the cascade's is what the head was trained on, and it does not transfer.)
- *F2-F4* against the closed label: 25.6 / 22.4 / 35.5 Hz (closed head) against 52.1 / 91.5 / 207.1 (acoustic head); expected, since the acoustic label moves F2-F4 by their own cycle means.
- *Omitted*: Hillenbrand dev bias per head was registered as reported and was not scored (the chain had no Hillenbrand step); it is not needed for the rule and can be added from the checkpoints.

*Reading*: the rule's "separable" holds as measured, but the rule cannot tell recovery from an offset, and the reported measures point to an offset: on frozen v3.1 features the closed-glottis F1 is recovered mainly by subtracting a typical coupling shift, which fails where coupling is absent (uncoupled clips, real voices with complete closure) or different (KL). That supports Hannah's lean towards the **acoustic F1 as the target**: the closed-glottis number is not reliably readable from these features. The registered next step for "separable" (an end-to-end arm on `train_v2`'s full mix) would therefore test whether end-to-end training learns the per-clip part, with the uncoupled-clip disagreement as its main measure, not C / A; whether to run it is Hannah's call.

*Addendum (2026-11-04, after the result; `scripts/analysis/d078_per_clip.py`, `reports/analysis/d078/per_clip.txt`; from the scoring caches, no new runs)*: does the closed head's correction follow each clip's own gap? On val_v2k the heads' disagreement (acoustic minus closed reading, F1) against the label gap (acoustic minus closed label): slope 0.35 per frame [0.33, 0.36], 0.41 per clip (r 0.71), the same on both seeds. But a third of the gap (R² 0.34 on held-out crops) is predictable from the visible formant pattern alone (a boosted-tree fit of the gap on the acoustic label's F1-F4, which is the vowel's df), and the disagreement follows that part (slope 0.61) and hardly the rest (slope 0.09, r 0.12). The rest is the part set by the glottis (the opening's mean, through the source draws), two thirds of the gap's variance. So the closed head applies a constant plus a vowel-dependent offset and reads almost nothing of the glottal opening from these features. This was the question a half-coupled rerun (proposed 2026-11-04 as D-079, not registered) would have asked less directly: in the cascade a coupled and an uncoupled clip share the same source draws, so that rerun would test whether coupling's within-cycle pole movement is detectable, not whether its size is, and real voices are always coupled. Not registered; superseded by this addendum. Caveat (Hannah): v3.1 never had a reason to keep source cues (H1-H2, tilt, open quotient) in its features, since its training had no coupling, so "not in these features" may mean discarded, not absent from the sound; only an end-to-end arm can tell.

*Addendum 2 (2026-11-04, the registered Hillenbrand measure the main chain left out; `scripts/analysis/chains/d078_hill.sh`, `reports/analysis/d078/hillenbrand_signed.txt`, paired reports in `reports/analysis/d078/paired/`)*: Hillenbrand dev F1 bias, model minus reference, median over tokens, seed means (men / women / boys / girls): v3.1 +12.6 / +27.3 / +32.0 / +34.7; closed head +7.0 / +18.5 / +26.4 / +25.6; acoustic head +20.1 / +38.9 / +48.7 / +51.9. The same pattern as PTDB-TUG: a level moved down or up for every group, the women's excess over men about the same (v3.1 14.7, closed 11.5, acoustic 18.8 Hz). The heads move F2 and F3 too (closed -7 to -25 Hz; acoustic +28 to +83 Hz), since each head's F2-F4 learned its label's own coupling shift. Hillenbrand's reference is whole-vowel LPC, not closed-phase, so neither head is "right" here; it adds nothing that changes the reading.

## D-079 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: the checks below, no probe result): do trunks trained on coupled audio keep the glottal opening? Frozen probes across D-023's paired models

*Status*: registered (Hannah, 2026-11-04, after D-078's addendum; the fine-tune with an opening head is D-080, the invariance arm later). D-078: on v3.1's frozen features a closed-glottis F1 head follows the gap's vowel part (slope 0.61) and hardly its glottal part (0.09). v3.1 never trained on coupling, so it may have discarded the source cues. D-023's small Conformers come in pairs that differ only in whether coupled audio was in training (same recipe, seeds, steps), so they ask that directly, without training anything but a probe.

- *Check before registering* (`scripts/analysis/d079_opening_share.py`, 200 clips drawn as train_v2k, seed 7902): <a> per voiced frame has mean 0.53, SD 0.17; with the clip's gap offset fixed at its midpoint (every other draw the same), 98 % of its variance remains, and the offset's own part is 14 %; 87 % of the variance is between clips. So <a> is set mostly by the open phase (Rd), which also shapes the source spectrum (H1-H2, tilt): largely visible in the sound, not only through coupling's pole movement. Ceiling (`d079_opening_share.py ceiling`): the frame's Rd label alone predicts <a> with R² 0.75 on val_v2ko's voiced frames (boosted trees fitted on the probe's training clips); what the source shape can expose, before any coupling cue. Probe smoke (20 clips, 50 steps, no R² read): the encoder can return a few frames fewer than a clip's labels, so each clip is trimmed to the shorter.
- *Data*: `train_v2ko` / `val_v2ko`, D-078's train_v2k / val_v2k re-rendered with the same settings and seeds plus the opening label `glottal_opening` (<a> per frame, the mean D-078's acoustic label already used; `SynthConfig.acoustic_labels`); audio and every existing label checked identical to D-078's render (`scripts/analysis/d079_render.py`). Probe training: the first 3000 clips of train_v2ko; scoring: all 1000 of val_v2ko; voiced frames, whole clips, clean audio.
- *Trunks* (frozen; the encoder's per-frame 128-d features, `Teacher.enc`):
  - **mix**: `runs/small/mix_conformer_s{0,1,2}_k35` (train_v2, no coupling);
  - **v2c2**: `runs/small/v2c2_conformer_s{0,1,2}_k35` (train_v2c2: coupling on every tube clip, 60 % of synthetic clips; F1 / B1 at full strength, F2-F5 scaled 0-1);
  - **v2c**: `runs/small/v2c_conformer_s{0,1,2}_k35` (train_v2c: every pole scaled 0-1; reported);
  - **v3.1**: `d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k` (reported; another generation, architecture and recipe).
  Same recipe within the small models (S2 mixed-crop Conformer, 2000 steps then branched to 3500, lr 1e-3, batch 32, no resynthesised sets).
- *Probe*: an opening head of the VTL head's form (LayerNorm -> 256 -> GELU -> 256 -> GELU -> 1, sigmoid), L1 on <a>, AdamW 1e-3 one-cycle over 4000 steps of 8192 frames, head seeds 1 and 2; plus a ridge fit (reported). `scripts/analysis/d079_probe.py`.
- *Measure*: R² of <a> on val_v2ko voiced frames, head-seed mean; per clip (clip means) reported.
- *Rule* (v2c2 against mix, paired by trunk seed): let D = R²(v2c2) - R²(mix).
  - **kept**: D >= 0.10 on the seed mean, with all three seed pairs positive: training on coupled audio makes a trunk keep the opening;
  - **not kept**: D < 0.03 on the seed mean, or the pairs split in sign;
  - between: partly.
  - Independently, **carried without coupling** if mix's R² >= 0.5 (seed mean): the trunk keeps the opening anyway.
- *Reading*: kept: the opening is readable from the sound (as the cascade renders it) and coupled training keeps it; D-080's fine-tune is expected to work. Carried without coupling: v3.1-style failures are about the F1 head not using it, so D-080 leans to the use side. Neither: unclear, since D-023's coupling was weak (median shift 4-12 Hz) and nothing trained on the opening directly; D-080 still runs. v3.1's R² against mix's says whether v3.1's trunk lost what the small ones keep (reported, confounded by generation).
- *Caveats*: the cascade's coupling only; all small trunks trained on clips like these (tube and cascade, train_v2 settings), v2c / v2c2 on coupled ones at a weaker strength than train_v2ko's (scale 1), so v2c2's advantage, if any, includes having seen this kind of audio. A probe measures what a frozen trunk exposes to a small head, not what a trunk could learn.
- Cost: render done (both sets identical to D-078's on every file, opening label on every frame); features for 11 trunks, a few minutes each; fits a few minutes each.
- Scripts: `scripts/analysis/d079_render.py`, `d079_opening_share.py`, `d079_probe.py`, decision `decide_d079.py`; chain `chains/d079.sh`; report `reports/analysis/d079/`.

*Result (2026-11-04; `reports/analysis/d079/decide_d079.txt`, per-trunk fits beside it)*: **not kept; not carried without coupling.** Every trunk exposes about the same small share of the opening: frame R² (head-seed means) mix 0.179 (0.191 / 0.194 / 0.150 by seed), v2c2 0.182, v2c 0.176, v3.1 0.189 (0.172 / 0.206); D = v2c2 - mix by seed +0.011, -0.008, +0.006 (mean +0.003), the pairs split in sign. Head seeds agree to 0.01, so the trunk seed is the variation (about 0.04 between seeds of one kind, ten times D). Per clip (clip means) R² is 0.42-0.50 everywhere; ridge 0.09-0.15. Against the ceiling (the frame's Rd label alone, 0.75), the trunks expose about a quarter per frame and well over half between clips.

*Reading* (as registered: neither, so unclear): D-023's coupled training did not make the small trunks keep any more of the opening than their uncoupled siblings, and v3.1, another generation, sits at the same level. So the "discarded" question gets no help here: every trunk, coupled or not, carries the opening only coarsely (a per-clip impression, little frame by frame), which fits D-078's 0.09 glottal slope. D-023's coupling was weak (median 4-12 Hz) and no trunk was trained on the opening, so this does not show the sound lacks it; D-080 (the fine-tune with an opening head) is the test that can. The opening's frame-level visibility in a trunk trained for it is D-080's first measure, with these numbers (about 0.18 frame, 0.45 clip) as the baseline to beat. The chain took 20 minutes alone on the GPU.

*Note (2026-11-04, Hannah, after the result)*: two more confounds on the small trunks: capacity (1.9 M parameters against v3.1's 4.2 M; a layer may be too narrow for the opening) and training length (3500 steps in all, warm-up and decay included, so possibly far from converged). Both weaken "not kept" further: weak coupling, small trunks, short training. v3.1 (4.2 M, 20000 steps) sitting at the same frame R² (0.189) speaks against capacity and length explaining the common ~0.18 level, but not against them hiding a coupled-training effect, since no large or long-trained coupled trunk exists to compare.

## D-080 (registered 2026-11-04; seen beforehand: D-079, a 60-step smoke branch (mechanics and loss balance only), no arm result): a glottal-opening head on v3.1's final stage, with coupled audio in the mix

*Status*: done (2026-11-05): the opening is learned, but only as an Rd proxy; the F1 head does not use it; guards failed (see Result). Registered (Hannah, 2026-11-04, after D-079). D-078: v3.1's frozen features give a closed-glottis F1 head only the gap's vowel part (glottal slope 0.09). D-079: no trunk, coupled-trained or not, exposes more than about 0.18 of the frame's mean glottal opening <a> (about 0.45 per clip), against 0.75 from the frame's Rd alone; unclear, because D-023's coupling was weak, its trunks small and short-trained, and nothing was ever trained on the opening. This entry trains on it: does a trunk that must predict <a> learn it from the sound, and does the F1 head then use it to read the closed-glottis F1?

- *Design: v3.1's own last stage, re-run with one change per arm.* v3.1 (`d040_5b_s{1,2}_b20k`) is a decay branch: from `d040_5b_s{1,2}/ckpt_016000.pt`, 4000 steps of the wsd decay (Muon + Adan at 2e-3, D-030 / D-040's recipe) to step 20000. Both arms are the same branch from the same checkpoint, so v3.1 itself is the unchanged reference, same schedule and steps:
  - **A0**: the coupled set added to the mix (below); nothing else.
  - **A1**: A0 plus the opening head.
  Branch seeds 1 and 2, each from its own seed's checkpoint (D-079: trunk seeds differ by about 0.04 R², ten times D-079's effect). Four runs.
- *Data*: v3.1's mix (train_v2; the resynthesised VocalSet and Zeroth sets) plus a coupled set as `synth_extra` (D-051's mechanism) at **42 % of items**, D-023's coupled share (`train_v2c` coupled every tube clip: 60 % of the 70 % synthetic share). With `synth_extra_frac` 0.42 the rest scales by 0.58, so `resynth_frac` goes to 0.517 to keep the resynthesised share at 30 %; train_v2 takes the remaining 28 %. Closed-glottis formant labels everywhere: v3.1's objective is unchanged, coupled clips simply appear.
  - The coupled set: **`train_v2kb`**, 20000 clips with train_v2ko's settings (every clip a tube clip, coupling at strength 1, opening label) and seed (78), so its first 6000 clips are train_v2ko's; at 42 % of 4000 x 32 items each clip is seen about three times, not nine. CPU render, about 2.5 h. Held-out scoring on `val_v2ko` (D-079's).
  - Strength 1, not D-023's 0-1 draw: at 1 the F1 shift is set by the vowel's df and <a> alone, so the opening label fully determines the glottal part; a hidden per-clip strength would cap the use measure for reasons that aren't the model's (D-075: strength 1 matches quasi-static physics).
- *Opening head* (A1): the VTL head's form on the formant trunk's per-frame vector (LayerNorm -> 256 -> GELU -> 256 -> GELU -> 1, sigmoid for <a> in (0, 1)), added at the branch in its own AdamW (weight decay 0.01) at `opening_lr` 1e-3 x the model's schedule (D-072's side-path pattern): the trunk detached for 300 steps, then the gradient gate ramped to 1 over 700. Loss L1 on <a> x 10, weight 1; frames without the label (uncoupled, resynthesised) carry none. Weight check (60-step smoke branch): the opening term is about 1.3 of a total near 5.6 (formants 2.6, f0 2.0), about 23 %. The draft's 'comparable with the VTL head' criterion is dropped (the VTL head's registered weight, 0.0125, makes its term about 0.04); weight 1 is kept, since the first question is learnability, which a weak weight would bias towards 'not learned', and the formant guard catches a cost.
- *Measures*, on val_v2ko (fixed crops, clean, D-078's scoring), branch-seed means with crop-bootstrap intervals:
  1. *Learned*: A1's opening head R² for <a> on voiced frames (per clip reported). Baselines: D-079's frozen probes (v3.1 0.19 frame, 0.48 clip); ceiling from Rd alone 0.75.
     Reported (Hannah, 2026-11-04: the vowel contrast that reveals <a> through F1 may be missing within a short context): A1's opening R² on fixed crops of 400, 100 and 50 frames, and by tercile of the crop's F1 spread (10-90 % range, cents). Within a val_v2ko clip F1 spans a median ~980 cents while <a> varies little (SD 0.035 against 0.17 overall), so whole clips carry the contrast; training crops run 32-400 frames. R² rising with length and spread points to the contrast route, flat to the source-shape route.
  2. *Used*: the F1 reading's glottal slope, D-078's addendum method: (acoustic label - predicted F1) against the label gap, the gap split into its formant-predictable part (one boosted-tree fit on half the crops' labels, frozen for every model) and the glottal rest; slope on the rest, A1 against A0. 1 = full recovery; D-078's frozen closed head 0.09. v3.1's own slope reported (its F1 was never trained on coupled clips).
- *Rule* (both branch seeds the same way):
  - (1) **learned** if A1's frame R² >= 0.5; **not learned** if <= 0.3 (D-079's frozen v3.1: 0.19); between, partly.
  - (2) **used** if A1's glottal slope exceeds A0's by >= 0.2, the crop-bootstrap interval of the difference above 0; **not used** if the difference is < 0.1; between, partly.
- *Guards* (A1 against A0; A0 against v3.1 reported): train_v2 validation F1-F4 MAE within +3 %; signed F1 on uncoupled train_v2 validation within +-3 Hz of A0's (no global offset, D-078's failure); resynthesised validation sets; f0 RPA, voicing, VTL as usual. Reported: Hillenbrand dev and PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase F1 bias by sex (this time in the chain); KL (D-060's files) against the closed label and D-077's response.
- *Reading*: learned and used: v3.1's trunk had discarded what the sound carries, and a closed-glottis F1 is readable on the cascade's coupling; the next questions are KL and real voices. Learned, not used: the information is there but the F1 head doesn't draw on it with these labels; the physics route (acoustic F1 / (1 + df <a>)) becomes the candidate, with the opening head as its input. Not learned: in the cascade's renders the frame's opening isn't recoverable from these features even when trained for; the acoustic F1 is the target by necessity (a from-scratch run, or a different front end, the only routes left). A0 against v3.1 says what coupled clips with closed-glottis labels do on their own (D-078 predicts an offset).
- *Caveats*: the cascade's coupling (quasi-static, linear in the opening; D-075, D-077); the opening's gap-offset part (14 % of its variance, D-079's check) is visible only through that coupling. A fine-tune starts from a trunk that may have discarded the cues; 4000 steps may not be enough to recover them, so "not learned" here is weaker than "learned" (Hannah, 2026-11-04). D-079's small-trunk confounds (capacity, training length) do not apply: this is v3.1's own size and training.
- *Not in this entry*: the invariance arm (an adversary keeping F1 information out of the opening head), a later decision.
- Cost: render about 2.5 h (CPU); four branch runs at about 1.6 h each (1.42 s per step), about 6.5 h; scoring about 1 h alone on the GPU. Plumbing: the opening head and its loss (TeacherConfig / Teacher / train), the label served by SynthCrops (NaN where absent), tests.
- Scripts: render `scripts/analysis/d080_render.py`, scoring `d080_score.py`, decision `decide_d080.py`; chain `chains/d080.sh` (the CLI's branch path with `synth_extra=data/synth/train_v2kb`, `synth_extra_frac=0.42`, `resynth_frac=0.517`; A1 adds `opening=true`, `opening_lr=0.001`); report `reports/analysis/d080/`. Code: `TeacherConfig.opening_head`, `TrainConfig.opening*`, the loss's "opening" term, `SynthCrops(opening=...)`, the evaluation's opening R²; test `tests/test_models.py::test_opening_head_gate_loss_and_label`.
- *Amendment (2026-11-04, after an outside review; no results seen: the first branch run had just started, nothing scored). Reported measures only; the rule, guards and arms are unchanged.* For A1's opening head on val_v2ko's 400-frame crops, per seed: (a) R² **within crops** (each crop's mean removed from label and prediction) and **between crops** (crop means). Only about 13 % of <a>'s variance is within clips (D-079's check). So D-079's frame-against-clip gap (0.18 against 0.45) means its frame predictions were noisy around their own clip mean, not that they missed the within-clip changes. Splitting the two parts shows which one a gain comes from. (b) R² **beyond Rd**: an Rd-only boosted-tree fit of <a> on even crops; on odd crops, R² of (label - fit) by (prediction - fit), with the Rd-only fit's own R² there (D-079's ceiling, 0.75, was fitted on train_v2ko). Rd largely sets the opening, here and in real voices, so reading it through the source's shape is legitimate. But the part that differs between voices with incomplete closure is the clip's gap offset, which Rd does not give. A head that scores well overall and near 0 beyond Rd has learned a phonation-type proxy. `d080_score.py` now also saves the Rd label; `decide_d080.py`'s `opening_parts`.
- *Result (2026-11-05; `reports/analysis/d080/decide_d080.txt`; Hillenbrand `hillenbrand_signed.txt` and `paired/`; KL by arm `kl_by_arm.txt` from `scripts/analysis/d080_kl_arms.py`, because the chain's `kl/main.txt` pools all four runs)*: **(1) learned; (2) not used; guards failed** (A1 against A0), on both seeds alike.
  - (1) A1's opening R² 0.658 / 0.662 (seeds 1 / 2). It is flat across crop length (400 / 100 / 50 frames: 0.660 / 0.658 / 0.646) and across the crop's F1 spread (terciles 0.630 / 0.690 / 0.672). So a short context or a narrow vowel range costs nothing, and Hannah's within-clip vowel-contrast idea gets no support here. Amendment measures: within crops 0.19 / 0.23, between crops 0.72 / 0.72, **beyond Rd -0.33 / -0.32**. An Rd-only fit on the same crops scores 0.716, more than the head. The head has learned the phonation type, the part of the opening that Rd sets, and less exactly than Rd itself. It holds nothing of the clip's gap offset beyond that, the part coupling makes audible. Caveat: the Rd-only fit uses the true Rd, which a model must itself read from the audio. So a negative score says the head's departures from Rd's prediction don't follow the offset; it does not prove the audio lacks it.
  - (2) Glottal slope: A0 +0.557 / +0.542, A1 +0.534 / +0.517, v3.1 +0.346 / +0.354. A1 - A0 is -0.023 [-0.044, -0.004] and -0.025 [-0.044, -0.008]: the head lowers the slope slightly instead of raising it.
  - Guards: train_v2 validation F1-F4 MAE, A1 against A0, +6.7 / +3.2 / +3.5 / +5.9 % (seed 1) and +8.8 / +8.3 / +5.8 / +8.4 % (seed 2), over the +3 % allowed. The opening loss at weight 1 (about 23 % of the total) costs formant accuracy. Signed F1 on uncoupled validation passes (A1 -2.9 / -2.5, A0 -3.0 / -2.6 Hz).
  - *Reported, A0 against v3.1 (not part of the rule; A0 changes the data, so this is the coupled set's own effect)*. On val_v2ko (seed 1), v3.1 reads 0.60 of the closed-to-acoustic label gap: signed +13.8 Hz against the closed label, -9.6 against the acoustic one. So v3.1's 0.35 slope means it only partly follows the cascade's coupling, not that it partly recovers the closed F1. A0 reads 0.32 of the gap (+1.1 / -25.3 Hz; mean |error| against the closed label 17.4 Hz against v3.1's 26.6). Costs: on uncoupled validation A0's signed F1 moves -3.0 / -2.6 Hz from v3.1's -0.3 / +0.1 (a global shift, D-078's pattern but smaller); train_v2 validation F1 MAE 23.2 against v3.1's 20.6 Hz (seed 1).
  - Real voices (seed means; A0 / A1 / v3.1). PTDB-TUG dev closed-phase F1 bias, women: -0.1 / +0.3 / +4.2 Hz; men: +3.7 / +3.3 / +5.7 Hz. Hillenbrand dev signed F1 (men, women, boys, girls): A0 +9.6 / +20.7 / +27.0 / +27.9; A1 +9.7 / +19.8 / +23.3 / +26.5; v3.1 +12.6 / +27.3 / +32.0 / +34.7. Women's excess over men: 11.1 / 10.1 / 14.7 Hz. Paired against v3.1:
    - F1 MAE, all speakers: A0 -3.0 / -3.6 Hz, A1 -3.8 / -5.3 Hz.
    - F1 MAE, women: A0 -4.6 / -5.5 Hz, A1 -5.9 / -7.9 Hz.
    - F4 MAE is worse: A0 +10.4 / +7.3 Hz, A1 +24.3 / +5.7 Hz.

    Men move by about the uncoupled offset (-3 Hz); women and children move about twice as far.
  - KL: the gap growth (gap 0.3 - 0, against the closed label) is unchanged. Men: A0 +17.9, A1 +17.4, v3.1 +19.0 Hz. Women: +16.5, +12.6, +15.0 Hz. The intervals overlap throughout. What the cascade's coupling teaches does not transfer to KL's physical coupling, as in D-078.
  - *Reading*: given the opening as a target, the trunk learns what Rd predicts, the phonation type, and not the gap offset that coupling makes audible. The F1 head draws nothing extra from it, and the head costs formant accuracy. This and D-079 together say that the opening's coupling-specific part is not what these models extract, whether frozen, probed or fine-tuned with a direct target. Coupled training data alone (A0) moves F1 readings on the cascade towards the closed label and improves real-voice F1, women's most (the overread's direction). But the gain comes with a global offset on uncoupled audio, a worse F4, a loss on train_v2 validation and no change on KL. So it is not shown to be coupling recovery; the improvement could be a calibration that happens to suit real voices. Adopting a coupled set would need its own registration, with these costs as guards. The invariance arm (deferred) has little to stand on: the head carries almost no gap-offset information to protect.

## D-081 (registered 2026-11-05; seen beforehand: D-080, and the code paths below, no oracle result): an oracle battery before the screening run. Could each candidate help with perfect information, and is the gap offset in the input at all?

*Status*: done (2026-11-05): the offset is audible; the opening alone does not make the closed F1 usable, the opening with df does; iVTL context helps aVTL; the aperiodicity and source heads do not help (see Result). Registered (Hannah, 2026-11-05, after D-080 and an advisor review). Hannah proposes a one-off screening run that relaxes many constraints at once: a wider trunk; long crops of up to 16 s; heads for the opening, spectral slope, the instantaneous formants and length, amplitude, and jitter / shimmer / aperiodicity; and possibly an arm of earlier changes that were neutral but not ruled out. Its questions: is the closed-phase F1 learnable at all (D-078 to D-080), does the aVTL / iVTL split need more context, and does anything we have thought of capture the missing information? A negative screening result can't be told apart from "impossible", and nothing says what a good result would look like. This battery supplies both, cheaply, before any long run. It follows Hannah's ordering: **could it help?** (oracle) -> **is it learnable?** -> **does it help?** (training). Whether this pre-check is worth making a standing rule is judged by this entry's own outcome (below).
- *Code-path findings (before any run)*: in the cascade (`voice._voiced_coupled`) the gap offset does not touch the source. It sets the opening a(t), and each coupled pole moves within the cycle as F (1 + df a(t)) and B (1 + (bm - 1) a(t)). So the offset can only be heard through (i) the cycle-mean shift and widening, which a different closed tract can mimic, and (ii) the within-cycle pole movement. The VQT's windows (up to 80 ms) average (ii) over many cycles. **Aspiration noise is filtered by the closed-glottis tract** (`tract_filter(x, traj)` with the uncoupled poles). That is a direct cue to the closed F1 that real voices, whose aspiration passes through the coupled tract, don't have: a cascade artefact.
- *Labels (no new draws, only with `acoustic_labels`; tested)*: `bandwidths_acoustic` (B (1 + (bm - 1) <a>)), `coupling_df`, `coupling_bm`, `gap_offset`, `open_quotient`. `d081_labels.py` regenerates train_v2ko's first 3000 clips and val_v2ko with these labels. The audio is identical (same config and seeds; checked against the flat labels), so D-079's cached v3.1 features (`cache/d079/v31_s{1,2}_*.npz`, `Teacher.enc`, 128-d) line up frame for frame. Clip-level jitter, shimmer and HNR come from the clip's meta.
- **I. Is the offset audible?** (`d081_identify.py`, CPU). 60 coupled cascade clips at train_v2ko's settings (seed 8101), each rendered in matched variants by forcing the clip's gap offset (the same draws otherwise):
  - R: offset -0.3;
  - U: offset +0.1 (the closed tract unchanged);
  - MF: offset +0.1, with the closed poles rescaled per control point so the cycle-mean acoustic F1-F4 equal R's;
  - MFB: MF with the bandwidths matched too;
  - references: R with F1 x 1.01 and x 1.03.

  The rescaled tract is a legitimate cascade render: aspiration goes through it too. Measure: RMS difference of the VQT log power (dB) against R on voiced frames, after removing the clip's mean level difference, over all bins and below 1.5 kHz; median over clips. Everything is done twice, with the clip's aspiration and without it (`hnr_db` None).
  - *Rule, on the variant without aspiration (the physical cue)*: **audible** if MFB's difference is at least that of F1 x 1.01; **not audible** if it is under a quarter of it; otherwise **weak**. Reported: MF / U and MFB / U, below 1.5 kHz, and the variant with aspiration. If the offset is audible only with aspiration, the cascade teaches the closed F1 through an artefact; flagged.
  - The contrast (-0.3 to +0.1) is the whole drawn range, so this is an upper bound on audibility.
- **A. Label-only fits** (`d081_label_oracle.py`, CPU; boosted trees fitted on the 3000 training clips, scored on val_v2ko voiced frames):
  - A1, *the measure*: the glottal slope (D-078's addendum, with the frame's clip as the item) with p = the closed label (should give 1), p = the acoustic label (0), and p = the acoustic label minus the vowel part of the gap (0). **Valid** if these come out within +-0.05 of 1 / 0 / 0; otherwise the slope is broken and A2 and B1 are not read.
  - A2, *the statistical ceiling*: the closed F1 from perfect acoustic-domain labels (acoustic F1-F4 and B1-B4, f0, Rd, open quotient), and the same without the bandwidths. Glottal slope of each; R² of the clip's gap offset from the same inputs; R² of <a> beyond Rd. **Recoverable in principle** if the full set's slope is >= 0.5; **not** if < 0.2; between, partly.
  - A3, *context for aVTL*: the anatomical length (`length_cm`) from the iVTL label at the frame, plus the iVTL track's min / mean / max and 10 / 90 % quantiles over centred windows of 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1 and 2 s (clips are 2-4 s, so the 16 s question is out of reach here), plus acoustic F1-F4. MAE (cm) and R² by window. **Context helps** if the 2 s window's MAE is at least 20 % below the frame-only one; reported as a curve.
- **B. Oracle-input heads on v3.1's frozen features** (`d081_oracle_heads.py`, GPU, minutes). D-079's head form (LayerNorm -> 256 -> GELU -> 256 -> GELU -> out), with the oracle quantity standardised and concatenated to h. L1 on standardised targets, 4000 steps, head seeds 1 and 2, both v3.1 trunks. Trained on the 3000 training clips' sonorant frames, scored on val_v2ko.
  - B1, closed F1: h; h + <a>; h + <a> + df1. Glottal slope as A1. **Usable** if h + <a> reaches >= 0.7 and beats h by >= 0.3 on both trunks; **not usable** if it beats h by < 0.1.
  - B2, closed F1: h + acoustic B1 (true). **Helps** if the slope beats h by >= 0.2 on both trunks.
  - B3, closed F1-F4 (log Hz): h + the clip's jitter, shimmer and HNR. **Helps** if the mean MAE over F1-F4 falls by >= 5 % on both trunks, beyond 3 x the head-seed spread.
  - B4, closed B1-B4 (log): h + Rd + open quotient; the same test.
  - B5, anatomical length (log cm): h + iVTL; the same test.
- *What follows*:
  - The closed-F1 question goes into the screening run only if I says audible (without aspiration), A1 is valid and B1 is usable. If A2 says not recoverable, the question is closed for the cascade's coupling.
  - A candidate head goes into the screening run only if its B test helps (or its role is the renderer's, said in the screening registration). A3's curve sets whether long crops are motivated by the aVTL split.
  - The oracle results become the screening run's targets: the B ceilings are the "how close did it get" landmarks.
- *Is the oracle step worth keeping?* Judged after the battery. **Useful** if it settles something the screening run alone could not: it drops a candidate, decides the closed-F1 question's inclusion, or gives a ceiling that changes how a measure is read. Otherwise not adopted as a standing pre-check.
- *Caveats*: synthetic labels only. A cue that is audible in the cascade may not exist in real voices, and the reverse (KL's coupling is not the cascade's, D-077). Frozen v3.1 features bound what this trunk exposes, not what a trained trunk could. An oracle bound is necessary, not sufficient.
- Cost: label regeneration about 30 min and identification about 30 min (CPU, together); B heads about 30-45 min (GPU, alone).
- Scripts: `scripts/analysis/d081_labels.py`, `d081_identify.py`, `d081_label_oracle.py`, `d081_oracle_heads.py`, `decide_d081.py`; reports `reports/analysis/d081/`.
- *Amendment (2026-11-05, after a smoke test of the identification script on 4 clips, before the full run; the rule is unchanged)*: on those clips MFB still differed from R by 1.3-5.8 dB against 0.16-0.66 dB for F1 x 1.01. That raised the possibility that the cycle-mean pole is just the wrong match: D-077 showed a time-varying pole is not heard at its cycle mean. Added as reported: **MFB\***, the closed F1 and B1 that bring the +0.1 render closest to R (all bins), searched on top of MFB (F1 x s in [0.9, 1.1], B1 x b in [0.5, 2], then s again). On 2 smoke clips it barely helped (1.25 and 1.47 dB against 0.28 and 0.38).
- *Note (2026-11-05, after the full identification run, before reading any other result)*: 5 of the 60 clips per variant had no voiced frames, which made every median NaN. The script's first summary therefore printed "weak" from NaN comparisons. Those clips are now left out and counted (`d081_identify.py summary`, from the saved per-clip JSON; nothing re-rendered). This is a data filter, not a choice made on the results.
- *Result (2026-11-05; `reports/analysis/d081/`: `decide_d081.txt`, `identify.txt`, `label_oracle.txt`, `heads_v31_s{1,2}.json`)*: **I audible; A1 valid; A2 partly; B1 not usable (by the rule); B2, B3, B4 no help; B5 helps; A3 context helps.**
  - I (55 voiced clips). Without aspiration, MFB differs from R by 1.47 dB (median, all bins), against 0.36 for F1 x 1.01 and 1.02 for F1 x 1.03. That is about 4x a 1 % F1 change, as large as a 4 % F1 error (per clip 4.2x; below 1.5 kHz 2.5x). The best-fit F1 / B1 on top (MFB\*) removes none of it (1.47 dB; F1 x 0.999, B1 x 0.94). Matching the cycle-mean tract removes only about a third of the offset's effect (MFB / U 0.64). The rest is the within-cycle pole movement, which no static tract mimics. With aspiration, MFB / U is 0.98: the closed-tract aspiration adds its own cue, the artefact named above.
  - A1: the slope reads 0.97 / 0.00 / -0.03 for the closed label, the acoustic label, and acoustic minus the vowel part. Valid.
  - A2: from perfect acoustic-domain labels (acoustic F1-F4, B1-B4, f0, Rd, OQ), the closed F1's glottal slope is 0.34 (0.27 without the bandwidths). The clip's gap offset can't be predicted from them (R² -0.09 per frame, 0.04 per clip mean), and <a> beyond Rd reaches 0.07. So cycle-mean summaries, however perfect, imply only a third of the glottal part.
  - B1 (glottal slope, seed means, trunks 1 / 2): h +0.472 / +0.469; h + <a> +0.524 / +0.503 (by the rule, not usable: +0.05 and +0.03); **h + <a> + df1 +1.073 / +1.052**. The opening alone barely helps. The opening together with the clip's coupling sensitivity df1 gives full recovery. Reported: v3.1's frozen h alone (0.47) exceeds the perfect-label ceiling (A2, 0.34). Some of the within-cycle signature is in its features, beyond any cycle-mean quantity. (The fitters differ, boosted trees against an MLP, so this is suggestive.)
  - B2: the true acoustic B1 adds +0.08 / +0.07 to the slope; no help.
  - B3: jitter, shimmer and HNR change F1-F4 MAE by -0.3 / -0.5 %; no help.
  - B4: Rd and OQ change B1-B4 MAE by -2.3 / -1.4 %; no help.
  - B5: iVTL lowers the aVTL MAE by 19.8 / 19.4 % (0.157 -> 0.126 cm; seed spread 0.001); helps.
  - A3: the aVTL MAE falls steadily with iVTL context, 0.194 (frame) -> 0.147 / 0.127 / 0.108 / 0.091 cm at 0.25 / 0.5 / 1 / 2 s (-53 %), with no plateau by 2 s. Clips are at most 4 s long, so whether it keeps falling towards 16 s is open.
  - *Reading*:
    - The closed F1 is identifiable in the cascade's audio. The offset leaves a within-cycle trace about the size of a 4 % F1 change, which neither a cycle-mean tract nor any perfect cycle-mean label captures. And the missing quantity is not the opening. Given the opening, a head on v3.1's features still lacks the clip's coupling sensitivity df, which depends on the glottis and tract together. Given both, it recovers the closed F1 fully.
    - So D-080 asked for the wrong target. The opening is mostly Rd, and even the true opening is not enough. The useful target is the shift itself, df x <a> (acoustic minus closed F1), learned as a shift head or as paired acoustic and closed formant heads.
    - The registered B1 rule tested only the opening and so says "not usable". Under the rule the closed-F1 question stays out of the screening run as an opening question. A screening run could carry it as a shift question; that needs its own registration.
    - For the aVTL split, iVTL is worth a head (or an input), and longer context keeps helping up to the longest window tested. That supports long crops, and long synthetic clips with a fixed anatomy.
    - Aperiodicity heads and source heads for bandwidth earn no place on these oracles.
    - The cascade's aspiration, filtered by the closed tract, is an artefact that leaks the closed F1. A screening render should send aspiration through the coupled tract, or leave it out of the closed-F1 measures.
  - *Was the oracle step useful?* Yes, by the registered test:
    - it dropped two candidate head groups (aperiodicity; source for bandwidths);
    - it changed the closed-F1 target from the opening to the shift;
    - it gave ceilings that change how measures are read (A0's 0.55 against an oracle 1.06 and a label ceiling 0.34);
    - it found the aspiration artefact.

    Proposed as a standing pre-check, Hannah's call.
- *Note (2026-11-05, results seen; from an advisor review; `scripts/analysis/d081_hnr_check.py`, `reports/analysis/d081/hnr_check.txt`)*: **the claim that v3.1's h carries a within-cycle signature beyond the label ceiling is withdrawn.** The audio behind h includes aspiration filtered by the closed tract (I's artefact), and that channel carries the closed F1 directly, which no cycle-mean label can. By the clip's HNR tercile (cut at 15.9 / 28.4 dB), the h head's glottal slope is 0.58 / 0.47 / 0.31 (trunk 1; trunk 2 0.58 / 0.47 / 0.31): it falls as aspiration falls, to about the label ceiling (0.34) at high HNR. Other corrections:
  - The closed label itself reads 0.88 on B1's frames (0.85-0.91 by tercile), not 1. Full recovery reads 0.88 on B's scale, and h + <a> + df1's 1.06 overshoots it. With h carrying the acoustic F1, closed = acoustic / (1 + df1 <a>) is nearly arithmetic, so that arm is the expected ceiling, not a finding about the audio. What it does show is that the opening alone adds only +0.03 to +0.05: df's variation matters.
  - Whether the shift (df x <a>) or df can be read from audio beyond its vowel part is untested (the "is it learnable?" step). It should be probed on features from aspiration-free audio, so "a shift head" is a hypothesis, not a result.
  - I's contrast is the whole drawn offset range. A typical clip-to-clip difference leaves a smaller trace than "4x a 1 % F1 change".
  - The same confound reaches D-080. A0's move to the closed label on cascade validation (+1 Hz) may partly be A0 reading the aspiration's closed-tract shape.
  - Unaffected: I without aspiration (audible); A1; B3, B4 and B5; A3; and the aspiration artefact itself. Of the oracle step's four uses, the ceiling comparison is now pending the aspiration-free check; the other three stand.
- *Note on D-080 (2026-11-05)*: see D-081's last note. The coupled sets' aspiration passes through the closed tract, so A0's cascade-validation gains may partly come from that artefact. The real-voice results (PTDB-TUG, Hillenbrand) are not affected by it.

## D-082 (registered 2026-11-06; seen beforehand: D-081 and its notes, no D-082 result): is the cascade's closed-F1 shift learnable from aspiration-free audio?

*Status*: registered (Hannah, 2026-11-06: "please go ahead and run the no aspiration probe"). D-081's last note withdrew the reading that v3.1's features carry a within-cycle signature beyond the label ceiling. The cascade's aspiration passes through the closed tract and leaks the closed F1. So "a shift head" is a hypothesis. This entry is the "is it learnable?" step, on audio without that channel.
- *Can it hold (before registering)*: without aspiration the only path from the gap offset to the audio is the within-cycle pole movement, and D-081 I found that audible without aspiration (MFB 1.47 dB, 4x an F1 x 1.01 change). So the cue reaches the input. Frozen features bound what v3.1 exposes, not what a trained trunk could learn: a negative here does not rule out a trained shift head; a positive supports registering one.
- *Data*: train_v2kn (train_v2ko's config, `d078_render.config()`, with `hnr_db` None; seed 78; 3000 clips) and val_v2kn (seed 7801; 1000 clips).
